# User manual — The Reader's apps and the other apps of gallaz.ch/eink

Edition of 2 October 2026 · gallaz.ch/eink


<!-- Before you start -->


# About this manual

*One chapter per app; every chapter has the same sections.*

## What it covers

This manual describes the apps published at gallaz.ch/eink: the Reader's family (a whole phone in black and white, text only), the four other apps offered by its installer, the three meditation apps, and a few browser extensions and Linux tools. Several Reader's apps also exist for Windows, macOS and Linux; the computer version is described in the same chapter as the phone version.

Each chapter was written from the app itself: its screens, its settings and the exact words it displays. A label printed in **bold** is a label you will find, spelled that way, in the app. The version described is given under each chapter's title; a newer version may have moved or added something.

## How each chapter is organised

| Section | What you find there |
|---|---|
| What it is | What the app does, and what it deliberately does not do |
| First start | What the app asks the first time, and what to enter |
| The screens | Every screen, and every control on it |
| How to | Step-by-step answers to practical questions |
| Settings | Every setting, its choices and its default |
| Your data | Where your data lives, what travels over the network, export and backup |
| With the other apps | Widgets, launcher tiles and links between apps |
| On the computer | The Windows, macOS or Linux version, when there is one |
| Limits | What the app does not do |
| Messages and problems | What the app's messages mean, and what to do |

## Asking an AI assistant

The manual is also meant to be given to an AI assistant, so that a new user can simply ask a question.

1. Give the assistant this file: the PDF, or the text version published next to it (the same content, lighter to read for a machine).
2. Ask your question in your own words, naming the app: "In Reader's Calendar, how do I make an event repeat every week?"
3. If the answer does not match what you see, tell the assistant which version of the app you have (Android shows it in its own settings, under Apps, at the bottom of the app's page).

A useful instruction to add: "Answer only from the manual. Quote the labels as they are written. If the manual does not say, say so."

## Where to get help

The apps are free and open source. Each app's page on gallaz.ch/eink has a **Source** link to its repository, where problems can be reported.


# Getting the apps

*Android phones and tablets; Windows, macOS and Linux for the desktop versions.*

## The short way: the installer

One small app, Reader's Installer and Updater, installs all the others and keeps them up to date. Every app of the Reader's family is in its list, with ePub Magazine Reader, Le dictionnaire Littré, Clavier Plume and Funky's 2P Games.

1. On the phone, open gallaz.ch/eink and tap **Download the installer (APK)**, then open the downloaded file. Android asks once to let the browser install apps: allow it.
2. Open Reader's Installer. The apps are in the list: tap one, then **Install**. Android asks once to let the installer install apps.
3. That is all. Updates are looked for in the background; on Android 12 and later they install silently for the apps the installer installed itself.

The installer is for 64-bit ARM devices, which is nearly every phone and tablet since 2017. An app installed earlier from F-Droid or from an APK file asks once for confirmation, then follows the installer's updates. The installer has its own chapter in this manual.

## Three other ways

The files are identical and carry the same signature whichever way you choose, so you can change your mind later without uninstalling anything.

### F-Droid (updates arrive by themselves)

1. Install the F-Droid client (Droid-ify or Neo Store work too).
2. Add the repository: on gallaz.ch/eink tap **Add to F-Droid** on the phone, or scan the code shown there from a computer. The repository's address is `https://funkypitt.github.io/fdroid-repo/repo` and its fingerprint is `64191E58C98AD1971BCA0669B6BFAB5C7135D6411A8EAA252741C2D35F16107C`.
3. Refresh, search for the app, install. F-Droid then keeps every app up to date.

### Obtainium (updates straight from GitHub)

Obtainium installs and updates apps from their GitHub releases, with no store in between. With Obtainium on the phone, tap the **Obtainium** button of any app on gallaz.ch/eink. The link opens Obtainium directly and goes through no third-party page.

### The APK file (last resort: no updates)

Each app on gallaz.ch/eink also has an **APK** link. Installed this way, an app never updates: you would have to come back for every new version. Use it to try an app, or on a device where neither F-Droid nor Obtainium can run.

## On the computer

Reader's Calendar, Reader's Tasks, Reader's Notes and Reader's Scanner also exist for Windows, macOS and Linux, and Reader's Podcasts for Linux. The download button is on each app's card on gallaz.ch/eink; in the installer, the app's page links to its desktop version.

### Debian, Ubuntu, Pop!_OS, Mint: the apt repository

The five desktop apps are in a small apt repository, so they update with the rest of the system:

```
sudo mkdir -p /etc/apt/keyrings
curl -fsSL https://funkypitt.github.io/apt-repo/funkypitt.gpg | sudo tee /etc/apt/keyrings/funkypitt.gpg >/dev/null
echo "deb [signed-by=/etc/apt/keyrings/funkypitt.gpg] https://funkypitt.github.io/apt-repo stable main" | sudo tee /etc/apt/sources.list.d/funkypitt.list
sudo apt update && sudo apt install readers-calendar readers-notes readers-tasks readers-scanner readers-podcasts
```

On Arch Linux, each app's repository has a PKGBUILD. Details are at funkypitt.github.io/apt-repo.

### Windows and macOS: the first opening

Reader's Calendar, Reader's Notes and Reader's Tasks come as an .exe for Windows and a .dmg for macOS, on each app's download page: one file, nothing to install. They are small free applications without a paid certificate, so the system distrusts them once, and once only.

- **Windows:** a blue "Windows protected your PC" panel appears the first time. Choose **More info**, then **Run anyway**.
- **macOS:** open the .dmg ("apple-silicon" for an M1 Mac or later, "intel" for an older one), drag the app onto Applications, then right click on the app, choose **Open**, and **Open** again. A double click at that point only says the app cannot be opened. Afterwards it opens like any other.


# Common to the Reader's apps

*What is the same from one Reader's app to the next: the look, the languages, your own server, the credentials file.*

## The look

Every Reader's app is text only, in black and white: no icons, no colours, no cards. Most of them let you choose between **white on black** and **black on white**, either as an entry of the **⋯** menu or as a **colours** setting; each chapter says where. The text size and the font are settings of each app.

## Languages

The Reader's apps exist in English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese and Russian. An app follows the language of the phone; on Android 13 and later, Android's own settings let you choose a language for one app. Where an app is not fully translated, its chapter says so. The meditation apps (Retreat Timer, Retreat Player, Retreat Walk) are in English only.

## No account, your own server

No app asks you to create an account with its author, and none shows advertising. Data stays on the phone unless you connect a server of your own; each chapter's "Your data" section says exactly what travels and to whom, including the few cases where a third party is involved.

Some apps can keep their data in step with your other devices through a server you already have. Two standard kinds of server are used:

| App | What it connects to | Needed? |
|---|---|---|
| Reader's Tasks (phone and computer) | a CalDAV server holding task lists | yes |
| Reader's Calendar on the computer | a CalDAV server holding calendars | yes |
| Reader's Calendar on the phone | nothing: it shows the calendars the phone already syncs | no |
| Reader's Notes (phone and computer) | a WebDAV folder | optional |
| Reader's Scanner (phone and computer) | a WebDAV folder | optional |
| Reader's Recorder | a WebDAV folder | optional |

### What to type for Infomaniak

- **Calendars and tasks (CalDAV):** server `https://sync.infomaniak.com`, a username of the form `AB12345`, and your password. If two-factor authentication is on, create an application password in the Infomaniak Manager and use that one.
- **Files (WebDAV, kDrive):** server `https://ID.connect.kdrive.infomaniak.com`, where ID is the number in your kDrive's web address, then your Infomaniak login and password (an application password if two-factor authentication is on). According to Infomaniak's help pages, WebDAV access is not included in the free kDrive that comes with a free ik.me address.

### What to type for Nextcloud

- **Calendars and tasks (CalDAV):** server `https://your-server/remote.php/dav`.
- **Files (WebDAV):** server `https://your-server/remote.php/dav/files/USERNAME`, where USERNAME is your Nextcloud login name.
- In both cases the username is your Nextcloud login name, and the password is best an app password, created in Nextcloud's security settings.

These Nextcloud addresses were checked with Reader's Tasks, Notes and Scanner on the phone and with Reader's Calendar, Tasks, Notes and Scanner on the computer.

### A CalDAV calendar on the phone

Reader's Calendar on the phone has no server settings: it shows the calendars that Android already holds. To see the calendars of a CalDAV server there, install an app that syncs a CalDAV account into the phone's calendars, such as DAVx5, and add your account in it. The calendars then appear in Reader's Calendar and in the agenda tile of Reader's Launcher.

## The credentials file

Several apps can write your server settings into a file, and read them back, so that a second phone or the computer is set up without typing: look for **export credentials** and **import credentials** in the settings or account screen. The same file serves all the apps: each app writes its own part into it and reads only its own part.

The file contains your passwords. Keep it private, send it only to yourself, and delete it once the other device is set up.

## Widgets and tiles

Most Reader's apps provide standard black-and-white widgets that any Android launcher can show. Reader's Launcher has, in addition, its own tiles for the calendar, tasks, notes, the current book, recordings and more. Each chapter's "With the other apps" section lists what the app offers; the launcher's chapter describes every tile.




<!-- The Reader's family -->


# Reader's Installer and Updater

*Android 1.0.2*

## What it is

Reader's Installer and Updater installs the Reader's apps and keeps them up to date. Its list already holds the whole family, plus four other apps by the same author; you tap one, then **Install**, and from then on the installer looks for new versions by itself. Under the phone's app icon it is called **Reader's Installer**.

It is a modified version of Obtainium, drawn in black and white. Everything Obtainium does is still there: any other app can be added from its page on GitHub, GitLab, Codeberg, F-Droid and many other places, with the same options.

It has no account and no tracking. It is not an app store: there are no descriptions, screenshots, ratings or payments, only a list of apps with their versions.

It runs on Android 8.0 and later, phones, tablets and Android TV. The interface exists in 29 languages; in French and some others a few labels remain in English.

## First start

### What the app asks

- **Notifications.** At the very first start Android asks whether the app may send notifications. They tell you that updates are available or were installed. Refusing does not stop the app from working.
- **Installing apps.** The first time you install something, the app shows **Please allow Reader's Installer to install apps** and Android opens its own screen where you allow this source. It is asked once. If you refuse, the installation stops with **Cancelled**.

There is no account to create and no server to enter.

### The welcome message

A box titled **Welcome** reads: "The Reader's apps are in the list. Tap one to install it; installed apps are then kept up to date by themselves. More on the project page:" followed by a link to the project's page. Close it with **Okay**. It is shown once.

### The list fills itself

The catalogue ships with the app. At the first start its 21 apps are added to the list and their latest versions are looked up:

- Reader's Installer and Updater itself;
- Reader's Launcher, Calendar, Tasks, Notes, Books, Bookshop, Recorder, Podcasts, Audio Player, Mindful Tool, Food Log, Scanner, Feeds, Detox, Files and Night Filter;
- ePub Magazine Reader, Le dictionnaire Littré, Clavier Plume and Funky's 2P Games.

At every start the app also reads the current catalogue from the project's page on GitHub, so an app published later appears by itself. Each app is offered once: one you remove from the list is not added back.

Apps you already have on the phone are recognised and show their installed version. The others show **Not installed**.

If the list is ever empty, it shows **No apps**.

## The screens

### Apps

The main screen, titled **Apps**. From top to bottom:

- **The gear**, top right (**Settings**), opens the settings.
- **The search bar** (**Search**). Type part of a name to narrow the list. At its right end:
  - a button showing the number of apps listed: tap it to select them all; when apps are selected it shows how many and a tap clears the selection;
  - a crossed-out funnel, only while a filter or search is active: it removes the filter;
  - the filter button (**Filter apps**), which opens the filter sheet described below.
- **The banner** **Install/update apps** with its button **Update**. It appears when at least two apps are waiting (to be updated or installed) and at least one of them is an update. With apps selected it reads **Install/update selected apps** and concerns only those.
- **The list.** Each row shows the app's icon (pale when the app is not installed), its name, **By** and the author, and on the right the version: **Not installed**, the installed version, or "installed → latest" when an update is waiting. Under the version is the release date; when it is underlined, tapping it shows what changed (a **Changes** box, or the release page in your browser). When an update is waiting, a round download button (**Update**) sits next to the version.
- **Add**, the button at the bottom right, opens **Add app**. While apps are selected it becomes **Actions**.

Apps with a waiting update are placed at the top. Pinned apps are in bold on a shaded row, above the others. An app that belongs to a category carries a stripe of the category's colour on its left edge.

Gestures on the list:

| Gesture | Effect |
|---|---|
| Pull the list down | Checks every app for a new version; a bar shows the progress |
| Tap a row | Opens the app's page |
| Long press a row | Selects it; further taps then add or remove rows from the selection; the back button clears it |
| Swipe a row to the right | Installs the app, or updates it (the row shows **Install** or **Update**) |
| Swipe a row to the left | Asks to remove the app |
| Double tap the icon | Opens the app, if installed |
| Long press the icon | Shows the app's source web page inside the installer; the back button returns to the list |

During a download the right side of the row shows a bar, the percentage, the size and a button to cancel; then **Installing**.

On a screen at least 840 points wide (most tablets in landscape) the list takes the left side and the app's page the right; until you choose an app the right side reads **Select an app to see details**.

On Android TV the bottom-right button is replaced by an **Add app** button at the end of the list, and two buttons at the top: one switches a selection mode on (**Actions**) and off (**Close**), the other is **Refresh**.

### Filter apps

A sheet with:

- **App name**, **Author**, **App ID**: text that must be contained;
- **Up to date apps** and **Non-installed apps**: two switches, both on; switch one off to hide those apps;
- **App source**: **None**, or one of the sources;
- your categories, to keep only the apps of the ones you tick (**No categories** if you have none).

**Continue** applies the filter, **Cancel** leaves things as they were. If nothing matches, the list shows **No apps for filter**.

### Actions

The sheet opened by **Actions** while apps are selected:

- **Remove**: asks to remove the selected apps.
- **Categorize**: puts them in the categories you tick. If they do not all have the same categories, the app first warns: "This will replace any existing category settings for the selected apps."
- **Pin to top**, or **Unpin from top** if one of them is pinned.
- **Install/update selected apps**, when at least one of them can be installed or updated.
- **Share selected app URLs**, **Share app configuration as HTML link**, **Share - Reader's Installer export**: see "Share an app".
- **Download release asset**: for each app, a box titled "Select release asset" asks which file; it is saved to the phone's Download folder.
- **Mark selected apps as updated**: asks "Mark … selected apps as updated?" with the note **Only works for apps with version detection disabled.**; answer **Yes** or **No**.

### The app's page

Opened by tapping a row. Pull it down to check this one app for a new version.

- **Header**: icon, name, **By** and the author. Tapping the icon opens the app if it is installed.
- **Version**: for example "1.4.0 Installed / Latest" when you are up to date, "1.4.0 Installed" and below it "1.5.0 Latest" when an update is waiting, or **Not installed** and the latest version. Under it, the name of the file that will be downloaded, then the date and time of the release (tap it to see the changes when it is underlined).
- **Last update check:** with the date and time, or **Never**.
- **Source**: the address the app is read from (a tap opens it in the browser, a long press copies it) and the app's identifier. For an installed app, the **Certificate Hash** of its signature follows; a long press copies it. Below, **Download release asset** saves a file of the release to the phone's Download folder without installing it.
- **Also on the desktop (Windows, macOS, Linux):** and a link, for the apps that have a computer version (Calendar, Tasks, Notes, Podcasts, Scanner). The link opens in the browser.
- **Categories**: tap a category to put the app in it or take it out; **+** creates a category; a long press on a category edits it.
- The text you may have typed in the app's **About** option.

The bar at the bottom holds, from left to right:

| Button | Effect |
|---|---|
| Pencil (**Additional options**) | Opens the options of this app |
| Gear (**Settings**), only if installed | Opens Android's own settings page for that app |
| Arrow leaving a square (**Open release page**), when the source gives one | Opens the release page in the browser |
| Check mark (**Mark updated**), only when version detection is off for the app | Tells the installer the latest version is the one you have |
| Circular arrow (**Reset install status**), only for an up-to-date app with version detection off or track-only | Marks the app as not installed |
| Bin (**Remove**) | Asks to remove the app |
| **Install** or **Update** | Downloads and installs; the size of the download is written under the label when known |

For a track-only app the main button reads **Mark installed** or **Mark updated**. The main button is grey when the app is up to date or another download is running. During a download a bar with a cancel button appears above the buttons. When it has finished the app shows **Installed** or **Apps updated** and returns to the list.

If the source reports that the project has moved, the page shows **Repository moved** with the **New URL** and two buttons: **Update URL** adopts the new address, **Dismiss** ignores it. Checks for that app are paused until you choose.

### Additional options

Opened with the pencil; the title is "Additional options for" and the app's name. There is no save button: going back saves.

The apps of the catalogue, read from the author's F-Droid repository, have these options:

| Option | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **App ID or name** | filled in | Which app of the repository this entry follows. Do not change it |
| **Auto-select highest version code APK** | off | Takes the file with the highest internal version number |
| **Try selecting suggested versionCode APK** | on | Takes the version the repository recommends |
| **Track-only** | off | Follows the versions without downloading or installing |
| **Trim version string with RegEx**, **Match group to use for "Trim version string with RegEx"** | empty | Keeps only part of the version text |
| **Reconcile version string with version detected from OS** | on | Version detection: compares with the version Android reports for the installed app |
| **Use release date as version string (pseudo-version)** | off | For apps whose version cannot be detected |
| **Use app versionCode as OS-detected version** | off | Compares internal version numbers instead of version names |
| **Filter APKs by regular expression**, **Invert regular expression (Filter APKs by regular expression)** | empty, off | Chooses among several files |
| **Attempt to filter APKs by CPU architecture if possible** | on | Keeps the files made for your phone's processor |
| **Minimum age for updates** | **Use global default** | Waits this many days before offering a new version: **None**, 1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 14 or 30 days |
| **App name**, **Author** | empty | Replaces the name or author shown in the list |
| **Set Google Play as the installation source (if Shizuku or Root is used)** | off | |
| **Allow insecure HTTP requests** | off | |
| **Expected signing certificate hashes** | empty | Up to four hashes; an update signed otherwise is refused |
| **Exempt from background updates (if enabled)** | off | This app is never installed in the background |
| **Skip update notifications** | off | No notification for this app |
| **About** | empty | A note of your own, shown on the app's page |
| **Refresh app details before download** | off | Checks the version again just before downloading |

Clavier Plume, read from GitHub, has the same options from **Track-only** downwards, and instead of the first three: **Include prereleases**, **Fallback to older releases** (on), **Filter release titles by regular expression**, **Filter release notes by regular expression**, **Verify the 'latest' tag**, **Sort method** (**Release date** by default, **Name (smart)**, **None**, **Name**), **Use latest asset upload as release date**, **Use release title as version string**, **Include ZIP files**, **Filter APKs inside ZIP**, **Include tarball files (tar.gz, etc.)** and **Filter APKs inside tarball**.

### Add app

Opened with **Add**. It serves to follow an app that is not in the catalogue.

- **App source URL**: the address of the app's page (for example its GitHub page). The **+** at the right adds the app once the address is recognised.
- **Search (some sources only)**: type a name and tap the magnifying glass; you tick the sources to search, then pick one result.
- At the bottom, **Supported sources** lists the sources the installer knows (tap one to open its site), and **Crowdsourced app configurations** opens, in the browser, a list of ready-made configurations kept by the Obtainium community.

Once an address is recognised the page shows **Override source** (to force the kind of source, useful for a self-hosted site), a note from the source if any, and the same options as on the **Additional options** page, plus your categories.

### Settings

Opened with the gear. Four rows lead to pages described under "Settings" below: **Import/export**, **Updates**, **Source-specific** and **Appearance**. Four more rows follow:

- **App source** opens the project's page on GitHub in the browser;
- **Based on Obtainium (GPL-3.0)** opens Obtainium's page;
- **Help/wiki** opens Obtainium's help pages;
- **App logs** opens the journal of what the installer did; if there is nothing, it shows **No logs**.

### App logs

The journal of the last seven days, each line with its date and its level (**Info**, **Warning**, **Error**, **Debug**). The text can be selected. The floating bar at the bottom has: two arrows to jump to the top and the bottom, a filter to show only the last 7, 5, 4, 3, 2 or 1 days, **Share** to send the text to another app, and the bin (**Remove**), which asks **Clear logs** ("This will delete all stored logs. Continue?") and confirms with **Logs cleared**.

## How to

### Install an app of the list

1. Tap the app in the list.
2. Tap **Install**.
3. The first time, allow the installer to install apps when Android asks.
4. Confirm in Android's installation box.

You can also swipe the row to the right. If you leave the installer during the download, a notification **Complete app installation** ("Reader's Installer must be open to install apps") calls you back.

### Install several apps at once

1. Long press one app, then tap the others (or tap the number in the search bar to select them all).
2. Tap **Actions**, then **Install/update selected apps**.
3. A box titled "Change" and the number of apps lists them under **Updates** and **Non-installed apps**, each with a tick. Updates are ticked; apps not yet installed are ticked only when there is no update in the box. Tick the ones you want (**Select all** ticks everything) and tap **Continue**.

### Update everything

When the banner **Install/update apps** is shown, tap **Update**, check the ticks and tap **Continue**. Otherwise update one app with the round button on its row, by swiping its row to the right, or with **Update** on its page.

### Check for new versions now

Pull the list down. To check a single app, open its page and pull it down.

### Let updates install themselves

Nothing to do: the installer checks every 6 hours and installs in the background what it can. An update is installed without asking only when all of this holds:

- the phone runs Android 12 or later;
- Android records the app as installed by Reader's Installer;
- the update is a single file;
- the app itself is built for a recent version of Android;
- **Enable background silent installs** is on and the app is not exempt;
- the app is not the installer itself.

In every other case you get a notification **Updates available** ("… has an update."); tap it, then **Update**. The installer's own updates always come this way.

### See what changed in a version

Tap the underlined date under the version, in the list or on the app's page.

### Remove an app from the list, or uninstall it

1. Swipe its row to the left, or tap the bin on its page, or select it and choose **Actions**, **Remove**.
2. The box **Remove app?** has two switches for an installed app: **Remove from Reader's Installer** (on) and **Uninstall from device** (off). Set them as you wish.
3. Tap **Continue**. Android asks you to confirm an uninstallation.

With only the first switch on, the app stays on the phone and simply stops being followed.

### Bring back a catalogue app you removed

1. Tap **Add** and type `https://funkypitt.github.io/fdroid-repo/repo` in **App source URL**.
2. In **Override source** choose **F-Droid third-party repo**.
3. In **App ID or name** type the app's name, for example "Reader's Notes".
4. Tap **+**.

### Add an app that is not in the list

1. Tap **Add**.
2. Paste the address of the app's page into **App source URL**, or search for it.
3. Tap **+**. If the release has several files, **Pick an APK** asks which one; choose and tap **Continue**.
4. The app's page opens: tap **Install**.

From another app (a browser, for instance) you can also share a page to **Reader's Installer**: **Add app** opens with the address filled in.

### Follow an app without installing it

On **Add app** or in the app's **Additional options**, switch on **Track-only**. The installer tells you about new versions; you mark them yourself with **Mark updated**.

### Sort apps into categories

On the app's page, tap **+** under the source, give a **Category name**, pick a **Colour** and tap **Continue**. To put several apps in a category, select them, tap **Actions**, **Categorize**. To show the list by category, set **Group by** to **Category** in **Appearance**. Categories are renamed, recoloured and deleted at the bottom of **Appearance** (tap one) or by a long press on the app's page.

### Pin an app to the top

Select it, tap **Actions**, then **Pin to top**. **Unpin from top** undoes it.

### Share an app

Select one or more apps and tap **Actions**:

- **Share selected app URLs** sends their addresses as text;
- **Share app configuration as HTML link** sends links that open in Reader's Installer on another phone and offer to import the app with its options;
- **Share - Reader's Installer export** sends a file holding these apps.

### Save the list and bring it back

1. **Settings**, **Import/export**, **Pick export directory**: choose a folder.
2. Tap **Reader's Installer export**. The app answers "Exported to" and the folder.
3. On the other phone, **Settings**, **Import/export**, **Reader's Installer import**, and choose the file.

### Change the language, the colours or the size of the rows

**Settings**, **Appearance**: **Language**, **Theme**, **Colour scheme**, **App list density**.

## Settings

### Updates

| Setting | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **Background update checking interval** | A slider from **Never - manual only**, then 15 minutes, up to 30 days | 6 hours |
| **Enable background silent installs** | on, off | on |
| **Disable background updates when not on Wi-Fi** | on, off | off |
| **Disable background updates when not charging** | on, off | off |
| **Run background update check now** | A button: runs at once what the background check does | |
| **Enable certificate pinning** | on, off. A stricter check of the secure connection to GitHub, Codeberg, GitLab and RuStore | off |
| **Check for updates on startup** | on, off | off |
| **Check for updates on opening an app detail page** | on, off | off |
| **Only check installed and track-only apps for updates** | on, off | off |
| **Global APK filter regex** | Text: a pattern applied to every app that has none of its own | empty |
| **Automatically remove externally uninstalled apps** | on, off. An app uninstalled outside the installer also leaves the list | off |
| **Include pre-releases by default** | on, off. For apps you add | off |
| **Show app downgrade errors** | on, off | on |
| **Hide downgrades as updates** | on, off. A "latest" version older than yours is not offered | on |
| **Skip update/install confirmation dialog** | on, off. **Update** on the banner starts at once | off |
| **Minimum age for updates** | **None**, 1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 14, 30 days | **None** |
| **Allow parallel downloads** | on, off | on |
| **Share new apps with "Verified Apps" (if available)** | on, off. Has an effect only if the Verified Apps app is installed; **About** opens its page | on |
| **Warn about signing certificate changes** | on, off | on |
| **Installation method** | **System**, **Shizuku**, **External app**, **Root** | **System** |
| **Set Google Play as the installation source (if Shizuku or Root is used)** (with Shizuku or Root) | on, off | off |
| **Choose an installer app** (with External app) | The installer apps found on the phone | **None chosen** |

The four settings from **Enable background silent installs** to **Run background update check now** are shown on Android 11 and later (or with the Shizuku method), and only while the interval is not **Never - manual only**.

The little help button on **Enable background silent installs** reads: "Background updates may not be possible for all apps." and "The success of a background install can only be determined when Reader's Installer is opened." The one on **Warn about signing certificate changes** reads: "Before installing, check that the downloaded APK is signed with the same certificate as the installed app. Apps with expected signing certificate hashes configured are always checked."

**Shizuku** and **Root** are for phones set up for them; if the access is not given the method goes back to **System**.

### Appearance

| Setting | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **Theme** | Three buttons: **Follow system**, **Light**, **Dark** | **Follow system** |
| **Use pure black dark theme** (not with Light) | on, off | on |
| **Colour scheme** | **Standard** (black, white and greys), **Vibrant**, **Expressive**, **Use Material You** (your phone's colours) | **Standard** |
| **Select colour** (not with Material You) | A colour picker with two tabs, **Standard** and **Custom** | grey |
| **App sort by** | **Author/name**, **Name/author**, **As added**, **Release date** | **Name/author** |
| **App sort order** | Two arrows: **Ascending**, **Descending** | **Ascending** |
| **Language** | **Follow system** or one of 29 languages | **Follow system** |
| **Use the system font** (Android 10 and later) | on, off | off |
| **Show source webpage in app view** | on, off. Tapping a row shows the source's web page first; the round button at the bottom (**More**) then opens the app's page | off |
| **Pin updates to top of apps view** | on, off | on |
| **Move non-installed apps to bottom of apps view** | on, off | off |
| **Group by** | **None**, **Category**, **Source** | **None** |
| **Don't show 'track-only' warnings** | on, off | off |
| **Collapse all groups upon startup** | on, off | off |
| **Don't show APK origin warnings** | on, off | off |
| **Highlight less obvious touch targets** | on, off. Shades the version and date you can tap | off |
| **Disable swipe actions on app list** | on, off | off |
| **Always use phone layout** | on, off. No two-pane view on wide screens | off |
| **App list density** | **Standard**, **Compact**, **Minimal (no icon or author)** | **Standard** |
| **Action banner** | **All** (also when only installations are waiting), **Updates**, **None** | **Updates** |
| **Enable tactile feedback** | on, off | on |
| Categories | Your categories as buttons, and **+**; **No categories** when there are none | none |

When the list is grouped, each group has a heading with the number of its apps; tap the heading to fold or unfold it.

### Source-specific

- **GitHub personal access token (increases rate limit)**: optional. Only useful if you follow many apps on GitHub and meet "Too many requests". The catalogue does not need it.
- **'sky22333/hubproxy' instance for GitHub requests**: optional address of a relay for GitHub (the app suggests "gh-proxy.com").
- **Check if repository was moved (increases API calls)**: off by default.
- **GitLab personal access token**, **Codeberg access token**: optional.

The question mark in a field opens the web page that explains how to get the token.

### Import/export

- **Pick export directory**: the folder for exports; a check mark shows it is set.
- **Reader's Installer export**: writes the file now.
- **Automatically export on changes**: off by default. Rewrites an export each time the list changes.
- **Auto-export file name (leave blank for a timestamped name)**.
- **Export installed apps only**: off by default.
- **Include settings**: **None**, **Exclude secrets** (the default), **All**.
- **Reader's Installer import**: reads an export file.
- **Import from URL list**: a page where you paste addresses, one per line, in **App URL list**, or load them with **Import from URLs in file (like OPML)**, then tap **Import**.
- **Import GitHub starred repositories**: asks a GitHub **Username** and offers the projects that person starred.

The last four export settings appear once a folder is chosen.

## Your data

The list of apps, their options, your categories and the settings live on the phone only, in the installer's own storage. There is no account and nothing is sent to the author.

What goes over the network:

- at each start, the catalogue is read from the project's page on GitHub;
- to check and download the catalogue's apps, the installer reads the author's F-Droid repository (hosted on GitHub Pages); Clavier Plume is read from GitHub's releases;
- for apps you add yourself, it contacts the source you gave;
- the links (**App source**, **Help/wiki**, desktop versions, release pages) open in your browser.

Downloaded files are kept in the installer's temporary storage and deleted once installed; unfinished downloads older than seven days are cleared at start. Files saved with **Download release asset** go to the phone's Download folder. The journal keeps seven days.

**Export.** The file is named "obtainium-export-" and the date, ending in ".json". An automatic export ends in "-auto.json", or carries the name you typed, and replaces the previous one. With **Include settings** on **All**, the app warns: "The exported file will contain all settings in cleartext, which may include sensitive values such as tokens. Anyone with the file can read them." With **Exclude secrets**, access tokens are left out.

**Import.** If the file contains apps already in your list, the app asks: "This will overwrite … existing app(s) and replace any settings included in the file. Continue?". Then it reports "Imported … apps", with "+ settings" when the file held settings.

Uninstalling the installer removes its list and settings. The apps it installed stay on the phone, and so do the export files in the folder you chose.

## With the other apps

- This is the app that installs and updates all the others of the family. Each of them can also be installed by other means; the installer recognises an app that is already there.
- It appears in Android's share menu as **Reader's Installer** for text: share a link to an app's page and **Add app** opens with it. If the text has no link, it says "No URL found in shared text".
- Links beginning with `readersinstaller://`, made by **Share app configuration as HTML link**, open in the installer.
- The pages of Calendar, Tasks, Notes, Podcasts and Scanner link to their computer versions.
- It has no home-screen widget.

## Limits

- Android only, version 8.0 or later.
- The installer cannot update itself without your confirmation.
- A background installation is only confirmed for sure when you open the installer; until then a notification may say an app "may have been updated".
- A version older than the one you have is not installed.
- With **External app** as the installation method, an app delivered as several split files cannot be installed.
- The catalogue offers each app once; a removed one comes back only if you add it yourself.
- The search on **Add app** works with some sources only.

## Messages and problems

| Message | Meaning and what to do |
|---|---|
| **Please allow Reader's Installer to install apps** | Android needs your permission; allow it on the screen that opens |
| **Cancelled** | You refused the permission or closed a box; start again |
| **Complete app installation** / "Reader's Installer must be open to install apps" | A download is ready; come back to the installer |
| **Updates available** / "… has an update." / "… and … more apps have updates." | Tap the notification and update |
| **Apps updated** / "… was updated to …." | Done in the background |
| **App updates attempted** / "… may have been updated to …." | The installer could not confirm; open it to see |
| **Failed to update applications** / "Failed to update … to …." | Update the app by hand from its page |
| **Error checking for updates** | A check failed in the background; the text names the app and the cause |
| **Apps removed** / "… was removed due to this error: …" | An entry could not be read and was taken off the list |
| "Too many requests (rate limited) - try again in … minutes" | The source limits the number of requests; wait, or add a token in **Source-specific** |
| **No APK found**, **Could not find a suitable release**, **Could not determine release version** | The source has no file the installer can use for this app |
| **App already added** | The app is in your list already |
| **URL does not match a known source**, "Not a valid … app URL" | The address is not an app page the installer understands |
| "… needs additional credentials (in Settings)" | The source wants a token; see **Source-specific** |
| **Download failed**, "Error" followed by a number | The download did not go through; try again later |
| **The APK could not be parsed (incompatible or partial download)** | The file is damaged or not made for this phone; try again |
| **Cannot install an older version of an app** | The version offered is older than yours |
| **Downloaded package ID does not match existing app ID** | The file is not the app expected; do not install it |
| **Signing certificate mismatch**: "… is signed with a different certificate than the installed version. Installing it will most likely fail. Only continue if you trust this source." | The update is not signed like your copy. **Don't install** stops; **Install anyway** tries |
| "The downloaded file for … does not match the signing certificate hash you configured. The install has been blocked." | Applies to apps where you filled in **Expected signing certificate hashes** |
| **Install confirmation was not received in time — the app may not have been installed** | Check the app's version, and install again if needed |
| **Warning**: "The app source is '…' but the release package comes from '…'. Continue?" | The file is hosted elsewhere than the app's page; **Don't show this again** silences the warning |
| "App is track-only" / "The app will be tracked for updates, but Reader's Installer will not be able to download or install it." | You chose **Track-only**, or the source only allows that |
| **The latest release is younger than the minimum update age (… days) and this source does not provide older releases.** | Caused by **Minimum age for updates**; wait or lower it |
| **Choose an external installer in Settings first** | **External app** is chosen but no app is picked |
| **Root access was not granted**, **Dhizuku or Shizuku services are not running**, **Old Shizuku version (<11) - update it** | The installation method you chose is not available; the method returns to **System** |
| **No file picker available. Please install a file manager app.** | Android found no app to choose a file or folder |
| **Invalid input**, **Invalid regular expression** | A field holds something the app cannot use |
| **Import errors**: "… of … apps imported." and **The following URLs had errors:** | Some addresses could not be added; each is listed with its reason |
| **A category with this name already exists** | Choose another name |
| **Some errors occurred** | A box listing, for each cause, the apps concerned; a long press copies the text |
| **An unexpected error occurred.** with **Close** | The app could not draw a screen; close and reopen it |

If an app keeps showing **Not installed** or a wrong version although it is on the phone, pull the list down. An app imported from a list of addresses may show "not installed" wrongly; installing it again through the installer puts this right and does not touch its data.


# Reader's Launcher

*Android 1.19.0 (Android 8.0 or later)*

## What it is

Reader's Launcher is a home screen for Android made of words only: black and white, no icons, no colours, no wallpaper. Each home page is a fixed column of tiles that never scrolls: app names, categories (a name that opens a list of apps), and text widgets such as the clock, the weather, the next event or the first task. A small grid of key apps can sit at the bottom, and a book or a reading app waits on each side of the home screen, one swipe away.

It deliberately has no icons (except the monochrome glyphs of the grid and of the weather), no wallpaper, no folders with pictures, no account and no tracking. It never talks to a calendar or task server itself; only the weather tile uses the network.

The app follows the language of the phone: English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese or Russian.

## First start

The first time, the page is empty and shows **hold to begin** in its middle. A long press anywhere opens the menu from which you add your first tile.

As long as Reader's Launcher is not the phone's home app, a line **set as default launcher** stays at the top of the home screen. Tap it: Android's home-app settings open, where you choose Reader's Launcher. The line disappears once this is done.

There is no account and no server to enter. Permissions are asked only when a function needs them:

| Permission | Asked when | What it is for |
|---|---|---|
| Approximate location | you add the **weather** tile | the weather of the place where you are; you can refuse and choose a city instead |
| Calendar (read) | you add or tap the **agenda** tile | showing your next events |
| Tasks.org tasks (read and write) | you choose Tasks.org in **tasks setup** | showing, ticking and adding tasks |
| Usage access (a special access given in Android's settings) | you open **open apps**, or **usage access (open apps list)** in the settings | the list of recently used apps |
| Placing a widget | you add a widget **from apps** | Android may ask you to allow each widget |

The launcher also lists the installed apps, opens the notification shade, vibrates lightly on long presses and can ask Android to uninstall an app; none of these shows a prompt. The other Reader's apps (Tasks, Books, Notes, Mindful Tool, Recorder, Night Filter) are read without any prompt.

## The screens

### The home screen

A home page is a column of tiles on a fixed grid of cells. An app or category name takes 2 cells, a two-line widget 3, the clock or the weather 4, a widget from another app the height you give it. When the cells are used up nothing more can be added to that page.

Under the tiles, if you have more than one page, a row of dots shows where you are (● for the current page, ○ for the others). At the very bottom, the grid of key apps, if you added one, is the same on every page.

Gestures on the home screen:

| Gesture | Effect |
|---|---|
| tap an app name | opens the app |
| tap a category name | opens its list of apps |
| double tap an app name or a grid square | lists the app's shortcuts |
| long press a tile | the tile's menu |
| long press on empty space | the home menu |
| double tap on empty space | flips between white on black and black on white |
| swipe up | the list of all apps |
| swipe up and hold | **open apps**, the recently used apps |
| swipe down | the notification shade |
| swipe left | the next home page; past the last one, the right side (book or reading app) |
| swipe right | the previous home page; before the first one, the left side (book or reading app) |
| the phone's Home button | back to the home screen from any screen of the launcher |

Several tiles use the sideways swipe for themselves (agenda, tasks, book, notes, recordings, mindful): to change page, swipe on another part of the screen.

### The home menu

A long press on empty space shows:

- **add app / category**: choose one or several apps (see "Add an app" and "Make a category").
- **add app grid**: adds the grid of key apps at the bottom (shown only while there is no grid).
- **add widget**: opens the widget list.
- **add a home page**: adds an empty page after the last one and goes to it.
- **remove this page**: shown on an empty page other than the first.
- **black on white** or **white on black**: switches to the other theme.
- **settings**.

When the page has no room left for a line of text, **the home screen is full** replaces **add app / category** and **add widget**.

The same entries are repeated, in smaller text, at the bottom of every tile's menu, because a full page has no empty space left to press.

### A tile's menu

A long press on a tile shows its name and, depending on the tile:

- **open**: opens the app or the category.
- **shortcuts** (app): the app's shortcuts, with **open** below them; **no shortcuts** when it has none.
- **edit** (category): the category's edit screen.
- **rename** (app, category).
- **configure** (tasks, mindful, some widgets from apps), **choose calendars** (agenda).
- **height: 3** (widget from an app): each tap adds one cell, from 2 to 8, then starts again at 2.
- the weather entries (see "The weather tile").
- **move up**, **move down**.
- **move to the next page**, **move to the previous page**, when such a page exists.
- **arrange tiles**, when the page has more than two tiles.
- **app info**: Android's page for the app.
- **uninstall**: for apps that are not part of the system.
- **remove**: takes the tile off the home screen (the app itself stays installed).

### The list of all apps (apps)

Swipe up. The screen **apps** lists every app alphabetically, with a **search** field already active: type a few letters (accents do not matter), and the keyboard's action key opens the first result. Apps of a work profile carry the word **work** under their name. Tap the title line (←) to go back.

Tap an app to open it. Long press it for:

- **open**, **shortcuts**;
- **add to home**: adds its name to the page you were on (not shown if it is already on a home page);
- **add to category**: then choose the category (shown when you have at least one);
- **add app grid**: puts the app in the first free square of the grid (shown when the grid has a free square);
- **rename**: the name shown everywhere in the launcher; type the original name again to drop the change;
- **hide**: removes it from this list;
- **app info**, **uninstall**.

The same list serves to choose apps: titled **choose apps** (tap to select, the title then reads **tap to select · 2 selected**, and **add** at the top right confirms) or **choose an app** (one tap chooses). In these lists hidden apps are shown too.

### A category

Tap a category name: its apps appear as a list, with **edit** at the top right. Tap an app to open it, double tap for its shortcuts, long press for **open**, **shortcuts**, **remove from category**, **app info**, **uninstall**. An empty category shows **add apps**.

The edit screen offers **rename**, **add apps** (the app list with the current apps already selected; deselect one to take it out), and the list of apps under **drag a tile to move it**: long press a line and drag it to reorder; tap a line for **remove from category**.

### The grid of key apps

A row of 3 to 5 squares at the bottom of every page, for the apps you cannot do without. Each app appears as a monochrome glyph of its icon; an empty square is a dot.

- Tap a filled square to open the app; double tap for its shortcuts.
- Tap an empty square, or long press any square, for its menu: **add app / category** (here it picks a single app for that square), or, on a filled square, **open**, **shortcuts**, **change app**, **clear**, **app info**; then **squares: 5** (each tap changes the number: 5, 3, 4, 5…) and **remove** (removes the whole grid).

### Add widget

The list has two parts.

**built in**:

| Widget | What the list says |
|---|---|
| **clock** | time and date |
| **weather** | time and today's weather, tap for five days |
| **mot du jour** | the Littré's word of the day, tap for the definition |
| **book** | the book you were reading, tap to carry on |
| **notes** | the latest note, swipe for the others, + writes one |
| **recorder** | record with one tap (Reader's Recorder); the latest recording under it |
| **recordings** | listen to Reader's Recorder's recordings, swipe from the latest to the older ones |
| **food log** | one touch opens Reader's Food Log's viewfinder for a new photo |
| **night filter** | the screen gray, warm and dim: Reader's Night Filter on and off |
| **mindful** | a bowl every X minutes and a timed meditation, started and stopped here (needs Reader's Mindful Tool) |
| **agenda** | next event, swipe for the following ones |
| **tasks** | first task of a list, swipe for the following ones |

**from apps**: every widget offered by the apps installed on the phone, with a **search** field. Tap one; Android may ask you to allow it, and the widget may open its own setup screen. If it does not fit in the cells left, it is refused with **the home screen is full**.

### The clock tile

The time in large figures (12 or 24 hours, as the phone is set) and the date under it. Tap it to open the alarms of the phone's clock app.

### The weather tile

The time on the left; on the right a weather glyph, the current temperature and the day's minimum / maximum; the place name under the time (**here** when it has no name). Tap to switch between today and five days (day, glyph, maximum/minimum). The forecast is refreshed every half hour.

Its menu adds:

- **source: MétéoSuisse** or **source: Open-Meteo** (information only);
- **refresh**;
- **units: °C** (tap for °F and back);
- **location: automatic**, with **choose a city** under it: tap to type a **city name**; the first place found is used;
- when a city is set, **location: (the city)** to change it, and **use automatic location**.

### The agenda tile

Two lines: the title of an event, then when it takes place (**today**, **tomorrow**, the weekday or the date, the hours or **all day**), followed by its rank such as 2/7 when there are several.

- Tap: opens the event in the calendar app. If there is no event, a page **agenda** opens instead, with **open the agenda**, **+ new event**, and the events of **today** and **tomorrow**.
- **+** at the right: creates an event in the calendar app.
- Swipe left for the next event, right for the previous one.

The tile chooses by itself which event to show first: an event with hours takes the place from one hour before it starts until it ends; otherwise today's all-day event is shown; otherwise the next thing to come. It looks one year ahead.

Its setup screen (when you add the tile, or **choose calendars** in its menu) has two lists. Under **opens with**: the calendar apps of the phone, Reader's Calendar first and chosen by default when installed, and **whatever the system picks**. Under **calendars to show**: every calendar of the phone with its account; tap to select or deselect (all are selected for a new tile). Confirm with **ok** at the top right.

### The tasks tile

A box ☐, the title of the first open task, and under it the name of the list and the rank (1/4). 

- ☐ completes the task.
- **+** opens the prompt **new task**; **add** creates it in the list.
- Swipe left for the next task, right for the previous one.
- Tap the text: opens the tasks app.

The tile refreshes as soon as the tasks app changes something.

### The mot du jour tile

The word of the day of the dictionary app Le Littré, with **mot du jour** under it. Tap for the definition on a page of its own, with **open in Le Littré** at the bottom. If Le Littré is not installed the tile reads "Le Littré" and a tap opens the app's web page.

### The book tile

The title of the book you last opened in Reader's Books, with **book**, its rank and the percentage read under it. Swipe left for the books opened before it, right to come back. Tap to carry on reading in Reader's Books. With an empty shelf the tile reads **open a book**.

### The notes tile

The title of the latest note of Reader's Notes (**untitled** if it has none), and under it **notes**, the rank and the beginning of the text. Swipe sideways through the thirty most recent notes; tap to open the note; **+** writes a new one. Without any note: **no note yet**.

### The recorder tile

**record** with a ● at the right, and the latest recording (title and length) under it. Tap ● to start: the tile reads **recording · 00:12** and **tap ■ to stop**; ■ stops. **paused** replaces **recording** while Reader's Recorder is paused. Tap the text to open the latest recording in Reader's Recorder (or the app itself while recording).

### The recordings tile

One recording of Reader's Recorder: its title, and under it **recordings**, the rank, the date and the length. ▶ plays it without leaving the home screen (❚❚ pauses; the line then shows position / length). Swipe left for older recordings, right for more recent ones. Tap the text to open the recording. Without any: **nothing recorded yet**.

### The food log tile

**photograph**, with **food log** under it and ◉ at the right. One touch opens the viewfinder of Reader's Food Log for a new photo.

### The night filter tile

**filter on** or **filter off**, with a switch at the right (● on, ○ off) that turns Reader's Night Filter on and off. Tap the text to open the app. When the caption reads **one step left in the app**, the filter still lacks an authorisation: the app opens and explains it.

### The mindful tile

Two functions of Reader's Mindful Tool: **a bowl every 10 min** and **meditate 20 min**. When you place the tile you choose **both** (swipe sideways to go from one to the other), **a bowl every X minutes only** or **meditation period only**; **configure** in the tile's menu changes this choice.

- Tap the words to change the minutes (**a bowl every how many minutes?**, **meditate how many minutes?**; from 1 to 240).
- ▶ starts, ■ stops.
- While bowls are running: **next at 14:30 · in 04:12**, then the number already **rung**.
- While a meditation is running: **until 14:50 · 12:40 left** and a thin progress line. A tap on the words then opens the app.

### Widgets from other apps

They show and work as on any home screen. A long press opens the launcher's tile menu, where **height** sets how many cells the widget takes and **configure** reopens the widget's own setup if it has one.

### Open apps

Swipe up and hold your finger still for a moment. **open apps** lists up to twelve apps used in the last six hours, the most recent first. Tap to go to one; long press for its Android info page. Without the authorisation, the line **allow usage access to list open apps** leads to Android's setting. With nothing to show: **no recent apps**.

### Arrange tiles

From a tile's menu or from the settings. The tiles of the page are listed by name (widgets with a dash in front), under a count such as **7 of 16 cells in use** and the hint **drag a tile to move it**. Long press a line, then drag it to its new place. With several pages, the line **page 1 of 2** / **tap for the next page** changes page.

### Hidden apps

In the settings. Lists the apps you hid; each line carries **show again**: tap it to bring the app back in the list of all apps. Empty: **nothing here**.

### A side of the home screen: the book

Swipe right on the first page for the left side, left on the last page for the right side.

An empty side shows:

- **open a book** (**epub, mobi, fb2 or plain text**): tap anywhere to choose a file.
- **or a reading app**: Kindle, Kobo… opened by the swipe instead of a book kept here.
- on the right side only, **or a second home page**: another page of tiles here instead of a book; the book slot then sits after the last page.
- **swipe sideways to go back home**.

With a book, the page shows the title at the top, the text, and at the bottom the chapter name, the page within the chapter and the percentage of the book (3/12 · 41%).

| Gesture | Effect |
|---|---|
| tap the right half | next page |
| tap the left half | previous page |
| long press | the reader's menu |
| swipe sideways, or Back | return to the home screen |

The reader's menu: **chapters** (the list of chapters, the current one highlighted; tap one to go there), **larger text** and **smaller text** (two points at a time, between 12 and 40; the menu shows the change, for example 20 → 22), **open another book**, **remove from this side**, **home**.

The book reopens where you stopped. The screen stays on while you read and is released after 15 minutes without a page turn. The reader uses the launcher's theme and font.

### Settings

Long press, then **settings**. Described under "Settings" below.

### Restore a backup

Shown after you choose a backup file. See "Move the home screen to another phone".

## How to

### Add an app to the home screen

1. Long press on empty space (or on any tile), then **add app / category**.
2. Tap the app; its line is highlighted.
3. Tap **add** at the top right.

Or: swipe up, long press the app, **add to home**.

### Make a category

1. Long press, **add app / category**.
2. Tap several apps.
3. Tap **add**, type the **name of the category**, then **add**.

To change it later: long press the category, **edit** (add, remove, reorder apps) or **rename**. To put one more app in it from the list of all apps: long press the app, **add to category**.

### Add a widget

1. Long press, **add widget**.
2. Tap a widget under **built in**, or one **from apps**.
3. Answer the setup screen if one appears (agenda, tasks, mindful, some widgets from apps).

### Move, reorder or remove a tile

- One step: long press the tile, **move up** or **move down**.
- Freely: long press the tile, **arrange tiles**, then long press a line and drag it.
- To another page: long press the tile, **move to the next page** or **move to the previous page**.
- Remove: long press the tile, **remove**.

### Add or remove a home page

- Add: long press, **add a home page**. Or, on the empty right side, **or a second home page**.
- Remove: empty the page (move or remove its tiles), long press on it, **remove this page**. The first page cannot be removed.

### Set up the grid of key apps

1. Long press, **add app grid**.
2. Tap a square, **add app / category**, choose the app.
3. To change the number of squares, long press a square and tap **squares: 5** until it suits you.

### Rename, hide or uninstall an app

- Rename: long press its tile, **rename**; or in the list of all apps, long press, **rename**.
- Hide: swipe up, long press the app, **hide**. To bring it back: settings, **hidden apps**, tap the app.
- Uninstall: long press the app (tile or list), **uninstall**; Android asks for confirmation.

### Switch between white on black and black on white

Double tap on empty space; or long press and tap **black on white** / **white on black**; or use the quick-settings tile **Reader's theme** (add it to Android's quick-settings panel by editing the panel); or the **theme** line in the settings.

### Set up the weather

1. Long press, **add widget**, **weather**. Accept the location request for the weather of where you are.
2. To use a fixed city instead: long press the tile, tap **location: automatic** (choose a city), type the **city name**, **ok**.
3. For Fahrenheit: long press the tile, **units: °C**.

### Set up the tasks tile

1. Long press, **add widget**, **tasks** (or settings, **tasks setup**, which also adds a tasks tile).
2. Tap **Reader's Tasks** or **Tasks.org**. One marked **not installed** opens its download page instead.
3. For Tasks.org, accept the access request (or tap **allow access to tasks**).
4. Under **choose a list**, tap the list. The tile is placed.

To change the list of a tile: long press it, **configure**.

### Show only some calendars in the agenda

Long press the agenda tile, **choose calendars**, tap the calendars to select or deselect, choose the app under **opens with**, then **ok**.

### Read a book beside the home screen

1. Swipe to an empty side and tap **open a book**, then choose the file.
2. Or, from a file manager or another app, open or share an ebook with Reader's Launcher: a menu **open a book** asks **left of the home screen** or **right of the home screen**.

To replace it: long press in the reader, **open another book**. To empty the side: **remove from this side**.

### Open Kindle, Kobo or another reading app with the swipe

1. Settings, **left of the home screen** or **right of the home screen**.
2. Tap **a reading app…** and choose the app. (On an empty side, **or a reading app** does the same.)
3. For Kindle, to open one book directly: tap the same settings line again, **the Kindle book to open…**, and paste the book's link (Share in Kindle or Amazon) or its ASIN. **open Kindle only** cancels this.

To go back to a book kept in the launcher: the same line, **a book kept in the launcher**. The book that was there has not moved.

### See the recently used apps

Swipe up and hold. The first time, tap **allow usage access to list open apps** and allow Reader's Launcher in Android's list.

### Move the home screen to another phone

1. On the first phone: settings, **back up the home screen**. Android's share sheet opens; send or save the file (readers-launcher-backup.json).
2. On the other phone: settings, **restore a backup**, choose the file.
3. The page shows when the backup was made, its pages and tiles, and **it replaces the home screen and the look of this phone**. Under **not on this phone — tap for its store page** are the apps the backup uses and this phone lacks, each with where it was (**on the home screen**, **in the grid**, **widget**, **in** a category); tap one to install it first. **widgets not available on this phone** lists widgets whose app is there but no longer offers them. If nothing is missing: **every app of this home screen is on this phone**.
4. Tap **restore** (or **restore anyway** if something is missing), or **cancel**.

After **home screen restored**, widgets from apps are placed again one by one; Android may ask to allow each. A widget not yet placed stays as a line **place the widget**: tap it to try again.

## Settings

Each line shows its current value; a tap moves to the next one.

| Setting | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **theme** | **white on black**, **black on white**, **follow system** | white on black |
| **font** | **serif**, **sans**, **mono** | sans |
| **text size** | **small**, **medium**, **large** | medium |
| **alignment** | **left**, **center** | left |
| **double tap flips theme** | **on**, **off** | on |
| **swipe down opens notifications** | **on**, **off** | on |
| **status bar** (shown or hidden on the launcher's screens) | **on**, **off** | on |
| **haptic feedback** (a light vibration on long presses and swipes) | **on**, **off** | on |
| **auto-rotate** (off keeps the launcher upright) | **on**, **off** | off |
| **double tap on an app shows its shortcuts** | **on**, **off** | on |

With **double tap on an app shows its shortcuts** on, a single tap on an app waits a short instant to see whether a second tap follows; turn it off for an immediate opening (the shortcuts stay in the long-press menu).

Then:

- **arrange tiles**, **hidden apps**, **tasks setup**: the screens described above.
- **left of the home screen**, **right of the home screen**: what the swipe to that side opens. The line shows the reading app (and the Kindle book), or the title of the book, or **a book kept in the launcher**.
- **default launcher**: Android's home-app settings.
- **usage access (open apps list)**: Android's usage-access settings.
- **back up the home screen** (a file for another phone: pages, tiles, apps, look — no password) and **restore a backup**.
- **reader's launcher 1.19.0**: tap for the line **black and white, text only. MIT licence.**

Settings kept on the tiles themselves: weather units and place, agenda calendars and app, task list, mindful mode and minutes, widget height. The reading text size is set in the reader's menu and applies to both books.

## Your data

Everything stays on the phone: the pages and tiles, the settings, the hidden apps and custom names, the books you opened (each is copied into the launcher's own storage, so it keeps working if the original file is moved or deleted) and the place you reached in them.

The network is used by the weather tile only. It sends the coordinates of the place (your approximate position, or the chosen city) to Open-Meteo; for a place in Switzerland it first asks the Swiss federal map service (geo.admin.ch) for the postal code, then MeteoSwiss for the forecast, and falls back on Open-Meteo if that fails. A city name you type is sent to Open-Meteo's place search, with the phone's language. No key, no account, no identifier. The last forecast is kept on the phone.

Events, tasks, notes, books, recordings and the word of the day are read on the phone from the apps that hold them; the launcher never contacts their servers.

The backup file contains the pages and tiles with their settings, the categories, the grid, the hidden apps, the custom names, the look (theme, font, sizes, gestures), the name of each app used, and the calendars of each agenda tile by name. It contains no password. It does not contain the books, the reading positions, the choice of a reading app for the sides, or the weather. On restore, calendars are found again by account and name (all calendars if none matches) and task lists by their title.

Uninstalling Reader's Launcher removes the home screen layout, the settings and the copies of the books. The other apps and their data are not touched.

## With the other apps

- **Quick-settings tile**: **Reader's theme** flips the launcher between its two themes from Android's quick-settings panel.
- **Reader's Tasks** or **Tasks.org**: the tasks tile. With Tasks.org 15.11 or later, tasks are ticked and added on the tile; with an older version the box opens the task in Tasks.org and **+** opens Tasks.org with the title filled in.
- **Reader's Calendar** (or any calendar app): the agenda tile reads the phone's calendars and opens events in the chosen app.
- **Reader's Books**: the book tile.
- **Reader's Notes**: the notes tile.
- **Reader's Recorder**: the recorder and recordings tiles.
- **Reader's Mindful Tool**: the mindful tile.
- **Reader's Food Log**: the food log tile.
- **Reader's Night Filter**: the night filter tile.
- **Le Littré**: the mot du jour tile.
- **Kindle, Kobo or any reading app**: opened by the swipe to a side.
- **Ebook files**: Reader's Launcher appears in "open with" and in the share menu for EPUB, MOBI (and Kindle AZW in "open with") and FB2 files.
- **Widgets of any app**: placed as tiles.

A tile of a Reader's app that is not installed shows that app's name in dim text (the mindful tile reads **Reader's Mindful Tool is not installed**); a tap opens the app's web page.

## Limits

- Android does not let a launcher replace the system's recent-apps view; **open apps** is a list of recently used apps, without previews, and without the apps of a work profile.
- A page never scrolls: what does not fit goes on another page. The cell count in **arrange tiles** tells how much room is left.
- The reader shows text only: no images, no footnote links, no styling. Formats: EPUB, MOBI/PRC/AZW without protection (compressed the usual PalmDoc way or not at all), FB2, plain text. No PDF. Books with DRM and Kindle-store files in the newer compression are refused.
- One book per side, two in all.
- The Kindle link that opens one book is Kindle's own, not a documented one: if Kindle stops answering it, the app alone opens.
- An app's shortcuts are available only while Reader's Launcher is the default launcher.
- The agenda tile holds at most the 60 next events of the coming year; the tasks tile the 50 first open tasks (sub-tasks are not shown); the notes tile the 30 latest notes.
- A city is chosen by name and the first match is taken: add the country if the wrong one comes up.
- The widget **mot du jour** keeps its French name in every language.

## Messages and problems

| Message | Meaning and what to do |
|---|---|
| **set as default launcher** | Reader's Launcher is not the home app yet. Tap the line and choose it. |
| **hold to begin** | The page is empty: long press to add something. |
| **an empty page — hold to add something, or remove it** | A further page with nothing on it. |
| **the home screen is full** | No cells left on this page. Remove a tile, lower a widget's **height**, or add a home page. |
| **could not add this widget** | Android could not open its request to place the widget. Try again. |
| **widget app was removed** | The app that provided this widget is gone or no longer offers it. Long press the line, **remove**. |
| **place the widget** | A widget from a restored backup is not placed yet. Tap the line and allow it. |
| **no location — hold to choose a city** | The weather has no position. Tap to allow location (or to type a city if location is allowed but unknown), or long press and choose a city. |
| **weather unavailable** | The forecast could not be fetched (no network). Tap to try again. |
| **…** | Loading. |
| **allow calendar access** | The agenda cannot read the calendars. Tap and allow. |
| **no upcoming event** | Nothing in the chosen calendars for the coming year; check **choose calendars**. |
| **(untitled)** | An event without a title. |
| **set up tasks** | The tasks app is missing or access was not given. Tap to open **tasks setup**. |
| **allow access to tasks** | Tasks.org has not yet allowed the launcher. Tap and accept. |
| **tasks unavailable** | The list could not be read from the tasks app. Check in **tasks setup** that the app and the list are still there. |
| **no open task** | The list has no task left to do. |
| **no list found — create one in the tasks app first** | The tasks app has no list yet. |
| **choose a list. With this version of Tasks.org, the box opens the task and + opens Tasks.org. From version 15.11 you can tick and add tasks right here.** | Your Tasks.org is older than 15.11: update it to tick and add from the tile. |
| **not installed** | That tasks app is not on the phone; tap to get it. |
| **Reader's Mindful Tool is not installed** | The mindful tile needs that app; tap to open its page. |
| **one step left in the app** | Reader's Night Filter still needs its authorisation; open it and follow its instructions. |
| **allow usage access to list open apps** | Tap, then allow Reader's Launcher in Android's usage-access list. |
| **no recent apps** | No app was used in the last six hours. |
| **no shortcuts** | The app offers none, or Reader's Launcher is not the default launcher. |
| **nothing here** | No app matches the search, or no app is hidden. |
| **app not found** | The app of this tile is no longer installed. Remove the tile. |
| **cannot open** (the app) | Android refused to start the app. |
| **cannot open the shortcut** | The shortcut is no longer valid. |
| **this file could not be read** | The file is not a book the reader understands, or it is protected. Tap to choose another. |
| **no ASIN found in that** | What you pasted is neither a Kindle or Amazon book link nor a ten-character ASIN. |
| **not a Reader's Launcher backup** | The chosen file is not a backup of this app. |
| **this backup comes from a newer Reader's Launcher (format 2): update the launcher first** | Update the app, then restore again. |
| **home screen restored** | The restore is done. |


# Reader's Calendar

*Android 1.15.1 · Windows, macOS, Linux 1.14.3*

## What it is

Reader's Calendar is a calendar in black and white, made of text only: agenda, month, week, workdays and day. The calendar an event belongs to is named in words; colour is an option, never a code you have to learn.

It comes as two programs. On an Android phone it shows and edits the calendars the phone already holds, the ones your accounts sync (Google, Infomaniak, DAVx⁵, Nextcloud…): it has no account and no network access of its own. On a computer (Windows, macOS, Linux) it connects by itself to your calendar server with CalDAV, to a Google account through an access you create yourself, and to read-only calendar addresses (feeds).

It deliberately leaves out invitations and guests, time zones other than your own, and alerts: a reminder is stored with the event, but neither program rings or shows a notification itself.

Both programs speak English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese and Russian, following the language of the device.

## First start

### On the phone

At the first start Android asks for access to the calendar. The app needs it to read your events and to write the ones you create or change. It asks for nothing else, and it has no permission to use the network.

If you refuse, the agenda shows the row **allow access to the calendars**; tap it to be asked again.

There is no account to enter. The calendars shown are the ones the phone already syncs. If the phone holds none, or nothing is planned in the coming days, the agenda says **nothing planned**.

The app opens on the week. Go back once to reach the agenda.

### On the computer

At the first start a dialog asks for your accounts. You can fill in one, two or all three parts, then press **connect**.

| Field | What to put |
|---|---|
| **server** | The address of your CalDAV server. The app gives the example of Infomaniak: `https://sync.infomaniak.com`. Nextcloud, Radicale and other servers work too. |
| **username** | Your login on that server. For Infomaniak, a login of the form `AB12345`. |
| **app password** | Your password; an application password if two-factor authentication is on. |
| **feeds** | Read-only calendars, one per line, written as a name, the sign `\|`, then the address. The address ends in `.ics` or starts with `webcal`. For Google Calendar: the calendar's settings › Integrate calendar › Secret address in iCal format. |
| **client ID**, **client secret** | Only for a Google account in read and write: the two values of an access (an "OAuth client") you create yourself at Google. See "Connect a Google account" below. |
| **Google account** | The button **connect the Google account**, and next to it the state of the connection. |

The dialog also holds **import credentials…** and **export credentials…** (see "Your data"), and **cancel**.

If you cancel without any account, the bottom of the left column says **not connected — Ctrl+, to set up**. Ctrl+, or the ⚙ sign reopens the settings, where every row under **accounts** opens this dialog again.

While it works, the bottom of the left column says **connecting…**, then **syncing…**, then **synced** followed by the time.

## The screens

### Phone: the ⋯ menu

Every view has a **⋯** sign at the top right. It opens the same menu everywhere:

- **today**: back to the current week, month or day (shown in the week and the month, and in the day view when another day is displayed);
- **+ new event**;
- **today (list)**: the agenda;
- **today (day view)**: today as a time grid;
- **week**;
- **workdays**;
- **month**;
- **calendars**;
- **black on white** or **white on black**: switches the colours at once;
- **settings**.

Tap outside the menu to close it. On every screen except the agenda, the title at the top left starts with **←**: tap it, or use the phone's back gesture, to go back.

Every view ends with the row **+ new event**.

### Phone: agenda

The coming days as a list, grouped by day. Each day has a heading (**today · …**, **tomorrow · …**, then the date). Each event shows its title and, under it, its hours (or **all day**) and its place.

- Tap an event: its page.
- Tap a day heading: that day as a time grid.
- Long press a day heading: a new event on that day.
- Tap the title (the month and year, followed by ▾): the month view.
- **show more days**, at the end of the list: 60 more days. The list starts with 60 days.

With the phone held sideways, the agenda takes the right side.
