The left side shows the month as a grid of numbers (a dot marks the days that hold something, today is inverted), then **week** and **workdays**. Tap a day in the grid for that day, long press it for a new event, tap the month's name for the month view.

### Phone: month

Six weeks that fill the screen. Each day shows its number and its events, one line each, all-day events first. When a day is full, **+2** (or another number) tells how many more there are. Today's number is inverted; the days of the neighbouring months are dimmed. On a wide screen the lines start with the hour.

- Tap a day (or an event line): that day as a time grid.
- Long press an empty part of a day: a new event on that day.
- Swipe sideways: the next or previous month.
- Long press an event line, then drag it to another day: the event moves there. The day under your finger is framed.

### Phone: week and workdays

A time grid: one column per day, the hours down the side. Events are solid blocks, so the gaps between them read as free time. Events that overlap sit side by side. All-day events are in a strip above the grid. Today's heading is inverted and a line marks the present moment.

- Tap a block: the event's page.
- Tap an empty slot: a new event at that hour, one hour long.
- Tap a day heading: that day alone.
- Swipe sideways: the next or previous week.
- Long press a block, then drag: the event moves, by quarter hours up and down and by whole days sideways. The block shows its new hours while you drag; the grid scrolls when your finger nears its top or bottom.

**workdays** shows Monday to Friday at full width. Saturday and Sunday are folded into a narrow strip at the right: one letter per day, and a dot when the day holds something. Tap the strip for the whole week.

### Phone: day

The same grid, one column wide. Each block shows the title, the hour and the place. Swipe sideways for the next or previous day. Taps, long presses and drags work as in the week.

### Phone: event

The page of one event: title, date, hours (or **all day**), the repetition, the reminder, then the calendar it belongs to and its account, the place and the description.

The place and the description are live text:

- a phone number opens the dialer with the number ready (it is never dialled by itself);
- a mail address opens the mail app;
- a web address opens the browser;
- the place opens the maps app.

Long press anywhere to select and copy text. Dates, prices and room numbers stay plain text.

At the bottom: **edit** and **delete**.

### Phone: new event and edit

The form is titled **new event** or **edit**. **save** is at the top right and at the bottom.

| Row | What it does |
|---|---|
| **title** | Opens a prompt to type the title. For a new event it opens by itself, keyboard up. |
| the date, marked **starts** | Opens a month grid; **‹** and **›** change the month; tap a day. Moving the start keeps the event's length. |
| the hours, marked **start time · end time** | Asks the start, then the end, in two prompts. Type digits: `1830`, `18.30`, `18h30` or `930` are all understood. Changing the start keeps the event's length. |
| **all day: off** / **all day: on** | Tap to switch. |
| **ends another day** | Opens the month grid for the end date. Once the end is another day (or the event lasts all day), a row marked **ends** shows it. |
| the calendar, marked **calendar** and its account | Lists the calendars you may write to. |
| **reminder** | **no reminder**, **at the time of the event**, **10 minutes before**, **30 minutes before**, **1 hours before**, **2 hours before**, **1 days before**, **2 days before**. |
| **repeat** | **does not repeat**, **every day**, **every week**, **every month**, **every year**. |
| **location** | Opens a prompt to type the place. |
| **description** | Opens a prompt to type the description, on one line. |

A new event starts at the next full hour (or at the hour you tapped in a grid), lasts one hour, goes to your default calendar and takes your default reminder.

An event whose rule is richer than the five choices (every Tuesday and Thursday, until a date…) shows **repeats (custom rule)**. The rule is kept as it is unless you pick another choice.

### Phone: calendars

The list of the phone's calendars, with the account each belongs to and the service that syncs it. The help line says: **tap a calendar to show or hide it; the ones shown are inverted**. A calendar you cannot write to is marked **read only**; the one new events go to is marked **default**.

### Phone: settings

See "Settings" below.

### Computer: the window

One window in two parts.

The left column holds:

- a small month, with **‹** and **›** to change month. A dot marks the days that hold something; today is inverted. Click a day: the agenda from that day. Click the month's name: back to today's agenda;
- the views: **agenda**, **day**, **week**, **workdays**, **month**;
- **search**;
- **+ new event**;
- the calendars, each with a dot of its colour. Click one to hide or show it: a hidden calendar has an empty ring and a dimmed name. A calendar you cannot write to says **read only**. The last calendar shown cannot be hidden;
- at the bottom, the status line and **⚙** for the settings.

The right side shows the chosen view.

### Computer: agenda

The days as a list, like on the phone. Click an event for its page, a day heading for that day's time grid, **show more days** for 60 more days. **nothing planned** when the list is empty.

### Computer: day, week and workdays

The time grid of the phone. Above it: **‹**, the dates, **today** and **›**.

- Click a block: the event's page.
- Click an empty slot: a new event at that hour, one hour long.
- Click a day heading (week): that day alone.
- Drag a block: the event moves, by quarter hours and whole days.
- Click an all-day event in the strip above: its page.
- In **workdays**, click the weekend strip at the right: the whole week.

### Computer: month

Six weeks; each day with its events: a dot of the calendar's colour, the hour when there is room, the title. All-day events are solid bars. **+n** when a day is full. Above: **‹**, the month, **today**, **›**.

- Click an event: its page.
- Click a day: its time grid.
- Double-click an empty part of a day: a new event that day at 09:00.
- Drag an event to another day: it moves there.

### Computer: event

**← back**, and at the right **edit** and **delete** (absent when the calendar is read-only). Then the title, the date, the hours, the calendar with its account (followed by **· read-only** when it is), the **repeat**, the **reminder**, the **location** and the description.

A mail address or a web address opens where it belongs. The place opens a map search in the browser. A phone number is copied (the status line says so). All text can be selected and copied.

### Computer: new event and edit

One column. **← cancel** at the top left, **save** at the top right; **cancel** and **save** again at the bottom.

- The **title** is typed in place. Enter saves.
- The date, marked **day**: click for a month grid (**‹** **›**, or the ← → keys).
- The hours, marked **time**: click to type the start, then the end (**start time**, **end time**).
- **all day** switches to an all-day event; **at set times** switches back.
- **ends another day** picks an end date; a row marked **ends** then shows it.
- The calendar, **reminder** and **repeat**: click for the choices. The repeat choices are the same as on the phone; the reminder choices stop at **1 days before**.
- **location** and **description** are typed in place.

When you edit one event of a series alone, the repeat row says **part of a series** and the calendar cannot be changed.

### Computer: search

A field marked **search events: title, place, notes**. Results appear as you type, under **upcoming** and **past**. Each shows the title, the day, the hours, the calendar, **repeats** for a series, and the place. Click one for its page; **← back** returns to the results.

### Computer: settings

See "Settings" below.

## How to

### Add an event (phone)

1. Tap **+ new event** at the bottom of any view, or tap an empty slot in a time grid, or long press a day in the month or a day heading in the agenda.
2. Type the title, then **ok**.
3. Tap the date or the hours to change them.
4. Change the calendar, the reminder, the repetition, the place or the description if needed.
5. Tap **save**. The event's page opens.

### Add an event (computer)

1. Click **+ new event** (or Ctrl+N), or click an empty slot in a time grid, or double-click a day in the month.
2. Type the title.
3. Click the day or the hours to change them.
4. Click **save** (or Ctrl+S, or Enter in the title).

### Change an event

1. Open the event.
2. Choose **edit**.
3. Change what you want, then **save**.

### Change one event of a series, or the series

When you edit, move or delete an event that repeats, the app asks what the change is for.

- On the phone: **only this event**, **this and the following events**, **the whole series**.
- On the computer, for example when editing: **edit only this event**, **edit this and the following events**, **edit the whole series** (and the same with **move** and **delete**).

On the first event of a series, "this and the following" is left out, since it is the whole series. For a series in a Google account on the computer, only "only this event" and "the whole series" are offered.

### Move an event by dragging it

- Phone: long press the event in a time grid or in the month, drag it, release.
- Computer: drag it with the mouse.

In a time grid the event moves by quarter hours and whole days; in the month, by whole days. If it repeats, a menu asks which events to move; close the menu without choosing and the event goes back. An event of a read-only calendar cannot be moved.

### Delete an event

1. Open the event.
2. Choose **delete**.
3. On the phone, confirm with **delete** under **delete this event?**. On the computer, click **delete** in the small menu.

### Make an event repeat

In the form, open **repeat** and choose **every day**, **every week**, **every month** or **every year**. Choose **does not repeat** to stop a series.

### Set a reminder

In the form, open **reminder** and choose. New events take the default reminder of the settings.

### Make an all-day event, or one over several days

- Phone: tap **all day: off** so that it reads **all day: on**. For several days, tap **ends another day** (or the row marked **ends**) and pick the last day.
- Computer: click **all day**. For several days, click **ends another day** and pick the last day.

### Move an event to another calendar

In the form, open the calendar row, choose the other calendar, then **save**. The event is written in the new calendar first, then removed from the old one. One event of a series cannot be moved alone.

### Show or hide a calendar

- Phone: **⋯** › **calendars**, then tap the calendar. Shown calendars are inverted.
- Computer: click the calendar in the left column.

### Choose where new events go

- Phone: **⋯** › **settings** › **new events go to: …**.
- Computer: **⚙** › **default calendar**.

### Go to another date, and come back to today

- Phone: swipe sideways in the week, the day or the month. **⋯** › **today** comes back.
- Computer: **‹** and **›**, or the ← and → keys; **today** comes back. In the small month, click any day.

### Call, write or find the way from an event

Open the event and tap or click the phone number, the address or the place. On the phone, if no app can handle it, the message **no app for this on the phone** appears.

### Find an event (computer)

1. Click **search** (or Ctrl+F).
2. Type one or several words. They are looked for in the title, the place, the description and the calendar's name; capitals and accents do not matter.

The search covers one year back and two years ahead. A series appears once. Escape empties the field, then leaves the search.

### Connect a calendar server (computer)

1. Press Ctrl+, then click the row **CalDAV** under **accounts**.
2. Fill in **server**, **username** and **app password**.
3. Click **connect**.

### Show a Google calendar without an account (computer)

1. In Google Calendar, open the calendar's settings › Integrate calendar, and copy the "Secret address in iCal format".
2. In the accounts dialog, add a line under **feeds**: a name, the sign `|`, then the address.
3. Click **connect**.

The calendar is read-only. Any other `.ics` or `webcal` address works the same way.

### Connect a Google account, read and write (computer)

Google gives no ready-made access to a program like this one: you create your own, once. The dialog sums up the steps:

1. At console.cloud.google.com, create a new project.
2. Under APIs & Services, enable the Google Calendar API.
3. Set up the OAuth consent screen: external, yourself as test user. Then choose "Publish app": left in testing, Google asks you to connect again every 7 days.
4. Under Credentials, create an OAuth client ID of type "Desktop app".
5. Copy its ID into **client ID** and its secret into **client secret**.
6. Click **connect the Google account**. The state reads **waiting for the browser…** and the browser opens on Google. At "Google hasn't verified this app", choose Advanced, then continue: it is your own access.
7. The browser ends on a page saying **Reader's Calendar is connected. You can close this tab.** The dialog reads **Google account connected**.
8. Click **connect**.

The calendars of the account then appear with the others. Those you may only read (shared with you, holidays…) stay read-only.

### Forget the Google account (computer)

Open the accounts dialog and click **forget the Google account**.

### Set up another computer

1. On the first computer: **⚙** › **export credentials…**, and choose where to write the file (it is proposed as `readers-credentials.json`).
2. Carry the file to the other computer.
3. There: **import credentials…** in the first-start dialog or in the settings.
4. Delete the file: it holds your passwords.

### Put a widget on the home screen (phone)

Use your launcher's way of adding a widget and choose **Reader's Calendar · next event** or **Reader's Calendar · today and tomorrow**.

### Change the look quickly

- Phone: **⋯** › **black on white** or **white on black**.
- Computer: Ctrl+T for the colours, Ctrl+= and Ctrl+- for the text size.

## Settings

### On the phone

**⋯** › **settings**. Tap a row to move to its next choice; the first two open a list.

| Setting | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **new events go to: …** | any calendar you may write to | the first one |
| **default reminder: …** | **no reminder**, **at the time of the event**, **10 minutes before**, **30 minutes before**, **1 hours before**, **2 hours before**, **1 days before** | **10 minutes before** |
| **week starts on: …** | **monday**, **sunday** | **monday** |
| **opens on: …** | **today (list)**, **week**, **workdays**, **day**, **month** | **week** |
| **theme: …** | **white on black**, **black on white**, **follow system** | **white on black** |
| **font: …** | **serif**, **sans**, **mono** | **sans** |
| **text size: …** | **small**, **medium**, **large** | **medium** |
| **alignment: …** | **left**, **center** | **left** |
| **haptic feedback: …** | **on**, **off** | **on** |
| **events: …** | **plain**, **in their calendar's colour** | **plain** |

**haptic feedback** is the short vibration felt when a long press lifts an event.

With **events: in their calendar's colour**, the blocks of the grids and the lines of the month are filled with the colour of their calendar, and the agenda puts a dot of that colour before each title.

The **workdays** view always starts on Monday.

The last two rows give the version (**reader's calendar 1.15.1**, **black and white, text only. MIT licence.**) and the credits.

### On the computer

**⚙** or Ctrl+,. Click a row to move to its next choice.

| Setting | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **colours** | **black on white**, **white on black** | **black on white** |
| **events** | **plain, with a colour dot**, **in their calendar's colour** | **plain, with a colour dot** |
| **text size** | 9 to 24 pt, with **−** and **+** | **13 pt** |
| the font (the name of the typeface used is shown under the choice) | **sans-serif**, **serif**, **mono** | **sans-serif** |
| **opens on** | **week**, **workdays**, **month**, **day**, **agenda** | **week** |
| **first day of the week** | **monday**, **sunday** | **monday** |
| **default reminder** | **no reminder**, **at the time of the event**, **10 minutes before**, **30 minutes before**, **1 hours before**, **2 hours before**, **1 days before** | **10 minutes before** |
| **default calendar** | any calendar you may write to | the first one |

Under **accounts**:

- **CalDAV**: the server's name, or — when there is none. Opens the accounts dialog.
- **Google**: **Google account connected**, or —. Opens the accounts dialog.
- **feeds**: the number of feeds. Opens the accounts dialog.
- **sync now**: reads the calendars again at once.
- **export credentials…** and **import credentials…**.

## Your data

### On the phone

Your events live in the phone's own calendar storage, where every calendar app of the phone finds them. The app reads and writes there and nowhere else. It never uses the network: your accounts and their sync apps carry the changes to your servers, at their own pace.

A change made elsewhere and synced while the app is open shows at once.

The app keeps only its settings on the phone: the look, the opening view, the default calendar and reminder, and the calendars you hid. Uninstalling removes these settings; your events stay in the phone's calendars and on your accounts.

There is no export or import.

### On the computer

Your events stay on your servers. The program keeps no copy: it reads them at each start, every 5 minutes, after each change you make, and when you press F5 or **sync now**. Without a connection it shows nothing.

What goes over the network, and to whom:

- to your CalDAV server: your login and password, and the events you read and write;
- to the address of each feed: a request for the calendar file;
- to Google, if you connected an account: the connection through your browser, then the events you read and write;
- nothing to anyone else. Clicking a place opens a map search on openstreetmap.org in your browser.

If an event was changed elsewhere while you were editing it, your change is refused rather than written over the other one. For Google the status line says **Google: the event changed elsewhere — shown again as it is now**. Guests of a Google event are not e-mailed about your changes; its guests, colours and meeting links stay as they were.

The settings file holds the server address, the login, the password, the feeds, the Google access and connection, the hidden calendars and the look. It is readable by you only. It is `config.json` in:

- Linux: `~/.config/readers-calendar/`
- Windows: the folder `Readers Calendar` of your application data (`%APPDATA%`)
- macOS: `~/Library/Application Support/readers-calendar/`

Uninstalling the program leaves this file in place; delete it yourself to remove your password from the computer.

### The credentials file

**export credentials…** writes your accounts (server, login, password, feeds, Google access and connection) into a file. Reader's Calendar, Reader's Tasks and Reader's Notes can share the same file, each in its own part: exporting into an existing file adds to it. The look and the other settings are not in it.

The file holds your passwords in clear. Carry it on a USB key or in your own cloud folder, not by e-mail, and delete it once imported.

## With the other apps

### Widgets (phone)

Two home-screen widgets, for any launcher. They follow the app's theme and show today's and tomorrow's events from the calendars you did not hide.

- **Reader's Calendar · next event**: one line with the title and the time; **+** for a new event. Tap the line to open the event. A timed event takes the line from one hour before its start until its end; an all-day event shows the rest of the time. **nothing planned** when nothing is left today or tomorrow.
- **Reader's Calendar · today and tomorrow**: a list, under the word **agenda** (tap it to open the app) and a **+** for a new event. Tap an event to open it.

### Opened by other apps (phone)

The app answers the phone's calendar links: another app can open it on a day, on an event, or on a new event. This is how the agenda tile of Reader's Launcher opens it, and why it can be chosen as the phone's calendar app.

### The computer and the phone together

The two programs do not talk to each other. They show the same events when the phone syncs the same accounts as the computer.

## On the computer

### How it differs from the phone

- It connects to your servers itself; the phone app relies on the phone's accounts.
- It has a search.
- The month view opens an event with one click; on the phone a tap opens the day.
- A phone number is copied instead of dialled.
- There are no widgets.
- The description is a multi-line field.

### Keyboard

| Key | Effect |
|---|---|
| ← / → | previous / next week or day in the grids, month in the month view and in the small month |
| Ctrl+N | new event |
| Ctrl+S, Ctrl+Enter | save the event being edited |
| Ctrl+F | search |
| Ctrl+D | today's agenda |
| Ctrl+J | today as a time grid |
| Ctrl+W | this week |
| Ctrl+Shift+W | workdays |
| Ctrl+M | this month |
| Escape | back (empties the search field first) |
| F5, Ctrl+R | sync now |
| Ctrl+T | white on black / black on white |
| Ctrl+= , Ctrl++ / Ctrl+- | larger / smaller text |
| Ctrl+, | settings |
| Ctrl+Q | quit |

### Command line

- `readers-calendar --export-credentials FILE` writes the accounts into FILE, without opening the window.
- `readers-calendar --import-credentials FILE` reads them back.

### Installing

- Debian, Ubuntu, Pop!_OS: the `readers-calendar` package.
- Windows: one `.exe` file, nothing to install. It is not signed: at "Windows protected your PC", choose More info › Run anyway.
- macOS: a `.dmg`. The first opening must be a right click on the app › Open.

## Limits

Both programs:

- no invitations, no guests;
- no time zone other than the device's: hours are shown and entered in local time;
- no alert: the reminder is stored with the event for the devices that do alert;
- one reminder per event;
- the repetition choices are every day, week, month or year; a richer rule made elsewhere is kept but cannot be built here.

Phone:

- no search;
- the description is typed on one line;
- the widgets look no further than tomorrow;
- all-day events cannot be dragged in the time grids (they can in the month).

Computer:

- one CalDAV account and one Google account;
- feeds are read-only;
- nothing is shown without a connection;
- a Google series offers "only this event" or "the whole series", not "this and the following";
- the search shows at most 150 results under each heading.

## Messages and problems

### On the phone

| Message | Meaning, and what to do |
|---|---|
| **allow access to the calendars** | The calendar permission was refused. Tap the row to grant it. |
| **nothing planned** | No event in the days shown, in the calendars shown. Check **calendars** if you expected some. |
| **the end is before the start** | The end must be after the start. Correct the hours or the end date. |
| **not saved** or **not saved: …** | The phone refused to write the event; the words after the colon are the system's own. |
| **no app for this on the phone** | No app can open this number, address or place. |
| **(hh:mm)** added to a time prompt | What you typed is not a time. Type hours and minutes. |
| **(untitled)** | The event has no title. |
| **repeats (custom rule)** | The event repeats by a rule richer than the app's choices. |

A calendar is missing: the app lists the calendars the phone holds and shows. Check the account or the sync app that brings it.

A change does not reach your other devices: the app wrote it on the phone; sending it is the work of the account's sync.

### On the computer

Messages appear in the status line, at the bottom of the left column.

| Message | Meaning, and what to do |
|---|---|
| **not connected — Ctrl+, to set up** | No account yet. Press Ctrl+, and open a row under **accounts**. |
| **wrong username or app password** | The server refused the login. Check both; use an application password if two-factor authentication is on. |
| **no calendar found at this address** | The server answered but holds no calendar there. Check the address. |
| **this server address is not valid** | The address is malformed, or lacks `https://` at its start. |
| **cannot reach the server** | No connection, or a wrong server name. |
| **the server did not answer in time** | The server is slow or unreachable. Try F5 later. |
| **the server refused the request (HTTP …)** | The server rejected a read or a change. With 412, the event changed elsewhere: press F5 and redo your change. |
| **the server sent an unreadable answer** | The address is probably not a CalDAV server. |
| **network error — …** | Another network failure; the details follow. |
| **feed: HTTP …** | A feed's address answered with an error. Check the address in **feeds**. |
| **this event comes from a read-only feed** | Feeds cannot be changed. |
| **this calendar is read-only** | You may only read this Google calendar. |
| **no writable calendar** | No calendar you may write to: a new event has nowhere to go. |
| **the end is before the start** | Correct the hours or the end date. |
| **title** | You saved without a title. Type one. |
| **Google: connect the account again (Ctrl+,) to edit its events**, **connected read-only — forget, then connect again to edit** | The Google connection only allows reading. Click **forget the Google account**, then connect again. |
| **Google: HTTP … — connect the account again** | Google no longer accepts the connection. Forget the account and connect again. |
| **Google: the event changed elsewhere — shown again as it is now** | Your change was not written. Redo it on the event as it now is. |
| **Google answered with an error (HTTP …): …** | Google rejected a request; its own words follow. |
| **Google: the browser did not come back** | The consent in the browser was not finished within five minutes. Try again. |
| **Google: access was refused in the browser** | You declined on Google's page. |
| **Google: the answer in the browser did not match this request — try again** | An old browser tab answered. Try again. |
| **Google: Google sent no authorisation code**, **Google refused access: …**, **Google refused the connection (HTTP …): …** | The connection failed at Google. Check the client ID and secret, then try again. |
| **Google: Google did not grant lasting access — remove this app from your Google account's connections, then connect again** | Do as it says, in your Google account's security settings. |
| **Something went wrong. Close this tab and try again.** | Shown in the browser when the Google connection failed. |
| **credentials exported to … — the file holds your passwords: keep it private** | The export worked. |
| **credentials imported** | The import worked; the program connects. |
| **not a Reader's credentials file** | The chosen file is not a credentials file. |
| **this file holds nothing for readers-calendar** | The file has no part for this program. |
| **cannot use this file — …** | The file could not be read or written. |
| **… copied** | The phone number you clicked is in the clipboard. |
| **searching further…** | The search is still reading the wider period. |
| **no event found** | Nothing matches the words typed. |
| **error — …** | Any other failure; the details follow. |


# Reader's Tasks

*Android 1.5.2 · Windows, macOS, Linux 1.6.2*

## What it is

Reader's Tasks shows the task lists you keep on a CalDAV server (Infomaniak, Nextcloud, Tasks.org Cloud, Radicale…) as plain text in black and white: a box to tick, a line to add a task, and very little else. It exists as an Android app and as a program for Windows, macOS and Linux; both talk to the same server, so a task ticked, renamed or moved in one shows the same in the other. There is no account with the app itself: only your own server, its address, your username and your password.

It deliberately has no priorities, no projects, no reminders, no notifications and no notes attached to a task. It does not create, rename or delete the lists themselves: that is done on your server or in another app.

Both programs speak English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese and Russian, and follow the language of the system.

This chapter describes the Android app first. The desktop program has its own section, "On the computer".

## First start

The Android app asks for no permission. It only uses the network, to reach your server.

At the first opening the screen shows the title **Reader's Tasks ▾** and one line: **set up your account**. Tap it to open the **account** screen. The screen starts with this help text: "Any CalDAV server with task lists: Infomaniak (https://sync.infomaniak.com, username like AB12345), Tasks.org Cloud, Nextcloud… Use an application password when two-factor authentication is on."

| Field | What to put in it | Example shown by the app |
|---|---|---|
| **server** | the full address of your CalDAV server, starting with `https://` | `https://sync.infomaniak.com` |
| **username** | your username on that server | `AB12345` |
| **app password** | your password; an application password created on the server when two-factor authentication is on | |

Then tap **connect**. The line **connecting…** appears. When the app has found your task lists, the screen closes by itself and the first list is displayed. If something is wrong, a message stays under **connect** (see "Messages and problems").

If you already use Reader's Tasks on another phone or on a computer, you can tap **import credentials** on the same screen instead of typing (see "Set up another phone or a computer").

A list with nothing to do shows **no open task**.

## The screens

### The tasks (main screen)

The main screen shows the open tasks of one list, the current list.

- **The title**, at the top, is the name of the current list followed by **▾**. Tap it to open the **lists** screen.
- **⋯**, at the top right, opens the menu of the screen (below).
- **Each task** is a line with a box **☐** and its text, on two lines at most. A task with a due date shows it in small letters underneath: **today**, **tomorrow**, **3 d late** (the number of days), the name of the weekday when it is less than a week away, otherwise the day and the month.
  - Tap **☐**: the task is completed.
  - Tap the text: a prompt titled **task** opens to rename it.
  - Long press without moving: the menu of the task opens (below).
  - Long press, then drag: the task moves up or down in the list.
- **show 4 done** (with the number of completed tasks) appears under the open tasks when the list has completed tasks. Tap it to display them, dimmed, with a ticked box **☑**, the most recently completed first; the line becomes **hide 4 done**. Tap the **☑** or the text of a completed task to reopen it; long press it for its menu. The completed tasks are folded away again when you leave the screen.
- **+ new task**, at the bottom, opens the prompt **new task**.
- **The last line**, in small letters, says **syncing…** while the app talks to the server, then **synced 14:32** (the time of the last sync), or the error if the sync failed.

The menu **⋯** has four entries:

| Entry | What it does |
|---|---|
| **lists** | opens the **lists** screen |
| **sync now** | reloads all lists and all tasks from the server |
| **black on white** or **white on black** | switches to the other look; the entry names the look you will get |
| **settings** | opens the **settings** screen |

### The menu of a task

A long press on a task opens a menu with the text of the task as its title:

| Entry | What it does |
|---|---|
| **complete** (open task) or **reopen** (completed task) | ticks or unticks the task |
| **call …**, **write to …**, **open …** | one entry for each phone number, e-mail address or web address written in the task (see "Call a number, write to an address or open a page written in a task") |
| **rename** | opens the prompt **task** with the current text |
| **due tomorrow** | sets the due date to tomorrow, replacing any other due date |
| **no due date** | removes the due date |
| **delete** | deletes the task from the server, at once and without asking |

Tap outside the menu, or press back, to close it without doing anything.

### The prompts **new task** and **task**

Both are one line of text with two buttons: **cancel** and **add** (new task) or **ok** (rename). The confirm key of the keyboard does the same as **add** or **ok**. An empty line cannot be confirmed. Tap outside the prompt, or press back, to cancel.

### lists

The **lists** screen shows the task lists of your server, each with its number of open tasks underneath. The current list is shown inverted.

- Tap **← lists** at the top, or press back, to return.
- Tap a list: it becomes the current list and the main screen comes back.
- Long press a list: a menu with **move up**, **move down** and **hide this list**. **move up** is missing on the first list, **move down** on the last one, and **hide this list** when only one list is left on screen.
- **2 hidden** (with the number of hidden lists) appears at the bottom when lists are hidden. Tap it to display them, each with **show again** underneath; tap one to bring it back among the others. **hide the hidden lists** folds them away.

### settings

Reached by **⋯** then **settings**. Tap **← settings** or press back to return. The screen holds, from top to bottom:

- **account**, with your username underneath (or **set up your account**): opens the **account** screen.
- **export credentials** and **import credentials** (see "Your data").
- the five settings of the look, described under "Settings". Each tap on a line moves to its next choice.
- **reader's tasks 1.5.2**, with **black and white, text only. MIT licence.** underneath, and **Pierre Gallaz · developed with Claude Code**. These two lines only inform.

### account

The three fields **server**, **username** and **app password**, the button **connect**, then **export credentials** and **import credentials**. See "First start".

## How to

### Add a task

1. Open the list the task belongs to.
2. Tap **+ new task** at the bottom.
3. Type the task and tap **add**.

The task is sent to the server and appears in the list. It has no due date.

### Complete a task

Tap its **☐**. The task leaves the list at once and is marked completed on the server. You can also long press it and choose **complete**.

### Reopen a task ticked by mistake

1. Tap **show … done** under the open tasks.
2. Tap the **☑** of the task, or its text.

### Rename a task

1. Tap the text of the task (or long press it and choose **rename**).
2. Change the text and tap **ok**.

The due date is kept. For a completed task, use the long press and **rename**: a tap on its text reopens it.

### Give a task a due date, or remove it

1. Long press the task.
2. Choose **due tomorrow** to set the due date to tomorrow, or **no due date** to remove it.

Tomorrow is the only date the app can set. Due dates set elsewhere (the server's web page, another app) are displayed under the task.

### Delete a task

Long press the task and choose **delete**. There is no confirmation and no way back.

### Put the tasks in your own order

1. Long press a task until it lifts (its line turns slightly grey).
2. Without lifting the finger, drag it up or down to its place; the list scrolls when you reach its top or bottom edge.
3. Let go.

The order is saved on the server, so the desktop program and other apps that follow the same convention show the same order. Tasks you have never moved come after the ones you placed, sorted by due date (tasks without a date last), then by creation date. Completed tasks cannot be dragged.

### Call a number, write to an address or open a page written in a task

A tap on a task never opens anything. When the text of a task holds a phone number (7 to 15 digits), an e-mail address or a web address (starting with `http://`, `https://` or `www.`), the menu of the task offers it:

1. Long press the task.
2. Choose **call** followed by the number, **write to** followed by the address, or **open** followed by the web address.

**call** opens the phone's dialler with the number filled in; the call does not start by itself. **write to** opens your mail app, **open** your browser. The menu offers up to eight of them per task.

### Switch to another list

Tap the name of the list at the top (or **⋯** then **lists**), then tap the list you want.

### Hide a list, or bring it back

1. Open **lists**.
2. Long press the list and choose **hide this list**.

To bring it back: on **lists**, tap **… hidden** at the bottom, then tap the list marked **show again**. Hiding only concerns this phone; nothing changes on the server.

### Change the order of the lists

On **lists**, long press a list and choose **move up** or **move down**, as many times as needed. The order is kept on this phone only.

### Sync now

Tap **⋯** then **sync now**. The app also syncs by itself: when it comes to the screen and the last sync is more than two minutes old, and after each of your actions (for the list concerned).

### Switch between white on black and black on white

Tap **⋯** then **black on white** (or **white on black**). This is the same setting as **theme** in **settings**.

### Change the account or the password

1. Tap **⋯**, **settings**, **account**.
2. Correct the fields and tap **connect**.

### Set up another phone or a computer

On the phone that already works:

1. Tap **⋯**, **settings**, **export credentials**. Underneath, the app reminds you: "a file to set up another phone or the desktop app — it holds the password: keep it private".
2. Android's share sheet opens with a file named `readers-credentials-tasks.json`. Send it to yourself by a private way (your own cloud folder, a message to yourself).

On the new phone:

1. Put the file where the phone can reach it (downloaded, or in a cloud folder).
2. In Reader's Tasks, tap **set up your account** (or **settings**), then **import credentials**, and choose the file.
3. The message **credentials imported** appears and the app connects.

On a computer, see "Move the account to another computer" under "On the computer". Delete the file once it has been imported: it holds your password, readable by anyone who opens it.

## Settings

All in **settings**. Each tap on a line moves to the next choice.

| Setting | What it changes | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| **theme** | the colours of the app and of its widgets | **white on black**, **black on white**, **follow system** | **white on black** |
| **font** | the typeface of all text | **serif**, **sans**, **mono** | **sans** |
| **text size** | the size of all text | **small**, **medium**, **large** | **medium** |
| **alignment** | text aligned on the left or centred | **left**, **center** | **left** |
| **haptic feedback** | a short vibration when you tick a box or long press | **on**, **off** | **on** |

**follow system** takes the dark or light mode of Android.

## Your data

### Where it lives

Your tasks live on your CalDAV server. The phone keeps a copy of the lists and tasks from the last sync, so the app opens at once and shows them even without a network. The server address, the username and the password are kept in the app's private storage on the phone, as are the look, the order of the lists, the hidden lists and the current list.

### What goes over the network

The app talks to one machine only: the server whose address you entered, with your username and password, over an encrypted connection (`https://`). Nothing is sent to anyone else: no statistics, no tracking. An address starting with `http://` is refused by the app, except for a server running on the device itself.

### Sync and conflicts

- The app reloads everything from the server when it opens with data older than ten minutes, when it returns to the screen with data older than two minutes, and when you choose **sync now**. After each action it reloads the list concerned.
- Every action (add, complete, reopen, rename, due date, delete, reorder) is sent to the server straight away. Nothing is queued for later: with no network the action fails and the last line shows the error. A task you ticked or deleted while offline disappears from the screen, then comes back at the next successful sync, because the server never received the change.
- When a task was changed elsewhere since your last sync, the server can refuse your change rather than overwrite the other one. The last line then shows **sync failed:** followed by the refusal. Choose **sync now**, then do it again.
- A list created on the server appears after the next sync, at the end of your lists. A list deleted on the server disappears from the phone.

### The credentials file

**export credentials** writes a small file, `readers-credentials-tasks.json`, with the server address, the username and the password, and hands it to Android's share sheet. **import credentials** reads such a file and replaces the account in the app, then connects. The file format is shared by the Reader's apps on phone and computer: each app reads only its own part. The file holds the password in clear. The temporary copy made by the app for sharing is erased at the next export and each time the app starts.

There is no other export: the tasks themselves are on your server.

### Uninstalling

Uninstalling removes the copy of the tasks, the account and the settings from the phone. Nothing is deleted on the server.

## With the other apps

### Home-screen widgets

The app offers two widgets, to add from the widget list of any launcher. Both show the current list of the app (the first visible list if there is none), in the colours of the app's **theme**.

| Widget | Size | What it shows |
|---|---|---|
| **Reader's Tasks · first task** | 4 × 1, can be widened | the first open task of the current list; under it the name of the list and, when there are several tasks, **1/5** (first of five) |
| **Reader's Tasks · open tasks** | 4 × 2, can be widened and made taller | the name of the list, then the open tasks as a scrolling list, each with its due date underneath |

In both widgets:

- **☐** completes the task on the server, without opening the app;
- **+** opens the app directly on the prompt **new task**;
- a tap on the text of a task, or on the name of the list, opens the app.

With no account, the widgets show **set up your account**; with nothing to do, **no open task**. The widgets display what the app loaded last; they are redrawn after each change in the app.

### Reader's Launcher

Reader's Tasks is the app behind the tasks tile of Reader's Launcher. The launcher reads the visible lists and their open tasks from Reader's Tasks, and can add a task or tick one through it. No permission is asked: the two apps recognise each other because they are signed by the same author. Reading the tasks from the launcher also starts a sync when the data is more than ten minutes old.

### Other apps on the same server

Any other app connected to the same CalDAV account sees the same tasks. The order given by dragging is stored in a way that Apple Reminders, Nextcloud Tasks and Tasks.org also use.

The app does not appear in Android's share menu and does not open files, apart from the credentials file.

## On the computer

The desktop program does the same job in one window. It is obtained as described in the chapter on getting the apps: `readers-tasks` from the apt repository or a `.deb` on Debian, Ubuntu and Pop!_OS, a PKGBUILD for Arch, a single `.exe` for Windows, a `.dmg` for macOS.

### First start on the computer

At the first start a dialog titled **reader's tasks** opens. It says: "CalDAV task lists. For Tasks.org Cloud: in the Android app, ⚙ › App settings › Tasks.org › Generate new password, and copy the URL, username and app password shown there."

1. Fill in **server** (the example shown is `https://caldav.tasks.org/…`), **username** and **app password**.
2. Click **connect**.

The dialog closes, the status line at the bottom of the window shows **connecting…**, then your lists appear. A wrong address or password is reported in that status line; press Ctrl+, to open the dialog again. If you click **cancel** at the first start, the status line says **not connected — Ctrl+, to set up**.

The dialog also has **import credentials…** and **export credentials…**, and shows the version at the bottom (**reader's tasks 1.6.2 · Pierre Gallaz · developed with Claude Code**).

### The window

- **On the left**, the column of your lists. Click a list to display it; the current list is shown inverted. Right click for the menu of the lists.
- **On the right**, the open tasks of the current list, each with **☐**, its text and, in small letters, its due date (same wording as on the phone). An empty list shows **no open task**.
- **show 4 done** / **hide 4 done**, under the tasks, displays or hides the completed tasks, dimmed, with **☑**.
- **The line + new task**: type a task and press Enter.
- **The status line**, at the very bottom: **3 open · synced 14:32**, or what the program is doing (**syncing…**, **adding…**, **renaming…**, **saving order…**), or an error.
- **⚙**, at the bottom right (tooltip **server settings (Ctrl+,)**), opens the setup dialog.

### What the mouse does

| Gesture | Effect |
|---|---|
| click **☐** | completes the task |
| click **☑** | reopens the completed task |
| double click on the text of a task | renames it: a dialog **rename** with the field **task** and the buttons **rename** and **cancel** |
| drag an open task up or down | puts it at its place; the order is saved on the server (**saving order…**) |
| right click on a task | **complete** (or **reopen**), **rename**, **delete** |
| right click on a list | **move up**, **move down**, **hide this list**, **show a hidden list** |

**delete** removes the task from the server at once, without asking. **show a hidden list** opens a second menu with the names of the hidden lists; click one to bring it back. The program refuses, without a message, to hide the last list left. The order of the lists and the hidden lists are kept on this computer only; they are independent from the phone's.

### Keyboard shortcuts

| Key | Effect |
|---|---|
| Enter in the line **+ new task** | adds the task to the current list |
| Ctrl+N | puts the cursor in the line **+ new task** |
| Ctrl+D | shows or hides the completed tasks |
| F5 or Ctrl+R | syncs now |
| Ctrl+T | switches between black on white and white on black |
| Ctrl+= or Ctrl++ | larger text |
| Ctrl+- | smaller text |
| Ctrl+, | opens the setup dialog (server, username, app password) |
| Ctrl+Q | quits |

### How it differs from the phone

- The lists are always visible on the left; there is no separate lists screen.
- It starts black on white. There is no settings screen: the look is changed by Ctrl+T and by Ctrl+= / Ctrl+- (text size from 9 to 30 points, 15 at the start). There is no choice of font or alignment, and no **follow system**.
- It cannot set or remove a due date; it displays the ones that exist. Its task menu has no **call**, **write to** or **open** entries.
- Subtasks made in another app are displayed as ordinary tasks; the phone app hides open subtasks.
- It syncs the current list every 5 minutes, at each change of list, after each action, and on F5 or Ctrl+R. The lists themselves are read when the program connects: a list created on the server appears at the next start, or after Ctrl+, and **connect**.
- It keeps no copy of the tasks: without a network the window stays empty and the status line shows the error.
- It has no widgets.

### Move the account to another computer

1. On the computer that works, press Ctrl+, and click **export credentials…**. Choose where to write the file; the name proposed is `readers-credentials.json` in your home folder. The dialog confirms: **credentials exported to … — the file holds your passwords: keep it private**.
2. Carry the file to the other computer by a private way (a USB key, your own cloud folder).
3. There, start Reader's Tasks and click **import credentials…** in the dialog, then choose the file. The dialog closes and the program connects.
4. Delete the file.

The export writes the account already saved in the program, not what you have just typed in the fields: connect first, export afterwards. If you choose an existing credentials file, the program adds its own part to it and keeps the parts of the other Reader's apps (Calendar, Notes): one file can carry all your accounts. The look (colours, text size) is not in the file. A file exported by the phone app can be imported on the computer, and the other way round.

### Command line

The same export and import work without opening the window:

```
readers-tasks --export-credentials FILE
readers-tasks --import-credentials FILE
```

Each prints its result (**credentials exported to …** or **credentials imported**) and stops.

The program's source also comes with a separate script, `copy_tasks.py`, which copies the tasks of one list into another list, on the same server or on another one, for instance when you change provider:

```
python3 copy_tasks.py --from URL USER PASSWORD "LIST" --to URL USER PASSWORD "LIST"
```

Add `--include-completed` to copy completed and cancelled tasks too, and `--dry-run` to see what would be copied without copying. The name of a list is matched without regard to capitals. Nothing is deleted on the source, and a second run skips the tasks already present on the target. The script speaks English only; it ends with a count such as `12 copied, 3 already there, 15 read from the source`, and names the available lists when it cannot find the one you asked for.

### Where its settings live

One file, `config.json`, readable by your user account only:

| System | Folder |
|---|---|
| Linux | `~/.config/readers-tasks/` |
| Windows | `%APPDATA%\Readers Tasks\` |
| macOS | `~/Library/Application Support/readers-tasks/` |

It holds the server address, the username, the password (in clear), the theme, the text size, the order of the lists, the hidden lists and the last list displayed. Deleting this file brings the program back to its first start.

## Limits

- One account, one server at a time.
- The app does not create, rename or delete lists.
- A task is one line of text. Descriptions, priorities, tags, reminders, repetition and attachments set in other apps are neither displayed nor editable; they are left untouched on the server when you tick, rename or move the task.
- No notifications and no reminders, even for a due date.
- On the phone the only due date you can set is tomorrow; on the computer none. The time of day of a due date is not displayed.
- On the phone, open subtasks are not displayed. Cancelled tasks are displayed in neither program.
- No search.
- No work is kept for later when the network is missing: an action made offline is lost.
- On the phone the server address must start with `https://`.
- The widgets display the first open task or the open tasks of the current list only; they cannot show another list than the app.

## Messages and problems

### On the phone

| Message | What it means, what to do |
|---|---|
| **set up your account** | No server is entered yet. Tap the line and fill in the **account** screen. |
| **connecting…**, **syncing…** | The app is talking to the server. Wait. |
| **synced 14:32** | Time of the last successful sync. |
| **no open task** | The current list has nothing left to do. |
| **wrong username or app password** | The server refused the login. Check the username; if two-factor authentication is on, create an application password on the server and use that one. |
| **no task list found at this address** | The server answered, but no list able to hold tasks was found there. Check the address in **server**, and that the account has at least one task list. |
| **this phone cannot send … to the server** | The phone's system cannot send one kind of request that CalDAV needs (its name is shown). The app cannot sync on this phone. |
| **sync failed: …** | Any other failure. The words after the colon come from the system or the server, often in English: no network, an address that does not exist, an address without `https://`, or a refusal by the server such as `PUT: HTTP 412` (the task was changed elsewhere: **sync now**, then try again). |
| **no app for this on the phone** | You chose **call**, **write to** or **open**, and the phone has no app able to do it. |
| **credentials imported** | The account was read from the file; the app connects. |
| **not a Reader's credentials file** | The file chosen was not made by **export credentials** of a Reader's app. |
| **this file holds nothing for Reader's Tasks** | It is a credentials file, but exported by another Reader's app only. Export from Reader's Tasks. |
| **the file cannot be read: …** | The phone could not open the file; the reason follows. |
| **no account to export yet** | Nothing has been entered in **account**. |
| **export failed: …** | The file could not be prepared for sharing; the reason follows. |

A task you ticked comes back by itself: the change did not reach the server. Look at the last line of the screen for the error, then tick again once **sync now** has worked.

A widget shows old tasks: open the app, which syncs and redraws the widgets.

### On the computer

| Message | What it means, what to do |
|---|---|
| **not connected — Ctrl+, to set up** | No server is entered. Press Ctrl+, or click **⚙**. |
| **connecting…**, **syncing…**, **adding…**, **renaming…**, **saving order…** | The program is talking to the server. |
| **3 open · synced 14:32** | Number of open tasks in the list and time of the last sync. |
| **no open task** | The current list has nothing left to do. |
| **wrong username or app password** | The server refused the login. Press Ctrl+, and correct them. |
| **no task list found at this address** | No list able to hold tasks at the address in **server**. |
| **the server refused the request (HTTP …)** | The server answered with an error; the number is its code. After ticking, renaming or moving a task, it usually means the task was changed elsewhere: press F5 and do it again. |
| **the server did not answer in time** | No answer after 30 seconds. Try again later. |
| **cannot reach the server** | No network, or the address leads nowhere. |
| **this server address is not valid** | The address in **server** is incomplete; it must start with `https://`. |
| **network error — …**, **error — …** | Another failure; the detail follows, often in English. |
| **the server sent an unreadable answer** | The address answers, but not as a CalDAV server does. Check the address. |
| **credentials exported to … — the file holds your passwords: keep it private** | The export worked; the file's place is shown. |
| **credentials imported** | The account was read from the file. |
| **not a Reader's credentials file** | The file chosen for import, or the existing file chosen for export, is not a Reader's credentials file. |
| **this file holds nothing for readers-tasks** | It is a credentials file, but without an account for Reader's Tasks. |
| **cannot use this file — …** | The file could not be read or written; the system's reason follows. |


# Reader's Notes

*Android 1.6.1 · Windows, macOS, Linux 1.3.2*

## What it is

Reader's Notes keeps plain-text notes: one list, one page, black and white. A note is a text file whose first line is its title. On the phone you can type a note or dictate it; the speech is turned into text on the phone itself and nothing you say is sent anywhere. If you wish, the notes are kept in step with a folder on your own WebDAV server (kDrive, Nextcloud…), one `.txt` file per note, so that the phone, the desktop app and any other device see the same plain files.

The app deliberately has no formatting (no bold, no lists, no images), no colours, no reminders, no account with anyone but your own server, and no tracking. Without a server the notes simply stay on the device.

Two programs share this chapter: the Android app, and the desktop app for Windows, macOS and Linux, described in "On the computer". Both exist in English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese and Russian, and follow the language of the system.

## First start

### On the phone

The app asks nothing when it opens. You see the title **notes**, an empty list with the line **nothing yet. The first line of a note is its title.**, and at the bottom two rows side by side, **+ new note** and **● dictate**. Under them a small line reads **on this phone only — tap to set up a WebDAV folder**.

You can write straight away. Two things are asked only when you need them:

- **The microphone**, the first time you tap **● dictate**. It is needed to record what you say. On recent versions of Android the app asks at the same moment for permission to show notifications: while you dictate, a notification shows the running time and a **stop** button.
- **A WebDAV folder**, only if you want the notes on several devices. Tap the small line under the list (or **⋯** then **settings**) and fill the four lines at the top:

| Line | What to put |
|---|---|
| **server** | The address of your server, starting with `https://`. For kDrive: `https://ID.connect.kdrive.infomaniak.com`, where ID is the number in the kDrive web address. Nextcloud and other servers take their usual WebDAV address. |
| **username** | Your login on that server (for kDrive, your Infomaniak login). |
| **password** | Your password; an application password if two-factor authentication is on. |
| **folder on the server** | The folder that holds the notes. It reads **Notes** from the start and is created on the server if it is missing. |

The first time you dictate, the speech model is downloaded (574 MB with the standard choice, see "Settings").

### On the computer

The first time the desktop app opens without a server, the settings window opens by itself with the same four fields. Fill them and press **save**, or press **cancel** to keep the notes on this computer only. The status line under the list then reads **on this computer only — Ctrl+, to set up a WebDAV folder**.

## The screens

### The list of notes (phone)

The title line reads **notes**, with **⋯** on the right. Each note takes two lines: its title (the first line of the note, or **untitled**), then a small line with when it last changed (the time for today, **yesterday**, otherwise the date), the next line of the note, and the sign **✎** when the note has not yet reached the server. While a dictation is being written into a note, that small line shows **fetching the model · N%** or **transcribing · N%** instead.

- **Tap a note** to open it.
- **Long press a note** to open its menu, titled with the note's title: one line for each phone number, mail address or web address found in the note (**call …**, **write to …**, **open …**), then **share**, **move to a folder** (only when folders are on) and **delete**.
- **+ new note** opens an empty note with the keyboard up.
- **● dictate** creates a note and opens it with the microphone on.
- **The small line at the bottom** shows the state of the sync (**syncing…**, **synced 21:03 2↑ 1↓ 1−**, or the error). Tap it to sync now. Without a server it reads **on this phone only — tap to set up a WebDAV folder** and a tap opens the settings. The figures count the notes sent (↑), received (↓) and deleted (−).

The **⋯** menu holds **+ new note**, **find**, **sync now** (only when a server is set), then under a line **black on white** or **white on black** (whichever the app is not showing at present) and **settings**.

When a search is active, the title line shows the searched words in quotation marks with **←** before them; tap it to return to the full list. If nothing matches, the list reads **nothing found**.

### The folders (phone, when folders are on)

With the **folders** setting on, the first page lists the folders instead of the notes: **all notes** first (filled folder sign, with the total number of notes), then each folder in alphabetical order with its number of notes, then **new folder** (dashed sign).

- **Tap all notes** to see every note; notes that sit in a folder show its name on their small line.
- **Tap a folder** to see its notes. The title line shows the folder's name with **←** to go back.
- **Long press a folder** for **rename** and **delete the folder**.
- **Tap new folder**, type the **name of the new folder** and confirm with **ok**.

The bottom rows **+ new note** and **● dictate** and the sync line are the same as in the list. A note started from the folders page or from **all notes** has no folder; a note started inside a folder is filed there. The **⋯** menu also holds **new folder**.

### The note (phone)

The title line shows the first line of the note (or **new note** while it is empty), with **←** to go back and **⋯** on the right. The rest of the page is the text. An empty page reads **write here. The first line is the title.** A tap places the cursor; a long press selects text to copy, cut or paste, as in any text field.

Everything is saved as you type; there is no save button. When you leave the note it is sent to the server if one is set. A note left empty is discarded.

At the bottom, **● dictate** speaks into this note. While the microphone is open the row turns to **■** followed by the running time, under a line that moves with your voice; tap it to stop.

The **⋯** menu holds the **call …**, **write to …** and **open …** lines for what the note contains, then **share** and **delete**, and under a line **black on white** or **white on black**.

### Settings (phone)

One page, reached from **⋯** then **settings**. Tap **←** to go back. Every line is described under "Settings".

### The main window (computer)

On the left: the **find** field, the list of notes (same two lines per note as on the phone), **+ new note**, then the status line and **⚙** for the settings. On the right: the title of the open note with **⋯**, and the page. See "On the computer".

## How to

### Write a note

1. Tap **+ new note**.
2. Type. The first line becomes the title.
3. Tap **←** when you have finished. The note is already saved.

### Dictate a new note

1. On the list, tap **● dictate**. Allow the microphone the first time.
2. Speak. The page reads **speak. Tap ■ when you have finished; the words appear a moment later.** The microphone stays open with the screen off; a notification, **dictating**, shows the time and a **stop** button.
3. Tap **■** (or **stop** in the notification).
4. Wait a moment: the row shows **transcribing · N%** (and **fetching the model · N%** the first time), then the text appears in the note, cut into paragraphs at your pauses.

You may leave the note or the app while the words are being written; the notification then reads **writing what was said**, and the text is added to the note when ready. The sound is deleted once its text is in the note. A recording shorter than about half a second is ignored.

### Dictate into an existing note

1. Open the note and tap where the words should go.
2. Tap **● dictate**, speak, tap **■**.

The words arrive at the cursor, with a space before or after when needed. If you have left the note meanwhile, they are added at its end, after an empty line. You can start another dictation while the previous one is still being written.

### Find a note

1. Tap **⋯**, then **find**.
2. Type a word or a few letters and tap **find**.

The list keeps only the notes whose text contains what you typed, capitals ignored. Tap the title line (**←**) to return to the full list. From the folders page the search covers all notes; from inside a folder it covers that folder only.

### Call a number, write to an address, open a link found in a note

1. Long press the note in the list, or open it and tap **⋯**.
2. Tap **call …**, **write to …** or **open …**.

The phone app opens with the number ready; it never calls by itself. A mail address opens your mail app, a web address your browser. Up to eight such lines are offered per note. Phone numbers need 7 to 15 digits, so dates, prices and room numbers are left alone; web addresses must begin with `http://`, `https://` or `www.`.

### Share a note

Long press the note (or open it and tap **⋯**), then **share**. The whole text is handed to the app you pick, with the title as subject.

### Delete a note

Long press the note (or open it and tap **⋯**), then **delete**. There is no confirmation and no bin. If a server is set, the file is deleted there too at the next sync.

### Turn text from another app into a note

In the other app, select or share text and choose Reader's Notes in the share menu. A new note opens with that text. If the other app gave a subject, it becomes the first line.

### Keep the notes in step with a server

1. Open **settings** and fill **server**, **username**, **password** and, if needed, **folder on the server**.
2. Tap **sync now** on the same page.

From then on the sync runs by itself: when the app opens (unless **sync when the app opens** is off), when you leave a note, when you leave the app with something not yet sent, after a deletion or a move, and whenever you tap **sync now** or the status line.

### File notes in folders

1. In **settings**, tap **folders** to turn it **on**.
2. Back on the first page, tap **new folder**, name it, confirm.
3. To file an existing note: open **all notes**, long press the note, tap **move to a folder**, then the folder. The current place is marked **●**; **no folder** takes the note out of its folder.

### Rename or delete a folder

1. On the folders page, long press the folder.
2. Tap **rename**, type the new name and confirm; or tap **delete the folder**, then **delete the folder, keep the notes**.

Deleting a folder never deletes its notes: they stay in **all notes**, without a folder.

### Set up another device with the same server

1. On the device that works, open **settings** and tap **export credentials**. Choose where to send the file (your own cloud folder, for instance). The file is named `readers-credentials-notes.json` and holds the password.
2. On the other phone, open **settings**, tap **import credentials** and pick the file. The line then reads **credentials imported** and a sync starts.

The desktop app reads the same file (see "On the computer").

### Switch between white on black and black on white

Tap **⋯** on the list or in a note, then the line **black on white** or **white on black**.

## Settings

### On the phone

| Setting | What it does | Choices | At the start |
|---|---|---|---|
| **server** | Address of the WebDAV server | text | empty |
| **username** | Login on the server | text | empty |
| **password** | Password on the server (shown as dots) | text | empty |
| **folder on the server** | Folder that holds the notes | text | Notes |
| **sync when the app opens** | Syncs each time the app comes to the front | **on** / **off** | **on** |
| **folders** | Files the notes in folders; the first page then lists them | **on** / **off** | **off** |
| **sync now** | Syncs at once; shows the last result. Present only when a server is set | – | – |
| **export credentials** | Shares a file with server, folder, username and password. Present only when a server is set | – | – |
| **import credentials** | Reads such a file | – | – |
| **careful** / **ordinary** | The speech model used for dictation; the chosen one is shown inverted | **careful** (574 MB, **recommended**, **four times slower**) / **ordinary** (190 MB) | **careful** |
| **language spoken** | The language you dictate in | the phone's language / english / **detected** | the phone's language |
| **colours** | The two colours of the app | **white on black** / **black on white** | **white on black** |
| **text size** | Size of all text | S / M / L | M |
| **font** | Typeface of all text | sans-serif / serif / mono | sans-serif |
| **haptic tick** | A short vibration when you start or stop a dictation | **on** / **off** | **on** |

Tap a line to change it: text lines open a small box to type in (**ok** to keep, **cancel** to leave unchanged); the others move to the next choice at each tap. A text line cannot be saved empty.

Under **folders** the page explains: *Notes filed in folders: the first page lists them, « all notes » first. On the server each folder is a subfolder of the notes folder. Off: all notes in one list.*

Each speech model shows its size, then **to fetch** until it is on the phone, or **fetching the model · N%** while it downloads. Choosing a model does not download it; that happens at the next dictation. If you choose **ordinary** but only **careful** is on the phone, **careful** is used.

The last two lines show the name of the app with **plain-text notes, one file each, in a WebDAV folder**, and the credits.

### On the computer

See "On the computer": the settings window holds the four server fields, **font** and **folders**; colours and text size are changed with keys.

## Your data

### Where the notes live

On the phone, notes are kept in the app's private storage. On the computer they are files in your user folder (see "On the computer"). With a server set, each note is also one `.txt` file in the folder you named, readable with any text editor. The file is named after the note's title; change the first line and the file is renamed at the next sync. A note without a title is named `untitled.txt`; two notes with the same title become `Title.txt` and `Title (2).txt`. Files ending in `.md` found in the folder are read as notes too.

### What goes over the network

- To **your WebDAV server**: the notes, with your username and password. On the phone the address must begin with `https://`.
- To **huggingface.co**, once per speech model: the download of the model. Nothing about you or your notes is sent.

Nothing else. What you dictate is turned into text on the phone.

### How the sync decides

- A note changed here and untouched on the server is sent.
- A note changed on the server and untouched here is received.
- A note changed on both sides: the server's text is kept as a second note whose title ends with **(server copy)**, and yours is sent. Nothing is overwritten without a copy.
- A note deleted here is deleted on the server, unless it changed there since; in that case it comes back as a new note.
- A note deleted on the server is deleted here, unless you changed it here since; in that case it is sent again.
- Anything new on either side crosses over.

### Folders on the server

Each folder is a subfolder of the notes folder, one level deep. A folder created here is created there; renaming it here moves its notes into a folder of the new name there. A folder deleted on the server disappears here. A folder deleted here leaves the server once no file is left in it; if someone else's files remain, it stays and comes back at the next sync. Subfolders whose name starts with a dot are ignored. Subfolders are looked at only when the **folders** setting is on, or once anything has been filed in a folder; turning the setting off afterwards shows one list again but keeps the folders in sync.

### Changing server or folder

On the computer, when you change the server or the folder, every note on the computer is sent to the new place.

On the phone it is different: notes that were already synced and are not found in the new place are taken as deleted on the server and removed from the phone at the next sync (they remain in the old folder on the old server). To move to a new folder from the phone, copy the files into it on the server first.

### The credentials file

**export credentials** writes a small file holding the server, the folder, the username and the password in clear. Keep it private and delete it once imported. The same kind of file is shared by Reader's Calendar, Tasks and Notes, each with its own section. If the file has no section for Notes but has one for Reader's Recorder, its server, username and password are taken (not its folder) and the app says **server and login taken from Reader's Recorder**. Colours, text size and font are never in the file.

### Dictation

The recording is kept on the phone only until its text is in the note, then deleted. If the writing was interrupted (no connection to fetch the model, the app closed), the recording waits and is taken up again the next time the app opens. The speech model is stored once and shared with Reader's Recorder, Reader's Audio Player and Reader's Podcasts when they are installed: whichever app has it lends it to the others.

### Backup and uninstalling

The phone app lets Android's own backup include its data, if you use it. Uninstalling the phone app removes the notes on the phone, the settings and the speech model it downloaded. Notes on your server are not touched.

## With the other apps

### Home-screen widgets

Two widgets work with any launcher, in the app's two colours:

- **Reader's Notes · latest note** (one line): the title of the most recent note, with when it changed and its next line. Tap it to open that note; tap **+** for a new note. Without notes it reads **no note yet**.
- **Reader's Notes · latest notes** (a list, resizable): the word **notes** (tap to open the app), **+** for a new note, and the notes, most recent first. Tap one to open it.

### Reader's Launcher

Reader's Launcher shows the latest notes on its notes tile and can open a note, start a new one, or start a new one with the microphone open. Only apps signed by the same author may read the notes this way.

### Other links

- **Share menu**: Reader's Notes appears in the share menu of other apps for plain text, and shares its own notes as plain text.
- **Speech model**: shared with Reader's Recorder, Reader's Audio Player and Reader's Podcasts.
- **Credentials file**: understood by the desktop app; a Reader's Recorder file can supply the server and login.

## On the computer

### How it differs from the phone

The list and the page sit side by side in one window. There is no dictation, no **share**, no **call … / write to … / open …** lines and no widget. The sync has no on/off switch: with a server set it runs when the window opens, every two minutes, 20 seconds after you stop typing, when you move to another note or another window, when you quit, and on demand. The app starts **black on white**.

### The window

- **find** field at the top left: the list narrows as you type and always searches all notes. **Enter** opens the first match, the **Down** arrow moves into the list, **Esc** clears the search.
- **The list**: click a note to open it. The app reopens the note you were last in.
- **+ new note** under the list. You can also just type on the empty page: that starts a note.
- **Status line**: **syncing…**, **synced 09:33 1↑ 2↓**, or the error; click it to sync (or to open the settings when no server is set). **⚙** opens the settings.
- **⋯** above the page: **+ new note**, **find**, **sync now** (with a server), **delete this note**, **white on black** or **black on white**, **settings**.
- **Right click on a note**: **move to** (with folders on; the current place is marked **●**, **no folder** first) and **delete**. Right click on the empty part of the list: **+ new note**. Right click on **all notes** or **+ new folder**: **+ new folder**.

A note is saved a moment after you stop typing. An empty note is dropped when you leave it. Deleting has no confirmation. A page open on screen follows the server when you are not typing in it; if the server's version arrives while you are typing, it is kept as a second note ending in **(server copy)**.

### Folders on the computer

In the settings, tick **file the notes in folders**. The list then starts with the folders: **all notes**, each folder with its count, **+ new folder**. Click one to open it; the line **← …** above the find field goes back to the folders. New notes go into the open folder. Right click a folder for **rename…** and **delete the folder** (the app asks *Delete the folder “…”? Its notes stay in “all notes”.*). Click **+ new folder** and type the **name of the new folder**.

### The settings window

Opened with **Ctrl+,**, **⚙**, or **⋯** then **settings**.

| Field | What it does | At the start |
|---|---|---|
| **server** | Address of the WebDAV server (the field shows a kDrive address as an example) | empty |
| **username** | Login | empty |
| **password** | Password | empty |
| **folder on the server** | Folder holding the notes | Notes |
| **font** | **sans-serif**, **serif** or **mono** | sans-serif |
| **folders** › **file the notes in folders** | Shows the folders at the top of the list | unticked |
| **import credentials…** | Reads a credentials file and applies it at once | – |
| **export credentials…** | Writes this app's saved server settings into a credentials file; if the file already holds other Reader's apps, they are kept | – |

**save** keeps the changes and syncs; **cancel** leaves everything as it was. Press **save** before exporting if you have just typed new values. The bottom line shows the version.

### Keys

| Key | Effect |
|---|---|
| Ctrl+N | new note |
| Ctrl+F | go to the find field |
| Alt+Up / Alt+Down | previous / next note |
| Ctrl+T | switch between white on black and black on white |
| Ctrl+= or Ctrl++ / Ctrl+- | larger / smaller text (9 to 24 points, 13 at the start) |
| F5 or Ctrl+R | sync now |
| Ctrl+, | settings |
| Ctrl+Q | quit |

### Command line

- `readers-notes --export-credentials FILE` writes the credentials file without opening the window.
- `readers-notes --import-credentials FILE` reads one.

### Where things are kept

| System | Notes and settings |
|---|---|
| Linux | `~/.local/share/readers-notes/` for the notes, `~/.config/readers-notes/config.json` for the settings |
| Windows | `%APPDATA%\Readers Notes\` |
| macOS | `~/Library/Application Support/readers-notes/` |

The settings file holds the password and is readable by your user account only.

## Limits

- Plain text only: no formatting, no images, no attachments, no checklists, no reminders.
- No bin and no undo for a deleted note; no confirmation before deleting a note.
- Folders are one level deep. A folder name keeps at most 60 characters; the characters `\ / : * ? " < > |` are replaced by spaces. A name already taken is refused without a message.
- The title is the first non-empty line, up to 80 characters.
- On the phone, a note is moved to a folder from its long press in the list, not from the open note.
- On the phone, the server address must be `https://`.
- On the phone, a settings line cannot be emptied: a server once entered can be replaced, not removed.
- Dictation exists on the phone only. Languages offered: the phone's language, English, or **detected**. The app keeps the phone awake for up to two hours of recording.
- The sync is with a WebDAV folder only; there is no other cloud service and no sharing of a note between people other than through that folder.
- Sorting is fixed: most recently changed first.

## Messages and problems

| Message | Meaning and what to do |
