|---|---|
| **on this phone only — tap to set up a WebDAV folder** / **on this computer only — Ctrl+, to set up a WebDAV folder** | No server is set. The notes stay on this device. Nothing to do unless you want the sync. |
| **syncing…** | A sync is running. |
| **synced 21:03 2↑ 1↓ 1−** | The last sync worked, at that time: notes sent, received, deleted. |
| **✎** on a note's small line | The note has changes not yet on the server. They go at the next sync. |
| **wrong username or password** | The server refused the login. Check **username** and **password**; with two-factor authentication use an application password. |
| **sync failed: …** / **sync failed** | The sync stopped; the words after the colon come from the system or the server (for instance no connection, or an address that does not exist). Check the **server** address and the connection, then **sync now**. |
| **this phone cannot send … to the server** | The phone's system cannot make a request the sync needs. The sync cannot work on this phone. |
| **cannot reach the server** (computer) | No connection, or a wrong address. |
| **the server refused: …** (computer) | The server answered with an error; check the address and the folder. |
| **not a WebDAV folder at this address** (computer) | The address answers, but not as a WebDAV server. Check **server**. |
| **… (server copy)** at the end of a title | The note was changed on two devices; this one holds the server's text. Compare the two notes, keep what you want, delete the other. |
| **nothing found** | No note contains the searched words. |
| **no app for this on the phone** | No app can handle the number, address or link you tapped. |
| **dictating needs the microphone** | The microphone was refused. Allow it in Android's settings for the app, then tap **● dictate** again. |
| **the microphone cannot be opened** | The phone did not give the microphone (another app may be using it). Try again. |
| **fetching the model · N%** | The speech model is being downloaded; an interrupted download carries on from where it stopped. |
| **transcribing · N%** | What you said is being written. |
| **nothing was heard** | The recording held no speech. |
| **not written yet: …** | The writing failed (often: no connection to fetch the model). The recording is kept and tried again the next time the app opens. |
| **the model cannot be read** | The speech model file cannot be opened. Dictate again; pick the other model in the settings if it persists. |
| **stopped** | The writing was interrupted. |
| **credentials imported** | The file was read; server, folder, username and password are set. |
| **server and login taken from Reader's Recorder** | The file had nothing for Notes; the Recorder's server and login were used. Check **folder on the server**. |
| **not a Reader's credentials file** | The file is not a credentials file. Pick the right one. |
| **this file holds nothing for Reader's Notes** (on the computer: **… for readers-notes**) | It is a credentials file, but with no section this app can use. |
| **the file cannot be read: …** / **cannot use this file: …** | The file could not be opened or written. |
| **credentials exported to … — the file holds your passwords: keep it private** (computer) | The export worked; the file is at the place shown. |


# Reader's Books

*Android 1.3.1*

## What it is

Reader's Books is a shelf of e-books and a reader for them, made for e-ink phones. The shelf is a plain list; a book is shown as pages of text cut at whole lines, turned by a tap, with no scrolling and no animation. It reads EPUB, MOBI, FB2 and plain-text files, shows the pictures of EPUB books inside the page, and remembers your place in every book.

The app is black and white only, with no icons. It has no store, no account and no network access at all: you bring your own files. It deliberately keeps only the text and the pictures of a book: the book's own fonts, colours, layout and links are dropped. It does not read PDF.

The app is available in English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese and Russian; it follows the language of the phone.

## First start

The app asks for nothing at first start: no permission prompt, no account, no server.

You see the shelf, titled **books**, with this message: **nothing on the shelf. Open an epub, mobi, fb2 or plain-text file: from here, from a file manager, or by sharing it to this app.** At the bottom of the screen is the line **+ open a book**.

Tap **+ open a book**, choose a file in the phone's file chooser, and the book opens on its first page.

## The screens

### The shelf (**books**)

The shelf lists your books, one per entry, the most recently opened (or added) first. Each entry shows:

- the title of the book (two lines at most);
- a smaller line with three items separated by dots: how far you are, as a percentage of the whole text (or **not started** if you have never opened it); when you last opened it (the time for today, **yesterday**, or the date); and the format (epub, mobi, fb2 or txt).

Controls:

- **Tap a book**: opens it at the page where you left it. A magazine issue opens on its contents instead (see "The contents of a magazine").
- **Long press a book**: opens a menu headed by the book's title, with **read** (open at the current page), **chapters** (the list of chapters; for a magazine this entry is called **contents**) and **remove from the shelf**.
- **+ open a book**, at the bottom: opens the phone's file chooser to add a book.
- **⋯**, at the top right: opens the shelf menu, with **+ open a book**, then the colour switch (it reads **black on white** when the app is white on black, and **white on black** when the app is black on white; tapping it switches), then **settings**.

A menu is closed by tapping outside it or with the phone's back button.

While a file is being added, a line **…** appears at the top of the list. If the file cannot be read, the line **this file could not be read** appears there instead.

### The page (reading a book)

The whole screen is the page:

- at the top, in small print, the title of the book;
- the text, and the pictures for EPUB books;
- at the bottom left, the title of the current chapter (nothing if the chapter has no title);
- at the bottom right, the page number within the current chapter and your position in the whole book, for example **3/12 · 45%**: page 3 of the 12 pages of this chapter, 45% of the book.

A very long chapter is cut into several parts. The second and following parts carry the chapter's title followed by a dot and a number (**· 2**, **· 3**…), and the page count is that of the part.

Controls:

- **Tap the right half of the screen**: next page. On the last page of a chapter, it goes to the first page of the next chapter.
- **Tap the left half of the screen**: previous page. On the first page of a chapter, it goes to the last page of the previous chapter.
- **Long press anywhere**: opens the book's menu.
- **The phone's back button**: leaves the book and returns to the screen you came from (the shelf, or the contents of a magazine).

The book's menu is headed by the book's title and has these entries:

| Entry | What it does |
|---|---|
| **chapters** | Opens the list of chapters. For a magazine the entry is **contents** and opens the contents of the issue. |
| **larger text** | Raises the reading size by 2. The line beneath shows the change, for example **20 → 22**. The largest size is 40. |
| **smaller text** | Lowers the reading size by 2. The line beneath shows the change, for example **20 → 18**. The smallest size is 12. |
| **remove from the shelf** | Removes the book and returns to the shelf. There is no confirmation. |
| **books** | Leaves the book, like the back button. |
| **black on white** / **white on black** | Switches the colours of the whole app. |

How the text is laid out: paragraphs are aligned on the left, without hyphenation; a paragraph that follows another is indented. Headings are larger and bold. In books and magazines that mark them, the lead paragraph is bold, and the section name, the author, captions and credits are smaller and grey. A page never ends in the middle of a line.

Pictures (EPUB only) are scaled to the width of the text, centred, and never cut: a picture that does not fit in what is left of the page moves whole to the next page, and a picture taller than the page is reduced to the height of the page. Very small pictures (less than 48 pixels wide or high) are left out.

### The chapters

This screen is headed by the title of the book, with an arrow **←**; tapping that line goes back, like the phone's back button.

It lists the chapters of the book. The chapter you are in is shown in inverted colours. Tap a chapter to go to its first page.

The titles come from the book's own table of contents; failing that, from the first heading of the chapter; failing that, the chapter is shown as a number. A plain-text file has a single entry, named after the book.

### The contents of a magazine

A magazine issue is an EPUB file whose contents page gives a section, a title and an author for each article. The app recognises such a file by itself and treats each article as a chapter.

The contents screen is headed by the title of the issue, with an arrow **←** to go back. It lists the articles under their section names (in small print, in capitals). Each article shows its title, its author if there is one, and on the right a small square of its first picture if it has one. Once you have started reading the issue, the article you are in is shown in inverted colours.

- **Tap an article**: opens it on its first page.
- In the article, pages turn as in a book; after the last page of an article, a tap on the right goes on to the next article.
- **The phone's back button** in an article returns to the contents.

### Settings (**settings**)

Reached from the shelf: **⋯**, then **settings**. The screen is headed **settings** with an arrow **←**; tapping that line goes back. Each line shows the current value in large print and the name of the setting beneath it; tapping the line changes the value. See the "Settings" section for the list.

At the bottom, two lines give information only: **Reader's Books** with **reads epub, mobi, fb2 and txt files**, and **Pierre Gallaz · developed with Claude Code**.

## How to

### Add a book from the app

1. On the shelf, tap **+ open a book** (at the bottom, or in the **⋯** menu).
2. In the phone's file chooser, tap the file.
3. The book is copied onto the shelf and opens. A magazine issue opens on its contents.

One file is added at a time.

### Add a book from a file manager

1. In your file manager, tap an EPUB, MOBI, AZW, AZW3, FB2 or text file.
2. If the phone asks which app to use, choose **Reader's Books**.
3. The book is copied onto the shelf and opens.

### Add a book by sharing it

1. In the app that holds the file, choose its share function.
2. Choose **Reader's Books** in the list.
3. The book is copied onto the shelf and opens.

Sharing works for EPUB, MOBI and FB2 files. A plain-text file cannot be shared to the app: open it from the shelf or from a file manager.

### Read and turn pages

1. On the shelf, tap the book.
2. Tap the right half of the screen for the next page, the left half for the previous page.

### Carry on where you left off

Tap the book on the shelf: it opens at the page where you left it. The book you read last is at the top of the shelf. You can also use the home-screen widget or the book tile of Reader's Launcher (see "With the other apps").

### Go to a chapter

1. In the book, long press the page.
2. Tap **chapters**.
3. Tap the chapter.

From the shelf: long press the book, then **chapters**, then the chapter.

### Read a magazine issue

1. On the shelf, tap the issue: its contents open.
2. Tap an article.
3. Press the phone's back button to return to the contents, or keep tapping on the right to pass to the next article.

To return to the contents from an article you can also long press the page and tap **contents**. To go straight back to the page where you stopped, long press the issue on the shelf and tap **read**.

### Make the text larger or smaller

1. In the book, long press the page.
2. Tap **larger text** or **smaller text**. Each tap changes the size by 2, between 12 and 40.
3. Repeat if needed: the menu closes after each choice.

Your place in the book is kept when the size changes. The same adjustment is in **settings**, where **automatic size** returns to the size chosen from the width of the screen.

### Switch between white on black and black on white

On the shelf, tap **⋯** then **black on white** or **white on black**. In a book, long press the page and tap the same entry. In **settings**, tap the **colours** line.

### Change the font

1. On the shelf, tap **⋯**, then **settings**.
2. Tap the **font** line until the font you want is shown: **sans-serif**, **serif** or **mono**.

The font applies to the page as well as to the shelf and menus.

### Remove a book

1. On the shelf, long press the book (or, in the book, long press the page).
2. Tap **remove from the shelf**.

The book is removed at once, without confirmation. Only the copy kept by the app is deleted, along with your place in it; the file you added it from is not touched.

### Replace a book with a newer file

Add the new file like any book. If a book with the same title and the same format is already on the shelf, the new file takes its place and your reading position is kept.

### Put the current book on the home screen

1. Long press an empty place on your home screen and choose to add a widget (the wording depends on your launcher).
2. Choose **Reader's Books · current book**.
3. Tap the widget to carry on reading.

## Settings

All settings are on the **settings** screen (shelf, **⋯**, **settings**).

| Setting | What it changes | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| **colours** | The colours of the whole app and of the widget. Tap to switch. | **white on black**, **black on white** | **white on black** |
| **reading size — tap for larger** | The size of the text in the page. Each tap adds 2, up to 40. The line shows the size, followed by **· auto** while the automatic size is in use. | 12 to 40 | automatic |
| **smaller text** | Lowers the reading size by 2, down to 12. The line beneath shows the change, for example **20 → 18**. | — | — |
| **automatic size** (**from the screen width**) | Returns the reading size to the automatic one, worked out from the width of the screen: between 17 and 24. | — | in use |
| **font** | The typeface of the page, the shelf and the menus. Tap to pass to the next one. | **sans-serif**, **serif**, **mono** | **sans-serif** |
| **size of the shelf and menus** | The size of the text everywhere except in the page. Tap to pass to the next one. | **S**, **M**, **L** | **M** |
| **keep the screen on while reading** | While a page is open, the screen does not switch off by itself, until fifteen minutes after the last page turn. When **off**, the phone's usual screen timeout applies. | **on**, **off** | **on** |
| **haptic tick** | A short vibration when a long press on the page opens the book's menu. | **on**, **off** | **on** |

## Your data

Everything stays on the phone. The app has no network permission: nothing is sent anywhere and nothing is downloaded. The only permission it uses is vibration, for the **haptic tick**; the phone does not ask you to grant it.

When you add a book, the app makes its own copy of the file in its private storage. You can then move, rename or delete the original: the book stays on the shelf. Next to the copies, the app keeps the list of books with, for each, its title, its format, the date it was last opened and your place in it, and your settings.

Your place is recorded at every page turn, as a position in the text and not as a page number: it stays right when you change the reading size, the font or the orientation of the screen.

There is no sync between devices, no export and no import of the shelf. The app does not exclude itself from Android's own backup; whether that backup runs depends on your phone and its settings.

Uninstalling the app deletes the copies of the books, your reading positions and your settings. The original files, where you kept them, are not touched.

## With the other apps

### Home-screen widget

The app offers one widget for any launcher: **Reader's Books · current book** (described as **the book you were reading; tap to carry on**). It is one row high, four cells wide, and can be made wider or narrower.

It shows the title of the book you opened last and, beneath, **book** followed by your position, for example **book · 45%**. Tap it to open that book at its current page. When the shelf is empty it shows **open a book** above **books**, and a tap opens the app.

The widget uses the colours chosen in the app. It is updated at every page turn.

### Reader's Launcher

Reader's Launcher can show a **book** tile with the book being read and your position; tapping it opens the book at its current page in Reader's Books. Only Reader's Launcher can read the shelf this way: other apps cannot see your list of books.

### Opening and sharing files

Reader's Books offers itself in the phone's "open with" list for EPUB, MOBI, AZW, AZW3, FB2 and plain-text files, and in the share list for EPUB, MOBI and FB2 files. In both cases the file is copied onto the shelf and opened.

## Limits

- No PDF, and no comics or audio-book formats. A file that is not recognised as EPUB, MOBI or FB2 is taken for plain text: a PDF added by mistake lands on the shelf and shows unreadable characters. Remove it.
- MOBI means the classic format, uncompressed or with standard PalmDoc compression. Files with copy protection (DRM) or with the other compression (HUFF/CDIC) are refused.
- Pictures are shown for EPUB only. Pictures in MOBI and FB2 files are left out.
- Plain-text and FB2 files are read as UTF-8. A plain-text file is one chapter; its title is the file name without the extension, with underscores replaced by spaces; paragraphs are separated by blank lines.
- The book's own styles are dropped: fonts, colours, italics, alignment, tables as grids. Links, including footnote links, cannot be followed.
- Pages are turned by tapping only: there is no swipe and no page turning with the volume buttons.
- No search in a book, no bookmarks, no highlights, no notes, no dictionary.
- No folders, no sorting choice and no search on the shelf: the order is always most recently opened first.
- Two books with the same title and the same format cannot be on the shelf together: the second replaces the first.
- One file is added at a time.
- No sync, export or import of the shelf and reading positions.

## Messages and problems

| Message | Meaning | What to do |
|---|---|---|
| **nothing on the shelf. Open an epub, mobi, fb2 or plain-text file: from here, from a file manager, or by sharing it to this app.** | The shelf is empty. | Tap **+ open a book**. |
| **…** | On the shelf: a file is being copied and read. In a book: the book is being opened. | Wait a moment. |
| **this file could not be read** on the shelf | The file you just added is not a book the app can read: damaged file, protected or unusually compressed MOBI, a file with no text in it, or a file the app was not allowed to read. Nothing was added. The line stays until another file is added successfully. | Try another copy of the book, or the same book in another format. |
| **this file could not be read** in place of the page | The app's copy of this book can no longer be opened. | Press the phone's back button, long press the book on the shelf, tap **remove from the shelf**, then add the file again. |
| **not started** | You have not opened this book yet. | — |

Other situations:

- **The book shows strange characters.** The file is probably not a supported format (a PDF, for example), or it is a text or FB2 file that is not in UTF-8. Remove it and use an EPUB, or convert the file to UTF-8.
- **The screen switched off while reading.** With **keep the screen on while reading** set to **on**, the screen stays on for fifteen minutes after the last page turn, then the phone's usual timeout applies again. Turn a page to start the fifteen minutes anew.
- **In a magazine, books in the menu does not go to the shelf.** When the article was opened from the contents, **books** returns to the contents, like the back button; press back once more to reach the shelf.


# Reader's Bookshop

*Android 1.2.1*

## What it is

Reader's Bookshop searches several free e-book catalogues at once (Project Gutenberg, Standard Ebooks, Wikisource, Bibliothèque numérique romande, Ebooks libres et gratuits, textos.info, the Internet Archive) and saves the file you choose to the phone. When a catalogue gives the author's dates, the app tells you whether the seventy years of Swiss and European copyright have passed. It is black and white, text only, with no account and nothing hosted by the app: a search goes from your phone to the site, the file comes from the site to your phone.

It does not read books: the file is handed to Reader's Books or to any other reading app. It does not decide for you whether you may download a book; it says what it knows and leaves the decision to you. One further source, Anna's Archive, is off unless you turn it on yourself after reading the warning the app shows.

The app's texts follow the phone's language: English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese or Russian (English for any other language). There is no language setting for the interface inside the app.

## First start

The app runs on Android 8.0 and later. It asks for no permission; it only uses the internet connection.

At the first start you see a single screen, **before the first search**, with the terms of use. They say, in short:

- the app is a search tool, like a web browser; it hosts no book, stores no catalogue and keeps no account;
- copyright differs by country: in Switzerland and in the European Union a text is free seventy years after its author's death, elsewhere the rules differ, so a book offered freely by a foreign site may still be protected where you are;
- you undertake to download only what you have the right to download where you are, and you alone are responsible for it;
- nothing is sent anywhere but to the sites you search; no statistics, no tracking, no logs.

Tap **I understand and accept** at the bottom. There is no other way past this screen. You can read the same text again later in **settings** → **terms of use**.

You then arrive on the **bookshop** screen, empty, with this text: "nothing here yet. Search for a book: the file appears in this list and in the phone's Download folder, ready for any reading app."

## The screens

On every screen except the bookshop, the title line at the top starts with **←**: tap it (or use the phone's back gesture) to go back. Menus and questions appear as a sheet at the bottom of the screen; tap outside the sheet or go back to close it without choosing.

### bookshop

The home screen: the list of the files you downloaded, newest first. Each line shows:

- the title;
- the author, the source and the format (for example "Project Gutenberg · epub");
- the state: the file's size and the time or date of the download when it is finished, or **waiting**, **finding the file…**, **downloading 42%**, **failed:** followed by the reason, or **file gone from the phone** when the file has been deleted or moved outside the app.

Controls:

- **Tap a finished line**: opens the file in a reading app.
- **Tap a line that is not finished** (waiting, downloading, failed, file gone), or **long-press any line**: opens the line's menu, with **open in a reading app** and **share** (finished files only), **try again** (a failed download, as long as the app has not been closed since), then **delete the file** (finished files) or **remove from the list** (the others). From this menu the deletion is immediate, without a question.
- **↗** at the end of a finished line: shares the file with another app.
- **×** at the end of every line: for a finished file, asks **delete this file from the phone?** with **delete the file** and **cancel**; for any other line, offers **remove from the list** and **cancel**.
- **+ search for a book** at the bottom: opens the search screen.
- **⋯** at the top right: the menu.

The **⋯** menu:

| Entry | What it does |
|---|---|
| **+ search for a book** | Opens the search screen. |
| **open the folder** (under it: **files in Download/Reader's Bookshop**) | Opens the folder of the downloaded files in the phone's file manager, or the phone's downloads screen if the folder cannot be opened directly. |
| **delete all files** | Shown only when the list is not empty. Asks **delete all N files from the phone?**, then **delete all files** or **cancel**. Every line goes, finished or not. |
| **sources** | Opens the sources screen. |
| **black on white** / **white on black** | Switches the colours at once; the entry names the colours you would get. |
| **settings** | Opens the settings screen. |

### search

From top to bottom:

- **The language line**, with **language of the search** under it: English, français, Deutsch, español, português, русский. Each tap moves to the next language. It decides which sources are asked and which language the books are in. At first it is the phone's language if it is one of the six, English otherwise.
- **The text field**, with the hint **title or author**.
- **The status line and the results** (see below).
- **search** at the bottom. The search key of the keyboard does the same. Nothing happens while the text field is empty.

Tapping **search** opens the sheet **before this search**. It reads: "I will download only books I have the right to download where I am: public domain, free licence, or a book I own where the law allows a copy. I am responsible for it." Tap **☐ I take this responsibility** so that it becomes **☑ I take this responsibility**, then **search**. The box is empty again at every search; **cancel** closes the sheet without searching.

While the search runs, the top of the list shows **searching…**, then **searching… · 3 of 7 sources answered**. Under it, one line per source says where it is: **searching…** (with the seconds elapsed after five seconds, as in "searching… 12 s"), **nothing**, **4 found**, or the reason it failed (for example **timed out**). At the end the status line reads **7 of 7 sources answered**, or **nothing found in the sources that answered · …**, or **no connection**.

The results follow, grouped in the fixed order of the sources, ten at most per source. Each result shows the title, then the author and a detail that depends on the source, then the name of the source:

| Source | Detail shown |
|---|---|
| Project Gutenberg | the book's number, as "#1234" |
| Standard Ebooks | "epub · azw3" |
| Wikisource | **N words on the first page** or **contents page**, then the beginning of the text |
| Bibliothèque numérique romande, Ebooks libres et gratuits, textos.info | the formats offered |
| Internet Archive | the year, then **scan** |
| Anna's Archive | format, size, year, publisher |

**Tap a result** to see what can be downloaded. A sheet may show **…** for a moment while the app asks the site, then:

- at the top, the rights line (see "Understand the rights line" below);
- one line per file: **download epub**, **download pdf**, **download txt**… with a note under it (the size, the kind of edition, **plain text**, **epub built from the pages**, or simply the name of the source). Tap one: the download starts and the result gets a **✓** after the source's name;
- **no file offered for this title** when the site has none;
- **open the page in the browser**: the book's page on the site.

The results stay when you go back to the bookshop and return to the search.

### sources

The list of the catalogues the app can search. Each line shows the name, then **on** or **off**, the languages it covers (en fr de es pt ru) and a sentence on how the app uses the site and what its terms allow. **Tap a line** to switch the source on or off. All are on at first, except Anna's Archive.

| Source | Languages | What the app says about it |
|---|---|---|
| Project Gutenberg | all six | Catalogue made for reading apps; one request per search and per book. Public domain in the USA; in Switzerland and the EU, the author's dates decide. |
| Standard Ebooks | English | Public search page and download links, used as a browser would. The files are dedicated to the public domain (CC0); the texts are public domain in the USA. |
| Wikisource | all six | Searched the way Wikimedia asks apps to. Volunteers check that each text is in the public domain (author dead for more than 70 years) or freely licensed. |
| Bibliothèque numérique romande | French | Public catalogue of a Swiss association; every book is in the public domain (author dead for more than 70 years). |
| Ebooks libres et gratuits | French | Public catalogue. Public domain texts (author dead for more than 70 years). |
| textos.info | Spanish | Public catalogue. Public domain texts. |
| Internet Archive | all six | Public search; books only available for lending are left out. Mostly scanned books; rights as stated by whoever uploaded them. |
| Anna's Archive | all six | Shadow library aggregating Library Genesis, Z-Library and others; most of its files are copyrighted. Only its public pages are read, as a browser does. Off unless you turn it on, and then only for files you have the right to download. |

When Anna's Archive is on, a block appears under the list:

- **mirrors · found on Wikipedia, fastest first**: the site's current addresses, each with its response time (**120 ms**), **unreachable** or **not tested**. The app takes the addresses from the Wikipedia article on Anna's Archive (at most once a day) and measures their speed (at most once an hour) when you search.
- **test the mirrors now**: fetches the list and measures again at once; the line shows **…** meanwhile.
- **add a mirror**: asks for an **address, https://…**. An address that does not begin with https:// is ignored.
- the key line, **membership key (fast downloads, optional)**: shows **none**, or four dots and the last four characters of the key. Tap it to type or change the key.
- **log**: the last things the sources did, kept in memory only until the app is closed. The last twelve lines are shown under it; tap **log** (**tap to share the last lines**) to send the lines as text to another app. **none** when there is nothing yet.

At the bottom, **other sites, in the browser**, followed by the language of the search: sites worth knowing that the app does not search. Tap one to open it in the browser. The list changes with the language of the search:

| Language | Sites (and the note shown under each) |
|---|---|
| French | Nos Livres (catalogue of the French public-domain ebook sites) · Bibliothèque électronique du Québec (public domain, Québec) · Bibebook (public domain epubs) · Efele.net (carefully made epubs) · Atramenta (public domain and Creative Commons) · Gallica (Bibliothèque nationale de France) · Les Classiques des sciences sociales (social sciences, Québec) · Libre Théâtre (plays) · Bibliothèque numérique TV5MONDE (classics) · e-rara (Swiss early printed books) |
| English | Faded Page (public domain in Canada: often not in Switzerland or the EU) · Project Gutenberg Australia (public domain in Australia) · Global Grey (public domain epubs) · Planet eBook (classics) · OpenStax (free textbooks) · Freeditorial (classics and contemporary authors) |
| German | e-rara (Swiss early printed books) · Projekt Gutenberg-DE (the largest German corpus; asks to be read in a browser) · Zeno.org (literature and philosophy) · TextGrid Repository (German literature, CC-BY) · Deutsches Textarchiv (1600–1900) · Ngiyaw eBooks (public domain epubs) |
| Spanish | Biblioteca Virtual Miguel de Cervantes (the Hispanic reference) · Elejandría (public domain epubs) · Ganso y Pulpo (carefully made epubs) · Freeditorial (classics and contemporary authors) |
| Portuguese | Domínio Público (Brazilian government library) · Biblioteca Nacional Digital (Biblioteca Nacional de Portugal) · Projeto Livro Livre (public domain epubs) · Literatura Brasileira (UFSC) (Brazilian literature) |
| Russian | Lib.ru (the classics before 1917) · ФЭБ (scholarly editions) · Русская виртуальная библиотека (annotated editions) · ilibrary.ru (classics) · Tolstoy.ru (the 90 volumes, free) |
| All six | DOAB (open access academic books) · OpenEdition Books (open access, humanities) · Open Library (borrowing of digitised books, with an account) |

### settings

See "Settings" below. The screen also holds **sources** (the sources screen), **export credentials**, **import credentials**, **terms of use**, and at the bottom the app's name and version with **searches free ebook catalogues and saves the books to the phone**, and the credits.

### before the first search (terms of use)

The text accepted at the first start. Opened from **settings** → **terms of use**, it ends with **ok**.

### browser check

This screen only appears with Anna's Archive. It shows a page of the site inside the app, in two cases:

- the site wants to check that a browser is visiting. The screen says: **the site wants to see a browser; when the page shows, the app carries on by itself**. If the page asks you something (a box to tick, a puzzle), answer it;
- a download page with a countdown. The screen says: **a download page: the countdown runs here, then the file follows**.

The app first tries to pass these pages by itself, out of sight; the screen is shown only when that is not enough. It closes by itself when the page is through. Tapping **← browser check** or going back gives up: the search or the download then fails with **browser check not passed**, and the app does not show the check again for one minute.

## How to

### Find and download a book

1. On the bookshop, tap **+ search for a book**.
2. Tap the language line until it shows the language of the book.
3. Type a title or an author in **title or author**.
4. Tap **search**.
5. Tap **☐ I take this responsibility**, then **search**.
6. Wait for the sources to answer, then tap a result.
7. Read the rights line at the top of the sheet.
8. Tap **download epub** (or another format).
9. Tap **← search** to return to the bookshop: the book is at the top of the list, with its progress.

You can start several downloads one after the other; they go on while you use the other screens of the app.

### Understand the rights line

The line at the top of the sheet of a result says what the app knows:

| Line | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **public domain in Switzerland and the EU: the author died in 1910** | The source gives the author's dates and more than seventy years have passed since the year of death. |
| **the author died in 1980: still protected in Switzerland and the EU** | The source gives the dates and the seventy years have not passed. The app still lets you download: the decision is yours. |
| **rights not stated: check before downloading** | The source gives neither dates nor a statement. |
| **public domain in the USA; files CC0** | Standard Ebooks. |
| **public domain, life + 70 years (BNR)** | Bibliothèque numérique romande. |
| **public domain (ELG)**, **public domain (textos.info)** | Ebooks libres et gratuits, textos.info. |
| **Wikisource** | A Wikisource text; see what the sources screen says about it. |
| **unknown: check yourself** | Anna's Archive. |

For Project Gutenberg, when the author's dates are missing, the line shows the rights statement sent by the site, in the site's own words. Author's dates come from Project Gutenberg and, when the record has them, from the Internet Archive.

### Read a downloaded book

Tap its line on the bookshop. The phone opens it in the reading app set for that kind of file, or asks which app to use. You can also long-press the line and choose **open in a reading app**.

### Send a book to another app or another device

Tap **↗** at the end of the line (or long-press the line, then **share**) and choose the app: a reader, a messaging app, a cloud folder.

### Find the files on the phone

Open **⋯** → **open the folder**. The files are in the phone's Download folder, in a subfolder named Reader's Bookshop, under the name "Author - Title" followed by the format.

### Delete a book

Tap **×** at the end of its line, then **delete the file**. The file is removed from the phone and the line from the list.

### Delete everything

Open **⋯** → **delete all files**, then confirm with **delete all files**.

### Try a failed download again

Tap the failed line, then **try again**. This entry exists only while the app has stayed open since the failure. Otherwise tap **remove from the list** and search for the book again.

### Clear a line whose file is gone

A line reading **file gone from the phone** means the file was deleted or moved with another app. Tap **×**, then **remove from the list**.

### Choose which sources are searched

Open **⋯** → **sources** and tap a source to switch it **on** or **off**. Only the sources that are on and that cover the language of the search are asked.

### Look on a site the app does not search

Open **⋯** → **sources**, scroll to **other sites, in the browser** and tap a site. To see the sites for another language, change the **language of the search** first (on the search screen or in the settings).

### Turn on Anna's Archive

1. Open **⋯** → **sources** and tap **Anna's Archive**.
2. Read the warning. It says: "Anna's Archive is a shadow library: it indexes Library Genesis, Z-Library and other collections, most of which hold copyrighted books, and courts have ordered its domains blocked. Ticking a box does not change the law. Turn it on only if you will use it for files you have the right to download: public-domain texts, open-access publications, or a copy of a book you own where your law allows it. Its pages are read by the app exactly as a browser would; a browser check may appear, and a download waits for the site's countdown."
3. Tap **turn on**, or **cancel** to leave it off.

The warning is shown the first time only. Tap the line again to switch the source off; switching it back on later no longer shows the warning.

With this source on, a search can take much longer (the app waits up to two minutes for it, against twenty-five seconds for the others), and the **browser check** screen may appear. Its files are offered as **slow download 1 (waits for the countdown)**, **slow download 2…**, and **fast download 1 (members)** when the page has such links.

### Enter an Anna's Archive membership key

1. Turn the source on, then, on the sources screen, tap the line **membership key (fast downloads, optional)**.
2. Type **the key from your Anna's Archive account** and tap **ok**.

A result from this source then offers **fast download (member key)** first, without countdown. To remove the key, tap the same line and choose **cancel**: closing this prompt in any way other than **ok** removes the key.

### Set up another phone with the same key

1. On the first phone: **settings** → **export credentials**, and send the file to yourself by the app of your choice. The file holds the key in readable form: keep it private.
2. On the other phone: **settings** → **import credentials**, and pick the file. The app answers **credentials imported**.

### Send the log when Anna's Archive fails without saying why

On the sources screen, with Anna's Archive on, tap **log** and choose the app to send the text with.

## Settings

Open **⋯** → **settings**. Each line shows the current value; each tap moves to the next choice.

| Setting | Choices | At first |
|---|---|---|
| **language of the search** | English, français, Deutsch, español, português, русский | the phone's language if it is one of the six, else English |
| **colours** | **white on black**, **black on white**, **as the phone** (follows the phone's dark mode) | **white on black** |
| **font** | **serif**, **sans serif**, **monospace** | **sans serif** |
| **size** | **small**, **medium**, **large** | **medium** |
| **haptic tick** | **on**, **off** | **on** |

The **haptic tick** choice is kept, but no control of the app vibrates in this version, whichever value is chosen.

The other lines of the screen are not settings: **sources**, **export credentials** (with the note **a file to set up another phone — it holds the Anna's Archive key: keep it private**), **import credentials**, **terms of use**.

Settings found on the sources screen: each source **on** or **off** (all on, Anna's Archive off), the added mirrors and the membership key (**none** at first).

## Your data

**On the phone only.** The app has no account and no server of its own.

- The downloaded files are in the phone's shared Download folder, subfolder Reader's Bookshop, where any file manager and any reading app can reach them. On Android 9 and older they go to the app's own folder on the phone's storage instead; **open the folder** shows its path.
- The list shown on the bookshop, the settings, the choice of sources, the mirrors and the membership key are kept in the app's private storage.
- The log is kept in memory and vanishes when the app is closed.

**What goes over the network.** Always over a secure (https) connection:

- your search words and the language, to each source that is on for that language, straight from the phone; then the requests for the book you tap and for the file you download;
- to the catalogues, the app announces itself by its own name; to Anna's Archive it presents itself as the phone's built-in browser and keeps the cookies the site sets;
- with Anna's Archive on: a request to Wikipedia for the list of addresses, a speed test of each address, and, if you entered one, your membership key to the site when you choose **fast download (member key)**.

Nothing else is sent: no statistics, no tracking, nothing to the app's author.

**No sync.** Nothing is synchronised between devices. The only thing you can carry over is the membership key, with **export credentials** and **import credentials**. The exported file is a Reader's credentials file; this app reads and writes only its own part of it, and only the key. The copy prepared for sharing is erased at the next export and at the next start of the app.

**Deleting.** The app deletes only files it downloaded itself. Deleting a line from the list deletes its file. A file deleted with another app shows as **file gone from the phone**.

**Uninstalling** removes the list, the settings and the key. The files in Download/Reader's Bookshop stay on the phone (on Android 9 and older, they are removed with the app). The app does not exclude itself from Android's own backup of app data, if that is enabled on the phone.

## With the other apps

- **Reading apps.** A tap on a finished line hands the file to a reading app. Reader's Books opens epub, mobi, azw3, fb2 and txt files handed over this way, and accepts epub, mobi, azw3 and fb2 files through **↗**.
- **Sharing.** **↗** sends the file to any app that accepts files. The log and the credentials file are shared the same way.
- **Browser.** **open the page in the browser** and the **other sites** open in the phone's browser.
- **File manager.** **open the folder** opens the phone's file manager.

The app offers no home-screen widget, receives nothing shared from other apps and opens no files itself.

## Limits

- The app does not display books; it needs a reading app.
- Six search languages only: English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese, Russian.
- Ten results at most per source and per search; there is no "more results". Make the search more precise instead.
- File types recognised: epub, mobi, azw3, fb2, txt, pdf, cbz, cbr. Other files a site may offer are not listed.
- The Internet Archive is searched by title only, among freely downloadable texts that have an EPUB; books available only for lending are left out. Many are scans.
- Bibliothèque numérique romande is searched by title; if no title matches, the books of the first matching author are shown.
- Standard Ebooks, Bibliothèque numérique romande, Ebooks libres et gratuits and textos.info each cover a single language and are not asked for the others.
- A Wikisource book is put together by the app from the pages of the site: text only, no images, 200 chapters at most. The search lists pages of the site, so a result may be a single chapter or a contents page, and the author is not shown in the results.
- The rights line covers Switzerland and the European Union (seventy years after the author's death) and only when the source gives the dates. It says nothing for other countries.
- Removing a line while its download is running does not stop the download: the file may still arrive in the folder, without a line in the list.
- A download does not survive the closing of the app: it shows **failed: interrupted** at the next start and must be started again from a new search.
- No notification is shown when a download finishes.
- A source waits at most 25 seconds (Anna's Archive: two minutes); past that it counts as **timed out**.

## Messages and problems

### On the search screen

| Message | Meaning, and what to do |
|---|---|
| **no source is on for this language: see sources in the menu** | Every source for the chosen language is off. Go back to the bookshop, **⋯** → **sources**, and switch one on. If a search does nothing at all, check this too. |
| **no connection** | The phone could not reach the sites. Check the connection and search again. |
| **nothing found in the sources that answered** | No source had the title. Try fewer words, the author alone, or another language. |
| **N of M sources answered** | Some sources did not answer; their own line says why. The results of the others are complete. |
| **timed out** (on a source's line) | The source was too slow. Search again later. |
| **browser check not passed** | Anna's Archive asked for a browser check that did not go through, or you left the check screen. Wait a minute, search again and let the page finish. |
| **too many requests, try later** | The site is limiting the number of requests. Wait before searching again. |
| **server answered 503** (or another number) | The site answered with an error. Try later. |
| **unexpected answer from the site** | The site sent something the app could not read. Try later; the site can still be reached with **open the page in the browser** or in the browser. |
| **connection failed** | The connection broke during the request. Try again. |
| **no mirror** | No address of Anna's Archive could be used. Open **sources** and tap **test the mirrors now**. |
| **no file offered for this title** | The site lists the book without a file the app can take. Try **open the page in the browser**. |
| **unknown error** | Something else went wrong. With Anna's Archive on, the **log** on the sources screen holds the detail. |

### On the bookshop

A failed line reads **failed:** followed by one of the reasons above or below.

| Message | Meaning, and what to do |
|---|---|
| **waiting** | The download is about to start. |
| **finding the file…** | The app is asking the site for the file's address, or waiting for a countdown. |
| **downloading 42%** | The file is arriving. For a Wikisource book, the figure is the share of the pages already fetched. |
| **download incomplete** | The connection dropped before the end, three times. Tap the line, then **try again**. |
| **the site sent a page, not the file** | The address led to a web page instead of the book. Try another format or another result. |
| **no download link** | The site gave no address for the file. Try again, or another result. |
| **the membership key gave no download link** | Anna's Archive did not accept the key for this file. Check the key, or use a slow download. |
| **no answer from the key** | The site did not answer the request made with the key. Try again later. |
| **page not found** | The Wikisource page no longer exists under that name. |
| **too many redirects** | The site sent the app from address to address without end. Try later. |
| **cannot create the file**, **cannot write the file** | The phone refused the file in the Download folder. Check the free space. |
| **interrupted** | The app was closed during the download. Remove the line and download again. |
| **file gone from the phone** | The file was deleted or moved outside the app. Remove the line. |

A message that comes from Anna's Archive itself may also appear as the site worded it, in English.

### In the settings

| Message | Meaning, and what to do |
|---|---|
| **no key to export yet** | There is no membership key to put in the file. Nothing else is exported. |
| **credentials imported** | The key from the file is in place. |
| **not a Reader's credentials file** | The file chosen is not a credentials file from a Reader's app, or it could not be read. |
| **this file holds nothing for Reader's Bookshop** | It is a Reader's credentials file, but without a key for this app. |

### On the sources screen

| Message | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **unreachable** | The address did not answer the last test. |
| **not tested** | The address has not been tested yet; tap **test the mirrors now**. |


# Reader's Recorder

*Android 1.10.1*

## What it is

Reader's Recorder is a voice recorder in black and white, made of text only. One tap records a memo, a lecture or a conversation, and the recording goes on with the screen off. The phone itself then writes the transcript, makes a cleaned copy that is easier to listen to and, if you turn the option on, lists the main points; none of this is sent anywhere.

You can add a folder on your own WebDAV server (kDrive, Nextcloud…) where the app copies each recording with its transcript, its cleaned copy and its main points. That folder is an export: the app never fetches anything back from it. There is no account, no advertising, and nothing goes to Google.

The app does not edit or trim recordings, does not let you correct a transcript, and does not import sound files made elsewhere.

## First start

At the first launch Android asks two permissions:

- **Microphone**: needed to record. If you refuse, the app asks again the next time you tap **● record**.
- **Notifications** (Android 13 and later): for the silent notification that stays on screen while a recording runs, with its **pause** and **stop** buttons, and for the ones shown during transcription and playback.

There is no account to create and no server to enter. The list is empty and shows **nothing recorded yet. ● below records; the notification or the widget stops.** Under it, a small line reads **on this phone only — transcribed here, nothing leaves the phone**, and the bottom row is **● record**.

The first time a recording has to be transcribed, the app downloads the transcription model (190 MB), once, over whatever connection the phone has at that moment. The progress is shown as **fetching the model · N%**.

A cloud folder is optional and can be added later from the settings (see "Set up a cloud folder").

## The screens

### The list (**recorder**)

The title line reads **recorder**, with **⋯** on the right for the menu.

Each recording is a row: the title in large type and, under it in small type, the date and time, the length, the kind (**memo**, **lecture** or **conversation**) and where it stands:

- **on this phone**: no cloud folder is set up;
- **to upload**: a cloud folder exists and the recording has not gone up yet;
- **in the cloud**: uploaded;
- **transcribed**: the transcript is written;
- while the phone works on it, the step in progress (**transcribing · N%**, **cleaning the sound**, **writing the main points, N%**…);
- if something failed, the error message instead.

A title is automatic: first the date and time, then the first words of the transcript once it exists. A title you type yourself stays. While the title is still the date, the date is not repeated under it.

Controls:

- **Tap a row**: opens the recording's page.
- **Long press a row**: opens a menu with **rename**, **share the audio**, **share the transcript** (only when there is a transcript), **delete** (with the note **here and in the cloud folder** when it has been uploaded) and **select several…**.
- **The small line above the bottom row** shows what is going on: the processing step in progress; or, with a cloud folder, the result of the last sync (**synced 21:03**, followed by the number of recordings sent and **↑**, or an error message) or **cloud folder:** and its name; or, without one, **on this phone only — transcribed here, nothing leaves the phone**. Tapping it starts a sync when a cloud folder exists, and opens the settings otherwise.
- **● record** (bottom row, inverted): starts a recording at once and opens the recording page.
- While a recording runs, its row in the list is inverted and reads **recording** with the running time, and the bottom row becomes **■ recording** with the running time. Tapping either opens the recording page; it does not stop the recording.

The **⋯** menu contains:

- **sync now** (only when a cloud folder is set up);
- the kind of the next recording (**memo**, **lecture** or **conversation**, with **kind of the next recording** under it): each tap moves to the next kind;
- **black on white** or **white on black**: switches the colours;
- **settings**.

### Selection mode

After **select several…**, each row carries a box (☐ or ☑), the title line shows **N selected** and the bottom row becomes **delete N**. Tap rows to tick or untick them; a long press also ticks. Tap **delete N** to delete all ticked recordings, on the phone and in the cloud folder. Tap the title line or press Back to leave without deleting.

### The recording page

The title line reads **recording** (or **paused**). Tapping it, or pressing Back, returns to the list while the recording goes on.

The page shows the running time in large figures, a level line that moves with the sound, the recording's title, and the kind with **tap to change** under it. Tapping the kind moves to the next one (memo, lecture, conversation); the choice also becomes the kind of the next recordings.

At the bottom: **pause** (which becomes **resume**) and **■ stop**. Stopping returns to the list. A recording shorter than half a second is discarded.

Opening the app while a recording runs leads straight to this page.

### A recording's page

The title line shows the recording's title (tap it to go back) and **⋯** for the menu.

- **The player line**: the position and the total length, and under them the date, the kind and the status. When a cleaned copy exists, the line ends with **playing the cleaned audio**: this is the copy you hear. On the right, **▶** plays and **❚❚** pauses.
- **The rule under the player** shows the position. Tap anywhere on it to jump there; if the recording was not playing, it starts from that point.
- **main points**: shown above the transcript when they have been written.
- **The transcript**, in paragraphs. When there is none yet, a line says why:
  - the step in progress, when the phone is working on this recording;
  - an error message; tap it to clear it and start again;
  - **the transcript will appear here once the phone has worked through it — tap to start now**;
  - **no transcription: it is turned off in the settings. Tap to change.**, which opens the settings.

The **⋯** menu contains, depending on the recording's state:

| Entry | When it is shown | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **rename** | always | asks for a title |
| **share the audio** | always | sends the sound file (the cleaned copy when there is one) to another app |
| **share the transcript** | when there is a transcript | sends the text to another app |
| **upload now** | cloud folder set up, recording not uploaded yet | starts a sync |
| **try again** | uploaded, and an error is shown | clears the error and syncs |
| **transcribe now** | not transcribed yet, the phone transcribes | starts the transcription |
| **write the main points (beta)** | the option is on, its model is on the phone, the recording is transcribed and has no points | asks for the points of this recording |
| **stop** | the phone is working on this recording | stops the work in progress |
| **delete** | always | deletes the recording, on the phone and in the cloud folder |

### Settings

Opened from the list's **⋯** menu. Each row shows the current value in large type and the name of the setting under it; a tap moves to the next choice. The section "Settings" below describes every row.

### Notifications

- **While recording**: **recording** (or **paused**) with the running time, and two buttons, **pause** / **resume** and **stop**. Tapping the notification opens the app. It is silent.
- **While the phone transcribes**: the recording's title, the step in progress with a progress bar, and **stop**.
- **During playback**: the recording's title, **playing** or **paused** with the position, and two buttons, **pause** / **resume** and **stop**.

In Android's notification settings the three categories are named **recording**, **transcription** and **playback**.

## How to

### Record

1. Tap **● record** at the bottom of the list (or the ● of the widget, or the tile in Reader's Launcher).
2. Speak. You can turn the screen off or leave the app.
3. Tap **■ stop** on the recording page, **stop** in the notification, or ■ on the widget.

Starting a recording stops any playback.

### Pause and resume

Tap **pause** on the recording page or in the notification, then **resume**. The time spent paused is not counted.

### Choose the kind: memo, lecture or conversation

- For the next recordings: list **⋯** menu, tap the kind; or settings, **kind of the next recording**.
- During a recording: tap the kind on the recording page.

The kind is shown in the list and, in the cloud folder, in the file name. It also decides how the microphone is used, and this is fixed when the recording starts: a memo uses the phone's voice-oriented sound processing; a lecture or a conversation uses the microphone's raw, unprocessed sound when the phone offers it. Lecture and conversation are otherwise treated alike.

### Listen to a recording

1. Tap the recording in the list.
2. Tap **▶**. Tap **❚❚** to pause.
3. Tap the rule to move to another moment.

Playback continues with the screen off and when you leave the app. It pauses when headphones are unplugged or when another app takes the sound, and stops at the end.

### Read the transcript

Open the recording: the transcript is under the player. It is written automatically after each recording when **who transcribes** is **this phone**. To start it at once, tap the line **the transcript will appear here… — tap to start now**, or **⋯** then **transcribe now**.

### Stop a transcription in progress

Tap **stop** in the transcription notification, or open the recording and choose **⋯** then **stop**. The work starts again the next time you open the app or finish a recording. To stop transcription for good, set **who transcribes** to **nobody**.

### Get the main points

1. In the settings, tap the row **also write the main points (beta)**. The first tap downloads its model (2105 MB); the row shows **fetching the model · N%**. You can leave the screen meanwhile.
2. When the download ends, the option turns **on** by itself.
3. From then on, every new recording gets its points after its transcript. They appear under **main points** on the recording's page.
4. For a recording made before you turned the option on, open it and choose **⋯** then **write the main points (beta)**.

The points are two sentences on the subject, then a list (up to eight points for a short recording, more for a long one). A transcript of fewer than about 120 words gets none. If writing the points fails twice for a recording, the app leaves it alone until you ask again with **write the main points (beta)**.

### Rename a recording

1. Long press the row and choose **rename**, or open the recording and choose **⋯** then **rename**.
2. Type the title and tap **ok**.

If the recording is in the cloud folder, its files there are renamed too.

### Share the sound or the transcript

Long press the row (or **⋯** on the recording's page), then **share the audio** or **share the transcript**, and pick the app to send it to. The sound shared is the cleaned copy when it exists, the original otherwise.

### Delete one recording

Long press the row and choose **delete**, or **⋯** then **delete** on its page. There is no confirmation and no undo. With a cloud folder, the recording's files there are deleted as well.

### Delete several recordings

1. Long press a row and choose **select several…**.
2. Tick the others.
3. Tap **delete N** at the bottom.

### Set up a cloud folder

1. Open the settings and tap **set up a cloud folder**.
2. **server — for kDrive, just the number in its web address**: for kDrive, type the number that follows /drive/ in kDrive's web address (the app gives the example ksuite.infomaniak.com/kdrive/app/drive/123456/…). For Nextcloud or any other WebDAV server, type the full https:// address. Tap **next**.
3. **username (e-mail)**: for kDrive, the e-mail you log in to Infomaniak with. Tap **next**.
4. **password (an application password if two-factor is on)**: your password; with two-factor authentication, create an application password instead (for Infomaniak: Manager, Security, Application passwords). Tap **connect**.

The app tries the connection at once, creates a folder named **Recordings** and starts sending. **how to set it up, step by step** shows these instructions inside the app.

To use another folder, tap the folder row (**folder on the server**) and type its name. To correct the server, the username or the password, tap the server row: the three questions come back with their current answers.

### Sync by hand

Tap the small line above **● record** in the list, or **⋯** then **sync now**, or the **sync now** row in the settings. The app also syncs each time it is opened, when a recording ends and when a transcription ends.

### Stop using the cloud folder

In the settings, tap **forget the cloud folder (recordings stay here)**. The server, username and password are erased from the phone. The recordings stay on the phone, and what was already sent stays on the server.

### Copy the cloud settings to another device

1. In the settings, tap **export credentials** (**a file to set up another device or app — it contains the password**).
2. Send the file to the other device by the means you choose.
3. On the other device, in the app's settings, tap **import credentials** and pick the file.

The app answers **credentials imported**, then syncs. A credentials file exported by Reader's Notes also works: the app takes its server, username and password (not its folder) and answers **server and login taken from Reader's Notes**.

### Change the look

In the settings: **colours**, **text size**, **font**. The colours can also be switched from the list's **⋯** menu.

## Settings

Cloud folder (top of the screen):

| Row | What it does |
|---|---|
| **set up a cloud folder** (**on this phone only**) | Shown when no folder is set up. Asks the three questions. |
| server row (**server** and the username under the address) | Shown once set up. Tap to answer the three questions again. |
| folder row (**folder on the server**) | The folder's name. Default: Recordings. |
| **sync now** | Starts a sync. Shows **syncing…**, then the result of the last sync. |
| **forget the cloud folder (recordings stay here)** | Erases the server, username and password from the phone. |
| **how to set it up, step by step** | Shows or hides the instructions. |
| **export credentials** | Shown once set up. Shares a file holding server, folder, username and password. |
| **import credentials** | Reads such a file. The result is shown under the row. |

Transcription and recording:

| Setting | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **who transcribes** | **this phone**, **nobody** | **this phone** |
| quality: **ordinary** (190 MB) or **careful** (574 MB, marked **recommended**) | tap one of the two rows; the chosen one is inverted | **ordinary** |
| **also make a cleaned copy (volume, rumble)** | **on**, **off** | **on** |
| **also write the main points (beta)** | **on**, **off** | **off** |
| **language spoken** | the phone's language, English, **detected** | the phone's language |
| **kind of the next recording** | **memo**, **lecture**, **conversation** | **memo** |

Look:

| Setting | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **colours** | **white on black**, **black on white** | **white on black** |
| **text size** | S, M, L | M |
| **font** | sans-serif, serif, mono | sans-serif |
| **haptic tick** | **on**, **off** | **on** |

Notes on these settings:

- **who transcribes**: with **nobody**, recordings are neither transcribed nor cleaned and get no main points; the three rows about quality, cleaned copy and main points disappear. Going back to **this phone** starts work on every recording not yet transcribed.
- **Quality**: **ordinary** is quick; **careful** makes fewer mistakes and is much slower. A model not yet on the phone is marked **to fetch**; it is downloaded at the next transcription. Changing the quality does not transcribe again what is already transcribed.
- **also make a cleaned copy (volume, rumble)**: after the transcript, the phone makes a second sound file with the low rumble removed and the loudness brought to a steady level, without compressing the dynamics. The original is kept. The copy is what you hear and what **share the audio** sends.
- **also write the main points (beta)**: needs a phone with about 6 GB of memory. On a smaller phone the row reads **off** with **this phone has N GB: not enough** and cannot be turned on. The model needs about 2.2 GB of free storage. When Reader's Audio Player already holds the model, the row says **shared with Audio Player** and nothing is downloaded.
- **language spoken**: tells the transcription which language to expect. **detected** lets it find out by itself.
- **haptic tick**: a short vibration when you tap **pause**, **resume** or **■ stop** on the recording page.

The last two rows show the app's name with **records and transcribes on the phone; a cloud folder is optional**, and the credits.

## Your data

**On the phone.** Recordings, transcripts, cleaned copies, main points and settings live in the app's private storage. Recordings are .m4a files (AAC, 128 kb/s, 48 kHz, mono). The cloud password, if you set a folder up, is kept in the app's settings on the phone. Uninstalling the app deletes all of it, including the downloaded models; only what was copied to a cloud folder remains.

**What goes over the network.**

- The transcription model (190 MB or 574 MB) and the main-points model (2105 MB) are downloaded once from the Hugging Face website. An interrupted download carries on from where it stopped.
- If you set up a cloud folder, the app talks to that server only, over https, with your username and password.
- Nothing else. Transcription, cleaning and main points all run on the phone.

**The cloud folder.** For each recording the app puts in the folder:

- the sound, named with the date, the time and the kind, for example 2026-03-14_09-30_memo.m4a; once you have typed a title, the title replaces the kind in the name;
- a small .json file beside it (title, kind, date, length, language);
- the transcript (.txt), the cleaned copy (the name ends with _nettoye.m4a) and the main points (the name ends with .resume.txt) as soon as the phone has made them; they follow at a later sync.

Renaming a recording in the app renames its files in the folder; deleting it deletes them. A sync never deletes anything by itself and never downloads anything: changes made in the folder from elsewhere do not come back to the phone, and a new phone connected to the same folder does not receive the old recordings.

**The credentials file.** **export credentials** creates a file named readers-credentials-recorder.json with the server, the folder, the username and the password in clear. Treat it as you would the password itself. The temporary copy the app keeps for sharing is erased at the next start.

**Models shared between apps.** Reader's Recorder, Reader's Audio Player and Reader's Podcasts use the same models. When one of them already holds a model, the others read it there instead of downloading it again.

## With the other apps

### Home-screen widgets

Two widgets work with any launcher. Both follow the app's colours and are one row high.

- **Reader's Recorder** (**record with one tap, stop with the next**): shows **record** with the latest recording and its length under it, and ● on the right. Tap ● to start. While recording it shows **recording** (or **paused**), **tap ■ to stop**, the running time and ■. Tapping the text opens the app. The microphone permission must already have been given in the app.
- **Reader's Recorder · listen** (**the latest recordings: ▶ plays, ‹ › step through them**): shows one recording at a time, the latest first, with its rank (for example 1/5), its date and its length. **▶** plays and **❚❚** pauses; **‹** goes to the more recent one, **›** to the older one; tapping the title opens the recording in the app. With no recording it reads **nothing recorded yet**.

### Reader's Launcher

Reader's Launcher offers two tiles for this app: **recorder** (the same one-tap record button, with the latest recording under it) and **recordings** (listen to the recordings, swiping from the latest to the older ones). What plays from a widget or a tile is the same playback as in the app: it shows on the recording's page and in the notification.

### Sharing

The app sends sound files, transcripts and the credentials file to other apps through Android's share menu. It does not receive anything shared from other apps.

## Limits

- One recording at a time, in mono. The format (.m4a, AAC 128 kb/s, 48 kHz) cannot be changed.
- No trimming or editing of the sound, no playback speed.
- A transcript cannot be corrected or selected in the app, and speakers are not told apart. To work on the text, share it to another app.
- The main points can be read in the app and are copied to the cloud folder, but there is no entry to share them.
- A recording already transcribed cannot be transcribed again with another quality or language.
- The cleaned copy is made together with the transcript. Turning the option on later does not add it to recordings already transcribed, and nothing is cleaned when **who transcribes** is **nobody**.
- The language choice is limited to the phone's language, English and **detected**.
- No import of sound files recorded elsewhere, no search, no folders.
- The cloud folder is WebDAV only, over https, and is one-way: the phone sends, it never receives. Syncing happens when the app is opened, when a recording or a transcription ends, or by hand; there is no sync on a timer.
- Changing the folder's name does not move what was already sent, and recordings already uploaded are not sent again to the new folder.
- Main points need about 6 GB of memory and a transcript of at least about 120 words.
- Deleting has no confirmation and no undo.

## Messages and problems

Processing steps, shown in the list, on the recording's page and in the notification:

| Message | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **waiting** | The phone is about to start on this recording. |
| **fetching the model · N%** | A model is being downloaded. |
| **transcribing · N%** | The transcript is being written. |
| **cleaning the sound** | The cleaned copy is being made. |
| **writing the main points, N%** | The main points are being written. |
| **(no speech detected)** | Written in place of the transcript when no speech was found. |

Transcription and models:

| Message | What to do |
|---|---|
| **the transcript will appear here once the phone has worked through it — tap to start now** | Wait, or tap the line. |
| **no transcription: it is turned off in the settings. Tap to change.** | Set **who transcribes** to **this phone**. |
| **the model is missing — try again to fetch it** | The model disappeared from the phone. Tap the message, or **⋯** then **transcribe now**. |
| **no connection** | The phone could not reach the server. Check the connection and try again. |
| **download refused (HTTP N)** | The download site refused. Try again later. |
| **download cut short — try again to carry on** | The download stopped before the end. Try again: it carries on from where it stopped. |
| **N% downloaded · tap to continue** | The main-points model is partly downloaded. Tap the row to carry on. |
| **not enough space: N MB needed** | Free that much storage, then tap the row again. |
| **this phone has N GB: not enough** | The phone has too little memory for the main points. |
| **no sound in this file** | The recording holds no sound that can be read. |
| **transcription failed:** followed by a reason | Tap the message on the recording's page to try again. |
| **download failed:** followed by a reason | Shown on the main-points row. Tap the row to try again. |

A recording that shows an error is left alone until you tap the message on its page or choose **transcribe now** or **try again** in its menu.

If the main points do not appear for a recording, the transcript may be too short, the phone may have lacked free memory at that moment, or two attempts may have failed. Open the recording and choose **⋯** then **write the main points (beta)**.

Cloud folder:

| Message | What to do |
|---|---|
| **syncing…** | A sync is running. |
| **synced** followed by the time | The last sync succeeded. A number with **↑** counts the recordings sent. |
| **cloud folder:** followed by the name | A folder is set up; no sync has run since the app started. |
| **wrong username or password** | Tap the server row in the settings and answer the three questions again. With two-factor authentication, use an application password. |
| **the server answered with an error (HTTP N)** | Check the server address and the folder name. |
| **this phone cannot send the … request** | This phone cannot speak to WebDAV servers in the way the app needs. |
| **sync failed:** followed by a reason | Check the connection and the server address, then sync again. |
| **upload failed:** followed by a reason | Shown on the recording. Choose **⋯** then **upload now**. |

Credentials file:

| Message | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **credentials imported** | The file was read and a sync has started. |
| **server and login taken from Reader's Notes** | The file came from Reader's Notes; its server, username and password were taken. |
| **not a Reader's credentials file** | The file is not a credentials file, or cannot be read. |
| **this file holds nothing for Reader's Recorder** | It is a credentials file, but with nothing this app can use. |


# Reader's Podcasts

*Android 1.1.2 · Linux 1.1.2*

## What it is

Reader's Podcasts is a podcast player that shows only text: you subscribe to podcasts, download or stream their episodes, and listen, and every episode resumes where you left it. On the phone it can also write down what is said in an episode and translate that text, entirely on the phone, so that you read along while you listen. A twin program for the computer shows the same lists in one window.

There is no account, no server, no artwork, no recommendations and no store. Two files you export and import yourself (the subscriptions and the settings with the listening positions) are the whole synchronisation between the phone and the computer.

The Android app runs on Android 10 and later. Both programs exist in six languages: English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese and Russian. The Android app does not handle YouTube; the computer program does, with the help of a separate tool.

In this chapter a podcast you follow is called a **channel**, as in the app.

## First start

### On the phone

On Android 13 and later the app asks once for permission to show notifications. They are used for three things: the playback controls in the notification shade and on the lock screen, the progress of downloads, and the progress of a transcription or translation. Each of these notifications carries a **stop** button. In Android's notification settings the last two appear as the categories **downloads** and **transcription**. The app asks for nothing else and needs no account.

With no subscription yet, the home screen shows:

"no subscriptions yet. + at the top finds a podcast by its name, or takes the address of its feed; sharing a link to this app works too."

Tap **+** at the top to add your first podcast (see "Subscribe to a podcast by its name").

### On the computer

Nothing is asked. The empty window says: "no subscriptions yet. + a feed below finds a podcast by its name, or takes the address of its feed." Click **+ a feed** at the bottom of the left column.

## The screens

This section describes the phone. The computer's window is described under "On the computer".

### Home

The home screen shows one list at a time. The title at the top names the list and ends with **▾**: tap the title to choose between **channels**, **episodes**, **favourites** and **downloaded**. The list being shown carries a ✓.

- **channels**: your subscriptions, the one that published most recently at the top. Under each name: the date of its latest episode and the number of episodes not yet heard. If the last refresh of that channel failed, the reason is shown in place of the date.
- **episodes**: every episode of every channel, newest first.
- **favourites**: the episodes you gave a star.
- **downloaded**: the episodes whose audio is on the phone.

To the right of the title:

- **↻** refreshes all channels.
- **+** opens **add a podcast**.
- **⋯** opens the menu.

Pulling the list down also refreshes: the line reads **pull to refresh**, then **let go to refresh**. While the channels are being fetched, a line counts them: "refreshing: 3 / 12".

A tap on a channel opens it: the title becomes the channel's name, **←** goes back to the channels (so does the phone's back button), **↻** refreshes that channel alone, and pulling down does the same. Inside a channel there is no **+**.

A long press on a channel opens its menu:

- **refresh**: fetches that channel alone.
- **on** or **off**, with **automatic download** written underneath: tap to switch automatic download for that channel.
- **unsubscribe**, with the feed's address underneath.

An episode takes up to two lines for its title, then one line that says, in order: ★ if it is a favourite, the channel (in lists that mix channels), the date, and the one thing worth knowing: **downloading 40 %**, **waiting**, an error, **heard**, "25 min left", **begun**, **on the phone**, or simply its length. Dates are written as words: **today**, **yesterday**, the day of the week within a week, then the date.

A tap on an episode opens it without starting it. A long press opens the episode menu:

- **play**
- **download**, or **stop the download** while it is coming down, or **remove from the phone** (with "the episode stays in the list") once it is there
- **keep as a favourite** or **remove from favourites**
- **mark as heard** or **mark as unheard**
- **share the link**
- **go to the channel** (absent when you are already in that channel)
