At the bottom of the home screen, the episode loaded in the player is shown as an inverted row. Tap it to return to the player; long press it for the menu.

### The ⋯ menu of the home screen

- **search**
- **refresh**
- **+ a feed**: opens **add a podcast**
- **import subscriptions**
- **export the subscriptions** (abonnements.opml)
- **black on white** or **white on black**: switches the colours at once
- **settings**

### add a podcast

One field, with the hint **a name, or a feed's address**. If the clipboard holds a web address when you open the screen, it is already in the field, selected, so typing replaces it.

- If the field holds an address, one row appears: **subscribe to this address**. Tap it, or press the keyboard's action key.
- If it holds a few words (two letters at least), the app looks them up in two public directories, Apple Podcasts and fyyd, shortly after you stop typing. It shows **searching the directories…**, then the podcasts found, each with its author or site underneath. Tap one to subscribe. A podcast you already follow reads **already followed**, and a tap opens it instead.

Under the results the app says where they come from: **found in Apple Podcasts and fyyd**, or, when one directory failed, "Apple Podcasts did not answer: these come from the other one" (or the same with fyyd).

After subscribing, the app opens the new channel and shows "subscribed to" followed by its name.

### search

Searches what is already in the app, so it works without a connection. The field's hint is **a channel, an episode**. From two letters on, the screen lists the channels whose name contains what you typed, then the episodes whose title does (the 300 most recent at most). A tap on a channel opens it; a tap on an episode opens the episode; a long press on an episode opens its menu. When you come back from an episode the results are still there.

### The episode screen (the player)

The top bar names the channel. **←** goes back, **☆** / **★** sets or removes the favourite, **⋯** opens the menu.

Below, from top to bottom:

- The whole title, then the date and the length.
- The position as a large clock. To its right the speed, for instance **1×**: each tap moves to the next of 0.8×, 1×, 1.25×, 1.5×, 1.75×, 2×. The speed is kept for all episodes. When the episode has been written down, **the text** appears beside the speed and opens the text screen.
- A thin line showing how far you are. Tap anywhere on it to move there (this also starts the episode if it was not loaded).
- **−5 s**, **▶** (which becomes **❚❚** while playing) and **+10 s**. The two jumps act on the episode that is loaded in the player.
- A chapters row, when the episode's notes contain a list of times and titles: it shows the current chapter and, underneath, "12 chapters". Tap it for the list; the current chapter carries a ✓; tap a chapter to go to its start.
- The episode's notes. Long notes are folded after eight lines: tap **read on…**. In the notes, a tap on a web address opens it; a tap on a time such as 12:34 sends the sound there; a long press on an address copies it; a long press anywhere else copies the whole text.
- A download row: **download** ("it plays over the connection, without being kept"), or the progress with **stop the download**, or **remove from the phone** ("on the phone").
- A text row: see "Write down what is said in an episode" and "Translate the text".

The **⋯** menu holds the same entries as the long-press menu, without **play** and with **stop** at the end. **stop** ends playback, keeps the position, removes the notification and returns to the list.

An episode does not need to be downloaded: **▶** plays it over the connection.

### The sheet before a transcription

When you tap **write down what is said**, a sheet opens with the episode's title and:

- **spoken language**, which reads **to choose** until you pick one. The list of languages opens by itself. It offers the phone's language, English, **let it work it out**, then French, German, Spanish, Portuguese, Russian and Italian, each written in its own language. If another episode of the same channel was written down before, its language comes first, marked **last time**.
- Two qualities: **ordinary** (190 MB, **recommended**) and **careful** (574 MB, **four times slower**). A quality whose model is not on the phone yet reads **to fetch**. **ordinary** is selected to begin with.
- **cancel** and **start**. **start** does nothing until a language is chosen.

### The text screen

Shows what was written down, line by line. The line being spoken is inverted and the list follows the sound, unless you are scrolling. Tap any line to send the sound there (it starts the episode if needed).

At the top: the episode's title, **←** and **⋯**. When a translation exists, two words let you switch: **text** and **translation**. Then **−5 s**, **▶** or **❚❚** with the position, and **+10 s**. The first lines of the episode's notes are shown above the text.

The **⋯** menu:

- **share the text**: sends the text being shown (original or translation) to another app.
- **open the .txt** ("a .txt in Documents/Transcriptions"): opens the saved text file in an app of your choice.
- **write it down again**: returns to the episode and opens the sheet again.

### settings

See "Settings". The last row gives the app's name and version, with: "podcasts, kept on the phone. it goes online only to find and fetch podcasts".

## How to

### Subscribe to a podcast by its name

1. On the home screen, tap **+** (or **⋯**, then **+ a feed**).
2. Type a few words of the podcast's name.
3. Wait for the results and tap the right one.

On the computer: click **+ a feed** at the bottom left, type, and click a result. Pressing Enter starts the search at once.

### Subscribe with a feed's address

1. Copy the address of the podcast's feed.
2. Open **add a podcast**: the address is already in the field. Otherwise paste or type it.
3. Tap **subscribe to this address**.

The address must be that of the feed itself. An address written without "https://" is accepted.

### Subscribe from another app

In a browser or any app, share the feed's link and choose **Add to Reader's Podcasts** in the share sheet. Links of the "feed:" and "podcast:" kind open the app directly.

### Listen to an episode

1. Tap the episode.
2. Tap **▶**.

Or long press the episode and choose **play**. Playback resumes where the episode was left. On the computer: double-click the episode, or select it and press Enter.

### Pause, jump, change speed

In the episode screen use **❚❚**, **−5 s**, **+10 s**, the line, and the speed. The same **−5 s**, play/pause, **+10 s** and **stop** are in the phone's media notification and on the lock screen, where the episode and its channel are named. Headset and Bluetooth buttons work, and playback pauses when headphones are unplugged.

### Go to a chapter or a time

Tap the chapters row and choose a chapter, or tap a time written in the notes.

### Download an episode, or remove it

Open the episode and tap **download**, or long press it and choose **download**. Downloads run one after the other; a notification shows the progress, for instance "downloading 40 % · 2 waiting". An interrupted download carries on from where it stopped when you ask again. To free the space, choose **remove from the phone**: the episode stays in the list with its position. To stop a download, tap **stop the download**, or **stop** in the notification (which stops all those waiting too).

### Have a channel download its new episodes by itself

Long press the channel and tap **off** (above **automatic download**) so that it reads **on**. After each refresh, the episodes that have just appeared are downloaded, three at most per refresh. Episodes already in the list are not fetched. These automatic downloads follow the setting **download on wifi only**.

### Keep an episode as a favourite

Tap **☆** in the episode screen, or long press the episode and choose **keep as a favourite**. Favourites are listed under **favourites**. They stay in the list even when the feed no longer carries them, and **delete when played** spares them.

### Mark an episode as heard or unheard

Long press it and choose **mark as heard** or **mark as unheard**. Either way its position returns to the beginning. An episode you listen to the end is marked **heard** by itself.

### Share an episode

Long press it and choose **share the link**: the title and the address of the audio are handed to the app you pick.

### Refresh

Pull the list down, tap **↻**, or choose **refresh** in the menu. Inside a channel, only that channel is refreshed.

### Unsubscribe

Long press the channel and tap **unsubscribe**. It takes effect at once, without confirmation, and removes the channel's episodes, their downloaded audio and the texts kept in the app.

### Find an episode you already have

**⋯**, then **search**, and type part of a title or of a channel's name.

### Write down what is said in an episode

1. Download the episode. Until then the row reads **write down what is said** with "download the episode first", and a tap starts the download.
2. Tap **write down what is said** ("on the phone; long, and nothing leaves it").
3. Choose the spoken language.
4. Choose **ordinary** or **careful**.
5. Tap **start**.

The first time, the model for the chosen quality is fetched ("fetching the model 30 %"). Then the row shows "writing it down 12 %", followed after a while by an estimate such as "20 min left", then **saving…**. The work goes on with the screen off; a notification shows it. To stop, tap the row (its second line reads **stop**) or **stop** in the notification.

When it is done, **the text** appears in the episode screen and a copy is saved as a .txt file named after the episode in Documents/Transcriptions. Writing the same episode down again replaces that file.

### Read the text while listening

Open the episode and tap **the text**. Tap a line to jump to it.

### Translate the text

Once an episode is written down, the row "translate into" followed by your phone's language appears, unless the episode is already in that language or already translated. Its second line reads "2490 MB · to fetch" the first time, then "on the phone, from the text above". Tap it. The model is fetched if needed, then the row shows "translating 8 %" and an estimate. The translation is made in passages of several sentences. When it is done, open **the text** and tap **translation**.

Translation needs a phone with 8 GB of memory. On a smaller one the row reads "needs a phone of 8 GB; this one has 6" and does nothing.

### Share or open the written text

In the text screen, **⋯**, then **share the text** or **open the .txt**.

### Move subscriptions to or from another podcast app

**⋯**, then **export the subscriptions**: choose where to save abonnements.opml. To bring subscriptions in, **import subscriptions** and pick an OPML file. The app reads each feed ("reading the feeds: 4 / 30") and ends with, for instance, "12 feeds added". Subscriptions you already have are skipped.

### Carry positions and settings between the phone and the computer

1. On the device you listened on, open **settings** (on the computer: **⋯**) and choose **export the settings**. Save reglages.json.
2. Move the file to the other device by any means you like.
3. There, choose **import settings** and pick the file.

The file carries the settings, the list of channels with their automatic download, and, for every episode begun, heard or starred, its position, its state and its star. Channels missing on the receiving side are subscribed to. For each episode, the side that was listened to most recently wins, so importing an older file does not undo what you heard since; a star in the file is always added.

## Settings

Open **⋯**, then **settings**. Each tap on a row moves to its next value.

| Setting | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **opens on** | **channels**, **episodes**, **favourites**, **downloaded** | **channels** |
| **download on wifi only** | **on**, **off** | **on** |
| **refresh on opening** | **on**, **off** | **on** |
| **delete when played** ("except favourites and transcribed episodes") | **on**, **off** | **off** |
| **colours** | **white on black**, **black on white** | **white on black** |
| **text size** | S, M, L | M |
| **font** | sans-serif, serif, mono | sans-serif |
| **haptic tick** | **on**, **off** | **on** |

- **opens on**: changing it switches the home screen to that list at once. After that, the app comes back to whichever list you were last looking at.
- **download on wifi only**: concerns the automatic downloads. A download you ask for yourself always starts, whatever the connection. The models for transcription and translation are also fetched whatever the connection.
- **refresh on opening**: when the app is opened, all channels are refreshed, at most once an hour.
- **delete when played**: when an episode is heard to the end, or marked as heard, its downloaded audio is removed. Favourites and episodes that were written down keep theirs.
- **haptic tick**: a short vibration on the player's controls.

Between these rows the screen offers the two exchange files, introduced by: "the two files that carry everything: the subscriptions as opml, which any podcast app reads, and the settings with where each episode was left." The rows are **export the subscriptions** (abonnements.opml), **import subscriptions**, **export the settings** (reglages.json) and **import settings**.

Two things are set elsewhere: the speed, in the episode screen (1× to begin with), and **automatic download**, per channel, in the channel's long-press menu (off to begin with).

## Your data

Everything is kept on the phone: the subscriptions, the list of episodes with positions and stars, the downloaded audio, the texts and translations, and the models. Only the .txt copy of a text is placed where other apps can see it, in Documents/Transcriptions.

The position of the episode playing is saved every few seconds.

The app uses the network for these things only:

- fetching the feeds you subscribed to, from their own servers;
- playing or downloading the audio, from the servers the feed names;
- looking up a name in **add a podcast**: the words you type go to Apple Podcasts and to fyyd, and your phone's country goes to Apple so that it answers with that country's podcasts first;
- fetching the transcription and translation models, once, from Hugging Face.

The audio and the text of an episode never leave the phone: writing down and translating are done on it.

For each channel the app keeps the 50 most recent episodes of the feed. Older ones leave the list, except those that are downloaded, begun, starred or written down.

There is no automatic synchronisation and therefore no conflict to resolve: the two files described under "Carry positions and settings between the phone and the computer" are the only exchange. Downloaded audio, texts and translations are not carried by these files. Text size, **haptic tick** and **download on wifi only** exist on the phone only and are not taken over by the computer.

Uninstalling the app removes everything it kept, including downloaded episodes and models. The .txt files in Documents/Transcriptions stay. Export the two files first if you want to find your subscriptions and positions again.

## With the other apps

- **Home-screen widget**: **Reader's Podcasts · last played** ("the last episode played, and a button to carry on"). One row, four cells wide, resizable in width. It shows the title of the last episode played and its position and length; **▶** resumes it and **❚❚** pauses, without opening the app; a tap on the title opens the episode. Before anything has been played it reads **nothing played yet**. It follows the app's colours.
- **Share sheet**: **Add to Reader's Podcasts** takes a feed's address shared from any app.
- **Links**: links of the "feed:" and "podcast:" kind open the app and subscribe.
- **Shared models**: the transcription and translation models are shared with Reader's Audio Player and Reader's Recorder. If one of them already holds a model, Reader's Podcasts uses it instead of fetching it again, and the other way round.
- **The written text**: the .txt in Documents/Transcriptions opens in any app that reads text, a book reader for instance.

## On the computer

The computer program is one window in the same two colours. It has no transcription, no translation and no widget. It adds YouTube.

### The window

- **Left column**: **episodes**, **favourites**, **downloaded**, each with its count, then your channels, the one that published most recently first, each with its number of unheard episodes. There is no **channels** row: the column is the list of channels. A channel whose last refresh failed says why when the pointer rests on it ("last refresh failed: …"). At the bottom: **+ a feed**.
- **Middle**: the name of the list, **⋯**, and the episodes. A click selects an episode; a double click, or Enter, plays it; a right click opens its menu.
- **Right**: the selected episode: title, channel, date, length; three actions, **listen** (or **pause**), **download** (or **stop the download**, or **remove from this computer**) and **keep as a favourite** (or **remove from favourites**); the **chapters**, each a link that sends the sound there; and the notes, where web addresses open in the browser and times send the sound there.
- **Bottom**: the position, the title with its length and channel (or **nothing playing**), the line (click to move), **−5 s**, **▶** / **❚❚**, **+10 s**, and the speed, for instance "1× speed" (click for the next of 0.8, 1, 1.25, 1.5, 1.75, 2).

Messages appear for a few seconds in the bar at the very bottom of the window.

### Menus

**⋯**: **search**, **refresh**, **+ a feed**, **import subscriptions**, **export the subscriptions**, **import settings**, **export the settings**, **black on white** or **white on black**, **settings**.

Right click on an episode: **play**, **download** / **stop the download** / **remove from this computer**, **keep as a favourite** / **remove from favourites**, **mark as heard** / **mark as unheard**, **copy the link**, **open the channel**.

Right click on a channel: **refresh**, **automatic download** (ticked when on), **unsubscribe**.

**search** shows a field above the episodes and keeps, in the list being shown, the episodes whose title contains what you type. Escape closes it.

### What differs from the phone

- **YouTube**: paste the address of a channel (an "@name" address, a "/channel/…" address) or of a playlist in **add a podcast**; it becomes a channel like any other. To listen to a video, the program first fetches its audio with yt-dlp, a separate tool that must be installed on the computer ("the audio is fetched first; it will play by itself"). Without it: "YouTube needs yt-dlp, which is not installed".
- **automatic download**: after each refresh, the three most recent episodes of the channel are downloaded if they are unheard and not yet on the computer.
- **refresh on opening** refreshes at every start.
- **delete once heard** spares favourites and YouTube videos.
- There is no **stop**: pause, or let the episode end. The position is also saved when the window is closed.
- The window reopens on the list it was showing when it was closed.
- There is no **download on wifi only**, no **haptic tick**, and the text size is a number instead of S, M, L.
- **unsubscribe** takes effect at once, as on the phone, and removes the channel's downloaded audio.

### Settings on the computer

**⋯**, then **settings**; confirm with **ok**.

| Setting | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **colours** | **white on black**, **black on white** | **white on black** |
| **font** | **sans-serif**, **serif**, **mono** | **sans-serif** |
| **text size** | 9 to 24 | 12 |
| **opens on** | **episodes**, **favourites**, **downloaded** | **episodes** |
| **refresh on opening** | ticked or not | ticked |
| **delete once heard** | ticked or not | not ticked |

**opens on** is stored and written into the exported settings file; the window itself reopens on the last list shown.

### Keyboard and command line

- Enter plays the selected episode; in **add a podcast**, Enter subscribes to an address or starts the search.
- Escape closes the search field.
- Started with a feed's address as its argument, the program subscribes to it: `readers-podcasts https://example.org/feed.xml`. Installed from the package, it is also offered by the system for "feed:", "podcast:" and "pcast:" links.

### Where its files live

- Subscriptions, episodes and downloaded audio: `~/.local/share/readers-podcasts` (audio in its `audio` folder).
- Settings: `~/.config/readers-podcasts/config.json`.

Export proposes `abonnements.opml` and `reglages.json` in your home folder.

Playing sound needs the system's Qt multimedia package; without it the program still opens and says "audio is missing: install python3-pyqt5.qtmultimedia".

## Limits

- One episode plays at a time; there is no queue and no "next episode".
- No sleep timer, no artwork, no video.
- The address you give must be the feed itself; the app does not look for a feed inside a web page.
- The Android app refuses YouTube addresses.
- Transcription works on a downloaded episode only, and is slow: count on a long wait for a long episode, four times longer with **careful**.
- Translation needs a phone with 8 GB of memory and a model of 2490 MB; it translates into the phone's language only.
- Chapters are read from the notes: the list is recognised only when it has at least two lines with a time and a title, in order.
- A channel's list holds its 50 most recent episodes, plus those you downloaded, began, starred or wrote down.
- Episode notes are kept up to 10 000 characters.
- No automatic synchronisation between devices.
- On the computer: no transcription, no translation, and **search** looks at episode titles in the current list only.

## Messages and problems

### Subscribing and refreshing

- **reading the feed…**, "reading the feeds: 4 / 30", "refreshing: 3 / 12": work in progress. On the computer: **refreshing…**.
- "that feed could not be read." followed by a reason: the address is not a feed, or the server did not answer. Check the address.
- **YouTube channels are not supported.** (phone): the address is a YouTube one.
- "no channel was found at that address" (computer): the YouTube address does not lead to a channel.
- **no connection**: the server could not be reached.
- "the server answered with an error (HTTP 404)": the feed or the file is no longer at that address (the number varies).
- "not refreshed: …", under a channel's name: the last refresh of that channel failed; the rest is the technical reason. On the computer the same information is in the tooltip, "last refresh failed: …".
- **no subscription in that file**: the file chosen holds no feed. On the computer the same message appears when every feed in the file is already followed.
- **that file could not be read**, **could not write the file**: the import or export did not go through; pick another file or another place.
- **subscriptions exported**, **settings exported**, **settings imported**, "12 feeds added": done.
- "the refresh failed. …", "the import failed. …" (computer): followed by the technical reason.

### Finding a podcast

- **no podcast of that name in the directories**: try other words, or paste the feed's address.
- **the directories do not answer — is the phone online?** (on the computer: "is the computer online?"): neither directory could be reached.
- "fyyd did not answer: these come from the other one": only one directory answered; the results may be incomplete.
- **nothing of that name here** (in **search**): nothing you follow matches.

### Downloading and playing

- **waiting**: the download is queued behind another.
- **waiting for wifi**: an automatic download was not made because the phone was not on wifi. Download the episode yourself, or switch **download on wifi only** off.
- **download cut short — try again to carry on**: the connection dropped; ask again and it resumes.
- "download failed: …" (computer: "the download failed. …"): followed by the reason. The full text is shown in the episode screen.
- **that episode could not be played**: the audio could not be read, often because there is no connection for an episode that is not downloaded.
- **nothing on the phone yet. a downloaded episode plays without a connection.**: the **downloaded** list is empty. On the computer: **nothing on this computer yet.**
- **no favourite yet. a long press on an episode keeps it here.**: the **favourites** list is empty. On the computer: **no favourite yet.**
- **nothing here yet. ⋯ refreshes.**: the list is empty; refresh. On the computer: **nothing here yet.**
- **copied**: the address or the text of the notes is in the clipboard.

### Writing down and translating

- **download the episode first**: the episode's audio must be on the phone.
- "fetching the model 30 %": the model is being fetched; this happens once per quality.
- **the model is not on the phone**: the model could not be opened; start again so that it is fetched.
- "download refused (HTTP 403)": the model's server refused; try later.
- "not enough space: 2600 MB needed": free that much storage and start again.
- **no sound in this file**: the downloaded file holds no audio the app can read.
- "could not write it down: …", "the translation failed: …": followed by the technical reason.
- "could not create" followed by a file name: the .txt could not be saved in Documents/Transcriptions.
- "needs a phone of 8 GB; this one has 6": this phone cannot translate.
- **that .txt is no longer where it was**: the file was moved or deleted; **write it down again** creates it anew.
- **nothing written down yet.**: the text screen has no text for this episode.


# Reader's Audio Player

*Android 1.6.1*

## What it is

Reader's Audio Player plays the audio files that are on your phone and resumes each one where you left it. It can also write down what is said in a file (a transcription), write the main points of that text, and translate it into the language of your phone. All three are done on the phone itself: the audio and the text are never sent anywhere.

The app is black and white, text only, and follows the language of the phone: English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese or Russian.

It deliberately does not manage a music library: there are no albums, no playlists, no previous or next track. One file plays at a time. It has no account and asks for no access to your storage; it only knows the files you open with it.

## First start

### What the app asks

On Android 13 and later, the app asks once for permission to show notifications. Notifications carry the playback controls (also shown on the lock screen) and the progress of a transcription, with a button to stop it.

Nothing else is asked: no account, no server, no storage permission. The app reaches the internet only to download the models it needs for transcription, main points and translation, the first time each is used.

### The empty screen

The first screen is titled **audio**. While the list is empty it shows:

> nothing here yet. + below opens audio files from the phone; “open with” from any app works too.

At the bottom, **+ open audio files** opens the phone's file picker.

## The screens

### The list (audio)

The list shows every file you have opened, the last one played first. Files never played come after, the most recently added first.

Each line shows the title of the file (its name without the extension) and, underneath, a status line:

- where you stopped and the total length, for example `12:34 / 45:00`, or the length alone if the file has not been started;
- then, if it applies: **transcript** when a transcription exists, the progress of the work under way (for example **transcribing · 42%**), **waiting to transcribe** when the file is queued, or an error message if the last attempt failed.

What you can do:

- **Tap a line**: the file starts playing where it was left and the player opens. If the file is already the one loaded in the player, the player simply opens.
- **Long press on a line**: opens the file's menu (see below).
- **The inverted line at the bottom** (white on black becomes black on white): the file currently loaded, preceded by **▶** when paused or **❚❚** when playing. Tap it to return to the player.
- **+ open audio files**: opens the phone's file picker, limited to audio files. You can pick several at once.
- **⋯** at the top right: a small menu with **+ open audio files**, then **black on white** or **white on black** (switches the colours at once) and **settings**.

### The file's menu

It opens by a long press in the list, or with **⋯** in the player. The title of the file is at the top. The entries depend on the state of the file:

| Entry | What it does | Shown when |
|---|---|---|
| **play** | Starts the file and opens the player. | From the list only. |
| **open the transcript** (*a .txt in Documents/Transcriptions*) | Opens the transcription in an app that reads text; the phone lets you choose which. | A transcription exists. |
| **transcribe** | Opens the transcription sheet. | No transcription yet, nothing in progress. |
| **transcribe again** | Opens the transcription sheet; the new text replaces the old one. | A transcription exists, nothing in progress. |
| **stop the transcription**, **stop the summary** or **stop the translation** | Stops the work in progress. | The file is being worked on or is waiting. |
| **share the audio…** | Sends the audio file to another app. | Always. |
| **share the transcript…** | Sends the transcription to another app. | A transcription exists. |
| **the main points (beta)** | Reads the main points, writes them, or fetches the model that writes them (see the player below). | A transcription exists, nothing in progress. |
| **translate into …** or **open the translation (…)** | Translates the transcription into the language of the phone, or opens the translation already made. | A transcription exists, nothing in progress, and a translation would change something. |
| **save a copy to a folder…** | Copies the audio file to a folder you choose. | Always. |
| **stop** | Stops playback, removes the playback notification and returns to the list. The position is kept. | From the player only. |
| **remove from the list** | Takes the file out of the list. | From the list only. |

Tap outside the menu, or use the back button, to close it.

### The player

The title of the file is at the top; tap it (or the arrow **←**) to go back to the list. **⋯** opens the file's menu.

From top to bottom:

- **The position**, in large figures, and under it the total length.
- **The progress line**: tap anywhere on it to jump to that point of the file.
- Three buttons: **−5 s** goes back five seconds, **▶** plays and **❚❚** pauses (the button is inverted while the file plays), **+10 s** skips ten seconds ahead.
  If the file shown is not loaded (for example after **stop**, when the player is opened from the widget), **▶** starts it, a tap on the progress line starts it at that point, and **−5 s** and **+10 s** do nothing until it plays.
- **speed**: shows the current speed, for example **1×**. Each tap moves to the next one: 0.8×, 1×, 1.25×, 1.5×, 1.75×, 2×, then back to 0.8×. The speed applies to every file.

Below the rule comes the transcription part, which changes with the state of the file:

- No transcription yet: **transcribe** (*choose the language and the quality*). Tap it to open the transcription sheet.
- Work in progress: one line showing the step and its progress — **fetching the model · 12%**, **transcribing · 42%**, **writing the main points, 30%**, **translating · 55%**, **saving the transcript** — with **stop the transcription**, **stop the summary** or **stop the translation** underneath. Tap the line to stop.
- File in the queue: **waiting to transcribe**, with the same stop line underneath. Tap to stop.
- A transcription exists:
  - **open the transcript** (*a .txt in Documents/Transcriptions*);
  - **share the transcript…**;
  - **the main points (beta)**, with a second line that says where things stand (see "Get the main points of a transcription");
  - **translate into …** or **open the translation (…)**, when it applies.

If the last attempt failed, the reason is written under these lines.

### The transcription sheet

It opens from **transcribe** or **transcribe again**, and is shown before every transcription. It has the title of the file at the top, then:

- **language spoken**: the language of the audio. It starts on the language of your phone. Tap it to choose among the phone's language, English, **detected** (the app works out the language by itself), French, German, Spanish, Portuguese, Russian and Italian. A **✓** marks the current choice.
- **ordinary** — *190 MB · recommended*: the quick model. It is the one selected when the sheet opens.
- **careful** — *574 MB · four times slower*: a more accurate model, with better punctuation, but much slower.
  The selected quality is the inverted line. A model that is not yet on the phone is marked **to fetch**; it is downloaded when the transcription starts.
- **main points too (beta)**: when this line is inverted, the main points are written right after the transcription. Its second line shows the size of the model (2105 MB) and **to fetch** if it is not on the phone, or **shared with …** when another Reader's app already holds it. If the model is not on the phone, a first tap downloads it (you can watch the progress on the line); tap again to switch the line on. On a phone with too little memory the line reads **this phone has … GB: not enough** and cannot be switched on.
- **cancel** closes the sheet; **transcribe** starts the work.

### The main points (beta)

This screen opens when you tap **the main points (beta)** on a file whose points are written (*written · tap to read them*). It shows the title of the file, then the text: two sentences on what the recording is about, then the points, one per line.

At the bottom:

- **open the transcript** (*a .txt in Documents/Transcriptions*): opens the full text file.
- **write them again**: asks for the **language spoken** — in fact the language the points will be written in — then writes them anew. The list is the same as in the transcription sheet, without **detected**. If the model is no longer on the phone, the line says so and a tap fetches it.

### Settings

Reached with **⋯** in the list, then **settings**. See the "Settings" section.

### The notifications

While a file plays, a media notification shows its title with **−5 s**, play or pause, **+10 s** and **stop**. The same controls are on the lock screen, and the buttons of a headset or a Bluetooth device work. Tapping the notification opens the player.

While the app transcribes, writes the main points, translates or downloads a model, a second notification shows the title of the file, the step and its progress, with a **stop** button. Tapping it opens the app.

## How to

### Open audio files

1. In the list, tap **+ open audio files**.
2. In the phone's file picker, choose one file, or several.
3. If you chose a single file, it starts playing and the player opens. If you chose several, they are added to the list.

A file opened this way stays where it is on the phone; the app only remembers where to find it.

### Open a file from another app

In a file manager or any other app, choose "open with" or "share" on an audio file and pick **Reader's Audio Player**. The file is added to the list, starts playing, and the player opens.

When the other app only lends the file for the moment, which is the usual case here, Reader's Audio Player keeps its own copy so that the file still plays later.

### Play, pause and resume

1. Tap a file in the list. It starts where you left it.
2. In the player, tap **❚❚** to pause and **▶** to resume.

The position is saved every few seconds while the file plays, and when you pause or stop. A file left within three seconds of its end starts again from the beginning; so does a file that played to the end. Playback pauses by itself when headphones are unplugged.

### Move within a file

- Tap **−5 s** or **+10 s**.
- Or tap the progress line at the place you want to reach.

### Change the speed

In the player, tap the **speed** line until the speed you want is shown: 0.8×, 1×, 1.25×, 1.5×, 1.75× or 2×.

### Stop playback

In the player, tap **⋯**, then **stop**. You can also use **stop** in the playback notification. The position is kept.

### Transcribe a file

1. Open the file in the player and tap **transcribe** (or long press the file in the list, then **transcribe**).
2. Check **language spoken**. Tap it to change it, or choose **detected** if you do not know.
3. Choose the quality: **ordinary** (recommended for long recordings) or **careful** (four times slower).
4. If you also want the main points, tap **main points too (beta)** so that the line is inverted (the first tap downloads the model if it is not on the phone).
5. Tap **transcribe**.

The first time, the model is downloaded (**fetching the model · …%**); then the progress reads **transcribing · …%**. You can leave the app: the work continues, and the notification shows where it is. When it is finished, the file shows **transcript** in the list and the player offers **open the transcript**.

You can start several files: they are done one after the other, and the ones waiting show **waiting to transcribe**.

### Stop a transcription, a summary or a translation

In the player, tap the line that shows the step under way, or choose **stop the transcription**, **stop the summary** or **stop the translation** in the file's menu, or tap **stop** in the notification. This stops the work in progress and also empties the queue: the files that were waiting are not processed.

### Read or share a transcription

- **open the transcript** opens the text in the app of your choice (any app that reads text files, Reader's Books for example).
- **share the transcript…** sends the text file to another app. When the text is short (under about 100,000 characters) it is also sent as plain text, so that note and messaging apps accept it.

### Get the main points of a transcription

There are two ways.

**With the transcription.** In the transcription sheet, switch on **main points too (beta)** before tapping **transcribe**. The choice is remembered for the next time.

**Afterwards, from a transcription already made.** In the player, look at the second line under **the main points (beta)**:

1. *2105 MB · to fetch*: tap to download the model. The line shows **fetching the model · …%**. If the download was interrupted, it reads **…% downloaded · tap to continue**: tap to carry on from there.
2. *written at the top of the transcript*: the model is on the phone. Tap to write the points; progress reads **writing the main points, …%**.
3. *written · tap to read them*: the points are ready. Tap to read them.

The points are written in the language chosen for the transcription; with **detected**, in the language of the phone. The instructions exist in English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese and Russian; for any other language the points come out in English. To have them in another language, open them and use **write them again**.

The result is two sentences on the subject, then a short list of points. They are also placed at the top of the transcription's text file, under the heading **MAIN POINTS**, above the full text.

This function is marked beta: it is written by a small model, on the phone, and can miss things.

### Translate a transcription

1. Make sure the file has a transcription.
2. In the player (or in the file's menu), tap **translate into …**. The language offered is the language of your phone if it is English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese or Russian; otherwise English.
3. The first time, the model is downloaded (2490 MB); the second line reads *2490 MB · to fetch* until then. Progress then reads **translating · …%**.
4. When it is done, the line becomes **open the translation (…)**. Tap it to read the translation.

The translation is a second text file, next to the transcription, named with the title followed by the language code in brackets, for example `title (en).txt`. Only the text of the transcription is translated, not the main points.

The line is not offered when the transcription was made in that same language. On a phone with too little memory it reads **needs a phone of 8 GB; this one has …** and does nothing.

### Share the audio file

In the file's menu, tap **share the audio…** and pick the app to send it to.

### Save a copy of the audio file

1. In the file's menu, tap **save a copy to a folder…**.
2. Choose the folder in the phone's file picker; the name of the file is proposed, and you can change it.
3. The app shows **copied**, or **could not copy the file** if it failed.

### Remove a file from the list

Long press the file in the list, then tap **remove from the list**. If it was playing, playback stops. The original audio file is not deleted, and neither are the text files in Documents/Transcriptions. The app's own copy of the file, if it had made one, is deleted, together with what the app kept about the file.

### Switch between white on black and black on white

In the list, tap **⋯**, then **black on white** or **white on black**. The same choice is in **settings**, on the **colours** line.

### Put the resume widget on the home screen

Add the widget named **Reader's Audio Player · last played** from your home screen's widget list. See "With the other apps".

## Settings

The screen opens with a reminder: *transcripts are plain .txt files in Documents/Transcriptions; any text or e-book app opens them.* Each setting changes with a tap on its line.

| Setting | What it changes | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| **colours** | The colours of the app and of its widget. | **white on black**, **black on white** | **white on black** |
| **text size** | The size of the text in the whole app. | S, M, L | M |
| **font** | The typeface. | sans-serif, serif, mono | sans-serif |
| **haptic tick** | A short vibration when you tap **−5 s**, **▶** / **❚❚** or **+10 s** in the player. | **on**, **off** | **on** |

The last line, **Reader's Audio Player**, only describes the app: *plays audio files and transcribes them on the phone; nothing leaves it*.

Other choices are made where they are used and are remembered:

| Choice | Where | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **speed** | In the player; applies to every file. | 1× |
| **main points too (beta)** | In the transcription sheet. | Off. It is switched on once the model has been downloaded. |

The language spoken and the quality are asked again before every transcription: the sheet always opens on the language of the phone and on **ordinary**.

## Your data

### Where things are

- **The audio files** stay where they are on the phone. The exception is a file received through "open with" or "share" from another app, which the app copies into its own private space.
- **The list**, the position in each file and the settings are kept in the app's private space, on the phone only.
- **The transcriptions** are plain text files in the phone's **Documents/Transcriptions** folder, named after the title of the file (`title.txt`). A new transcription of the same file replaces the previous one. When main points have been written, they are at the top of that same file. A translation is a second file beside it (`title (en).txt`).
- The app also keeps its own copy of each transcription, of its main points and of its translation. If the translation's text file has been deleted or moved, **open the translation (…)** writes it out again.
- **The models** (190 MB, 574 MB, 2105 MB and 2490 MB) are in the app's private space.

### What goes over the network

Only the downloads of the models, from the Hugging Face website (huggingface.co), the first time each is needed. Nothing is uploaded: neither the audio, nor the transcriptions, nor the list. There is no account, no synchronisation and no statistics.

A download that was cut is resumed from where it stopped at the next attempt.

### Shared models

The models are shared with Reader's Recorder, Reader's Podcasts and Reader's Notes when they carry the same signature, which is the case for the published versions: a model downloaded by one of them is used by the others without being downloaded again. In the transcription sheet, the main-points line then reads **shared with …**.

### Export, backup, uninstalling

There is no export or import of the list. To keep a transcription elsewhere, use **share the transcript…** or copy the files from Documents/Transcriptions; to keep an audio file elsewhere, use **save a copy to a folder…**.

Uninstalling the app removes the list, the positions, the settings, the copies of audio files the app had made, its own copies of the texts and the downloaded models. The text files in Documents/Transcriptions and your original audio files are not removed.

## With the other apps

### Home-screen widget

The app offers one widget, **Reader's Audio Player · last played** (*the last file played: ▶ resumes it where you stopped*). It is one row high and four cells wide, and can be made wider or narrower.

- It shows the title of the last file played and, under it, the position and the length.
- **▶** resumes the file where you stopped, without opening the app; **❚❚** pauses it. While the file plays, the time runs on the right and the length is shown under the title.
- Tap the title to open the player.
- Before anything has been played it reads **nothing played yet**.

The widget uses the colours chosen in the app.

### Opening and sharing

- Reader's Audio Player appears in "open with" and in "share" for audio files in other apps.
- Transcriptions and translations are ordinary text files: any app that reads text opens them, Reader's Books among them.
- **share the audio…** and **share the transcript…** send to any app that accepts them.

## Limits

- One file plays at a time; there is no playlist, no queue of files to play, no previous or next.
- No folders, no search, no sorting other than "last played first"; a file cannot be renamed in the app.
- The speed goes from 0.8× to 2× in six steps; there is no sleep timer and no bookmark.
- The app opens only audio files, and plays the formats the phone itself can read.
- The main points need a phone with about 6 GB of memory; the translation needs a phone of 8 GB. Each needs its model, and room to download it: about 2.2 GB and 2.6 GB of free storage.
- Main points are not written for a very short text (under about 120 words).
- The translation goes only towards the language of the phone (English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese, Russian; English for any other phone language). You cannot choose another target language.
- Once a translation exists, the app offers to open it, not to make it again.
- A passage that could not be translated is left in the original language in the translation.
- The careful quality is about four times slower than the ordinary one; a long recording can take a long time.
- Stopping a job also cancels all the jobs that were waiting.

## Messages and problems

| Message | What it means, what to do |
|---|---|
| **this file cannot be opened — open it again with +** | The file could not be read: it was moved, deleted, or is not in a format the phone can play. Open it again with **+ open audio files**. |
| **the transcript file is no longer there** | The text file in Documents/Transcriptions was deleted or moved. The file goes back to offering **transcribe**. |
| **(no speech detected)** | Written in the transcription when no speech was found in the audio. |
| **no connection** | A model could not be downloaded because the internet could not be reached. Connect and try again. |
| **download refused (HTTP …)** | The server refused the download. Try again later. |
| **download cut short — try again to carry on** | The download stopped before the end. Start again: it continues from where it stopped. |
| **download failed: …** | Another download error; the text that follows comes from the phone. Try again. |
| **…% downloaded · tap to continue** | A download of the main-points model was interrupted. Tap to resume it. |
| **not enough space: … MB needed** | The phone lacks storage for the model. Free the amount shown and try again. |
| **the model is missing — try again to fetch it** | The transcription model could not be found after its download. Start the transcription again. |
| **no sound in this file** | The file contains no audio track that can be transcribed. |
| **transcription failed: …** | The transcription stopped on an error; the text that follows comes from the phone. Try again. |
| **could not create …** | The text file could not be created in Documents/Transcriptions. |
| **the main points could not be written** (possibly followed by a reason) | The model returned nothing usable, the text is too short, or the phone had too little free memory at that moment. Close other apps and try again. The transcription itself is not affected. |
| **this phone has … GB: not enough** | The phone does not have the memory needed to write main points. The function is not available on it. |
| **needs a phone of 8 GB; this one has …** | The phone does not have the memory needed to translate. The function is not available on it. |
| **the translation failed** (possibly followed by a reason) | The translation could not be made. Try again. |
| **could not copy the file** | **save a copy to a folder…** failed. Check the folder chosen and the free space. |
| **waiting to transcribe** | Another file is being worked on; this one comes next. |
| **nothing played yet** | Shown by the widget while no file has been played. |

If main points were asked together with a transcription and do not appear, the transcription is kept all the same: the points are attempted after the text has been saved, and a failure at that step is not reported. Ask for them again from **the main points (beta)** in the player.


# Reader's Mindful Tool

*Android 1.0.2*

## What it is

Reader's Mindful Tool is a meditation bowl with three uses: one strike every X minutes until you stop it, a sitting of X minutes closed by three bowls, and bowls at set times every day. It is black and white and made of text only, like the other Reader's apps. Every bowl is set as an alarm-clock alarm and played on the phone's alarm channel, so it sounds on time through silent mode, Do Not Disturb and hours of sleep with the screen off. It has no guidance, no statistics, no streaks and no account, and it cannot use the network at all.

The app exists in English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese and Russian. It follows the language of the phone; there is no language setting inside the app.

## First start

On Android 13 and later, the app asks once for permission to show notifications. The only notification it ever shows is the silent one displayed while a session runs, which carries the buttons **stop the bowls** and **stop meditating**. If you refuse, you stop a session from the app or from the widget instead.

There is no account, no server and nothing to fill in. The first screen, titled **mindful**, already works:

- **a bowl every** 10 min, with the line **one strike, again and again, until you stop it**;
- **meditate** 20 min, with the line **silence, then three bowls at the end**;
- **bowls at set times**, with the line **none yet — tap to add a time of day**;
- the current bowl, **Tibetan bowl (E♭)**, with the word **bowl** under it.

The app starts white on black.

One thing is worth doing on the first day: open **settings** and tap **allow running in the background** (see "Let the app run in the background" below). The app does not ask for it by itself.

## The screens

### Home (mindful)

The home screen has four lines, separated by thin rules.

**a bowl every N min.** The number of minutes is underlined: tap the words to change it (see "The minutes prompt"). Tap **▶** at the end of the line to start. The first bowl sounds N minutes after the start, not at the start. While it runs, **▶** becomes **■** on a filled square, the minutes are no longer underlined and cannot be changed, and the line under the title reads **next at 14:30 · in 04:12**, followed by **· 3 rung** once at least one bowl has sounded. Tap **■** to stop.

**meditate N min.** Same controls. Nothing sounds at the start. While it runs, the line under the title reads **until 14:50 · 12:40 left** and a thin line below fills from left to right as the sitting goes on. At the end three bowls sound and the line returns to **▶**. Tap **■** to stop early; no bowl sounds then.

The time left is shown as minutes and seconds, or hours, minutes and seconds beyond one hour.

The two functions are independent: you can run both at the same time.

**bowls at set times.** The line under it lists the times that are switched on, for example **07:00 ×3  12:30** (×3 marks a time that rings three bowls). Tap the line to open the screen "bowls at set times".

**The bowl.** The last line shows the name of the bowl in use, with **bowl** under it. Tap it to open the settings, where the bowl is chosen.

**⋯** at the top right opens the menu:

| Entry | What it does |
|---|---|
| **bowls at set times** | Opens the screen of the daily times. |
| **hear the bowl** | Plays one strike of the bowl in use. |
| **black on white** / **white on black** | Switches to the other colours; the entry names the colours you will get. |
| **settings** | Opens the settings. |

Tap outside the menu, or press the phone's back button, to close it without choosing.

### The minutes prompt

Tapping the words of **a bowl every** opens a small panel titled **every how many minutes?**; tapping the words of **meditate** opens **how many minutes?**. The current number is already in the field. Type a whole number from 1 to 240 and tap **ok** (or the confirm key of the keyboard). **cancel**, a tap outside the panel or the back button close it without changing anything.

If what you typed is not a number from 1 to 240, the panel stays open and its title shows **(1–240)**.

### bowls at set times

This screen lists the bowls that ring every day at a fixed time. At the top: **Every day at these times, one strike or three. Tap a time to change it.** When the list is empty it shows **no time set yet**.

Each line shows the time and **one bowl** or **three bowls**, with **on** or **off** under it. Times that are on are shown in inverted colours. The list is sorted by time of day.

Tap a line to open its menu, titled with the time:

| Entry | What it does |
|---|---|
| **turn off** / **turn on** | Stops this time from ringing without deleting it, or switches it back on. |
| **one bowl** / **three bowls** | Changes the number of bowls; the entry names what you will get. |
| **change the time** | Opens the time prompt with the current time. |
| **delete** | Removes the time at once, without asking for confirmation. |

**+ new time** at the bottom opens a panel titled **time (hh:mm)**. Type the time on the 24-hour clock and tap **ok**. You may type **7:30**, **07:30**, **7h30**, **7.30**, **0730**, or just **7** for 07:00. A new time is switched on and rings three bowls. If the time is not understood, the panel stays open and its title shows **(hh:mm)**.

Tap the title line with the arrow **←**, or press back, to return to the home screen.

### settings

From top to bottom:

- The line **The bowl for every sound in the app. Tap one to hear it.**, then the five bowls: **Tibetan bowl (E♭)**, **singing bowl**, **gong bowl**, **Satipanya**, **enpleineconscience.ch**. The one in use is shown in inverted colours. Tapping a bowl chooses it and plays one strike.
- **your own sound…**, with the line **an mp3 or wav file from the phone, in place of the single strike; three bowls = played three times**. Tapping it opens the phone's file picker. Once a file has been chosen, this line shows the file's name with **your own recording; three bowls = played three times**, and tapping it opens a menu with **use this sound** and **pick another file…**.
- **allow running in the background**, with the line **so the bowl always sounds on time, even after hours of deep sleep**. Once allowed, it reads **runs in the background: allowed** and tapping it does nothing.
- **colours**, **text size**, **font**, **haptic tick**: each line shows the current choice above the name of the setting; tap to move to the next choice (see "Settings").
- **Reader's Mindful Tool**, with **a bowl every X minutes, a timed sit, bowls at set times**, and **Pierre Gallaz · developed with Claude Code**: information only.

### The notification

While a session runs, or while a bowl is sounding, the phone shows one silent notification in the category **running session**. Its text is:

- **a bowl every 10 min · next at 14:30** while the repeated bowl runs;
- **meditating 20 min · until 14:50** while a sitting runs, with a progress bar and a countdown;
- both lines when both run;
- **the bowl** while a bowl sounds with no session running (a bowl at a set time, or one you asked to hear).

It carries the buttons **stop the bowls** and **stop meditating**, each shown only when that function runs. Tapping the notification opens the app. It disappears when nothing runs any more and the sound has finished.

### The widget's set-up screen

When you place the widget on a home screen, a screen titled **Reader's Mindful Tool** asks **What should this widget show? Both functions stacked, or just one.** and offers **both**, **a bowl every X minutes only** and **meditation period only**. Tapping the title line with **←** cancels and the widget is not placed.

## How to

### Ring a bowl every X minutes

1. On the home screen, tap the words **a bowl every** and type the number of minutes (1 to 240), then **ok**.
2. Tap **▶** on that line.
3. Put the phone down; the screen can go off. One strike sounds every X minutes, counted from the moment you started.
4. To stop, tap **■** on the line, or **stop the bowls** in the notification.

The bowls go on with no end until you stop them.

### Meditate for X minutes

1. Tap the words **meditate** and type the length in minutes (1 to 240), then **ok**.
2. Tap **▶** on that line. Nothing sounds at the start.
3. At the end, three bowls sound and the sitting ends by itself.
4. To stop early, tap **■**, or **stop meditating** in the notification.

### Have a bowl partway through a sitting

Start both functions. For example **meditate** 30 min and **a bowl every** 10 min, started one after the other, give a strike at about 10 and 20 minutes and three bowls at the end. The repeated bowl is not stopped by the end of the sitting: tap its **■** afterwards.

### Ring a bowl at the same time every day

1. Tap **bowls at set times** on the home screen (or in the **⋯** menu).
2. Tap **+ new time**, type the time, tap **ok**.
3. The time rings three bowls. For a single strike, tap the time and choose **one bowl**.

These bowls ring every day whether the app is open or not, and whether a session runs or not.

### Pause, change or remove a daily time

1. Open **bowls at set times** and tap the time.
2. Choose **turn off** to keep it without ringing (and **turn on** to bring it back), **change the time** to type another time, or **delete** to remove it.

### Choose the bowl

1. Open **settings** (tap the bowl's name at the bottom of the home screen, or **⋯** then **settings**).
2. Tap one of the five bowls. It plays one strike and becomes the bowl for everything: the repeated bowl, the end of a sitting and the daily times.

### Hear the bowl

Tap **⋯** on the home screen, then **hear the bowl**. In the settings, tapping a bowl also plays it.

### Use your own sound

1. Open **settings** and tap **your own sound…**.
2. Choose an audio file in the phone's file picker.
3. The app keeps its own copy of the file, plays it once, and uses it from then on. Where one strike is due the file is played once; where three bowls are due it is played three times in a row.

To go back to a built-in bowl, tap that bowl. To return to your file afterwards, tap the line with the file's name and choose **use this sound**. To replace it, choose **pick another file…**; the new file takes the place of the old one.

### Set the loudness

The bowl plays on the phone's alarm channel. Its loudness is the phone's alarm volume, not the media or ringtone volume. Use **hear the bowl** to check it.

### Let the app run in the background

1. Open **settings**.
2. Tap **allow running in the background**.
3. Accept the phone's request to stop optimising battery use for this app.

The line then reads **runs in the background: allowed**.

### Switch between white on black and black on white

Tap **⋯** on the home screen and then **black on white** or **white on black**; or, in **settings**, tap the line above **colours**.

### Put the widget on a home screen

1. Add a widget the way your launcher does it and choose **Reader's Mindful Tool** (described as **a bowl every X minutes, a timed meditation — play and stop**).
2. Choose **both**, **a bowl every X minutes only** or **meditation period only**.
3. On each line, tap **−** or **+** to change the minutes and **▶** to start. While it runs the line shows a countdown and **■**; tap **■** to stop.

## Settings

| Setting | What it changes | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| Bowl (first block of the settings) | The sound used for every bowl in the app | **Tibetan bowl (E♭)**, **singing bowl**, **gong bowl**, **Satipanya**, **enpleineconscience.ch**, or your own file | **Tibetan bowl (E♭)** |
| **your own sound…** | Uses an audio file from the phone as the bowl | any file the phone can play (mp3, wav) | none |
| **allow running in the background** | Exempts the app from the phone's battery optimisation | allowed or not | not allowed |
| **colours** | The two colours of the app and of its widget | **white on black**, **black on white** | **white on black** |
| **text size** | The size of all text in the app | **small**, **medium**, **large** | **medium** |
| **font** | The typeface of the app | **sans**, **serif**, **mono** | **sans** |
| **haptic tick** | A short vibration when you tap **▶** or **■** in the app | **on**, **off** | **on** |

Two more values are remembered without being in the settings screen: the minutes of **a bowl every** (10 at first) and of **meditate** (20 at first). They are the last ones you set, in the app, on the widget or on the Reader's Launcher tile.

The five built-in bowls are recorded at matched loudness.

## Your data

Everything stays on the phone: the settings, the two numbers of minutes, the list of daily times, the state of a running session, the choice made for each widget, and the copy of your own sound file. The app does not have permission to use the network, so it sends nothing to anyone and receives nothing.

There is no sync, no export and no import. The app does not opt out of Android's own device backup: if you have switched that on in the phone's settings, Android may include the app's data in it.

Your own sound is copied into the app when you choose it. The original file is not changed, and you can move or delete it afterwards without affecting the app.

A running session survives a restart of the phone, a change of time or time zone and an update of the app: the repeated bowl carries on at the same instants as before, a sitting carries on if its end is still ahead, and the daily times are set again. A sitting whose end passed while the phone was off is simply ended, without bowls.

Uninstalling the app removes all of this, including the copy of your sound.

## With the other apps

### Home-screen widget

The widget **Reader's Mindful Tool** works in any launcher. It can be resized and shows one line per function, in the app's colours:

- stopped: the name (**a bowl every** or **meditate**), then **−**, the minutes, **+** and **▶**;
- running: the name, a second line (**next at 14:30 · 2 rung**, or **until 14:50**), a live countdown and **■**; a sitting also shows a thin progress line.

**−** and **+** move through fixed values: 1, 2, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 30, 45, 60, 90, 120 minutes for the repeated bowl, and 5, 10, 15, 20, 25, 30, 40, 45, 60, 90, 120 minutes for the sitting. The widget and the app share the same minutes: a number set on the widget is the one the app shows. A number typed in the app reaches the widget the next time the widget is redrawn, that is when a session starts or stops, or within half an hour. Tapping the words opens the app.

What a widget shows (both functions or one) is chosen when it is placed. To change it, remove the widget and place it again.

### Reader's Launcher

Reader's Launcher has a tile called **mindful** that shows the same two functions: both on one tile, with a sideways swipe to go from one to the other, or only one. You tap the words to change the minutes, **▶** to start and **■** to stop. The tile needs Reader's Mindful Tool to be installed.

### Nothing else

The app does not receive shared content and does not open files, apart from the audio file you choose as your own sound.

## Limits

- Minutes go from 1 to 240, in whole minutes, for both functions.
- The repeated bowl is always one strike and the end of a sitting is always three bowls. Only the daily times let you choose between one and three.
- There is no bowl at the start of a sitting, no preparation time and no intermediate bowl inside a sitting, other than by running the repeated bowl alongside it.
- The daily times ring every day. There is no choice of days of the week.
- One bowl serves the whole app; you cannot give a different sound to each function. Only one own sound is kept at a time.
- A bowl that could not sound at its time (the phone was off) is skipped, never rung late.
- If two bowls fall at the same moment, the second cuts off the first.
- There is no volume setting in the app: the phone's alarm volume decides.
- The widget's **+** stops at 120 minutes; larger values, up to 240, are typed in the app.
- No history of sittings, no statistics, no guidance, no export.

## Messages and problems

| What you see | What it means, what to do |
|---|---|
| **(1–240)** added to the title of the minutes prompt | The number is not accepted. Type a whole number from 1 to 240. |
| **(hh:mm)** added to the title of the time prompt | The time is not understood. Type hours from 0 to 23 and minutes from 00 to 59, for example 07:30. |
| **none yet — tap to add a time of day** | No daily time is switched on. Tap the line to add one or switch one back on. |
| **no time set yet** | The list of daily times is empty. Tap **+ new time**. |
| **allow running in the background** | The phone may still restrict the app to save battery. Tap the line and accept. |
| **runs in the background: allowed** | Nothing to do. |
| Notification **the bowl** | A bowl is sounding outside a session; the notification goes away when the sound ends. |

**The bowl is silent or too quiet.** Raise the phone's alarm volume, then try **hear the bowl**.

**Nothing happens after choosing your own file.** The phone could not read it as sound, so the app kept the bowl it had. Choose another file, mp3 or wav.

**A session that was running shows ▶ again when you open the app.** Its alarm had been lost and could not be set again; the app ends such a session rather than show a countdown that would never reach zero. Start it again, and allow running in the background in the settings.

**An alarm icon appears in the phone's status bar.** Each bowl is set as an alarm-clock alarm, the kind the phone rings exactly on time; the phone may show its usual alarm sign for it.

**The bowl rings in silent mode or in Do Not Disturb.** This is intended. To have silence, stop the session and turn off the daily times.

**The widget's + or − does nothing.** The minutes are already at the end of the widget's list of values. While a function runs, **−** and **+** are not shown: the minutes cannot be changed until you stop it.

**The widget still shows the old minutes after you changed them in the app.** The widget catches up when a session starts or stops, or within half an hour. Until then, set the minutes on the widget itself with **−** and **+**.


# Reader's Food Log

*Android 1.2.1*

## What it is

Reader's Food Log is a food diary made only of photos. One touch opens the viewfinder, a second touch takes the photo, and the screen closes by itself; the app labels the photo **breakfast**, **lunch**, **dinner** or **snack** from the time of day and files it under its day. You can also note a physical activity in one tap and your weight once a day, with a curve of how it moves.

It deliberately has no calories, no food database, nothing to type about what you eat, no account and no network access: everything stays on the phone. The app is black and white, text only, and follows the phone's language (English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese, Russian).

## First start

The app opens straight on its home screen, titled **food log**. There is no account to create and nothing to configure.

The journal is empty: the home screen shows a single line, **today**, with **nothing yet** under it, and three buttons at the bottom: **activity**, **weight** and **photograph**.

The only permission the app uses is the camera, to take the photos. Android asks for it the first time the viewfinder opens. If you refuse, the viewfinder shows **The camera is all this screen does. Touch to allow it.** Touch the screen to be asked again; if Android no longer shows its question, the touch opens the app's page in Android's settings, where you can allow the camera. When you come back, the viewfinder starts.

The app runs on Android 8.0 and later.

## The screens

### Home (food log)

The title **food log** is at the top left, **settings** at the top right. Touch **settings** to open the settings.

Below is the list of days, the most recent first:

- **today** is always the first line, even when it is empty (it then reads **nothing yet**).
- Then come **yesterday** and the earlier days, written in full in lower case (for example "monday 14 september"; a day of another year is written short with its year, "mon 14 sep 2025"). A day appears only if it holds at least one photo, one activity or a weight.
- Under each day, a summary: the number of photos, the number of activities and the weight, for example "3 photos · 1 activity · 72.4 kg".

Touch a day to open it.

At the bottom of the screen, always under the thumb:

- **✎ activity** opens the sheet **physical activity, now**.
- **⚖ weight** opens the weight screen. Once today's weight is entered, this button shows the weight itself (for example "72.4 kg") instead of the word.
- **◉ photograph**, the wide band in reversed colours, opens the viewfinder.

The back button on the home screen leaves the app.

### The viewfinder

The viewfinder fills the screen, always upright (portrait). It uses the back camera, or the front one if the phone has no back camera. The screen stays lit while it is open.

- A band at the top shows the label for the current time (**breakfast**, **lunch**, **dinner** or **snack**).
- A band at the bottom says **touch anywhere**.

Touch anywhere on the screen, or press a volume key (up or down) or the phone's camera key, to take the photo. The phone gives a short vibration, the screen shows a check mark with the label and the time (for example "lunch · 12:34") for about a second, and the viewfinder closes by itself. You are back where you were: the app, the home screen, or the lock screen.

There is no zoom, no flash control, no choice of camera, and no preview to accept or retake. To leave without taking a photo, use the back button.

If the photo could not be taken, the bottom band reads **that did not work — try again**.

The viewfinder can open over the lock screen (from the quick-settings tile). It only adds a photo; it shows nothing of the journal.

### A day

The title is the day's name (**today**, **yesterday**, or the date); touch it, or the arrow in front of it, to go back.

The screen shows, from top to bottom:

- the day's weight, if one was entered, with the word **weight** under it. Touch it to type it again.
- the photos and the activities, mixed, in the order of the clock. Each photo has its label and its time above it (times are on the 24-hour clock). Each activity is a line of text with its time and the word **activity** under it.

An empty day shows **nothing yet**.

**Touch a photo** to open its menu. The menu's title is the time of the photo, and it holds:

- the four labels **breakfast**, **lunch**, **dinner**, **snack**; the current one is marked with a filled dot (●), the others with an empty one (○). Touch a label to give it to the photo.
- **share**: sends the photo to another app through Android's share sheet.
- **delete**: asks **delete this photo?** with **delete for good** and **cancel**.

**Touch an activity** to open its menu, titled with its time and text:

- **change the text**: opens **what did you do?** with the present text, to correct it.
- **delete**: removes the activity at once, without asking.

Touch outside a menu, or press back, to close it without doing anything.

On **today**, the three buttons **activity**, **weight** and **photograph** are at the bottom, as on the home screen. On the other days they are absent: photos and activities are always added to today, at the present time.

### The sheet "physical activity, now"

Opened by **✎ activity**. It offers three one-tap choices, **strength training**, **intense activity** and **endurance**, and below a line, **other…**.

- A touch on one of the three choices records it at the present time and closes the sheet.
- **other…** opens **what did you do?**: type one line, then touch **ok** (or the keyboard's confirm key). **cancel**, back, or a touch outside closes it without recording anything.

### Weight

Opened by **⚖ weight**. If today's weight is not entered yet, the question **weight (kg)** (or **weight (lb)**) is already open with the number pad.

The title **weight** is at the top left (touch it to go back). At the top right is the period shown by the curve; it starts on **90 days**. Each touch moves to the next one: **90 days**, **1 year**, **everything**, **30 days**.

**The curve.** A line through the weights of the period. The highest weight of the period is written at the top left, the lowest at the bottom left, and the most recent weight at the top right. Under the curve, one line gives the change between the first and the last weight of the period, with its sign, and the two days, for example "-1.2 kg · monday 3 august → today". With fewer than two weights in the period, the screen says instead **The curve appears with the second weight.**

**Today's line.** Under the curve, today's weight with **today** under it, or, if there is none yet, **enter today's weight** in reversed colours. Touch it to type or retype today's weight.

**The earlier weights.** The list of all the other days with a weight, the most recent first (whatever period the curve shows). Touch one to open its menu, titled with the day and the weight:

- **change the weight**: opens the question for that day.
- **delete**: removes that day's weight at once, without asking.

**The question.** Its title is **weight (kg)** or **weight (lb)**, followed by **last** and the most recent weight known up to that day (for example "weight (kg) · last 72.4 kg"). Type the number, with a point or a comma for the decimals, then **ok**. The app accepts a weight between 20 and 400 kg (about 44 to 882 lb); with any other value the question simply stays open. **cancel** closes it without changing anything.

### Settings

Opened by **settings** on the home screen. See "Settings" and "Your data" below. The last line shows the app's name and its version number.

## How to

### Photograph what you are about to eat

1. Open the viewfinder: touch **◉ photograph** in the app, or the widget on your home screen, or the quick-settings tile **Food photo**.
2. Aim, then touch anywhere on the screen (or press a volume key).
3. The label and the time appear for a moment, and the screen closes by itself.

The photo is filed under today with the label for the time it was taken.

### Take a photo without unlocking the phone

1. Add the tile **Food photo** to Android's quick settings (pull down the quick settings, edit them, and drag the tile in).
2. Pull the quick settings down and touch **Food photo**.
3. Touch anywhere to take the photo.

### See what you ate on a given day

1. On the home screen, touch the day.
2. Scroll through the photos and activities, in the order of the clock.

### Change the label of a photo

1. Open the day and touch the photo.
2. Touch the right label: **breakfast**, **lunch**, **dinner** or **snack**.

### Share a photo

1. Open the day and touch the photo.
2. Touch **share**, then choose the app to send it with.

### Delete a photo

1. Open the day and touch the photo.
2. Touch **delete**.
3. Touch **delete for good**. The photo cannot be recovered.

### Note a physical activity

1. Touch **✎ activity** (home screen or **today**).
2. Touch **strength training**, **intense activity** or **endurance**.

It is recorded under today at the present time.

### Note an activity in your own words

1. Touch **✎ activity**, then **other…**.
2. Under **what did you do?**, type one line.
3. Touch **ok**.

### Correct or delete an activity

1. Open the day and touch the activity.
2. Touch **change the text**, correct it and touch **ok**; or touch **delete**.

The time of an activity cannot be changed.

### Enter today's weight

1. Touch **⚖ weight**. The question opens by itself if today has no weight yet.
2. Type the number and touch **ok**.

There is one weight per day: typing it again replaces it.

### Correct today's weight

1. Touch the weight at the bottom of the home screen (it has replaced **⚖ weight**).
2. Touch today's line, under the curve.
3. Type the right number and touch **ok**.

You can also open **today** and touch the weight at the top of the day.

### Correct or delete the weight of an earlier day

1. Touch **⚖ weight** (or the weight shown in its place).
2. In the list under today's line, touch the day.
3. Touch **change the weight** and type the new number, or touch **delete**.

### See how your weight moves

1. Touch **⚖ weight** (or the weight shown in its place).
2. Touch the period at the top right to move between **90 days**, **1 year**, **everything** and **30 days**.

### Weigh in pounds instead of kilograms

1. On the home screen, touch **settings**.
2. Touch the line **weight unit**: it switches between **kg** and **lb**.

Every weight already entered is shown in the new unit.

### Move the journal to a new phone

1. On the old phone: **settings**, then **export the journal**. Choose where to save the zip file (its name is proposed as "food-log-" followed by the date). Wait for **journal exported**.
2. Carry the file to the new phone (a cable, a memory card, a cloud folder, a message to yourself).
3. On the new phone, install Reader's Food Log, then **settings**, **import a journal**, and choose the file. The app says how many entries were added.

### Keep a backup copy of the journal

Do step 1 above from time to time and keep the zip file somewhere other than the phone.
