### Change the look

In **settings**, touch **colours**, **text size** or **font**; each touch moves to the next choice.

## Settings

The settings screen starts with the reminder **Everything stays on this phone. To move to a new phone, export the journal here and import it there.**

| Line | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| **export the journal**, with **one zip file, where you choose** under it | Writes the whole journal into one zip file at the place you choose. While it runs, the line reads **working…**. |
| **import a journal**, with **adds, replaces nothing** under it | Reads a zip file exported by the app and adds what the phone does not have yet. |

The result of an export or an import is written just below these two lines.

Then the settings themselves. Each line shows the current value in large letters and the setting's name under it; touch the line to move to the next value.

| Setting | Choices | Default |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **colours** | **dark** (white on black), **light** (black on white) | **dark** |
| **text size** | **S**, **M**, **L** | **M** |
| **font** | **sans-serif**, **serif**, **mono** | **sans-serif** |
| **weight unit** | **kg**, **lb** | **kg**; **lb** when the phone's region is the United States, Liberia or Myanmar |
| **vibration on touch** | **on**, **off** | **on** |

**colours** also sets the colours of the two home-screen widgets.

**weight unit** only changes how weights are typed and shown; the journal itself always keeps them in kilograms, so you can switch at any time without losing anything.

**vibration on touch** is the short vibration when you touch **activity**, **weight** or **photograph**. The vibration given when a photo is taken in the viewfinder does not depend on this setting.

Below the settings, a paragraph recalls how the labels are chosen: **The label comes from the time of the photo: breakfast 4:00–10:30, lunch 11:30–14:30, dinner 18:00–22:00, snack the rest of the time. Touch a photo to change it.** These hours cannot be changed.

The last line, **Reader's Food Log**, shows the version number. Touching it does nothing.

## Your data

**Where it lives.** The whole journal is on the phone, in the app's private storage: one folder per day, holding the day's photos, its activities and its weight. The photos do not appear in the phone's gallery. Photos are saved small on purpose (about 1600 × 1200 points), so that the journal and its exports stay light.

**The network.** The app has no network permission: it cannot send or receive anything. There is no account, no synchronisation, no statistics. A photo leaves the app only when you **share** it or when you export the journal.

**Android's backup.** The app is excluded from Android's automatic backup. The only copy of your journal outside the phone is the one you export yourself.

**Export.** **export the journal** writes one zip file containing one folder per day, named by its date; in it, the photos as ordinary JPEG images (named by their time and label), a small text file for the activities and another for the weight in kilograms. You can open the zip on a computer and look at the photos there. The look settings (colours, text size, font, unit, vibration) are not part of the export.

**Import.** **import a journal** merges the file into the journal already on the phone and replaces nothing:

- a photo already on the phone is left as it is, even if its label differs in the file (the phone's label is kept, and the photo is not added twice);
- activities are added one by one, except those the phone already has with the same time and the same text;
- a day that already has a weight keeps it; the file's weight is added only on days without one.

You can therefore import the same file twice, or import onto a phone that already has a journal, without creating duplicates.

**Uninstalling.** Uninstalling the app, or clearing its data in Android's settings, deletes the whole journal. Export it first if you want to keep it.

## With the other apps

### Home-screen widgets

The app offers two widgets for any Android home screen. Both take the colours chosen in the app (**dark** or **light**).

- **Food photo** (one cell): a single ◉. One touch opens the viewfinder.
- **Food log** (four cells wide, one high): the title **food log**, and under it **today** followed by the day's count, for example "today · 2 photos · 1 activity", or "today · nothing yet". Touch the text to open the app. Touch **✎** to open the app on the sheet **physical activity, now**. Touch **◉** to open the viewfinder. The count is brought up to date after each photo or activity.

Both can be resized.

### Quick-settings tile

**Food photo**, to add to Android's quick settings. One touch opens the viewfinder, from any app and from the lock screen.

### Shortcuts on the app's icon

A long press on the app's icon offers two shortcuts: **Photograph** (opens the viewfinder) and **Activity** (opens the app on the sheet **physical activity, now**).

### Reader's Launcher

Reader's Launcher has a **food log** tile. It shows **photograph** with **food log** under it and a ◉; one touch opens the viewfinder. If Reader's Food Log is not installed, the tile shows the app's name in grey and a touch opens the app's page on the web.

### Sharing

**share** on a photo hands the image to any app that accepts pictures (messages, mail, notes). The app does not receive anything from other apps, and opens no file other than its own journal zip.

## Limits

- Photos only: no text, no calories, no nutrients, no caption on a photo.
- Photos are taken in the app's viewfinder; you cannot add a picture from the gallery or from another app.
- Photos and activities are always recorded at the present time, under today. You cannot add a photo or an activity to an earlier day, nor change the time of an entry.
- The four labels and their hours are fixed.
- One weight per day. You can correct the weight of a day that has one, but not add a weight to an earlier day that has none.
- Today's weight can be retyped but not removed; **delete** is offered for the earlier days only.
- Deleting an activity or a weight is immediate, without confirmation; only photos ask **delete this photo?**.
- A weight must lie between 20 and 400 kg.
- An activity is one line of text.
- The curve's period goes back to **90 days** each time the weight screen is opened.
- No search, no statistics on meals, no reminders or notifications.
- No synchronisation between phones: the journal moves by export and import.
- The viewfinder has no zoom, no flash control and no camera choice, and works upright only.
- There is no language setting: the app uses the phone's language, or English if that language is not one of its six.

## Messages and problems

| Message | What it means, what to do |
| --- | --- |
| **nothing yet** | Nothing recorded on that day. On the home screen and the widget it concerns today. |
| **touch anywhere** | The viewfinder is ready: touch the screen or press a volume key. |
| **The camera is all this screen does. Touch to allow it.** | The camera permission is missing. Touch the screen: Android asks again, or the app's page in Android's settings opens so that you can allow the camera there. |
| **that did not work — try again** | In the viewfinder: the camera could not start or the photo could not be saved. Touch again; if it persists, close the viewfinder and reopen it. In the settings: the export file could not be written; choose another place and export again. |
| **The curve appears with the second weight.** | The period shown holds fewer than two weights. Enter a weight on another day, or choose a longer period at the top right. |
| **working…** | An export is being written. Wait for the result. |
| **journal exported** | The zip file is written where you chose. |
| "3 entries added" (the number varies) | The import added that many photos, activities and weights. |
| **nothing new in this file** | The file is a journal, but the phone already has everything in it. |
| **this file is not a food log journal** | The file is not a zip exported by Reader's Food Log, or it could not be read. Choose the right file. |

**The weight question does not close when I touch ok.** The number is outside 20 to 400 kg (44 to 882 lb), or is not a number. Check the unit shown in the question's title, **weight (kg)** or **weight (lb)**, and type it again.

**ok does nothing in "what did you do?".** The line is empty; type something, or touch **cancel**.

**The label is wrong.** The label follows the clock: breakfast from 4:00 to 10:30, lunch from 11:30 to 14:30, dinner from 18:00 to 22:00, snack otherwise. Touch the photo and choose another label.

**My photos are not in the gallery.** That is intended: they are kept inside the app. Use **share** on a photo, or **export the journal**, to get them out.


# Reader's Scanner

*Android 1.1.2 · Windows, macOS, Linux 1.0.2*

## What it is

Reader's Scanner turns paper into searchable PDFs. On the phone you photograph the pages with the camera: the app finds the page, straightens it, cleans it and reads its text. On the computer the same app drives a real scanner (glass or sheet feeder). Each document becomes one PDF whose text can be searched and copied, named with its date and hour and, if you wish, a name of your own, and filed in plain folders.

The text is always read on your own device: your documents are never sent anywhere to be read. If you enter a WebDAV folder of your own (kDrive, Nextcloud or any WebDAV server), the phone and the computer show the same documents and the same folders.

The app is black and white and made of text. It has no account, no advertising, no cloud of its own, and it does not name documents for you: a document you do not name is called by its date and hour.

## First start

### On the phone: which engine?

The first screen is **Which engine?**. It explains: **Two ways of finding the page and reading its text. Both work on the phone: your documents are never sent to be read.** You choose one of two entries by touching it (each ends with **→ this one**):

- **Google ML Kit (recommended)**: **Finds and straightens pages most reliably, on any background, and reads text very well. Closed Google code: it needs Google Play services and sends usage statistics to Google (not your documents). Latin script only: Russian is read by Tesseract.** On a phone without Google Play services this entry is greyed out and reads **Not available on this phone: Google Play services are missing.**
- **Open source**: **The app's own page finder and Tesseract: free software, nothing goes to Google, seven languages. Less sure of the page on pale backgrounds (the corners are easy to adjust), blurred shots are flagged at once.**

The screen ends with **You can change this at any time in the settings.** The choice sets two settings at once, **finding the page** and **reading the text**, which you can later change separately.

### Permissions on the phone

- **Camera**: asked the first time the app's own capture screen opens. It is used only to photograph pages. If you refuse, the capture screen shows **This screen needs the camera. Touch to allow it.**; touching it asks again, or opens the app's page in Android's settings when Android no longer asks.
- **Network access**: used only for your WebDAV folder (if you set one), to download a "best" reading model when you ask for one, and by Google ML Kit for its usage statistics if you chose it.

The app asks for no access to your files or photos: photos are picked through Android's own photo picker.

### What you see when it is empty

The home screen shows one folder, **all scans**, with the number 0, a dashed tile **new folder**, and a wide bar **+ new scan** at the bottom. An empty folder shows **No scans here yet.**

No account or server is needed. The WebDAV folder is optional and is entered in the settings (see "Set up the WebDAV folder").

## The screens

These are the screens of the phone. The computer's window is described in "On the computer".

A menu is a sheet of text lines at the bottom of the screen; touching outside it, or the back button, closes it.

### Home

The title is **scanner**. On its right, **⌕** opens the search and **⋯** opens the settings.

Below, a grid of folders, two per row. Each shows the number of documents it holds and its name.

- **all scans** (drawn filled) holds every document, whatever its folder. Touch it to list them all.
- Your folders: touch one to open it; a long press opens a menu with **rename** and **delete the folder**.
- **new folder** (dashed): touch it, type the **name of the new folder**, then **ok**.

At the bottom, **+ new scan** opens the capture screen.

### A folder

The title is the folder's name; touching it (or **←**) goes back. **⌕** opens the search.

Documents are listed newest first. Each row shows a small picture of the first page, the document's name (or its date and hour if it has no name), then a line with the date and hour, the number of pages and, in **all scans**, the folder's name. A third line may say **reading the text 2/5**, **text to be read** or **text could not be read**. A document scanned on another device shows the word PDF in place of the picture until its PDF has been downloaded.

Dates are written **today 11:32**, **yesterday 09:10**, or the day and month.

- Touch a row to open the document.
- A long press on a row starts a selection. The title becomes **1 selected**, **2 selected**…; touches then add or remove documents; **all** selects everything and becomes **none**. A bar at the bottom offers **share**, **move**, **delete** and, when exactly one document is selected, **⋯** (the document's menu). The back button or **←** ends the selection.

At the bottom, **+ new scan** opens the capture screen; the document will be offered this folder first.

### Capture (the app's own camera)

This screen is shown when **finding the page** is set to **built-in (free software)**. It is always upright; if you hold the phone sideways the photo follows your hand. The screen stays lit.

- At the top, seven language buttons: **EN FR DE IT ES PT RU**. The one that is filled is the language the text will be read in; the line below confirms it, for example **text read in English**.
- On phones with a flash, **☼** at the top right turns the lamp on (**☀**) and off.
- In the middle, the camera's view. When the app sees the page it outlines it in white.
- Under the view, one line of guidance: **hold the page in view, touch to take it**, then **page found — touch or press a volume key**, then **hold still…** while the app waits for your hands to stop moving and focuses on the page. **the camera did not answer — try again** means the photo failed.
- To take the page: touch the view, touch the round button, or press a volume key.
- Bottom left: **from photos** (before the first page) brings in pictures from your gallery. Once a page is taken, a small picture of the last page with the number of pages replaces it; touching it opens the review.
- Bottom right: **done →** opens the review.

If a page comes out blurred, a white bar says so at once, for example **page 2 is blurred**, with **retake** (the next photo takes that page's place) and **keep** (the page stays as it is).

The back button leaves the capture if no page was taken; otherwise it opens the review.

### Capture with Google's scanner

When **finding the page** is set to **ML Kit (Google)**, **+ new scan** opens Google's own scanner screen instead, with its own buttons. When you finish there, the pages arrive in the review described below. Because Google's screen has no language buttons, the review of a new document then shows the seven language buttons at its top, with the line **text read in …**.

### Review

The title says where you are: **page 2 of 5**, followed by **· blurred** if this page is blurred, or by **· 1 blurred** if another page is. Swipe sideways to go from page to page.

Under the page, four buttons:

- **crop** opens the crop screen for this page.
- **turn** turns the page a quarter turn.
- The third button shows the page's look and changes it at each touch: **clean** (white paper, colours kept), **grey**, **b & w**, **photo** (the straightened page as the camera saw it).
- **⋯** opens a menu titled with the page number: **move earlier**, **move later**, **« clean » for every page** (the current look, given to all pages), **from photos**, **take this page again**, **delete this page**.

At the bottom, **+ page** returns to the capture, and **save** files the document. **save** reads **working…** while pages are still being prepared.

A page may carry a small label: **straightening…** while it is being made, **this photo could not be read** if the picture could not be used, **blurred — ⋯ to take it again** if it is blurred.

The back button asks **discard this page?** or **discard these 3 pages?** (when editing an existing document: **discard these changes?**), with **discard** and **keep them**.

### Crop

The whole photo is shown, with the page's outline and a round handle on each corner. The line at the top says **drag the corners onto the corners of the page**. While you drag a corner, a magnifier in a top corner of the picture shows it three times larger.

- **whole photo** puts the corners on the corners of the photo.
- **find the page** lets the app look for the page again (**searching…**). If it finds none: **no page found in this photo — place the corners by hand**.
- **cancel** leaves without change; **ok** applies the new outline.

### Filing sheet

**save**, for a new document, opens a sheet with:

- **name this document**: a field (it shows **optional** while empty), with the keyboard already open. Below it: **The file name starts with the date and hour; the name follows them. Without a name, they stand alone.**
- **file in**: **▣ all scans**, then one line per folder (**▢** and its name), then **+ new folder**. If the scan was started from inside a folder, that folder comes first and is highlighted, and the list ends with **▣ no folder (all scans only)**.

Touching a line files the document there. **+ new folder** asks for the **name of the new folder** and files the document in it. The app then shows the folder the document went into, and the text is read in the background.

Touching outside the sheet closes it without filing.

### A document

The title is the document's name (or its date). Under it, one line of information: date and hour, number of pages, language of the text, who read it (**Tesseract fast**, **Tesseract best** or **ML Kit (Google)**), and when it applies **scanned elsewhere**, **reading the text 2/5**, **text to be read** or **text could not be read**.

The pages are shown one under the other. Touch a page to see it full screen.

At the bottom:

- **share** opens the share menu.
- **text** shows the text read on the pages, which you can select and copy; the button then reads **pages** to come back. With several pages, each page's text is headed **— 1 —**, **— 2 —**… If there is nothing to show: **The text has not been read yet.** or **No text was found on these pages.**
- **+ page** opens the capture to add pages.

**⋯** at the top right opens the document's menu, titled with the full file name: **share…**, **open with…**, **save a copy of the pdf**, **rename**, **move to a folder**, **edit the pages**, **read the text again**, **delete**.

A document scanned on another device has no **+ page**, **edit the pages** or **read the text again**: its pages can only be changed where it was scanned. Its PDF is downloaded the first time you open it; the information line then says **downloading the PDF… 40 %**.

### A page, full screen

The page on a black background, with **← 2 / 5** at the top (touch it to go back). Swipe sideways for the other pages. Pinch to zoom (up to six times); a double tap zooms in, a second one zooms out.

### Share menu

Titled with the file name, or **3 documents** when several are selected:

- **PDF**: **the pages with their text, searchable** (for several: **one PDF per document, text searchable**).
- **images**: the pages as pictures; the line says how many, for example **3 JPEG pictures**.
- **text**: **the words read on the pages**. If some text is not ready: **some text is not read yet**.

Android's share sheet then lets you choose the receiving app.

### Search

The field says **search names and text**. Results appear as you type: the document's name and a few words around the place where the text matches. **nothing found** when there is no match. Touch a result to open the document.

### Settings

See "Settings" below. Each line shows its current value in large type and its name underneath; touching the line changes the value or opens a field.

## How to

### Scan a document

1. Touch **+ new scan** (on the home screen, or inside the folder the document belongs to).
2. Check the language at the top (**EN FR DE IT ES PT RU**) and touch another one if needed.
3. Hold the phone over the page until it is outlined, then touch the view or press a volume key. Keep still until the flash on the screen.
4. Repeat for each page.
5. Touch **done →**. Go through the pages; correct what needs it.
6. Touch **save**. Type a name if you want one, then touch the folder.

The document appears in its folder with **text to be read**, then **reading the text 1/3**… When that line disappears, its PDF is searchable.

### Scan with Google's scanner

1. In the settings, set **finding the page** to **ML Kit (Google)**.
2. Touch **+ new scan** and follow Google's screen.
3. Back in the review, choose the language of the text at the top, then **save**.

If Google's scanner cannot start, the app says **Google scanner unavailable — the app's own camera instead: …** and opens its own capture screen.

### Turn photos you already have into a document

1. Touch **+ new scan**, then **from photos** at the bottom left. (In the review: **⋯**, then **from photos**.)
2. Pick the photos (up to 30 at a time).
3. The app finds the page in each one. Check them in the review, then **save**.

### Retake a blurred page

- On the capture screen, when the bar **page 2 is blurred** appears: touch **retake** and photograph the page again. Touch **keep** if the page is fine as it is (a page that is mostly a photograph may be flagged wrongly).
- In the review: go to the page, touch **⋯**, then **take this page again**. The new photo takes the place of the old one.

### Correct the outline of a page

1. In the review, go to the page and touch **crop**.
2. Drag each corner onto the corner of the sheet, using the magnifier.
3. Touch **ok**.

### Turn, reorder or remove pages, change their look

In the review: **turn** turns the page; the look button goes through **clean**, **grey**, **b & w**, **photo**; **⋯** gives **move earlier**, **move later**, **« … » for every page** and **delete this page**.

### Change the pages of a document already filed

1. Open the document, touch **⋯**, then **edit the pages**. To add pages only, touch **+ page** at the bottom of the document.
2. Change what you want in the review, then **save**.

The document keeps its name, date and folder. Its text is read again, in the language currently chosen for the text.

### Name or rename a document

- When filing: type the name in **name this document** before touching the folder.
- Later: open the document, **⋯**, **rename**. The field is titled **name (empty: the date alone)**: empty it to go back to the date alone.

The file is always called date, hour, then name, for example `2026-09-24 11h32 Electricity bill.pdf`.

### Create, rename or delete a folder

- Create: **new folder** on the home screen, or **+ new folder** in the filing sheet.
- Rename: long press on the folder, **rename**.
- Delete: long press on the folder, **delete the folder**. The app asks **delete the folder « … »? Its scans stay in all scans.** Touch **delete the folder, keep the scans**. The documents are not deleted; they remain in **all scans**.

### Move documents to another folder

- One document: open it, **⋯**, **move to a folder**, then touch the folder (**●** marks the current one). **no folder (all scans only)** takes it out of its folder.
- Several: in a folder, long press on one document, touch the others, then **move** and the folder.

### Read, copy or re-read the text

- Open the document and touch **text**. Press and hold on the text to select and copy it.
- If the text is wrong because the language was wrong: **⋯**, **read the text again**, then the language under **read the text again in**. Each language shows which reader will be used.

### Find a document

Touch **⌕**, type one or several words. A document is found when every word is in its name, its date or its text, in any order; capitals and accents do not matter.

### Share documents

1. Open the document and touch **share** (or select several documents in a folder and touch **share**).
2. Choose **PDF**, **images** or **text**.
3. Choose the app to send to.

Shared text is sent as a message; a very long text (over 60 000 characters) is sent as a text file instead.

### Open the PDF in another app, or save a copy

Open the document, **⋯**, then **open with…** (Android offers your PDF apps) or **save a copy of the pdf** (you choose where to save it).

### Delete documents

- One: open it, **⋯**, **delete**, then **delete for good** under **delete this document?**.
- Several: select them in a folder, **delete**, then **delete for good** under **delete these 3 documents?**.

With a WebDAV folder, the document is also deleted from the server and from your other devices.

### Set up the WebDAV folder

1. Home, **⋯** (settings).
2. Touch **server** and type the address, which begins with https://. For kDrive the app gives: **https://ID.connect.kdrive.infomaniak.com (the ID is the number in the kDrive web address)**.
3. Touch **username**, then **password**. For kDrive: **your Infomaniak login, and an application password if two-factor authentication is on**.
4. **folder on the server** is **Scans** unless you change it.
5. Touch **send now**. The line under it says **sending…**, then **up to date at 11:32**, followed when it applies by **2 sent**, **1 removed**, **3 received**.

### Copy the server settings to another device

- On the device already set up: settings, **export credentials** (**a file to set up another device — it holds the password**), and send the file to yourself by a private route.
- On the other device: settings, **import credentials**, and pick the file. The line then says **credentials imported**.

A credentials file exported by Reader's Notes or Reader's Recorder also works: **server and login taken from Reader's Notes — the folder stays yours**. One from Reader's Tasks or Reader's Calendar gives the login only: **login taken from Reader's Tasks — now enter the WebDAV server**.

### Get a more accurate reading (Tesseract)

In the settings, under **More accurate reading: the "best" model of a language, downloaded once (slower, and larger).**, touch the line of the language: **fast model — download the best one (4 MB)**. It shows **downloading the best model… 40 %**, then **best model in use — touch to remove it**. **other languages…** shows the other six languages.

### Change the engine

In the settings, touch **finding the page** to switch between **built-in (free software)** and **ML Kit (Google)**, and **reading the text** to switch between **Tesseract (free software)** and **ML Kit (Google)**. The two are independent.

## Settings

The settings of the phone, in the order of the screen. Touching a line with a fixed list of values moves to the next value.

| Setting | What it does | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| **server** | Address of your WebDAV server | free text, https://… | empty |
| **username** | Login on that server | free text | empty |
| **password** | Password (shown as dots) | free text | empty |
| **folder on the server** | Folder that receives the scans | free text | **Scans** |
| **send when the app opens** | Syncs with the server each time the app comes to the front | **on**, **off** | **on** |
| **send now** | Syncs at once; the result is shown under it. Shown only when a server is set | | |
| **export credentials** | Shares a file holding server, folder, login and password. Shown only when a server is set | | |
| **import credentials** | Reads such a file | | |
| **page format** | Paper you usually scan. **A page of that paper then gets its exact proportions, and the PDF its size. Receipts, cards and other shapes keep their own.** | **automatic (any sheet)**, **A series (A4, A5, A3)**, **US Letter** | **automatic (any sheet)** |
| **look of new pages** | Look given to each new page until you change it in the review | **clean**, **grey**, **b & w**, **photo** | **clean** |
| **language of the text (also on the capture screen)** | Language the next scans are read in | English, français, Deutsch, italiano, español, português, русский | the phone's language if it is one of these, otherwise English |
| "best" model lines | Download or remove the more accurate Tesseract model of a language | per language | fast model |
| **finding the page** | Who finds and straightens the page | **built-in (free software)**, **ML Kit (Google)** | your first-start choice |
| **reading the text** | Who reads the text | **Tesseract (free software)**, **ML Kit (Google)** | your first-start choice |
| **colours** | White on black or black on white | **dark**, **light** | **dark** |
| **text size** | Size of the app's text | S, M, L | M |
| **font** | Typeface of the app | sans-serif, serif, mono | sans-serif |
| **vibration on touch** | A short vibration on buttons | **on**, **off** | **on** |

Explanations shown on the screen:

- Under the language: **The text is read on this phone, never sent anywhere to be read. Clean: white paper, colours kept. Grey and b & w suit plain text. Photo: the straightened page as the camera saw it.**
- Above the two engine lines: **Each step can use Google's ML Kit or the free engines. ML Kit's scanner needs Google Play services and shows its own screen (the language is then chosen in the review); its reader knows the Latin script only (Russian is read by Tesseract). It sends usage statistics to Google, never your documents.**

The "best" models weigh: English 15 MB, français 4 MB, Deutsch 9 MB, italiano 9 MB, español 14 MB, português 8 MB, русский 15 MB.

The last line shows the app's name and version, followed by the credits: **Text recognition: Tesseract 5 and Leptonica (Apache 2.0) through Tesseract4Android, language models tessdata (Apache 2.0); or Google ML Kit (Google's terms), if chosen.**

## Your data

### Where it lives

On the phone, everything is kept in the app's private storage: the photos as taken (so that a page can be cropped again later), the pages as shown, the text, and the PDF. Android's backup is switched off for this app. Uninstalling the app removes all of it from the phone; what was sent to your WebDAV folder stays there.

On the computer, see "On the computer".

### What goes over the network

- To your WebDAV server, if you set one: the PDFs and a small description of each document (which contains its text). Nothing is sent before a server is entered.
- To github.com, only when you download a "best" reading model: the model file of the Tesseract project is fetched from there.
- To Google, only if you chose ML Kit: usage statistics, according to the app's own notice; never your documents.

The text is read on the device in every case.

### The WebDAV folder

In the folder you named (**Scans** by default) you will find:

- one subfolder per folder of the app, holding its PDFs;
- directly in the folder, the PDFs that are in **all scans** only;
- a hidden folder `.readers-scanner` with one small description file per document: its name, date, folder, language and text. It is what lets another device list and search a document without downloading its PDF.

PDFs you put in that folder yourself, without a description, are left alone and are not shown in the app.

### How the sync behaves

The phone syncs when the app opens (if **send when the app opens** is **on**), after a document is filed, once its text is read, after every rename, move or deletion, and when you touch **send now**.

- A document goes up once its text has been read (or could not be read), never before.
- Renaming or moving a document renames or moves its file on the server; the PDF is not sent again. If a file of that name already exists there, " (2)" is added to the name.
- Documents scanned on another device appear in the list at once, marked **scanned elsewhere**, and can be searched. Their PDF is downloaded when you open, share or save them. You can rename, move, share and delete them; a device never sends the PDF of a document it did not scan.
- Changed on two devices: the latest change wins.
- Deleted on one device: deleted on the others, unless it was changed there in the meantime, in which case it comes back.
- Folders: a folder created on either side appears on the other. A folder deleted on the server disappears from the app; its documents stay in **all scans**. A folder deleted or renamed in the app is removed from the server once no file is left in it.

### The credentials file

**export credentials** makes a file named `readers-credentials-scanner.json` and hands it to Android's share sheet. It holds the server, the folder, the login and the password in clear text: send it only by a route you trust, and delete it afterwards. The copy the app made for sharing is erased at the next export and at each start of the app.

## With the other apps

- **App shortcut**: a long press on the app's icon offers **New scan**, which opens the capture directly.
- **Sharing**: documents leave as PDF, JPEG pictures or text through Android's share sheet; **open with…** hands the PDF to any PDF app.
- **Credentials**: the credentials file is the same for the Reader's apps. Reader's Scanner reads the server and login from a file exported by Reader's Notes or Reader's Recorder, and the login alone from Reader's Tasks or Reader's Calendar.
- **The computer**: the desktop app shares the same WebDAV folder (next section).

The app offers no home-screen widget and does not appear in the share menu of other apps.

## On the computer

The desktop app (Windows, macOS, Linux) does the same job with a real scanner: one window, one **scan** button, the same folders as the phone. Its window follows the system language (English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese, Russian).

### How it differs from the phone

- Pages come from a scanner or from files, not from a camera. There is no crop screen, no blur warning and no choice of engine: the text is read by Tesseract.
- Most scanners made since about 2015 (AirScan, Mopria) are found by themselves, on the network or by USB. The others need NAPS2, a free program you install separately from naps2.com.
- The looks are **as scanned**, **clean**, **grey**, **b & w**; the default is **as scanned**.
- From the feeder, blank sheets and blank backs are left out, and pages fed upside down or sideways are set upright.
- There is no share menu: you open the PDF, save a copy, save the pages as pictures, or copy the text.

### First start

The window opens with **put the pages on the scanner, press « scan »** and, under it, **In the feeder or on the glass: the scanner takes what it finds. The text is read on this computer, and the document becomes a PDF you can search.**, with the buttons **scan** and **from files…**. Once you have documents, it says **scan, or choose a document**.

The app looks for a scanner by itself: the bottom left says **looking for the scanner…**, then the scanner's name, or **no scanner found yet**. Nothing has to be set up to scan. The second line says **on this computer only** until you enter a WebDAV folder.

### The window

On the left, from top to bottom:

- **find**: the search field.
- The list. At first it shows **all scans**, your folders (each with its number of documents) and **+ new folder**. Click a folder to list its documents, newest first; a bar **← all scans** (or the folder's name) at the top goes back. When pages are scanned but not yet filed, a line **scan not filed yet** stays at the top of the list.
- Three choices, each a click away: **from: automatic ▾** (**automatic**, **glass**, **feeder**, **both sides**), **look: as scanned ▾**, **text: English ▾** (the language the text is read in).
- The big **scan** button. During a scan it reads **cancel**.
- Two status lines and **⚙**. The first line names the scanner (click: settings). The second is the sync: **on this computer only**, **syncing…**, or **synced 14:05** followed by the numbers sent (↑), received (↓) and removed (−); click it to sync now. **⚙** opens the settings.

On the right: a message, the open document, or the pages just scanned.

In the list:

- Click a document to show it on the right. Double-click (or Enter) opens its PDF in your PDF viewer.
- Ctrl-click or Shift-click selects several documents.
- Right-click a document (or a selection) for its menu; right-click a folder for **rename…** and **delete the folder**; right-click **all scans** for **+ new folder**; right-click the empty part of the list for **scan** and **from files…**.

### A document

At the top its name and **⋯**; under it the date, number of pages, folder, language, reader (**Tesseract** or **Tesseract best**) and, when it applies, **scanned elsewhere**, **reading the text 2/5**, **text to be read**, **text could not be read**, **downloading… 40 %**. Then the pages, one under the other.

At the bottom: **open the PDF**, **save a copy…**, **copy the text**, and **text** (which becomes **pages**) to read the text in place of the pages.

The **⋯** menu (and the right-click menu): **open the PDF**, **save a copy…**, **save the pages as pictures…**, **copy the text**, **rename…**, **move to** (with **no folder (all scans only)**, your folders and **+ new folder…**), **edit the pages**, **add pages from the scanner**, **read the text again in** (the seven languages), **delete**. The **⋯** menu adds **from files…**, **sync now** (when a server is set), **white on black** or **black on white**, and **settings**.

### Scan a document

1. Put the sheets in the feeder or a page on the glass.
2. If needed, change **from**, **look** and **text** above the button. **automatic** takes the feeder when it holds paper, the glass otherwise.
3. Click **scan** (or Ctrl+N). The right side shows **scanning…**, with **from the feeder**, **from the glass** or **both sides**, then **page 1**, **page 2**…, then **setting the pages upright…**. **cancel** (or Escape) stops the scan.
4. The pages appear as small sheets. Under each: its number, **←** and **→** (move earlier, move later), **⟳** (turn), **✕** (delete this page). The dashed sheet **+ page** scans one more page. At the top you can still change **look** (for all pages of this scan) and **text**. If blank pages were left out, **1 blank page left out · keep it** puts them back.
5. The name field (**name this document — or leave it to its date**) already has the keyboard: type a name or leave it empty.
6. Click the folder (**all scans**, one of your folders, or **+ new folder**), or press Enter. The line under the folders tells where Enter files it: **a click on a folder files the document there · Enter: « all scans »**. Enter uses the folder open in the list, otherwise the last folder used.

The document opens on the right and its text is read. **discard** (or Escape) throws the pages away after asking **Discard this page?** or **Discard these 3 pages?** (when you were editing a filed document, it leaves the document as it was, without asking). Pages not yet filed are kept if you close the app.

Scans are made at 300 dpi in colour.

### Bring in pictures and PDFs

Drop files on the window, press Ctrl+O, or choose **from files…**. Accepted: JPEG, PNG, TIFF, BMP, WebP and PDF. The app says **bringing the pages in…**, then shows the pages in the same review as a scan.

### Change a document already filed

- **edit the pages** opens its pages in the review (turn, reorder, delete, look); **add pages from the scanner** does the same and starts a scan. Finish with **save**.
- **rename…** (F2): the field is titled **name (empty: the date alone)**.
- **move to**, **read the text again in**, **delete** (the app asks **Delete “…”?** or **Delete these 3 documents?**).
- **save a copy…** saves the PDF where you choose; with several documents selected it asks for a folder (**save the copies in…**). **save the pages as pictures…** writes one JPEG per page in the folder you choose. **copy the text** puts the text on the clipboard (**text copied**).

Folders: **+ new folder** asks **name of the new folder**; **rename…** asks **new name of the folder**; **delete the folder** asks **Delete the folder “…”? Its scans stay in all scans.**

### Search

Type in **find** (Ctrl+F). The list shows the documents that contain every word, in their name or text, with a few words around the match; **nothing found** otherwise. Escape empties the field; the Down arrow or Enter moves to the first result.

### Settings on the computer

**⚙**, Ctrl+, or **settings** in the **⋯** menu. Changes apply with **save**; **cancel** leaves them.

| Setting | What it does | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| **server** | WebDAV address (the field shows the form of a kDrive address as an example) | free text | empty |
| **username**, **password** | Login on that server | free text | empty |
| **folder on the server** | Folder shared with the phone | free text | Scans |
| **import credentials…**, **export credentials…** | Read or write a Reader's credentials file | | |
| **scanner** | The scanner used. **look again** searches again (**looking…**) and lists what it found | the scanners found | the first one found |
| **page format** | Paper size asked of the scanner and given to the PDF | **automatic (A4 here)** or **automatic (Letter here)**, **A series (A4)**, **US Letter** | automatic: Letter in the United States, Canada, Mexico, the Philippines, Chile and Colombia, A4 elsewhere |
| **reading** | **the most accurate models (fetched once per language, 4 to 15 MB)** | ticked, not ticked | ticked |
| **font** | Typeface of the window | sans-serif, serif, mono | sans-serif |

Under **scanner**, a line says **NAPS2 … is here, for the scanners that do not answer by themselves** or **NAPS2 is not installed: only the scanners that do not answer by themselves (AirScan) need it.** with a link to naps2.com.

Under **reading**, a line gives the state for the current language: **English: the best model is here**, **English: downloading the best model… 40 %**, **English: the standard model for now**, **English: its model will be fetched at the first reading**, or **Tesseract is not installed: the pages are kept without their text**.

The bottom of the window shows the version.

Two settings are not in that window: the colours (Ctrl+T, or **white on black** / **black on white** in the **⋯** menu; black on white at first) and the text size (Ctrl+= and Ctrl+-).

To use the phone's folders, enter the same server, folder and login as on the phone, or import the credentials file exported by the phone. If the app's own section is missing from the file, the server and login of Reader's Notes or Reader's Recorder are used: **server and login taken from Reader's Notes**. **export credentials…** writes `readers-credentials.json` in your home folder unless you choose another place; if that file already exists and is a Reader's credentials file, the app's section is added to it and the other apps' sections are kept. The app then says **credentials exported to … — the file holds your passwords: keep it private**.

### Keyboard shortcuts

| Keys | Action |
|---|---|
| Ctrl+N | scan (during a scan: cancel) |
| Enter, in the name field | file the scan in the folder named under the folders |
| Ctrl+O | bring in pictures and PDFs |
| Ctrl+F | search |
| F2 | rename the open document |
| Delete | delete the open or selected documents |
| F5 or Ctrl+R | sync now |
| Ctrl+T | white on black / black on white |
| Ctrl+= or Ctrl++, Ctrl+- | larger, smaller text |
| Ctrl+, | settings |
| Escape | cancel the scan; else empty the search; else discard the pages in review; else go back to the folders |
| Ctrl+Q | quit |

### Sync on the computer

The rules are those of the phone (see "Your data"). The computer syncs at start, every five minutes, after every change, once a text is read, and on F5. When you close the window, the app finishes reading the texts and syncs once more before it quits.

If you change the server or the folder in the settings, the documents scanned on other devices are removed from this computer (they belong to the old server), and the documents scanned here are sent to the new one.

### Command line

- `readers-scanner --export-credentials FILE` writes the credentials file.
- `readers-scanner --import-credentials FILE` reads one.

### Where things are kept

| System | Settings | Documents |
|---|---|---|
| Linux | `~/.config/readers-scanner/config.json` | `~/.local/share/readers-scanner` |
| Windows | `%APPDATA%\Readers Scanner` | the same folder |
| macOS | `~/Library/Application Support/readers-scanner` | the same folder |

The settings file holds the WebDAV password in clear text; on Linux and macOS it is readable by you alone. The documents folder holds, for each document scanned here, the scan as it came, the page as shown, the text and the PDF; for a document scanned elsewhere, its description, its text and, once opened, its PDF. A file `errors.log` there records unexpected errors. Uninstalling the app does not delete these folders.

The "best" reading models are downloaded from github.com (the Tesseract project) into the documents folder. Apart from that and your WebDAV server, the app contacts only your scanner.

## Limits

- Seven languages for the text: English, French, German, Italian, Spanish, Portuguese, Russian. One language per document.
- Google ML Kit reads the Latin script only; Russian is always read by Tesseract. ML Kit's scanner needs Google Play services.
- Folders are one level deep: no folder inside a folder.
- A folder name keeps at most 60 characters, a file name 120; the characters \ / : * ? " < > | are replaced by spaces. Two folders cannot differ only by capitals.
- The app does not invent names: without a name, a document is called by its date and hour.
- On the phone, photos are imported 30 at a time at most.
- The phone accepts only an https:// server address.
- The pages of a document can be changed only on the device that scanned it. Elsewhere it can be read, searched, renamed, moved, shared and deleted.
- On the phone there is no export other than PDF, JPEG pictures and text, and the phone does not take in PDFs. The computer takes in pictures and PDFs.
- The capture screen of the phone works upright only.
- The computer version has no crop and no blur check. According to its own documentation it has been tried on one real scanner, on Linux; the Windows and macOS versions pass the same tests but had not yet been tried with a real scanner.

## Messages and problems

### On the phone

| Message | Meaning, and what to do |
|---|---|
| **This screen needs the camera. Touch to allow it.** | The camera permission is missing. Touch the message. |
| **the camera did not answer — try again** | The photo failed. Take it again; leave the capture and come back if it persists. |
| **hold still…** | The app waits for the phone to stop moving before taking the photo. |
| **page 2 is blurred** / **blurred — ⋯ to take it again** | The page seems blurred. **retake**, or **keep**. |
| **this photo could not be read** | The picture could not be used. Delete the page and take it again. |
| **no page found in this photo — place the corners by hand** | The app did not find the sheet. Drag the corners yourself, or use **whole photo**. |
| **working…** (in place of **save**) | Pages are still being prepared. Wait a moment. |
| **text to be read** | The document waits for its text to be read. It is taken up again at the next start if the app was closed. |
| **text could not be read** | Reading failed. Try **read the text again**. The PDF exists, without searchable text. |
| **The text has not been read yet.** / **No text was found on these pages.** | Shown in the **text** view. |
| **some text is not read yet** | In the share menu: the text shared may be incomplete. |
| **Google scanner unavailable — the app's own camera instead: …** | Google's scanner could not start; the app uses its own camera. |
| **Not available on this phone: Google Play services are missing.** | ML Kit cannot be chosen on this phone. |
| **download failed — touch to try again** | A "best" model could not be downloaded. Check the connection and touch the line. |
| **sending…**, **up to date at 11:32** | The sync is running; the sync succeeded. |
| **not sent: wrong username or password** | The server refused the login. Check **username** and **password** (kDrive with two-factor authentication: an application password). |
| **not sent: no connection to the server** | No network, or a wrong server address. |
| **not sent: this phone cannot talk to the server** | This phone cannot send the requests WebDAV needs. |
| **not sent:** followed by something else (for example HTTP and a number) | The server's own answer. Check the address and the folder. |
| **scanned elsewhere** | The document was scanned on another device. |
| **the PDF is not on the server (any more)** | The PDF of a document scanned elsewhere was moved or deleted there. The next sync sorts it out. |
| **scanned elsewhere: its PDF needs the WebDAV folder** | No server is set, so the PDF cannot be downloaded. |
| **not a Reader's credentials file** | The file chosen is not a credentials file. |
| **this file holds nothing for Reader's Scanner** | The file has no section this app can use. |

### On the computer

| Message | Meaning, and what to do |
|---|---|
| **no scanner found** (with **look again**, **get NAPS2**, **from files…**) | No scanner answers by itself and NAPS2 is not installed. The text says: **Most scanners made since 2015 (AirScan, Mopria) are found by themselves, on the network or by USB: is yours switched on? The others need NAPS2, a free program installed separately, from naps2.com. Pictures and PDFs can be brought in from files meanwhile.** |
| **no scanner found — is it switched on?** / **the scanner is not answering — is it switched on?** | **Switch the scanner on and check its cable; then scan again.** |
| **the feeder is empty** | **Put the pages in the feeder, or choose « glass ».** |
| **this scanner has no feeder** / **this scanner cannot scan both sides** | Choose another source under **from**. |
| **the scanner is busy** / **the scanner is warming up — try again in a moment** | Wait and scan again. While it waits by itself the app says **the scanner is getting ready…**. |
| **the scanner's cover is open** / **paper jam in the scanner** / **two sheets went in together** | See to the scanner, then **scan again**. |
| **the connection to the scanner was interrupted** | Check the cable or the network, then scan again. |
| **SANE is not installed (the scanner drivers)** | On Linux, NAPS2 needs SANE to reach this scanner. |
| **scan cancelled** / **the scan did not work** | The scan was stopped, or failed without a known reason. |
| **nothing could be read in these files** | The files brought in hold no usable picture. |
| **poppler-utils is needed to read a PDF** | On Linux, install poppler-utils: it is needed to bring in PDFs, and to show the pages of documents scanned elsewhere. |
| **Tesseract is not installed: the pages are kept without their text** | Install Tesseract (Linux); the PDF is made without searchable text. |
| **no reading model for français — it could not be downloaded** | The language's model is missing and could not be fetched. Check the connection, then **read the text again in**. |
| **cannot reach the server** / **wrong username or password** / **not a WebDAV folder at this address** | Shown on the sync line. Check the settings. |
| **its PDF needs the WebDAV folder** / **the PDF is not on the server (any more)** | As on the phone, for a document scanned elsewhere. |
| **an error — see errors.log** | An unexpected error was written to `errors.log` in the documents folder; the app goes on. |

After a failed scan the right side offers **scan again**, **back to the pages** (if pages are waiting) and **from files…**.


# Reader's Feeds

*Android 1.2.1*

## What it is

Reader's Feeds is a reader for RSS and Atom feeds: you give it the addresses of the sites you follow, and it shows their latest articles as one plain list, title in black, source and age in grey. An article is read inside the app as text only, cut into pages that end on a whole line, so you turn pages instead of scrolling; this is made for e-ink screens. It also reads paid Substack publications once you have signed in.

Everything is two colours, without icons, images or animation. There is no account, no server of its own and no synchronisation: the app talks only to the sites you follow.

It deliberately does not show pictures, does not mark articles as read or unread, does not notify you of new articles and does not fetch anything while it is closed.

The app is written in six languages (English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese, Russian) and follows the language of the phone; any other language gives English.

## First start

The app asks for no permission. It only uses the internet connection.

There is no account to create and nothing to enter. One source is already in the list and enabled: **Wikipedia — Featured Articles**. On opening, the title line shows **fetching…** while the app fetches the feeds, then the list of articles appears.

If nothing has been fetched yet, the title line reads **feeds** and the page shows one of these lines:

- **nothing yet. Pull down to refresh.** when sources are enabled but no article has arrived;
- **no source enabled. ⋯ › sources.** when every source is disabled or the list of sources is empty.

To add your own sources, tap **⋯** at the top right, then **sources**.

## The screens

Every screen has a grey title line at the top. On the left, **←** goes back to the previous screen. On the right, where there is one, **⋯** opens the menu of the screen. Menus open from the bottom of the screen as a list of words; tap outside a menu to close it. Short messages from the app appear for three seconds in a band at the bottom.

### The list of articles (home)

This is the screen the app opens on. It gathers the latest articles of all enabled sources.

The title line shows the count, for example **12 articles · 3 sources**; **· …** is added at the end while a refresh is running. Tapping the title line refreshes the list.

Each article takes one row: the title (two lines at most) and, under it in grey, the name of the source, the age of the article and the word **saved** if you have saved it. The age reads **now**, then minutes (**5 min**), hours (**3 h**), days (**2 d**) and, after seven days, the date as day.month.year.

- Tap an article to read it.
- Long-press an article to open its menu: **read**, **save for later** (or **forget** if it is already saved), **open in the browser**, **share**.
- Pull the list down to refresh it (when the lists are set to **scrolling** and the list is not empty).

When the lists are set to **pages** (see Settings), the list does not scroll. Each page holds as many rows as fit; the bottom line shows **‹**, the page number such as **1 / 4**, and **›**. Tap **›** or swipe up for the next page, tap **‹** or swipe down for the previous one. In this mode there is no pull-down gesture: refresh by tapping the title line or with **⋯** › **refresh**.

The **⋯** menu holds:

- **sources**: opens the list of sources.
- **saved for later**: opens the saved articles.
- **refresh**: fetches all enabled sources again.
- **order: latest first** or **order: one per source**: the entry shows the order in use; tap it to switch to the other one.
- **white on black** or **black on white**: switches the colours of the whole app to the one named.
- **settings**: opens the settings.

The two orders:

- **order: latest first**: the most recent article first, all sources mixed. A source never takes more than two rows in a row: its third consecutive article is pushed a little further down.
- **order: one per source**: the most recent article of each source, then the second of each source, and so on. The sources come in the order of their most recent article.

The app always starts in **order: latest first**.

The list is refreshed when the app opens, and when you come back to the app after more than five minutes. At most 20 articles are taken from each source. An article that appears in two sources is shown once.

### The article (reader)

The title line shows the name of the source. While the text is being prepared the page reads **fetching the text…**.

The first page starts with the title of the article and a thin line; the text follows. The bottom line shows the page number, for example **3 / 12**, with **‹** and **›** when there is a page before or after.

- Tap the right half of the screen for the next page, the left half for the previous page.
- Swipe to the left for the next page, to the right for the previous page.

The text comes from the feed when the feed carries the full article; otherwise the app loads the article's web page and keeps its text. Pictures, videos, captions, menus and comments are left out.

If the text cannot be obtained, the page shows **the text could not be extracted** and, under it, **open in the browser**, which opens the article in your browser.

The **⋯** menu, headed by the title of the article, holds:

- **save for later** (or **forget** if it is already saved);
- **open in the browser**: opens the article's web page in your browser;
- **share**: hands the title and the address of the article to another app;
- **larger text** and **smaller text**: one step at a time; the pages are cut again and you stay at about the same place;
- **serif** or **sans-serif**: switches to the font named;
- **white on black** or **black on white**: switches to the colours named.

### Saved for later

Opened with **⋯** › **saved for later** from the home list. It shows the articles you saved, the most recently saved first. Under each title: the source, how long ago you saved it, and **saved**.

Tap to read, long-press for the same menu as in the home list (**read**, **forget**, **open in the browser**, **share**). **forget** removes the article from this list.

A saved article stays 90 days, then leaves the list by itself. When the list is empty it reads **nothing saved. Long-press an article to keep it here for 90 days.**

### Sources

Opened with **⋯** › **sources** from the home list. Each source takes one row:

- a box on the left: **■** enabled, **□** disabled. Tap the box to enable or disable the source. A disabled source is written in grey and is no longer fetched;
- the name of the source. Tap it to see the articles of this source alone;
- under the name, in grey: **signed in** for a paid Substack that holds a sign-in, **no answer** if the source is enabled but gave no article at the last refresh, then the site of the feed.

Long-press a source for its menu, headed by its name:

- **disable** or **enable**;
- **articles**: the articles of this source alone;
- **sign in again**: only for a source that is signed in; opens the Substack sign-in to replace the sign-in kept;
- **forget the sign-in**: only for a source that is signed in; erases the sign-in and keeps the source;
- **remove**: deletes the source from the list at once, without asking.

At the bottom of the screen, **+ add a feed** adds a source.

The **⋯** menu holds:

- **add a feed**
- **add a paid Substack**
- **import OPML**
- **export OPML**
- **import Substacks (CSV)**
- **export Substacks (CSV)**

When the list is empty it reads **no source yet. ⋯ › add a feed, or import an OPML file.**

Sources stay in the order in which they were added.

### The articles of one source

Opened by tapping the name of a source, or with **articles** in its menu. The title line shows the name of the source, followed by **· …** while it is being fetched. The list works like the home list, without the name of the source under each title. It also works for a disabled source.

If the feed returns nothing, the page reads **nothing came back from this feed.**

### Substack sign-in

Opened by **add a paid Substack** and by **sign in again**. The screen shows the Substack sign-in web page inside the app, under the title **Substack sign-in**. Sign in there as you would in a browser.

If the app can pick up the sign-in from the page, the screen closes by itself. If it does not, tap **paste** at the top right: a box opens with the text **if signing in here does not work: in a computer's browser, signed in to Substack, open DevTools › Application › Cookies › substack.sid and paste its value here.** Paste the value and tap **save**.

**←** leaves the screen without signing in; nothing is added or changed.

### Settings

Opened with **⋯** › **settings** from the home list. See the Settings section below for each row.

## How to

### Read an article

1. In the list, tap the title.
2. Tap the right half of the screen to go forward, the left half to go back.
3. Tap **←** to return to the list.

### Refresh the list

Use any of these: tap the title line of the home list; open **⋯** and tap **refresh**; or pull the list down (lists set to **scrolling**, list not empty).

### Change the order of the list

1. On the home list, tap **⋯**.
2. Tap **order: latest first** to switch to one article per source in turn, or **order: one per source** to return to the most recent first.

### Save an article for later

1. Long-press the article in a list, or open it and tap **⋯**.
2. Tap **save for later**.

The word **saved** appears under its title. To find it again: home list, **⋯** › **saved for later**.

### Take an article out of the saved ones

1. Long-press the article (in any list), or open it and tap **⋯**.
2. Tap **forget**.

### Open an article in the browser

Long-press the article, or open it and tap **⋯**, then tap **open in the browser**.

### Share an article

1. Long-press the article, or open it and tap **⋯**.
2. Tap **share**.
3. Choose the app to send it with. The title and the address of the article are sent.

### Add a feed

1. Home list, **⋯** › **sources**.
2. Tap **+ add a feed** at the bottom (or **⋯** › **add a feed**).
3. Under **address of the feed (or of the site)**, type the full address of the feed, starting with `https://`, and tap **ok**.
4. Under **name**, keep the name proposed (the site's name) or type your own, and tap **ok**.
5. Go back to the home list and refresh it to see the articles.

The address must be that of the RSS or Atom feed itself. Only for an address at substack.com does the app complete the address of the site into the address of its feed. The app does not check the address when you add it: if it is wrong, the source will show **no answer** after the next refresh.

### Follow a free Substack publication

Add it as a feed (see above) with the address of the publication, for example `https://example.substack.com`. The app adds the feed's ending itself.

### Add a paid Substack

1. Home list, **⋯** › **sources**, then **⋯** › **add a paid Substack**.
2. Under **address of the publication**, type the address, for example `https://example.substack.com`, without a slash at the end, and tap **ok**.
3. On the **Substack sign-in** screen, sign in to your Substack account.
4. If the screen does not close by itself once you are signed in, tap **paste**, paste the value described in the box and tap **save**.

The publication appears in the sources with **signed in** under its name. Its name is taken from its address.

### Sign in again to a Substack, or forget the sign-in

1. In **sources**, long-press the publication.
2. Tap **sign in again** to sign in anew, or **forget the sign-in** to erase the sign-in from the phone.

### Disable or enable a source

In **sources**, tap the box in front of its name (**■** enabled, **□** disabled), or long-press the source and tap **disable** or **enable**. Refresh the home list afterwards.

### See the articles of one source only

In **sources**, tap the name of the source.

### Remove a source

1. In **sources**, long-press the source.
2. Tap **remove**. The source is deleted at once.

### Move your list of sources to another device

On the first device:

1. **sources**, **⋯** › **export OPML**.
2. Choose where to send the file `readers_feeds.opml` (a message to yourself, a cloud folder, a file manager).

On the other device:

1. Get the file onto the device.
2. **sources**, **⋯** › **import OPML**, and pick the file.

The app answers with the number of sources, for example **12 sources added**. A source whose name is already in your list is not added a second time. Imported sources are enabled.

The same **import OPML** takes an OPML file exported from another feed reader.

### Move your Substack sign-ins to another device

Two ways lead to the same result.

With the credentials file:

1. On the first device: **settings** › **export credentials**, and send the file `readers-credentials-feeds.json` to yourself.
2. On the other device: **settings** › **import credentials**, and pick the file. The app answers, for example, **2 Substack sign-ins imported**.

A publication already in the list receives the sign-in from the file; one that is missing is added.

With the CSV:

1. On the first device: **sources**, **⋯** › **export Substacks (CSV)**, and send the file `readers_feeds_substacks.csv` to yourself.
2. On the other device: **sources**, **⋯** › **import Substacks (CSV)**, and pick the file. The app answers, for example, **2 Substacks added**.

The CSV only adds publications that are missing; it does not change the sign-in of one already in the list.

Both files contain your sign-ins: anyone who has the file can read your paid publications. Keep them private and delete them once used.

### Change the size of the text

- While reading: **⋯** › **larger text** or **smaller text**.
- In **settings**: tap the row showing the size to enlarge it, long-press it to reduce it. **back to the automatic size** returns to the size chosen from the screen.

### Change the font

While reading: **⋯** › **serif** or **sans-serif**. Or in **settings**, tap the row **reading font**.

### Switch to white on black

On the home list or while reading: **⋯** › **white on black**. To come back: **⋯** › **black on white**. Or in **settings**, tap the row **colours**.

### Make the lists turn pages instead of scrolling

In **settings**, tap the row **scrolling**: it becomes **pages**.

### Put the latest titles on the home screen

1. Long-press an empty spot of your phone's home screen and open the widgets.
2. Find **Reader's Feeds** (described as **Latest articles from your RSS feeds**) and place it.

## Settings

Each row shows the value in use, with its name in grey under it. Tap a row to change it.

| Row (grey line under it) | What it changes | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| **colours** | The colours of the whole app | **black on white**, **white on black** | **black on white** |
| **text size — tap for larger, long-press for smaller** | The size of the reading text; titles, menus and small lines follow it. Tap for one step larger, long-press for one step smaller | 13 to 30 | automatic, shown as the size followed by **· auto** |
| **back to the automatic size** (**from the screen size**) | Drops your own size and returns to the automatic one | — | — |
| **reading font** | The font of the text, in the reader and in the rest of the app | **sans-serif**, **serif** | **sans-serif** |
| **lists — pages suit e-ink screens** | Whether the lists of articles scroll or turn pages (home list, articles of one source, saved articles) | **scrolling**, **pages** | **scrolling** |
| **export credentials** (**a file to set up another device — it holds your Substack sign-ins**) | Creates the credentials file and opens the share sheet | — | — |
| **import credentials** (**Substack sign-ins from a credentials file**) | Lets you pick a credentials file and takes its Substack sign-ins | — | — |

The automatic size depends on the width of the screen: between 17 and 24, larger on a larger screen.

The last two rows are information: **Reader's Feeds**, **a black-and-white RSS reader, born from Pluralis. GPL-3.0.** and **Pierre Gallaz · developed with Claude Code**.

The order of the home list (**order: latest first** / **order: one per source**) is not a setting: it is chosen in the menu of the home list and returns to **order: latest first** each time the app starts.

## Your data

Everything stays on the phone: the list of sources and whether each is enabled, your Substack sign-ins, the saved articles (their title, address and source, not their text) and the settings.

The articles themselves are not kept: the list is fetched anew each time the app opens, and the text of an article is fetched when you open it. Without a connection there is nothing to read.

What goes over the network:

- at each refresh, a request to the feed address of every enabled source;
- when you open an article whose text is not carried by the feed, a request to the article's web page;
- for a paid Substack, your sign-in is sent with the request to that publication's feed, and to nothing else;
- the Substack sign-in screen loads Substack's own sign-in page.

Nothing is sent to the author of the app or to any other service. There is no synchronisation between devices, so there are no conflicts; a list of sources travels only by the files you export and import yourself.

Export and import:

- **export OPML** creates `readers_feeds.opml` with all your sources, enabled or not (name, feed address, site address). It holds no sign-in.
- **export Substacks (CSV)** creates `readers_feeds_substacks.csv` with the signed-in publications: name, address, feed address and sign-in.
- **export credentials** creates `readers-credentials-feeds.json`, the Reader's credentials file, with the same signed-in publications and their sign-ins. When importing, the app only reads its own part of such a file, so a credentials file that also holds the accounts of other Reader's apps can be used.

Exported files are handed to the Android share sheet; the app does not write them to a folder of its own choosing. The copy of the credentials file that the app prepares for sharing is erased the next time the app starts or exports.

Saved articles are not part of any export.

Uninstalling the app removes everything: sources, sign-ins, saved articles and settings.

## With the other apps

### Home-screen widget

The app offers one widget, **Latest articles from your RSS feeds**. It can be resized in both directions.

- The top line shows **feeds** and **open ›**: tap it to open the app.
- Under it, the latest titles (ten at most), with the source under each. The list scrolls.
- Tap a title to open the article in your browser.
- When there is nothing to show, it reads **nothing yet**.

The widget shows what the app last fetched, in the **latest first** order: it is updated each time the app refreshes its list, and does not fetch anything by itself. It is always black on white, whatever the colours chosen in the app.

### Sharing

**share** sends the title and the address of an article to any app that accepts text. **open in the browser** hands the address to your browser. The export entries hand a file to the app you choose.

The app does not appear in the share sheet of other apps and does not open links or files sent from other apps; OPML, CSV and credentials files are picked from inside the app.

## Limits

- Text only: no pictures, no videos, no links you can tap inside an article.
- No mark for read or unread articles, no search, no folders or categories for sources.
- No notification and no fetching in the background; the list is refreshed when the app is opened or when you ask.
- Nothing is kept for reading without a connection, not even the saved articles: saving keeps the title and the address, and the text is fetched again when you open it.
- A saved article is fetched from its web page without your Substack sign-in.
- At most 20 articles per source.
- When the text comes from the feed, it is cut at 50,000 characters and ends with **…**.
- A source that takes more than 10 seconds to answer is skipped for that refresh; an article page that takes more than 15 seconds gives **the text could not be extracted**.
- **add a feed** needs the address of the feed itself; it does not look for the feed on a site's page, except for addresses at substack.com.
- A source cannot be renamed or moved in the list; remove it and add it again.
- **remove** has no confirmation and no undo.
- Paid publications are supported for Substack only.
- The text size goes from 13 to 30.
- No version for the computer.

## Messages and problems

| Message | Meaning and what to do |
|---|---|
| **fetching…** | The first refresh is running. Wait. |
| **nothing yet. Pull down to refresh.** | No article has arrived. Tap the title line or **⋯** › **refresh**; check your connection and the sources. |
| **no source enabled. ⋯ › sources.** | Every source is disabled, or there is none. Open **⋯** › **sources** and enable or add one. |
| **nothing could be fetched. Pull down to try again, or check the sources.** | The refresh failed. Check the connection, then tap the title line or **⋯** › **refresh**. |
| **no answer** (under a source) | The source is enabled but gave no article at the last refresh: wrong address, site out of reach, or a feed that is not RSS or Atom. It also shows on a source just added or enabled until the next refresh. |
| **signed in** (under a source) | A Substack sign-in is kept for this source. |
| **nothing came back from this feed.** | The feed of this source returned no article. Check its address; for a paid Substack, try **sign in again**. |
| **no source yet. ⋯ › add a feed, or import an OPML file.** | The list of sources is empty. |
| **fetching the text…** | The article is being loaded. |
| **the text could not be extracted** | The web page did not answer, or no article text was found in it. Tap **open in the browser** under the message. |
| **nothing saved. Long-press an article to keep it here for 90 days.** | You have no saved article. |
| **12 sources added** | The OPML file held this many sources. Those whose name was already in your list were not added again. |
| **no source in this file** | The file picked with **import OPML** holds no feed. |
| **2 Substacks added** | The CSV held this many publications. Those already in your list were left as they were. |
| **no Substack in this file** | The file picked with **import Substacks (CSV)** holds no usable line. Each line needs four values: name, address, feed address, sign-in. |
| **no paid Substack to export** | No source is signed in, so **export Substacks (CSV)** and **export credentials** have nothing to write. |
| **2 Substack sign-ins imported** | The credentials file was read and its sign-ins are in place. |
| **not a Reader's credentials file** | The file picked with **import credentials** is not a Reader's credentials file. |
| **this file holds nothing for Reader's Feeds** | The file is a Reader's credentials file but holds no Substack sign-in for this app. |
| **could not read this file** | The file could not be opened or is not in the expected format. |

Other situations:

- A paid Substack no longer gives its articles in full: renew the sign-in. In **sources**, long-press the publication and tap **sign in again**.
- A new source shows no article on the home list: go back to the home list and refresh.
- The home list is empty and pulling down does nothing: tap the title line, or **⋯** › **refresh**.
- An article shows menus or unrelated text, or misses a part: the text is taken from the web page as well as the app can; use **open in the browser** for the original.


# Reader's Detox

*Android 1.7.1*

## What it is

Reader's Detox keeps the social networks out of your phone: YouTube, Instagram, TikTok, Reddit, X and Substack, in their apps as well as in the browser. You choose the sites, optionally the hours of the day when they may open, and you start the blocking.

It works by filtering names. Each time an app wants to reach a site, the phone first looks up the site's name to find its address. Reader's Detox sets itself up as a VPN that stays inside the phone and receives only these name lookups. For a chosen site it answers "no address", so the site cannot load. Every other lookup is passed on untouched, and the rest of your traffic (pages, videos, messages) never goes through the app at all.

It is a detox you impose on yourself, not a parental lock: there is no password and no account, and you can lift the blocking whenever you want. What it adds is friction: the sites, the hours and the maths switch are locked while blocking is on, and you can ask the app to make you solve five maths problems before it stops.

The app is black and white, text only, and speaks English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese and Russian (it follows the phone's language).

## First start

The app opens directly on its main screen. Nothing is blocked yet: the top of the screen reads **not blocking**, **every site opens normally**. All six sites are already marked **blocked**, there are no allowed hours (**none — blocked all day**) and the maths problems are **off**.

No account, no server, nothing to type.

The app asks for the following, in this order:

1. **Notifications** (Android 13 and later), when the app opens. While blocking is on, the app must show a permanent notification, **Reader's Detox is blocking**; this permission lets it appear.
2. **The VPN connection request**, the first time you tap **start blocking**. This is Android's own dialog. It must be accepted: the VPN is the mechanism that lets the app receive the phone's name lookups. If you refuse, nothing starts. Android asks only once.
3. **Running in the background**, right after blocking starts. Android asks whether the app may ignore battery optimisation, so that the system never stops the blocking to save power. The request comes back at each start as long as you have not allowed it.
4. **The always-on VPN advice**, once, after the first start. A sheet reads: **For the strongest protection, make Reader's Detox the always-on VPN: Settings › Network › VPN › Reader's Detox › Always-on VPN.** Tap **open VPN settings** to go to Android's VPN settings, or **later** to close the sheet. You can find the same shortcut afterwards in **settings**.

## The screens

### Main screen (detox)

The title **detox** is at the top left, the **⋯** menu at the top right.

**The state.** A large line reads **blocking** or **not blocking**. While blocking, this block is shown in inverted colours. The small line under it gives the detail:

| Detail | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **every site opens normally** | Blocking is off. |
| **YouTube, Instagram… blocked all day** | Blocking is on, no allowed hours: the named sites never open. |
| **YouTube, Instagram… blocked outside 6–8, 18–22** | Blocking is on and you are outside the allowed hours: the sites are blocked now. |
