| **inside the allowed hours: open now, blocked again at 8:00** | Blocking is on, but the clock is inside one of your allowed hours: the sites open until the hour shown. |
| **no site chosen: nothing to block** | Blocking is on, but every site is set to **open**. |

**The sites.** Under the heading **sites**, six lines: **YouTube**, **Instagram**, **TikTok**, **Reddit**, **X**, **Substack**. Each shows **blocked** or **open** on the right. Tap a line to switch it. While blocking, the heading becomes **locked while blocking, except Substack**: the first five lines are dimmed and no longer react; only **Substack** can still be switched, and the change applies at once.

**The allowed hours.** A line shows your ranges in short form, for instance **6–8, 18–22**, or **none — blocked all day**, with the caption **allowed hours**. Tap it to open the *allowed hours* screen. While blocking, the caption reads **allowed hours · locked while blocking** and the line no longer reacts.

**The maths problems.** A line shows **on** or **off** with the caption **5 maths problems before stopping**. Tap it to switch. While blocking, the caption reads **5 maths problems before stopping · locked while blocking** and the line no longer reacts, so you cannot remove the obstacle once it is in place.

**The action line**, at the very bottom, starts or stops the blocking. It reads:

- **start blocking** when blocking is off;
- **stop blocking** when blocking is on and the maths problems are off: one tap stops it;
- **stop blocking — 5 problems first** when blocking is on and the maths problems are on: one tap opens the problems screen.

**The ⋯ menu** opens a sheet at the bottom of the screen. Tap outside it, or press back, to close it. Its entries:

- the same action as the bottom line (**start blocking**, **stop blocking** or **stop blocking — 5 problems first**);
- **allowed hours** (only when blocking is off): opens the *allowed hours* screen;
- **black on white** or **white on black**: switches to the colours named;
- **settings**: opens the settings screen.

### Allowed hours

The title **← allowed hours** is at the top; tap it (or press back) to return.

A note explains the rule: **The chosen sites open during these hours and are blocked the rest of the day. With no hours, they are blocked all day. A range ending earlier than it starts runs past midnight.**

Below it, one line per range, for instance **6:00 – 8:00**. A range that crosses midnight, such as **22:00 – 6:00**, carries the mention **over midnight**. With no range, the screen reads **none — blocked all day**.

- Tap a range to open a small menu with **change** and **delete**.
- Tap **+ allowed hours**, at the bottom, to add a range.

Adding or changing a range asks two questions, one after the other, each with a number keyboard and the buttons **cancel** and **ok**: **open from which hour? (0–23)**, then **from 6:00 until which hour? (0–23)** (with the hour you just gave). When you change a range, the old hour is already written and selected: type to replace it, or just confirm.

Ranges are in whole hours, from 0 to 23. The sites open from the first hour and are blocked again from the second: **6:00 – 8:00** means open from 6:00 until 8:00. The list is kept sorted by starting hour, and a range entered twice is kept once.

If blocking is on while this screen is displayed, the ranges are dimmed and **locked while blocking** replaces **+ allowed hours**.

### Stop blocking (the maths problems)

This screen appears when you ask to stop while **5 maths problems before stopping** is **on**. The title is **← stop blocking**.

It shows:

- the explanation **Five problems, each harder than the last. Solve all five to stop blocking.**;
- where you are, for instance **question 1 of 5 · easy**, and a progress line;
- the problem;
- the answer field, marked **your answer**, with a number keyboard;
- **±**, to the right of the field: tap it to put or remove a minus sign in front of your answer (some answers are negative, and not every number keyboard has a minus key);
- **check**, at the bottom, to submit your answer (the keyboard's confirm key does the same).

The five levels are **easy**, **medium**, **hard**, **very hard** and **expert**. The problems are drawn at random each time:

| Level | Kind of problem |
|---|---|
| **easy** | An addition and a multiplication, such as 47 + 23 × 6 = ? |
| **medium** | A percentage (**What is 35% of 240?**) or a product minus a square |
| **hard** | A power minus a number, or an equation (**Solve for x: 7x + 12 = 61**) |
| **very hard** | The larger root of a quadratic equation, a square root plus a number, or two equations (**If x + y = 30 and x − y = 8, what is x?**) |
| **expert** | The sum of an arithmetic series, a remainder (**What is 9³ mod 11?**), a count of arrangements, or the sum of both roots of a quadratic equation |

After a right answer the screen shows **correct** and moves to the next problem. After the fifth, blocking stops and you return to the main screen.

After a wrong answer the screen shows **wrong answer, try again** and empties the field; you may try as often as you like. From the second wrong answer on the same problem, a line **hint: …** appears with a pointer to the method.

You can type a decimal with a point or a comma.

Leaving this screen (tap the title or press back) gives up: blocking stays on, and next time you start again from question 1 with new problems.

### Settings

Opened from **⋯** then **settings**. The title **← settings** takes you back. Each line shows the current value in large type and its name under it; a tap changes it. See the *Settings* section for the details.

At the bottom, two lines of information: **Reader's Detox**, **blocks social networks on this phone**, and **Pierre Gallaz · developed with Claude Code**. They do nothing when tapped.

### The notification

While blocking is on, a permanent notification reads **Reader's Detox is blocking**, **social networks blocked**. It stays during the allowed hours too, because the app keeps running then and simply lets the sites through. Tap it to open the app. It cannot be swiped away; it disappears when blocking stops. In Android's notification settings its category is called **blocking**.

## How to

### Start blocking

1. On the main screen, check under **sites** that each site you want out reads **blocked**; tap a line to switch it.
2. Tap **start blocking** at the bottom.
3. The first time, accept Android's VPN connection request.
4. If Android asks whether the app may run in the background, allow it.

The top of the screen turns to **blocking**, in inverted colours, and the notification **Reader's Detox is blocking** appears.

### Choose which sites are blocked

1. Blocking must be off (stop it first if needed).
2. Under **sites**, tap a site to switch it between **blocked** and **open**.

Substack is the exception: you can switch it at any time, even while blocking.

### Let the sites open at certain hours

1. Blocking must be off.
2. Tap the **allowed hours** line (or **⋯**, then **allowed hours**).
3. Tap **+ allowed hours**.
4. To **open from which hour? (0–23)**, type the starting hour, for instance 18, then **ok**.
5. To **from 18:00 until which hour? (0–23)**, type the ending hour, for instance 22, then **ok**.
6. Repeat for another range if you want several in the day.
7. Go back and tap **start blocking**.

The sites then open from 18:00 to 22:00 and are blocked the rest of the day. Blocking stays "on" all the time; the app lets the sites through during the allowed hours by itself.

### Set a range that crosses midnight

Give an ending hour smaller than the starting hour: from 22 until 6 gives **22:00 – 6:00**, marked **over midnight**. The sites open from 22:00 in the evening until 6:00 in the morning.

### Change or delete a range

1. Blocking must be off.
2. Open **allowed hours**.
3. Tap the range.
4. Tap **change** and answer the two questions again, or tap **delete**.

### Block all day

Delete every range in **allowed hours**. The line reads **none — blocked all day**.

### Make stopping harder

1. Blocking must be off.
2. On the main screen, tap the line captioned **5 maths problems before stopping** so that it reads **on**.
3. Tap **start blocking**.

From now on the bottom line reads **stop blocking — 5 problems first**, and the switch itself is locked until blocking has stopped.

### Stop blocking

Without the maths problems: tap **stop blocking**. It stops at once.

With the maths problems:

1. Tap **stop blocking — 5 problems first**.
2. Type the answer to each problem and tap **check**.
3. After the fifth right answer, blocking stops.

### Change the sites, the hours or the maths switch while blocking

You cannot, apart from Substack. Stop blocking first, make your change, then start again.

### Keep the blocking alive after a restart of the phone

The app starts the blocking again by itself after the phone restarts and after an update of the app, if blocking was on. For the firmest hold:

1. Open **⋯**, then **settings**.
2. Tap **always-on VPN**: Android's VPN settings open. Choose Reader's Detox there and turn on Android's always-on VPN option.
3. Back in **settings**, tap **allow running in the background** and accept. The line then reads **runs in the background: allowed**.

### Switch between black on white and white on black

Open **⋯** and tap **black on white** or **white on black** (the menu names the colours you will get). The same switch is in **settings**, on the **colours** line.

### Start or stop from the home screen

Add the Reader's Detox widget to your home screen (see *With the other apps*) and tap its switch.

## Settings

Open **⋯**, then **settings**.

| Setting | What it does | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| **always-on VPN** (**so the blocking survives reboots**) | Opens Android's VPN settings, where you can make Reader's Detox the always-on VPN. | — | — |
| **allow running in the background** (**so Android never stops the blocking**) | Asks Android to exempt the app from battery optimisation. Once granted, the line reads **runs in the background: allowed**. | — | not allowed |
| **colours** | The colours of the app and of its widget. | **white on black**, **black on white** | **white on black** |
| **text size** | The size of the text in the app. Each tap moves to the next size. | **small**, **medium**, **large** | **medium** |
| **font** | The typeface of the app. Each tap moves to the next one. | **sans-serif**, **serif**, **mono** | **sans-serif** |
| **haptic tick** | A short vibration when you switch a site, the maths problems, or start and stop. | **on**, **off** | **on** |

The settings that decide what is blocked are on the main screen:

| Setting | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **YouTube**, **Instagram**, **TikTok**, **Reddit**, **X**, **Substack** | **blocked**, **open** | **blocked** for all six |
| **allowed hours** | any number of ranges in whole hours | **none — blocked all day** |
| **5 maths problems before stopping** | **on**, **off** | **off** |

## Your data

**On the phone only.** The app stores your choices (sites, allowed hours, maths switch, whether blocking is on, colours, text size, font, haptic tick) in its private storage on the phone. There is no account and no synchronisation. The app keeps no history of the sites you visit or of the names looked up.

**What goes over the network.** Reader's Detox has no server of its own and sends nothing to its author. Here is exactly what it does while blocking is on:

- Only the phone's name lookups reach the app. Everything else (the pages, the videos, your messages) leaves the phone the usual way, without passing through the app.
- A lookup for a blocked site, outside the allowed hours, is answered by the app itself, on the phone. It goes nowhere.
- Every other lookup is passed on, unchanged, to the name servers of the network you are connected to (your Wi-Fi or your mobile operator), the same ones the phone would use without the app.
- If those servers do not answer, the app tries public name servers instead: Google's (8.8.8.8) and Cloudflare's (1.1.1.1), and their IPv6 equivalents. In that case, and only then, these two companies receive the names being looked up. The app then goes on using the server that answered until it fails.

**Mobile networks.** The app also works on mobile networks that use IPv6 only.

**Backup, export, import.** There is none. The app's data is excluded from Android's backups. On a new phone you set your sites and hours again.

**Uninstalling** removes everything, and of course ends the blocking.

## With the other apps

### Home-screen widget

The app offers one widget, described in the widget list as **blocking on or off; stopping asks the maths problems when they are on**. It can be resized.

On the left it shows the state in words:

| First line | Second line |
|---|---|
| **not blocking** | **every site opens normally** |
| **blocking** | **blocked all day** (no allowed hours) |
| **blocking** | **allowed hours: open until 8:00** (inside a range) |
| **blocking** | **blocked until 18:00** (outside the ranges; the hour is when the next range opens) |

On the right, a switch reads **on** (filled) or **off** (outlined).

- Tap the text to open the app.
- Tap the switch when it reads **off**: blocking starts at once. If Android's VPN connection request has not been accepted yet, the app opens on that request instead.
- Tap the switch when it reads **on**: blocking stops at once, or, if **5 maths problems before stopping** is on, the app opens on the problems. The widget is no shortcut around them.

The widget follows the **colours** setting of the app. Its text is refreshed at every change of state and every hour while allowed hours are set.

### Other apps

Reader's Detox has no link with the other Reader's apps, receives nothing shared from other apps and opens no files. It can be installed beside the original SocialBlocker app.

## Limits

- **Six sites, no more.** You cannot add a site of your own. The list of names behind each site is fixed in the app.
- **What each site covers.** YouTube: youtube.com, youtu.be, youtube-nocookie.com, YouTube Kids, YouTube Music and the addresses that serve its images and videos. Instagram: instagram.com, ig.me and its image servers. TikTok: tiktok.com, musical.ly and its video servers. Reddit: reddit.com, redd.it and its image servers. X: x.com, twitter.com, their image servers, and t.co (so shortened links from X do not open either). Substack: substack.com and its own servers; a newsletter published under its own address is not covered.
- **Whole hours only.** A range runs from one full hour to another; no minutes, and the same ranges apply every day of the week.
- **One VPN at a time.** Android allows a single active VPN. While Reader's Detox is blocking, you cannot use another VPN; starting another one stops the blocking.
- **Not a lock.** You can always stop it. Turning the VPN off in Android's settings, or starting another VPN, also stops the blocking, without the maths problems. For a child's phone, use the original SocialBlocker, which has a password.
- **Name lookups only.** The app filters the lookups the phone sends to its usual name server. An app that finds addresses by its own means (for instance a browser whose "secure DNS" option is on) is not filtered.
- **No per-day statistics, no history, no timer.** The app does not count the time you spend or the attempts blocked.
- **Android 7.0 or later.**

## Messages and problems

| Message | Meaning and what to do |
|---|---|
| **locked while blocking, except Substack** | Shown above the sites while blocking. Stop blocking to change the other five. |
| **locked while blocking** | Shown beside **allowed hours** and **5 maths problems before stopping**, and at the bottom of the allowed hours screen. Stop blocking to change them. |
| **no site chosen: nothing to block** | Blocking is on but every site is **open**. Stop, set at least one site to **blocked**, start again (Substack can be switched without stopping). |
| **inside the allowed hours: open now, blocked again at 8:00** | Not a fault: the clock is inside one of your allowed hours. |
| **(0–23)** added after the question | The hour you typed was not accepted. Type a whole number from 0 to 23. The ending hour must also differ from the starting hour. |
| **a number, please** | On the problems screen: what you typed is not a number. |
| **wrong answer, try again** | The answer is not right. The field is emptied; try again. A **hint: …** appears from the second miss. |
| **correct** | The answer is right; the next problem follows. |

**A site still opens while the app says blocking.** Check the detail line: you may be inside the allowed hours, or the site may be set to **open**. Otherwise, see *Name lookups only* under *Limits*.

**The app says not blocking although you did not stop it.** The VPN was turned off in Android's settings, or another VPN app took its place: both count as stopping. Tap **start blocking** again; Android may ask for the VPN connection request once more.

**Blocking does not come back after a restart.** Open **settings**, tap **allow running in the background**, and follow **always-on VPN** to make Reader's Detox the always-on VPN.

**Nothing happens when you tap start blocking.** Android's VPN connection request was refused. Tap **start blocking** again and accept it.

**No notification appears.** The notification permission was refused. To see the notification, allow notifications for Reader's Detox in Android's settings.

**The answer is negative and the keyboard has no minus key.** Type the number and tap **±**.


# Reader's Files

*Android 1.0.0*

## What it is

Reader's Files is a file manager for Android. It opens on a page of plain words (**Downloads**, **Videos**, **Images**, **Audio**, **Documents**, **Apps**, **Archives**, then your storages with the space left on each) and shows everything in black and white, whatever your wallpaper.

It browses folders in tabs, copies, moves, renames, deletes and compresses files, searches by name or inside text files, lists the installed apps, and has its own viewers for pictures, video, audio, PDF and text. ZIP files are browsed like folders; 7z, rar, tar and many other archives are unpacked into a folder beside them.

It is a modified version of Prism File Explorer, and beyond the home page most screens are still the original Prism ones. It asks for no network access: it never connects to the Internet, has no cloud storage, no FTP, no network shares, and does not check for updates by itself (updates come from F-Droid or Reader's Installer).

The app follows the language of the phone: English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese (Brazil), Russian, Polish, Turkish, Chinese and Persian. A few labels exist only in English; they are written in quotation marks in this chapter.

## First start

At the first start the app shows a screen titled **Storage Access Required** with the text "To manage your files, we need access to your device storage. This allows you to save, organize, and access your content seamlessly." and two buttons:

- **Grant Access**: on Android 11 and later this opens the Android page "All files access" for Reader's Files; switch it on and come back. On Android 8 to 10 the usual Android permission question appears instead.
- **Close**: leaves the app. Nothing works without this access.

If you come back without having given the access, the app says **Storage access permission is required** and stays on that screen.

There is no account and no server to enter. Once the access is given you land on the **Home** tab.

Other permissions the app holds, none of which asks you a question at the first start:

- installing packages: used when you tap **Install** or **Update** on an APK file;
- seeing all installed apps: used by the **Apps** tab;
- adding shortcuts to the home screen: used by **Add to home screen**;
- running in the foreground and notifications: used while a long copy, move, deletion or compression continues in the background. The app does not ask for the notification permission itself; on Android 13 and later, allow notifications for Reader's Files in the Android settings if you want to see this notification.

## The screens

### The top bar and the tabs

At the very top:

- an icon with three lines on the left: it opens the **About** card (see "About and Logs" below), not a menu;
- the title of the current tab (**Home**, **Files**, **Apps**, or the name of a category) and, in a folder, a small line such as "folders: 3, files: 12", with " (2 selected)" when files are selected, or "empty folder";
- the three-dot menu on the right. Its entries depend on the tab:
  - in a folder: **Show hidden files** (with a check box) and **Display mode**;
  - on the home page: **Customize home tab**;
  - always: **Manage startup tabs** and **Preferences**.

Under it, the row of tabs:

- **+** adds a new **Home** tab.
- Tap a tab to show it. Swipe the page left or right to change tab. On the last tab, pulling the page further to the left also adds a new **Home** tab.
- Tap the tab that is already shown to open its menu: **Add to startup tabs** or **Remove from startup tabs**, **Close** (not offered for the first tab), **Close others**, and **Home** (replaces this tab with the home page).
- Hold a tab and drag it to change the order.
- Swipe a tab downwards to close it (the last remaining tab cannot be closed).

The name of a folder tab is the folder's name; long names are shortened.

The Back button, in order: clears a selection; goes up one folder; at the top folder of a storage, replaces the tab with the home page; on the home page of a tab other than the first, closes that tab; on the first tab, leaves the app after a second press (**Press again to exit**). Three settings under **Behavior** change this.

### Home

The home page is a list of words. In its default layout it shows, from top to bottom:

1. The categories: **Downloads** (the Download folder), **Videos**, **Images**, **Audio**, **Documents** (PDF, Word, Excel and PowerPoint files), **Apps** (the installed apps) and **Archives** (zip, rar, tar, gzip and 7z files). Each word opens in the current tab. Videos, images, audio, documents and archives are gathered from the whole storage, whatever folder they are in.
2. The storages: **Internal Storage**, any SD card or USB drive under the name Android gives it, and **Root** (the system folders of the phone). Under each name a caption reads "12 GiB free of 64 GiB" and a thin line shows how full it is.
3. **Pinned Files**, shown only when you have pinned something: tap a file to open it or a folder to enter it; hold a row to reveal a bin on its right, and tap the bin to unpin.
4. **Bookmarks**, shown only when you have bookmarks: opens the list of bookmarked files and folders.
5. **Recycle bin**: opens the folder that holds deleted files.

Two more sections are switched off by default and can be added with **Customize home tab**:

- **Recent Files**: a horizontal row of the files changed in the last 24 hours (25 at most). Tap one to open it; hold it to see it in its folder; **More** at the end opens the full list (last five days, 100 files at most). When there is nothing, the text **No Recent Files** is shown.
- **Jump to path**: opens a small window where you type a path.

### Customize Home Tab

Reached from the three-dot menu of the home page. Each section of the home page is a row with its name, a short description, a handle (two lines) and a switch:

| Section | Description shown | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **Categories** | Quick access categories | on |
| **Storage** | Storage devices and locations | on |
| **Pinned Files** | Quick access important files | on |
| **Bookmarks** | Bookmarked files and folders | on |
| **Recycle bin** | Deleted files | on |
| **Recent Files** | Recently modified files | off |
| **Jump to path** | Quick path navigation | off |

Use the switch to show or hide a section, drag the handle to change the order. The circular-arrow icon at the top right puts the default layout back. The back arrow saves. If you switch everything off, the app keeps **Categories** and **Storage**.

### Customize startup tabs

Reached from **Manage startup tabs** in the three-dot menu. It lists the tabs opened when the app starts (by default a single **Home** tab). Each row shows the kind of tab (**Home**, **Apps** or **Files** with the folder's path), a handle to reorder and a bin to remove it (at least one tab stays). The circular-arrow icon puts back the single **Home** tab. To add a tab to this list, use **Add to startup tabs** in the menu of a tab.

### A folder (the Files tab)

From top to bottom:

**The path bar.** A house icon returns to the folder this tab started from. Then every folder of the path: tap one to go there, hold one to copy its path (message **Path copied to clipboard**). In a category (Images, Videos…) the path bar is replaced by a row of filters: **ALL**, then one per folder that contains such files.

**The list.** Each row shows an icon or a thumbnail (pictures, videos, audio covers, PDF first page, app icons), the name, and a line of details: date, then for a file its size and its extension. A small padlock marks what cannot be read.

- Tap a folder to enter it, tap a file to open it.
- Hold a row to select it. While something is selected, a tap selects or deselects, and holding another row selects everything between the two.
- Hold a row that is already selected to open the options sheet.
- In list view, tapping the icon of a row selects it directly.
- Pull the list down to refresh it.
- The Back button first clears the selection, then goes up one folder.

An empty folder shows **Empty**, and, when hidden files are not shown, **Show hidden files in settings to see all files**. A folder you may not read shows **This folder is inaccessible**.

**The bottom bar.** Without a selection: **Task**, **Search**, **Create**, **Sort**, **Bookmarks**. With a selection: **Task**, **Search**, **Create**, **Select All** (tap again to deselect all), **Options**, and above them a row of five icons: bin (delete), scissors (cut), two sheets (copy), a letter A (rename), an "i" (properties). Swiping up on the bottom bar opens the bookmarks; swiping up on the row of icons opens the options sheet. **Create** is absent where nothing can be created (Root, the categories). In the recycle bin, **Task** is replaced by **Empty** and **Create** is absent.

### The options sheet

Opened by **Options**, or by holding a selected row. At the top, the name of the file (with "and 2 more" when several are selected), then a row of icons: bin (delete), scissors (cut), two sheets (copy), letter A (rename), share (only when the selection contains no folder), "i" (properties). Then, depending on what is selected:

- **Open in new tab**: one folder.
- **Open with**: one file; choose the app.
- **Add to bookmarks**, or **Remove from bookmarks** when you are in the bookmarks list.
- **Pin to home tab**. **Unpin from home tab** is offered instead only when your selection is exactly the whole set of pinned files; the simple way to unpin is on the home page.
- **Add to home screen**: one file or one folder, in a real folder (not in a category); asks your launcher to add a shortcut.
- **Edit with text editor**: one file.
- **Convert to APK**: one file ending in .apks, .xapk or .apkm.
- **Compress**: not offered inside a ZIP file.
- **Remove default opening method**: one file; forgets the app you chose as default for this kind of file.

### Tasks

Copying and moving work with tasks. **Cut** or **Copy** does not act at once: it adds a task to a list and says **New task has been added**. You then go to the destination folder, tap **Task**, and tap the task to run it there.

The **Tasks** sheet groups them under:

- **Running tasks**: tap one to bring its progress window back.
- **Paused tasks**: stopped by you, or waiting for your answer to a conflict. Tap to continue.
- **Failed tasks**: tap to try again.
- **Pending tasks**: waiting for a destination. Tap one to run it in the folder you are in. In a category or in a search list the app answers **Tasks cannot be run here**.
- **Invalid tasks**: their source files no longer exist.

Each task shows its kind (**Copy**, **Move**, **Compress**…), "Content: 3" (the number of items) and the time it was created. Hold a task to unfold the names of its files or, for a failed task, the reason. Swipe a task to the left and tap the bin to remove it. With no task the sheet says **Empty** and **No tasks available**.

While a task runs, a window shows its title, the current step and file (**Counting files…**, **Copying**, **Moving**, **Deleting source files…**, **Deleting**, **Compressing**, **Renaming**, "Extracting", **Merging**, **Signing**, **Aligning APK file**, **Updating**, **Finalizing**, **Completed**), a progress bar, a count such as "12/40" and a percentage. **Hide** lets the task go on in the background (not offered for renaming and for converting to APK); **Stop** interrupts it (the button reads **Stopping…**). A notification titled **Tasks** then reads **Tasks are running…**, or **Action required** when a task waits for you. In Android's notification settings its category is named **Task Running Notification**.

When a file with the same name already exists at the destination, a window titled **Conflict** says "File NAME already exists in destination folder." Tick **Apply to subsequent conflicts** to answer once for all, then choose **Replace**, **Skip**, or **Cancel** (the task stays under **Paused tasks**).

At the end the app says **Task completed**, **Task failed** or **Task has been paused**.

### Search

**Search** in the bottom bar opens a full page. Type in the field **Search Query** and tap the magnifier (or the search key of the keyboard). The search covers the current folder and everything under it, hidden files included; in a category it covers the listed files. While it runs, **Searching…**, a bar and the name being examined are shown; a square button stops it.

The sliders icon on the right unfolds **Advanced Options**:

| Option | What it does | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **Ignore Case** | Upper and lower case count as the same | on |
| **Use Regex** | The query is a regular expression (if it is not a valid one, it is searched as plain text) | off |
| **By Extension** | The query is compared with the extension instead of the name | off |
| **In Content** | Also looks inside text and code files (up to 50 MB each); the first matching line is shown with "Line 12" | off |
| **Max Results: 1000** | Buttons **10**, **100**, **1K**, **5k**, **10K** | 1000 |

Under "Results (n)": **Clear** empties the list; **Expand** opens the results as a tab named **Search**, where you can select and act on them like in a folder. Tap a file in the results to open it. Hold a result for **Locate** (closes the search and shows the file in its folder) and **Copy path** (message **Copied to clipboard**).

### Sort by

**Sort** opens a sheet titled **Sort by**:

- **Apply to this folder only**: on when the sheet opens. Leave it on to sort this folder alone; switch it off to change the sorting of all folders that have no sorting of their own.
- Under "Sort Method": **Name (A-Z)** (default), **Date (newer)**, **Size (smaller)**, **Type**.
- Under "Display Options": **Folders first** (default on), **Reverse** (default off).

The choice is applied when you close the sheet.

### View Configuration

**Display mode** in the three-dot menu opens a sheet titled **View Configuration**:

- **Apply to this folder only**.
- **Display mode**: **Grid View** or **List View** (default).
- **Properties**: a slider "Column Count: 1" (1 to 5) in list view, "Cell Count: 4" (3 to 8) in grid view; then **Item Size**, a slider from **Small** to **Large** in five steps (it is named **Font Size** in grid view with cropped thumbnails).
- **Display Options**: **Crop-in Thumbnails**; in grid view also **Gallery Mode** (the name is written over the picture) and **Hide names from media files** (in gallery mode, no name on pictures and videos).

The choice is applied when you close the sheet.

### Bookmarks

**Bookmarks** in the bottom bar opens the list of bookmarked files and folders. Tap a file to open it; tap a folder to open it in a new tab. Swipe a row to the left and tap the bin to remove the bookmark. Bookmarks whose file no longer exists are removed by themselves. Empty, the sheet says **Empty** and **No bookmarks available**.

### File Properties

The "i" icon opens a sheet, titled **Loading Properties…** with the text **Analyzing files…** for the first moment. For one item, **File Properties**:

- **General**: **Name**, **Location**, **Type**, **Size**, **Modified**.
- **System**: **Owner**, **Permissions** (r, w, x).
- **Analysis**: for a folder, **Contents** ("120 files, 8 folders") and its total size; for a file, **MD5** and **SHA-256**. **Calculating…** is shown meanwhile, **Error calculating** if it fails.

For several items, **Selection Properties** with a **Selection Summary**: **selected** ("5 items"), **Total Size**, **Total Contents**.

Hold any value to copy it (**Copied to clipboard**). **Close** closes the sheet.

### Open with

A sheet titled **Open with** lists the apps able to open the file. A drop-down at the top lets you present the file as another kind: its own type, "image", "Video", "Audio", "Text" or "Any". Tap an app to open the file with it. Hold an app and choose **Set as default for this type** to always use it for files with this extension.

### An APK file

Tapping an .apk file opens a sheet with the app's icon, name, package name, version, and **Installed** if it is already on the phone. Buttons:

- **Explore**: browse the inside of the APK like a ZIP file.
- **Install**, or **Update** if the app is installed: hands the file to Android's installer.
- **Properties**: unfolds two tabs. **Details**: **Version Code**, **Size**, **Minimum SDK**, **Target SDK**, and when installed **Installed Version**, **Install Date**, **Last Update**. **Permissions**: the list, or **No permissions requested**.

A file that is not a valid APK shows **Invalid APK** and only **Explore**.

### Apps

The **Apps** tab lists installed apps with name, package name, version, size and the word **System** for system apps. At the bottom: a magnifier (search by name or package name, field **Search Query**), three choices **User** (default), **System**, **ALL**, and a sort button offering **Name**, **Size**, **Install Date**, **Update Date**.

Tap an app for its sheet: version, **Debug** if it is a debug build, and **Extract**, which adds a copy task for the app's APK file. Three tabs: **Details** (**Version**, **Size**, **Category**, **Install Date**, **Last Update**), **Advanced** (**Target SDK**, **Minimum SDK**, **UID**, **Data Directory**, **APK Path**, **Debug Mode** with **Enabled** or **Disabled**), **Permissions**. **Close** closes it.

### The text editor

Text and code files open in the built-in editor (txt, log, md, csv, json, xml, ini, html, js, py, java, kt and similar).

- Top bar: back arrow, file name, undo, redo, save (the diskette shows a pencil when there are unsaved changes), and a menu.
- Under it, the path of the file, then, once you move the cursor, its position as "line:column", the number of selected characters in brackets and "search result: 2/7" during a search; "UTF-8" on the right.
- Bottom bar: a "Tab" key (indents; with a selection, hold it to unindent), the symbols `_ = { } / \ < > | ? + - *`, two arrows that move the cursor (hold: start or end of the line; with a selection, hold to choose which end the arrows move, message **Controlling left cursor** or **Controlling right cursor**), and a folder icon.
- The folder icon opens **Recent Files**: the files opened in the editor since the app started, unsaved ones marked with a leading `*`. Tap one to switch to it. Swipe one to the left and tap the bin to close it; the file being shown answers **This file is currently open**.

The menu: **Search**, **Format** (only for .json, .java, .kt and .xml files), **Word wrap**, **Read only**, **Jump to line**, and **Preferences**, a sub-menu with **Use ICU selection**, **Pin Numbers line**, **Auto symbol pair**, **Delete empty lines**, **Delete tabs**, **Auto indentation**, **Magnifier**.

**Search** shows a panel with the fields **Find** and **Replace**, the boxes **Regex** and **Case sensitive**, and the buttons **REP** (replace this match), **ALL** (replace all), **PREV**, **NEXT**.

**Jump to line** opens **Jump to position** with a field "Line:Column (e.g. 23:11)" and the button **Go**.

### The PDF viewer

Pages scroll vertically; pinch to zoom. A bar on the left edge scrolls fast and shows the page number as "3 of 20". The top bar (back arrow, file name, "i") hides when you scroll; tap the page to bring it back. "i" opens **PDF Info**: **Name**, **Page Count**, **Size**, **Path**, **Last modified**.

### The image viewer

Pinch to zoom; tap to show or hide the controls. At the top: back arrow, name, size and dimensions, and "i" for the details sheet ("Image Details": **Name**, **Size**, **Dimensions**, **Format**, **Last modified**, **Path**). At the bottom, four buttons: background behind the picture (none, white, grey, black), fitting (fit, crop, fill the width, fill the height, stretch, never enlarged), rotate by a quarter turn, and a pencil that hands the picture to another app (**Edit with…**).

### The audio player

Cross to close, loudspeaker (**Volume** slider), bars (**Equalizer**). The cover, title, artist and album (**Unknown Title**, **Unknown Artist**, **Unknown Album** when the file says nothing), a position slider with elapsed and total time, previous, play/pause, next, a speed button (0.5x, 1.0x, 1.25x, 1.5x, 2.0x in turn) and a repeat button. Playback starts when you press play.

### The video player

Tap to show or hide the controls; pinch to zoom. Top: back arrow, file name, loudspeaker. Middle: back 10 seconds, play/pause, forward 10 seconds. Bottom: position slider with elapsed and total time. The video opens with the sound off: tap the loudspeaker to hear it.

### Preferences

See "Settings" below. Each group has a title you can tap to fold it.

### About and Logs

The three-line icon at the top left (or **About** at the bottom of the preferences) opens a card with the app's name and version and three rows:

- **Github**, **View source code**: opens the project's page in your browser.
- "Prism File Explorer", **Reader's Files is a modified version of this app (GPL-3.0)**: opens Prism's page in your browser.
- **Logs**, **View application logs**: opens the list of the app's last errors, warnings and information messages (the 5 last errors, 10 last warnings, 30 last messages). Filters at the top: "All", "Errors", "Warnings", "Info". Hold an entry to copy it. The bin opens **Clear logs** ("Are you sure you want to clear all logs? This action cannot be undone."), which clears the kind currently filtered.

## How to

### Browse a storage or a folder

1. On **Home**, tap **Internal Storage** (or your SD card, or a category).
2. Tap folders to go down. Use the Back button or tap a folder in the path bar to go up.
3. The house icon of the path bar returns to where the tab started. To return to the home page, press Back at the top folder, or tap the tab's name and choose **Home**.

### Open a file

Tap it. Pictures, videos, audio, PDF and text files open in the built-in viewers; ZIP files open like folders; other archives are unpacked; APK files show their sheet; anything else is handed to another app. To choose the app yourself, hold the file, tap **Options**, then **Open with**.

### Always open a kind of file with the same app

1. Hold the file, tap **Options**, then **Open with**.
2. Hold the app you want and tap **Set as default for this type**.

To undo it: **Options**, then **Remove default opening method**.

### Select several files

Hold the first one, then tap the others; or hold the first and then hold the last to take everything in between. **Select All** takes the whole folder. Back clears the selection.

### Copy or move files

1. Select the files.
2. Tap the two-sheets icon (copy) or the scissors (move). The app says **New task has been added**.
3. Go to the destination folder (it must be another folder than the one the files are in).
4. Tap **Task**, then tap the task under **Pending tasks**.
5. If a file already exists there, answer the **Conflict** window with **Replace** or **Skip**.

You can prepare several tasks and run each where you want. A task can be run once.

### Create a folder or an empty file

1. Tap **Create**. The window **New Content** opens.
2. Type the name. Tick **Create and open** to enter the new folder at once.
3. Tap **Folder** or **File**.

The characters `: / * ? " < > |` are not accepted.

### Rename a file

1. Select it and tap the letter A.
2. Change the name and tap **Rename**.

### Rename several files at once

1. Select them and tap the letter A. The page **Batch Rename** opens.
2. In **New Name**, write the pattern. It starts as `{p}{e}` (same name, same extension). The buttons under the field insert the pieces: **Name** `{p}`, **Extension** `{s}`, **Extension with a dot** `{e}`, **Last modified** `{t}` (as year-month-day_hour-minute-second), **Number increment** `{n}` and **Zero-padded number increment** `{zn}`. For the two numbers, replace `n` with the number to start from: `{1}` gives 1, 2, 3…; `{zz1}` gives 01, 02, 03…; `{zzz1}` gives 001, 002… The "i" in the field shows this **Syntax**.
3. If you wish, fill **Find and replace**: **Text to find** and **Replace with**, with **Regex** for a regular expression.
4. Under **Preview**, set the order of the files (it decides the numbering): **Reverse**, the sort button (**Custom**, **Name (A-Z)**, **Date (newer)**, **Size (smaller)**, **Type**), or hold a row and drag it.
5. Tap **Preview**: each file shows its new name. If two names collide, the button stays greyed until you change the pattern.
6. Tap **Rename**.

Example: `Holiday {zz1}{e}` renames the selected photos Holiday 01.jpg, Holiday 02.jpg…

### Delete files, and get them back

1. Select the files and tap the bin.
2. In **Delete Confirmation** ("Do you really want to delete the selected files?"), **Move to Recycle Bin** is ticked: the files go to the recycle bin. Untick it to delete them for good. If you change the box, **Remember this choice** appears; tick it to keep that choice for next time.
3. Tap **Confirm** (**Dismiss** cancels).

To get a file back: open **Recycle bin** on the home page. Each deletion is a folder named with a number; open it, select the files, tap the scissors, go to the folder where you want them, tap **Task** and run the task.

To empty the recycle bin: open it, tap **Empty**, then **Confirm**. This deletes for good.

### Compress files into a ZIP

1. Select the files or folders, tap **Options**, then **Compress**.
2. In **Create Archive**, the name proposed is the current folder's name followed by .zip; change it if you wish and tap **Create**. The ZIP is made in the current folder.

**Minimize** closes the window and keeps the task under **Pending tasks**; go to another folder, open **Task** and tap it to get the window again and create the ZIP there.

### Open or unpack an archive

- A .zip (or .jar) file: tap it. You browse it like a folder. You can open its files, create, rename and delete inside it, and copy files out of it or into it with the usual tasks. A file opened from a ZIP and changed in another app is put back into the ZIP when you return to the list.
- Any other archive (7z, rar, tar, tar.gz, tar.bz2, tar.xz, iso, cab, lzh, cpio, deb, rpm and a few more): tap it. The app says "Extracting NAME…", unpacks it into a new folder beside it, named after the archive ("book.tar.gz" gives "book", or "book (2)" if that exists), opens that folder and says "Extracted to “book”".
- A single compressed file ("notes.txt.gz", .bz2, .xz, .zst…): tapping it puts "notes.txt" beside it.

### Search for a file

1. Go to the folder to search in (the storage itself to search everything).
2. Tap **Search**, type part of the name, tap the magnifier.
3. Tap a result to open it, or hold it and choose **Locate** to see it in its folder.

To search by extension or inside text files, open the sliders icon and switch on **By Extension** or **In Content**.

### Show hidden files

Open the three-dot menu in a folder and tap **Show hidden files**. The same setting is in **Preferences**, **File List**. Hidden files are those whose name starts with a dot.

### Change how a folder is displayed or sorted

- Three-dot menu, **Display mode**: list or grid, number of columns, size, thumbnails.
- **Sort**: by name, date, size or type.

In both sheets, **Apply to this folder only** keeps the choice for this folder alone.

### Bookmark a file or a folder

Hold it, tap **Options**, then **Add to bookmarks** (message **Added to bookmarks**). Find it under **Bookmarks** in the bottom bar or on the home page.

### Pin a file or a folder to the home page

Hold it, tap **Options**, then **Pin to home tab** (message **Done**). It appears under **Pinned Files** on the home page. To unpin, hold its row on the home page and tap the bin.

### Put a shortcut on the phone's home screen

Hold one file or one folder, tap **Options**, then **Add to home screen**, and confirm in your launcher. The shortcut opens the app on that folder, or opens that file.

### Share files

Select one or more files (no folder), tap **Options**, then the share icon, and choose the app.

### See the size of a folder or the checksum of a file

Select it and tap the "i" icon. For a folder, **Size** and **Contents** are computed; for a file, **MD5** and **SHA-256**. Hold a value to copy it.

### Go straight to a path

Switch on **Jump to path** in **Customize home tab**, tap it on the home page, type the path in **Destination path** and tap **Open**. The button reads **Invalid path** as long as the path does not exist. The folder opens in a new tab.

### Copy the path of a folder

Hold its name in the path bar. For a file, use **Location** in its properties, or **Copy path** in a search result.

### Choose the tabs opened at start

Open the folder you want, tap its tab, choose **Add to startup tabs** (message **Added as startup tab**). Reorder or remove them in **Manage startup tabs**. Or switch on **Remember last session** in the preferences to reopen the tabs you had when leaving.

### Install an APK, or look inside it

Tap the .apk file, then **Install** (or **Update**). **Explore** shows its contents.

### Save the APK of an installed app

1. Open **Apps** from the home page, tap the app, tap **Extract** (message **New task has been added**).
2. Go to the folder where you want the file, tap **Task** and run the task.

### Turn an .apks, .xapk or .apkm file into an APK

Hold the file, tap **Options**, then **Convert to APK**. The result is created beside it: "name-signed.apk", or "name-unsigned.apk" when **Auto sign merged APK bundle files** is off.

### Edit a text file

1. Tap the file (or **Options**, **Edit with text editor** for a file of another kind). Files over 15 MB are refused.
2. Edit, then tap the diskette. The app says **Saved**.

If you leave the app while files are unsaved, it asks "Some files in the text editor are not saved. Do you want to save them now?" with **Save** and **Ignore**.

### Move or copy the app's settings to another phone

1. **Preferences**, **Other**, **Export preferences**. The app says **Exported to /.prism/preferences.prismPrefs**.
2. Copy that file to the other phone (the .prism folder is hidden: show hidden files to see it).
3. There, tap the file in Reader's Files. In **Import Preferences** ("Do you want to override your current preferences with the ones contained in the selected file?") tap **Confirm**. The app says **Preferences imported successfully**.

## Settings

Three-dot menu, **Preferences**.

### Appearance

| Setting | What it changes | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Theme** | Black on white or white on black | **Light**, **Dark**, **Follow system** | **Follow system** |
| **Show bottom options bar labels** | The words under the icons of the bottom bar | on, off | on |
| **Date time format** | How dates are written in lists and properties | yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss; yyyy-MM-dd; dd-MM-yyyy HH:mm:ss; dd-MM-yyyy; MMM dd, yyyy HH:mm:ss; MMM dd, yyyy; MMMM dd, yyyy; EEE, MMM dd, yyyy | MMM dd, yyyy HH:mm:ss |
| "Hide main toolbar" | Removes the top bar; the line of counts moves under the path bar, **+** and the three-dot menu move to the right of the tabs, and **About** joins that menu | on, off | off |

### File List

| Setting | What it changes | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| **File list size** | Size of icons and text in lists | **Extra small**, **Small**, **Medium**, **Large**, **Extra large** | **Medium** |
| **Show hidden files** | Shows files whose name starts with a dot | on, off | off |
| **Show folder's content count** | Adds "folders: 2, files: 9" to each folder's line | on, off | off |

### File Operation

| Setting | What it changes | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **Auto sign merged APK bundle files** | "Automatically sign merged APK bundle files with a test key": the APK made by **Convert to APK** is signed so that it can be installed | on |

### Behavior

| Setting | What it changes | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **Long click to show file's options menu** | Holding a file selects it and opens the options sheet at once | off |
| **Disable pull down to refresh** | Pulling a list down no longer refreshes it | off |
| **Skip home screen when closing tabs** | Back at the top folder no longer replaces the tab with the home page | off |
| **Back navigation to close tabs** | Back on a tab other than the first closes it | on |
| **Remember last session** | Reopens the tabs you had when leaving ("if enabled, startup tabs will be ignored") | off |
| **Confirm before exiting the app** | Back must be pressed twice to leave (**Press again to exit**) | on |
| **Use built-in viewers** | "Automatically open supported files with built-in viewers"; off, text, pictures, audio, video and PDF go to other apps | on |

### Recent Files

| Setting | What it changes | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **Extensive recent files scan** | "Scan hidden files and folders": recent files inside hidden folders are listed too | on |
| **Excluded paths** | "Manage excluded paths": folders left out of the recent files. Type a path in **New Path** (the example shown is /storage/emulated/0/example) and tap **+**; the bin removes one. Empty, the sheet says **No excluded paths** and **Add new path to be excluded from the recent file scanner** | none |

### Text Editor

| Setting | What it changes | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **Recent files limit** | "Choose the maximum number of recent files to show": 5, 10, 15, 25 or "Unlimited" | "Unlimited" |
| **Pin Numbers line** | Line numbers stay in place when scrolling sideways | on |
| **Auto symbol pair** | Typing an opening bracket or quote adds the closing one | off |
| **Auto indentation** | A new line starts at the indentation of the previous one | on |
| **Magnifier** | A magnifier appears while moving the cursor | on |
| **Use ICU selection** | Another way of finding where a word starts and ends when you select it | off |
| **Delete empty lines** | Backspace removes an empty line in one go | off |
| **Delete tabs** | Backspace removes a run of spaces in one go | on |

**Word wrap** and **Read only**, both off by default, are set from the editor's menu.

### Other

- **Export preferences** ("To import, locate and open the exported file").
- **About**.

Settings kept outside this screen: the default sorting (sheet **Sort by**), the default display (sheet **View Configuration**), the home layout, the startup tabs, and whether deleting goes through the recycle bin (**Remember this choice** in the delete window).

## Your data

Everything stays on the phone. The app has no network permission and sends nothing to anyone. The two rows **Github** and "Prism File Explorer" of the About card only ask your browser to open a web page.

What the app keeps:

- In its private storage: all settings, bookmarks, pinned files, startup tabs, the sorting and display chosen per folder, the default apps per kind of file, and the logs.
- In a hidden folder named ".prism" at the top of the internal storage: the recycle bin (folder "bin", one numbered folder per deletion), the exported settings ("preferences.prismPrefs") and the text editor's folder ("textEditor", with "symbols.txt", the list of symbols of the editor's bottom bar).
- Temporary copies of files opened from inside a ZIP, removed when the app starts and when it closes.

The recycle bin is never emptied by itself: files moved there keep using space until you empty it.

Export and import: **Export preferences** writes one file with all the settings listed above; opening such a file in the app replaces all current settings with its contents. There is no sync.

Uninstalling removes the private storage (settings, bookmarks, logs). The ".prism" folder is an ordinary folder of your storage: the recycle bin and the exported settings stay there until you delete them. The app lets Android's own backup save its settings.

## With the other apps

- Reader's Files is offered by Android as a viewer when another app opens a PDF, a text file, a picture, an audio file or a video.
- The text viewer opened that way is the same editor; when you leave with unsaved changes it asks "Do you want to save the file?" with **Save** and **Ignore changes**.
- Files are shared to other apps with the share icon of the options sheet, opened in other apps with **Open with**, and pictures are handed to an editor with the pencil of the image viewer.
- **Add to home screen** puts a shortcut to a file or a folder on any launcher that accepts shortcuts.
- Updates come from the F-Droid repository of the Reader's apps or from Reader's Installer.

The app offers no home-screen widget.

## Limits

- No network: no cloud, FTP, SMB or other remote storage.
- Android closes the "Android/data" folders to every file manager since Android 11. Most folders under **Root** are not readable either: the app does not use root access.
- Archives: only ZIP files can be created, and only ZIP files (with .jar, .apk, .apks) are browsed in place; the other formats are unpacked whole, without a progress window. Password-protected 7z, rar and similar archives are refused. Links stored inside an archive are not recreated.
- Copying or moving to the folder the files already are in is refused; there is no "duplicate".
- The recycle bin has no "restore" button: you move the files back yourself.
- The text editor refuses files over 15 MB. Searching inside files skips files over 50 MB and looks only at text and code files.
- The audio player's **Equalizer** sliders move but do not change the sound.
- The audio and video players play one file at a time; there is no playlist.
- The **Documents** category lists PDF, Word, Excel and PowerPoint files only; **Archives** lists zip, rar, tar, gzip and 7z files only.
- In the category lists, the app relies on Android's media index: a file just copied may take a moment to appear.
- A few labels are in English whatever the language: "Hide main toolbar", "Sort Method", "Display Options", "Image Details", the log filters, "Unlimited", "and 2 more", "3 of 20", the kinds in the **Open with** sheet.

## Messages and problems

### Starting and browsing

- **Storage access permission is required**: you came back without granting the access. Tap **Grant Access** again and switch on the access for Reader's Files.
- **This folder is inaccessible**: Android does not let the app read this folder (system folders, "Android/data").
- **Empty** with **Show hidden files in settings to see all files**: the folder has no visible file; it may contain hidden ones.
- **Press again to exit**: press Back a second time within two seconds to leave.
- **Invalid path**: the path typed in **Jump to path** does not exist.

### Opening files

- **Could not find any app that can open this type of files**: no installed app accepts this file. Try **Open with** and the kind "Any".
- "No app found to open this file.": the app chosen as default for this kind of file is no longer installed. Use **Remove default opening method**.
- **Failed to open this file**: the other app refused the file.
- **Invalid zip file**: the ZIP is damaged or is not a ZIP.
- **Failed to extrcat file** (spelled this way in the app): a file inside a ZIP could not be taken out to be opened.
- "Extracting NAME…", "Extracted to “NAME”": an archive is being unpacked, then is unpacked.
- **This archive is protected by a password and cannot be extracted**: the app does not ask for passwords.
- **The archive is empty**: nothing to unpack.
- "The archive could not be extracted: REASON": the archive is damaged or of a format the app does not read.
- **Invalid APK**: the file is not a valid app package.
- **Invalid PDF**, **Failed to load PDF** (under **Error**): the PDF cannot be read.
- **Loading PDF…**, **Loading image…**: wait.
- **Failed to load image**, **Check if the file exists and try again**: the picture is missing or damaged.
- **No app found to edit this image**: no installed app edits pictures.

### Creating, renaming, deleting

- **Invalid file name**, **Invalid folder name**: the name is empty or contains one of `: / * ? " < > |`.
- **File with similar name already exists**: choose another name.
- **Failed to create file**, **Failed to create folder**: the folder cannot be written to.
- **Error creating recycle bin folder**: the recycle bin could not be prepared; nothing was deleted.

### Tasks

- **New task has been added**: the copy or move is waiting; go to the destination and run it from **Task**.
- **Tasks cannot be run here**: you are in a category or a search list; go to a real folder.
- **Task completed**, **Task failed**, **Task has been paused**: the outcome. A failed or paused task waits in the **Tasks** sheet; hold it to read why, tap it to continue.
- **Invalid destination directory.**: the destination is the folder the files are already in.
- **Some files are moved into their subdirectories, those will be excluded.**: you tried to move a folder into itself.
- **No source files were found.**, **The destination was missing.**: the files or the destination disappeared meanwhile.
- "Something went wrong:" followed by a reason: the operation failed at that point (no space left, no right to write…).
- **Task is not ready.**, **Task not found.**, **Task is not in pending state**, "Task cannot be continued from current state: …", **This task is currently running**: the task cannot be started or removed in its present state; wait for it to end or remove it from the list.
- **Task aborted**: you stopped it.
- **Unsupported source/destination combination**, **unsupported source file**: this operation is not possible between these places.
- **Failed to copy files to zip**, **Failed to extract files from zip**, **Failed to copy zip entries**, **Failed to replace original zip file**, **Failed to delete original zip file**: an operation inside a ZIP failed; the ZIP may be read-only or damaged.
- **Failed to delete file**, **Failed to rename file**: the file is protected or no longer there.
- **Invalid APK bundle file**, **Failed to convert APK bundle to APK**, **Failed to sign the merged APK file**: **Convert to APK** could not use this file. **APK bundle file successfully converted to APK** is the success message.

### Search

- **Something went wrong, check logs**: the search stopped on an error; open **Logs** from the About card.

### Text editor

- **Saved**, **Failed to save**: result of saving.
- **Permission denied**: a file opened from another app cannot be written back.
- **File too large, consider using a dedicated text editor instead.**: over 15 MB.
- **File not found**, **File no longer exists**: the file was deleted or moved meanwhile.
- **Warning**, "The source file has been changed, do you want to reload it?": another app changed the file. **Reload** takes the new contents, **Cancel** keeps yours.
- **Warning**, "This file has not been saved, do you want to save it?": you are closing an unsaved file from **Recent Files**. **Save** or **Ignore changes**.
- **Invalid position**: what you typed in **Jump to position** is not a line number or "line:column".
- **Failed to load symbols file**: the file "symbols.txt" of the editor is damaged; the standard symbols are used.

### Settings

- **Exported to /.prism/preferences.prismPrefs**: the export succeeded.
- **Preferences imported successfully**, **Invalid preferences file**: result of an import.
- **Added to bookmarks**, **Done**, **Added as startup tab**, **Removed from startup tabs**, **Copied to clipboard**, **Path copied to clipboard**: confirmations.


# Reader's Night Filter

*Android 1.0.0*

## What it is

Reader's Night Filter is one switch that turns the phone's screen gray, as warm as the phone allows, and dim, for reading in the evening. It does not lay a coloured veil over the screen: it drives three of Android's own display settings together (colour correction set to grayscale, Night Light at its warmest, extra dim) and puts them back as they were when you switch off. The same switch is available in the app, in the quick settings, in a home-screen widget and as a tile in Reader's Launcher.

The app needs a permission that Android only gives from a computer, once. It does not need root. It cannot remove all the blue from the screen, it has no timetable (you switch it on and off yourself), and it never uses the network.

The app runs on Android 9 and later, in six languages: English, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese and Russian.

## First start

### The permission, given once from a computer

Android lets an app change the screen's colours only after a special permission. It cannot be granted on the phone itself: it is given once, from a computer. Until then the app shows, instead of the switch, a page titled **one step left** with this text:

"Android lets an app change the screen’s colours only after a permission that is given once, from a computer. Turn on USB debugging in the phone’s developer options, connect the phone to the computer by USB, and run this command on the computer:"

Under the text the app displays the command:

```
adb shell pm grant com.freedomfighter.readersnight android.permission.WRITE_SECURE_SETTINGS
```

The steps, as the app gives them:

1. On the phone, turn on USB debugging in the developer options.
2. Connect the phone to the computer by USB.
3. On the computer, run the command above. It uses `adb`, Android's tool for computers, which must be present on the computer; the app does not install it.
4. Look at the phone: the page **one step left** gives way to the switch by itself within a couple of seconds. The app says so at the bottom of the page: "This page gives way to the switch as soon as the permission is there."

To avoid retyping the command, tap **copy the command**: the command is placed in the phone's clipboard and the line reads **copied** for a moment. You can also select the command's text on the page with a long press.

The permission stays after the phone restarts and after the app is updated. It is lost when the app is uninstalled: after a new installation, run the command again.

### No account, nothing else to allow

The app asks for no other permission, no account and no server. Once the permission is given, the main screen shows **filter off** and "the screen shows its usual colours". All three parts of the filter are already chosen: grayscale on, warm tint on, dimming medium.

## The screens

### The main screen: night filter

The title at the top reads **night filter**. On the right of the title, **⋯** opens the menu.

Under the title, a large block shows the state:

- **filter off**, with "the screen shows its usual colours";
- **filter on**, on an inverted background, with the parts in use, for example "grayscale · warm tint · dimmed". If you switched all three parts off, it reads "nothing chosen: the screen stays as it is".

Tapping this block switches the filter on or off.

Under **what the filter does**, three rows choose the parts. Each row shows its name on the left and its current value on the right; tap the row to change it.

- **grayscale**: **on** or **off**. Removes the colours from the screen.
- **warm tint**: **on** or **off**. Sets Android's Night Light to the warmest the phone accepts.
- **dimming**: each tap moves to the next value, **off**, **light**, **medium**, **strong**, then back to **off**. Darkens the screen beyond the lowest brightness, with Android's extra dim. This row only appears on Android 12 and later.

A change made while the filter is on is applied to the screen at once.

Below the rows, a note recalls the limit: "The warm tint is the warmest the phone allows. It removes much of the blue, not all of it: Android does not let an app go further."

At the very bottom, a row reads **turn the filter on** or **turn the filter off**, depending on the state. It does the same as tapping the state block.

### The menu (⋯)

- **turn the filter on** / **turn the filter off**: the same switch. This entry is absent as long as the permission has not been given.
- **black on white** or **white on black**: switches the app's colours to the ones named.
- **settings**: opens the settings screen.

Tap outside the menu, or press back, to close it.

### The page "one step left"

Shown in place of the state block and the rows until the permission has been given. It contains the explanation, the command, the row **copy the command** and the sentence about the page giving way to the switch. See "First start". The menu **⋯** remains available.

### Settings

Title **settings**, with **←**: tap the title to go back. The screen holds:

- **add to quick settings**, with "the switches under a swipe down from the top of the screen" beneath it (Android 13 and later). On Android 9 to 12 this row is replaced by a text explaining how to do it by hand.
- the four rows about the app's look: **colours**, **text size**, **font**, **haptic tick** (see "Settings" below). Each row shows the current value in large letters and the setting's name beneath it.
- **Reader's Night Filter**, "a gray, warm and dim screen for the evening", and the credits line. These two rows do nothing when tapped.

### The quick-settings tile

Named **Night filter**. It is lit while the filter is on. A tap switches the filter on or off without opening the app. A long press opens the app. If the permission has not been given, a tap closes the quick settings and opens the app on the page **one step left**.

### The widget

On the left, **filter on** or **filter off** and, beneath, the parts chosen (for example "grayscale · warm tint · dimmed"), or **one step left** if the permission is missing. On the right, a switch reading **on** (filled) or **off** (framed).

- Tap the switch: the filter goes on or off.
- Tap the words: the app opens.
- Without the permission, the switch opens the app as well.

## How to

### Give the permission from a computer

1. Open the app. It shows **one step left**.
2. On the phone, turn on USB debugging in the developer options.
3. Connect the phone to the computer by USB.
4. On the computer, run: `adb shell pm grant com.freedomfighter.readersnight android.permission.WRITE_SECURE_SETTINGS`
5. Wait a moment: the page is replaced by the switch.

### Switch the filter on or off

In the app: tap the state block at the top, or the row **turn the filter on** / **turn the filter off** at the bottom, or the same entry in the menu **⋯**.

Outside the app: tap the **Night filter** tile in the quick settings, the switch of the widget, or the switch of the tile in Reader's Launcher.

### Choose what the filter does

1. Open the app.
2. Under **what the filter does**, tap **grayscale** or **warm tint** to set it **on** or **off**.
3. Tap **dimming** until it shows the strength you want: **light**, **medium**, **strong**, or **off**.

The choices are kept. They apply at once if the filter is on, otherwise the next time you switch it on.

### Keep the colours but warm and dim the screen

Set **grayscale** to **off** and leave **warm tint** on, with the **dimming** you want.

### Add the switch to the quick settings

On Android 13 and later:

1. Open the menu **⋯**, then **settings**.
2. Tap **add to quick settings**.
3. Confirm in the box Android displays.

On Android 9 to 12, the app gives the manual way: "To add the switch to the quick settings: swipe down twice from the top of the screen, tap the pencil, and drag “Night filter” among the switches."

### Put the widget on the home screen

1. In your launcher's list of widgets, find **Reader's Night Filter** (described as "the night filter, on or off").
2. Place it on the home screen. It takes about four cells by two, and can be resized in width and height.

### Open the app from the quick settings

Press the **Night filter** tile for a long time.

### Change the app's colours

Open the menu **⋯** and tap **black on white** (or **white on black**). The same change is in **settings**, row **colours**.

## Settings

The parts of the filter are chosen on the main screen:

| Setting | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **grayscale** | **on**, **off** | **on** |
| **warm tint** | **on**, **off** | **on** |
| **dimming** (Android 12 and later) | **off**, **light**, **medium**, **strong** | **medium** |

The app's look is chosen in **settings**. Tap a row to move to the next choice.

| Setting | Choices | Default | What it changes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **colours** | **white on black**, **black on white** | **white on black** | The colours of the app and of its widget. |
| **text size** | **small**, **medium**, **large** | **medium** | The size of all text in the app. |
| **font** | **sans-serif**, **serif**, **mono** | **sans-serif** | The typeface of the app. |
| **haptic tick** | **on**, **off** | **on** | A short vibration when you switch the filter or change one of its parts in the app. |

The widget takes the app's colours when you leave the app.

## Your data

Everything stays on the phone. The app has no network permission: it cannot send or receive anything, there is no account and nothing is collected.

The app keeps only: your choices (the three parts, the look), whether the filter is on, and the values Android's display settings had just before the filter was switched on, so that it can put them back.

What switching off restores:

- colour correction: its previous state and its previous mode;
- Night Light: on or off as before, your own strength, your own schedule. While the filter is on with **warm tint**, the Night Light schedule is suspended, so that sunrise does not end the tint;
- extra dim: Android does not let an app read this setting, so the app cannot know what it was. Switching the filter off turns extra dim off, and leaves its strength where the filter set it.

There is no export, no import and no synchronisation. The app's data is not included in Android's backups.

Uninstalling removes the choices and the permission. Switch the filter off before uninstalling: your display settings are put back only at the moment the filter is switched off.

The state of the filter and its switch are open to one other app only, Reader's Launcher, which the app recognises by its signature.

## With the other apps

### Quick settings

The tile **Night filter** (see "The screens").

### Home-screen widget

One widget, **Reader's Night Filter**, for any launcher that accepts widgets: the state in words and a switch.

### Reader's Launcher

Reader's Launcher has its own tile for the filter. In the launcher's menu, choose **add widget**, then **night filter** ("the screen gray, warm and dim: Reader's Night Filter on and off").

The tile shows **filter on** or **filter off**, with **night filter** beneath, and a switch on the right: **●** when on, **○** when off.

- Tap the switch: the filter goes on or off.
- Tap the words: Reader's Night Filter opens.
- If the permission has not been given, the tile reads **one step left in the app**, and the switch opens the app.
- If Reader's Night Filter is not installed, the tile shows its name in gray, without a switch; a tap opens the app's page on the web.

## Limits

- The warm tint stops at the warmest Night Light the phone accepts. It removes much of the blue, not all of it; on a Pixel 10, about a fifth of the blue light and half of the green remain. A red-only screen would need root, which the app does not use.
- The permission can only be given from a computer.
- Dimming needs Android 12 or later. On Android 9 to 11 the filter has two parts, grayscale and warm tint.
- The button **add to quick settings** needs Android 13 or later; before that, the tile is added by hand.
- No schedule: the filter does not switch itself on in the evening or off in the morning.
- The warm tint has one strength, the warmest. The three dimming strengths are the only ones.
- If you had Android's extra dim on before using the filter, it is off afterwards: switch it back on in the system settings.

## Messages and problems

**one step left** (in the app, or under the state in the widget) / **one step left in the app** (tile of Reader's Launcher): the permission has not been given, or was lost after a reinstallation. Run the command from a computer, see "First start".

**The tile or the widget opens the app instead of switching.** Same cause: the permission is missing.

**copied**: the command is in the phone's clipboard.

**nothing chosen: the screen stays as it is**: the filter is on but grayscale, warm tint and dimming are all **off**. Switch at least one of them on.

**the screen shows its usual colours**: the filter is off.

**The page "one step left" stays after the command.** The page checks every second or two while it is open. If it does not change, the command did not reach the phone: check that USB debugging is on, that the phone is connected by USB, and run the command again.

**The filter shows "filter off" although I did not switch it off.** If you turn grayscale off in the system settings while the filter is on, the app follows: the next time you open the app or the quick settings, it considers the filter off and puts the other settings back. When **grayscale** is not among the chosen parts, the same applies to Night Light switched off in the system settings.

**There is no "dimming" row.** The phone runs Android 9, 10 or 11.

**There is no "add to quick settings" row.** The phone runs Android 12 or earlier; follow the text shown in its place.

**The screen still looks a little blue.** That is the limit Android sets, recalled by the note on the main screen.




<!-- Also in the installer -->


# ePub Magazine Reader

*Android 0.2.25-magazine*

## What it is

ePub Magazine Reader reads newspapers and magazines delivered as EPUB files, on a phone or an e-ink tablet. An issue opens as a contents page with one card per article (section, title, author, picture), then each article is shown on its own, cut into full pages that you turn with a tap: nothing scrolls. New issues can be fetched by themselves from a shared kDrive folder or from a WebDAV folder, picked from a folder on the device, or opened from another app.

It is built on the book reader Book's Story, of which it keeps the library, the file browser and the themes. It is deliberately not a general book reader: it opens EPUB files only, and it has no bookmarks, no highlighting, no search inside the text, no dictionary and no choice of fonts.

On the phone the app is named **ePub Magazine reader**.

## First start

The app asks for no Android permission. It only reads the folders you choose yourself, and uses the network for the sync you set up.

At the first start a welcome screen appears: **Welcome to ePub Magazine reader!**

1. Under **Scan preferences**, tap **Add folder** and choose, in the Android folder picker, the folder where your EPUB files are. You can add several folders. Each one is listed with a cross to remove it. This step is optional.
2. Tap **Next**.
3. The screen shows **Everything is done!** and **Setup completed.** Tap **Done**.

The app then adds every EPUB found in the chosen folders (subfolders included) to the library and shows the **Library**. If files were found, the message **All selected books were successfully added.** appears.

Pressing the Back button on the welcome screen closes the app.

When the library is empty it shows **Read your favorite books and sort them between categories** and a button **Add a book**, which leads to the **Browse** screen. When **Browse** has nothing to show, it displays **Expand the library's book collection with your downloads** and a button **Set up scanning**, which leads to the folder settings.

There is no account to create. A sync folder is entered later, in **Settings** then **Sync** (see "Fetch the issues from a shared kDrive folder" and "Fetch the issues from a WebDAV folder").

## The screens

The app has two main screens, **Library** and **Browse**, reached with the bar at the bottom (on a wide screen, a column on the left side). The three-dot button at the top right of both opens a panel with **About app** and **Settings**.

While the app is on screen, the display never goes to sleep by itself.

### Library

The list of your issues. At the top: the title **Library** followed by the number of issues, then three buttons.

- The magnifying glass opens a search field (**Search…**). Type part of a title: the list is filtered about half a second after you stop typing. The arrow on the left closes the search.
- The sort button opens a panel with two orders, **Last modified** and **Alphabetically**. Tap an order to choose it; tap the chosen order again to reverse it (the arrow shows the direction).
- The three dots open **About app** and **Settings**.

Each line shows the cover, the title and a small percentage badge. The badge stays at 0 %: the app remembers the article where you stopped, not a percentage.

- Tap an issue to open it.
- Long press an issue to select it. The top bar then shows a cross (cancel the selection), **1 selected** (the count), a "select all" button and a bin. Tap other issues to add them to the selection or take them out.
- Pull the list down to refresh it. If a share link or a WebDAV URL is set in **Sync**, this also fetches the new issues at once.

The Back button cancels a selection, then closes the search, then leaves the app.

### Browse

The files found in your scan folders that are not yet in the library, grouped by folder. Each group has a heading with the folder's path and a pin: tap the pin to keep that folder at the top of the list.

Each file shows its name, its size and its date. The screen lists files ending in .epub, .pdf, .fb2, .txt, .html, .htm and .md, but only EPUB files can be added to the library.

- Tap a file to select it, tap again to deselect it.
- Long press a file to select every file of its folder.
- With a selection, the top bar shows a cross, the count, a "select all" button and a check mark. The check mark opens the **Add books?** dialog (see "Add issues from a folder of the device").

At the top, the magnifying glass searches by file name. The filter button opens a panel with three tabs:
