- **Sort**: **Alphabetically**, **File format**, **Last modified**, **File size**. Tap the chosen order again to reverse it.
- **Filter**: one box per file type (.epub, .pdf, .fb2, .txt, .html, .htm, .md). With no box ticked, every type is shown. The filter button is coloured when a filter is on.
- **Display**: **Display mode** (**List** or **Grid**) and, in grid mode, **Grid size**.

Pull the list down to scan the folders again. The Back button returns to the **Library**.

### Contents of an issue

Opening an issue shows its contents, cut into pages that fit the screen.

- The bar at the top has three zones. The left arrow goes to the previous page of the contents, the right arrow to the next one. The middle zone shows the title of the issue (or its publisher and date when it has no title); tapping it returns to the **Library**.
- Each card shows the section, the title of the article, the author when the issue gives one, and the article's picture on the right when it has one. Tap a card to read the article.
- At the bottom, a counter such as **2 / 5** gives the page of the contents when there is more than one.
- The bar at the very bottom has a house in the middle: it returns to the **Library**. The minus and plus on either side are greyed on this screen.

The contents are not swiped: use the two arrows. The Back button returns to the **Library**.

When an issue is opened again after another one, or after the app was restarted, the contents open on the page that holds the article you last read, and that card is framed.

An EPUB that holds a single article opens straight on that article, without contents.

An EPUB that is not laid out as a magazine opens the same way: each chapter becomes a card, labelled with the publisher or the title of the book.

### Article

The article fills the screen, black on white whatever the theme, in full pages.

- Tap the right third of the screen to go to the next page. On the last page, the same tap opens the next article.
- Tap the left third to go back one page.
- Tap the middle third to show or hide the two bars.

The top bar: the left arrow opens the previous article, the right arrow the next one, and the middle zone, which shows the section, returns to the contents.

The bottom bar: the minus makes the text smaller, the plus makes it bigger, and the house in the middle, next to the current size in percent, returns to the **Library**.

The bars hide again each time the article changes. The Back button returns to the contents.

### Settings

Reached with the three dots, then **Settings**. Five entries: **General**, **Appearance**, **Library**, **Browse**, **Sync**. Each is described under "Settings" below.

The button at the top right of this screen shows the welcome screen of the first start again.

### About app

Reached with the three dots, then **About app**.

- **App version**: the name and the version of the app.
- **Based on Book's Story by Acclorite**, with the line **Pierre Gallaz · developed with Claude Code**.
- **Open source licenses**: the list of the software libraries used; tap one to read its licence.
- **Credits**: the projects the original reader thanks; tap one to open its web site.
- **Report a bug**, **Contributors**, **Help translate**, **Support development**, a tap on **App version**, and the small icons at the bottom open pages of Book's Story, the original project, in your browser, not pages of this app.

### The crash screen

If the app stops on an error, a screen titled **Oops! The app crashed.** shows the technical report. **Copy** puts it in the clipboard, **Report** opens the Android share sheet to send it with the app of your choice.

## How to

### Add issues from a folder of the device

1. Open **Settings**, **Browse**, and under **Scan** tap **Add folder**. Choose the folder in the Android picker.
2. Go to **Browse**. The EPUB files of the folder and of its subfolders are listed.
3. Tap the files you want, or long press one to take the whole folder.
4. Tap the check mark at the top right. The **Add books?** dialog lists the files with their title and author; tap a line to leave it out. A file that could not be read is shown in red.
5. Tap **OK**. The app returns to the **Library** and shows **All selected books were successfully added.**

The files stay where they are: the app reads them in your folder.

### Open an EPUB received in another app

In a file manager, a messaging app or a browser, tap the EPUB file and choose **ePub Magazine reader** in the Android "Open with" list. The app keeps its own copy of the file and opens the contents of the issue.

### Fetch the issues from a shared kDrive folder

1. In kDrive, create a public share link for the folder that receives your issues (a link without password).
2. In the app, open **Settings**, **Sync**.
3. Tap **kDrive share link**, paste the link and tap **OK**.
4. Tap **Sync now** to fetch the issues at once.
5. Turn on **Enable sync** to have it done by itself, and set **Sync interval**.

### Fetch the issues from a WebDAV folder

This works with any WebDAV server (Nextcloud, kDrive and others).

1. Open **Settings**, **Sync**. Leave **kDrive share link** empty: when a share link is present, it is used instead of WebDAV.
2. Under **WebDAV (alternative)**, tap **WebDAV URL** and enter the full address of the folder that holds the EPUB files.
3. Tap **Username**, then **Password**, and enter those of your server. All three fields are needed.
4. Tap **Sync now**, then turn on **Enable sync** if you want it automatic.

### Fetch the new issues right now

Either pull the **Library** down, or open **Settings**, **Sync** and tap **Sync now**. In **Sync**, the line shows **Syncing…**, then the result, for example **2 new, 14 skipped**: the number of issues downloaded and the number already on the device.

### Read an issue

1. In the **Library**, tap the issue.
2. In the contents, turn the pages with the two arrows at the top and tap the card of an article.
3. Turn the pages by tapping the right of the screen. At the end of the article, one more tap on the right opens the next article.
4. To go back to the contents, press Back, or tap the middle of the screen and then the middle of the top bar.

### Go on where you stopped

Tap the issue in the **Library**. If you had read an article of it, the app opens that article directly, at its first page. The contents are right behind: press Back to reach them.

### Jump to the previous or the next article

Tap the middle of the screen to show the bars, then the left or right arrow of the top bar.

### Make the text bigger or smaller

In an article, tap the middle of the screen, then the plus or the minus in the bottom bar. The size goes from 70 % to 220 % in steps of 10. It starts between 100 % on a narrow phone and 140 % on a tablet. The size is kept while you move from article to article; it returns to the starting value when you go back to the contents.

### Find an issue

In the **Library**, tap the magnifying glass and type part of the title.

### Sort the library

In the **Library**, tap the sort button and choose **Last modified** (the date of the file) or **Alphabetically**. Tap the same line again to reverse the order.

### Remove issues from the library

1. In the **Library**, long press an issue, then tap the others to remove.
2. Tap the bin.
3. The **Delete books?** dialog reminds you that the issues leave the library but that the files are not deleted. Tap **OK**.

The message **All selected books were successfully deleted.** confirms it. An issue that came by sync and is still in the folder on the server is downloaded again at the next sync.

### Show the library as a grid of covers

Open **Settings**, **Library**, and under **Display mode** choose **Grid**. **Grid size** and **Title position** then appear.

### Change the language

Open **Settings**, **General**, and tap a language under **App language**.

### Switch to a dark theme

Open **Settings**, **Appearance**, and under **Dark theme** choose **Enabled**, or **System** to follow the phone. The article page stays black on white.

### Stop giving access to a folder

Open **Settings**, **Browse**, and tap the cross next to the folder. Neither the files nor the issues already in the library are deleted, but an issue whose file sits in that folder can no longer be opened.

### See the welcome screen again

Open **Settings** and tap the button at the top right. At the end, **Done** adds to the library every EPUB of the scan folders that is not in it yet.

## Settings

### General

| Setting | What it does | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| **App language** | Language of the app | 20 languages | The language of the phone if it is in the list, otherwise English |
| **Double back press to exit** (**Press back twice to exit the app**) | In the **Library**, a first press on Back shows **Press back again to exit**; a second one within a second and a half closes the app | on, off | off |

### Appearance

Under the heading **Theme preferences**.

| Setting | What it does | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Dark theme** | Light or dark screens | **System**, **Disabled**, **Enabled** | **Disabled** |
| **App theme** | Colour palette of the app | **Mercury**, **Neptune**, **Earth**, **Io**, **Enceladus**, **Pluto**, **Mars**, **Callisto**, **Jupiter**, **Ceres**, **Eris**, **Saturn**, **Ganymede**, **Venus**, **Makemake**, **Uranus**, **Iapetus** | **Mercury** (the colours of your Android wallpaper; offered from Android 12), otherwise **Neptune** |
| **Theme contrast** | Strength of the contrast. Shown only for **Neptune**, **Earth**, **Pluto**, **Mars**, **Jupiter**, **Eris**, **Saturn**, **Venus** and **Uranus** | **Standard**, **Medium**, **High** | **Standard** |
| **Pure dark** | Black background in dark theme. Shown only when the dark theme is on | **Disabled**, **Enabled**, **Saver** (only while the phone's battery saver is on) | **Disabled** |
| **Absolute dark** (**Change background color to be completely black**) | Shown only when the pure dark background is active | on, off | off |
| **Show navigation labels** (**Show navigation bar labels under icons**) | Shows the words **Library** and **Browse** under the icons of the bottom bar | on, off | on |

### Library

Under the heading **Display**.

| Setting | What it does | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Display mode** | List of lines or grid of covers | **Grid**, **List** | **List** |
| **Grid size** | Number of covers per row. Shown in grid mode | **Auto**, or 1 to 10 **per row** | **Auto** |
| **Title position** | Where the title goes in grid mode | **Hidden**, **Below**, **Inside** | **Below** |
| **Show progress** | Shows the percentage badge on each issue | on, off | on |

The sort order is chosen in the **Library** itself: **Last modified** or **Alphabetically**; by default **Last modified**, the most recent file first.

### Browse

**Scan**: the list of the folders the app may read, each with a cross to remove it, and **Add folder**. The note reads: **These folders are used to scan and retrieve files in the Browse. Removing a folder here will not delete actual files or books from the Library. To revoke access, click the corresponding button.**

**Display**:

| Setting | What it does | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Display mode** | List or grid of files | **List**, **Grid** | **List** |
| **Grid size** | Number of files per row. Shown in grid mode | **Auto**, or 1 to 10 **per row** | **Auto** |

The sort order and the file-type filter are chosen in **Browse** itself, with the filter button; by default **Last modified**, the most recent first, and no filter.

### Sync

| Setting | What it does | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Enable sync** (**Automatically sync EPUBs from kDrive**) | Fetches the new issues by itself: in the background at the chosen interval, and each time the app is opened if the last sync is more than two minutes old | on, off | off |
| **kDrive share link** (**Paste a public kDrive folder link**) | The public share link of a kDrive folder. Once set, the line shows the end of the link | text | empty |
| **Sync interval** | Hours between two background syncs | 1 to 24 h | 6 h |
| **Sync now** | Fetches the new issues at once. Below it: **Never synced**, or **Last sync:** with the date and time, or the result of the sync just done | | |
| **WebDAV URL** (**kDrive folder URL**) | Address of the WebDAV folder | text | empty |
| **Username** | Your name on the WebDAV server | text | empty |
| **Password** | Your password on the WebDAV server. Shown as dots once set | text | empty |

The last three are under the heading **WebDAV (alternative)**. They are used only when **kDrive share link** is empty. Tapping a text setting opens a dialog titled **Edit** with **Cancel** and **OK**.

## Your data

**On the device.** The list of issues, their covers and the article where you stopped in each are kept in the app's private storage. Issues fetched by sync and files opened from another app are copied there too. Issues added from **Browse** are not copied: the app reads them in your folder, and they must stay there.

**Over the network.** The app connects only for the sync:

- with a share link, to the kDrive service, without any account;
- with WebDAV, to the server whose address you entered, with your username and password.

Nothing else is sent, and nothing is ever uploaded. The WebDAV password is kept in the app's settings on the device, without encryption. The links of **About app** open in your browser.

**How the sync works.** The app lists the files placed directly in the folder (not in its subfolders) whose name ends in .epub, and downloads those whose file name is not already the name of an issue in the library. It is one way: it deletes nothing on the server and nothing on the device. An issue replaced on the server under the same name is not downloaded again. A file whose name contains `_blacklisted_` is ignored. The background sync needs a network connection and is tried again later after a failure.

**Removing.** Removing an issue from the library deletes neither your file nor the copy the app holds.

**Backup, export, import.** There is none, and the app is excluded from Android's own backup. Uninstalling the app deletes the library, the settings and the copies it holds; the files in your own folders are not touched.

## With the other apps

- The app offers itself in Android's "Open with" list for EPUB files.
- It has no home-screen widget, and it is not a share target.
- **Report**, on the crash screen, uses the Android share sheet.

## Limits

- EPUB only. The other file types listed in **Browse** cannot be added.
- No bookmarks, notes, highlighting, search inside the text, dictionary, choice of font or margins.
- The article page is always black on white.
- The text size is not kept from one reading session to the next.
- The position inside an article is not kept: an article reopens at its first page.
- The percentage badge of the library stays at 0 %.
- The whole EPUB file is loaded in memory when an article is opened.
- Sync: one way, top level of the folder only; the kDrive link must be public, without password.
- Many screens exist in English only, whatever the language chosen: the **Sync** settings, the messages of the reading screens.
- Android 8.0 or later.

## Messages and problems

| Message | Meaning and what to do |
|---|---|
| **This ePub is not a supported magazine** | The file is not a readable EPUB. Check that it is complete. |
| **Could not read the file.** / **Could not open the file.** | The file handed over by the other app could not be copied or read. Save it on the device first, then open it again. |
| **Loading…** | The issue or the article is being prepared. |
| **Could not access ePub file.** | The file is no longer where it was, or the app no longer has access to its folder. Put it back, or add the folder again in **Settings**, **Browse**. |
| **Could not parse this ePub.** | The app found no article in the file. |
| **Could not read article.** / **Article not found.** | The article is missing from the file or is damaged. |
| **File not found:** followed by a path | The copy of a file opened from another app is gone. Open the file again from that app. |
| **Book #… not found** | The issue is no longer in the library. Return to the **Library**. |
| **Nothing to display.** / **No content.** | The issue or the article is empty. |
| **Sync failed:** followed by a reason (in the **Library**) / **Failed:** followed by a reason (in **Sync**) | The sync did not complete. The reasons are listed below. |
| … **Invalid kDrive share link** | The text pasted is not a kDrive share link. Copy the link again from kDrive. |
| … **Share is not public** | The link is protected. Create a public link without password. |
| … **WebDAV credentials not configured** | **WebDAV URL**, **Username** or **Password** is empty. |
| … **WebDAV PROPFIND failed: 401** | Wrong username or password. Other numbers: 404, wrong folder address; 403, access refused. |
| … **HTTP** followed by a number | The kDrive service refused the request; check the link. |
| A result ending in **failed**, for example **1 new, 3 skipped, 2 failed** | Some files could not be downloaded or read. They are tried again at the next sync. |
| **Never synced** | No sync has succeeded yet. |
| **Something went wrong.** | Shown when you tap a red file in **Add books?**: the file is not an EPUB or cannot be read. |
| **No app to choose folder found.** | Android has no folder picker available for **Add folder**. |
| **No browser app found.** | No browser to open a link of **About app**. |
| **No app to share found.** | No app to send the crash report with **Report**. Use **Copy**. |
| **Copied** | The crash report is in the clipboard. |
| **Press back again to exit** | **Double back press to exit** is on. |
| **Slava Ukraini!** | Shown when you tap the trident icon at the bottom of **About app**. |
| **Closed source** | In **Open source licenses**, marks a library whose code is not public. |


# Le dictionnaire Littré

*Android 1.1.1*

The app exists in French only: every label on screen is in French whatever the language of the phone. In this chapter the labels are quoted as the app writes them, with their meaning in brackets the first time. On the phone, the app appears under the name **Le Littré**.

## What it is

Le dictionnaire Littré puts the whole of Émile Littré's *Dictionnaire de la langue française* (Paris, Hachette, 1873–1874) on your phone: 78 599 entries with their definitions, quotations and etymologies, and the 1878 supplement where an entry has one. You look a word up by typing its first letters, or you search inside the text of the definitions. A word of the day is shown in the app and can be placed on the home screen as a widget.

Everything is inside the app. It asks for no permission, not even Internet access, has no account and no advertising. It is a reading tool for one dictionary: it does not translate, does not pronounce words aloud, and does not let you add notes or edit anything.

## First start

The app asks nothing: no permission, no account, no server.

The first time it opens, it unpacks the dictionary into its own storage on the phone (about 165 MB). A turning circle is shown for a moment, then the search screen appears with the word of the day.

At that point the **Favoris** (favourites) screen is empty and says **Aucun favori pour le moment** (no favourite for now), and its **Historique** (history) tab says **Aucun historique** (no history).

## The screens

A bar at the bottom of the app gives three screens: **Recherche** (search), **Favoris** (favourites) and **À propos** (about). A fourth screen, the article, opens on top of them when you tap a word.

### Recherche

This is the screen the app opens on. Its title is **Littré**.

At the top is the search field, with the hint **Rechercher un mot…** (search for a word…). To the right of the field:

- a cross, shown only when the field contains text: it empties the field;
- the search-mode button. It switches between the two ways of searching. When it shows the letters A–Z, you are in **Recherche par mot** (search by word). When it shows an open book, in colour, you are in **Recherche dans les définitions** (search in the definitions). A long press on the button displays the name of the mode you are in.

Under the field:

- When the field is empty, the screen shows the **Mot du jour** (word of the day): a card with the word, its grammatical nature, the beginning of its definition, and the line **Appuyez pour lire l'article complet** (tap to read the full article). Tapping the card opens the article.
- When you type, the results appear by themselves a fraction of a second after you stop typing; there is no button to press. Each result is a card with the headword, its grammatical nature in italics (for example *s. f.*), and the first two lines of the definition. Tapping a card opens the article.
- When nothing matches, the screen says **Aucun résultat** (no result).

**Recherche par mot** lists the entries whose headword begins with what you typed, in alphabetical order, twenty at most. Capitals and accents do not matter: typing `etre` finds ÊTRE.

**Recherche dans les définitions** looks for what you typed in the headword, the text of the article (definitions, quotations, the historical section) and the etymology. It finds whole words only, and when you type several words it looks for them together, in that order, as a phrase. Capitals and accents do not matter. It returns fifty entries at most, the most relevant first. The 1878 supplement is not searched.

The search mode goes back to **Recherche par mot** each time the app is restarted.

### The article

The article screen shows, from top to bottom:

- the headword, in capitals as in the dictionary (for example MAISON, or ABAISSÉ, ÉE for an adjective with its feminine);
- its grammatical nature in italics and, when the dictionary gives one, Littré's pronunciation between square brackets;
- the body of the article: the senses, numbered when there are several; the quotations in italics, each followed by its author and reference between square brackets; then, where the entry has them, sections with a heading such as **PROVERBE** or **HISTORIQUE** (the old quotations, century by century);
- a framed box **ÉTYMOLOGIE** (etymology), when the entry has one;
- a tinted box **SUPPLÉMENT (1878)**, when the entry has an addition from Littré's supplement.

Underlined words in colour are cross-references: tapping one opens the article it points to, on top of the current one. The back arrow returns to the previous article, then to the list you came from.

At the top right are two buttons:

- the star: **Ajouter aux favoris** (add to favourites). When the article is a favourite the star is filled and yellow, and tapping it again does **Retirer des favoris** (remove from favourites);
- the copy button: **Copier l'article** (copy the article). It puts the article on the clipboard as plain text and confirms with **Article copié dans le presse-papiers** (article copied to the clipboard).

Opening an article, by any route, adds it to the history.

### Favoris

This screen has two tabs; you tap a tab or swipe sideways to change.

- **Favoris**: the articles you starred, in the order you added them (the oldest first). Tapping one opens it. When there are none, the screen says **Aucun favori pour le moment** and **Appuyez sur l'étoile dans un article pour l'ajouter ici.** (tap the star in an article to add it here).
- **Historique** (history): the last articles you opened, the most recent first, one hundred at most. An article opened again moves back to the top instead of appearing twice. Tapping one opens it. At the bottom, **Effacer l'historique** (clear the history) empties the list at once, without asking for confirmation. When the list is empty, the screen says **Aucun historique** and **Les articles consultés apparaîtront ici.** (the articles you have read will appear here).

### À propos

An information page, with nothing to tap. It shows the name **Littré**, the subtitle **Dictionnaire de la langue française**, a version line, and three cards:

- **Données lexicographiques** (lexicographic data): « Le Littré » by François Gannaz, littre.org, **Licence Creative Commons BY-SA 3.0**;
- **Texte original** (original text): Émile Littré, *Dictionnaire de la langue française*, Paris, Hachette, 1873–1874, **Domaine public** (public domain);
- **Application**: **78 599 entrées — consultation hors ligne** (78 599 entries, offline reading), and the author's credit.

The version line on this page reads **Version 1.0.0** even though the app is version 1.1.1; to know which version is installed, look at the app's page in the phone's settings.

## How to

### Look up a word

1. Open the app; you are on **Recherche**.
2. Check that the button at the right of the field shows the letters A–Z (**Recherche par mot**). If it shows the open book, tap it once.
3. Type the beginning of the word. Accents and capitals are not needed.
4. Tap the entry you want in the list.

If the word is not among the twenty entries shown, type more letters.

When a word has several entries (PRIME has eight), each one appears as its own card; the grammatical nature and the first lines of the definition tell them apart.

### Find a word written with œ or æ

The search by word does not find headwords containing the joined letters œ or æ (CŒUR, ŒIL, BŒUF…), whether you type `coeur` or `cœur`. To reach them:

1. Tap the search-mode button so that it shows the open book (**Recherche dans les définitions**).
2. Type the word with its joined letter, for example `cœur`.
3. Look for the entry in the results. They are sorted by relevance, not alphabetically, and limited to fifty, so the entry may be further down the list (CŒUR comes fourth for `cœur`, ŒIL twenty-eighth for `œil`).

### Search inside the definitions

1. On **Recherche**, tap the search-mode button so that it shows the open book.
2. Type a word, or several words that follow each other in the text (for example the start of a proverb or of a quotation).
3. Tap an entry in the results.

Type whole words: the beginning of a word is not enough in this mode. If you had already typed something before switching mode, the search is run again at once in the new mode.

### Go back to the search by word

Tap the search-mode button again so that it shows the letters A–Z.

### Empty the search field

Tap the cross at the right of the field. The word of the day comes back.

### Read the word of the day

Open the app with the search field empty: the word of the day is in the middle of the screen. Tap the card to read the whole article.

### Follow a cross-reference

In an article, tap an underlined word. The article it points to opens; the back arrow brings you back.

If nothing happens, see "Messages and problems".

### Add an article to the favourites

1. Open the article.
2. Tap the star at the top right. It turns yellow.

### Remove an article from the favourites

1. Open the article (from **Favoris**, for example).
2. Tap the yellow star. It becomes an outline again and the article leaves the list.

### Find an article you read earlier

1. Tap **Favoris** in the bottom bar.
2. Tap the **Historique** tab.
3. Tap the article.

### Clear the history

1. Tap **Favoris** in the bottom bar, then the **Historique** tab.
2. Tap **Effacer l'historique** at the bottom. The list is emptied immediately and cannot be recovered.

You cannot remove a single line from the history.

### Copy an article to paste it elsewhere

1. Open the article.
2. Tap the copy button at the top right (**Copier l'article**).
3. Go to the other app and paste.

The copied text contains the headword, the grammatical nature, the body of the article and, under the line "Étymologie :", the etymology. The pronunciation and the 1878 supplement are not copied, and the text has no italics or other formatting.

### Put the word of the day on the home screen

1. On your home screen, open the list of widgets the way your launcher provides (often a long press on an empty area, then "Widgets").
2. Under **Le Littré**, choose the widget **Mot du jour**; its description is **Le mot du jour du Littré** (the Littré's word of the day).
3. Place it. It takes four cells by two and can be resized in both directions.

Tapping the widget opens the app.

## Settings

The app has no settings screen. There are only two things that vary:

| What | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|
| Search mode (button at the right of the search field) | **Recherche par mot**, **Recherche dans les définitions** | **Recherche par mot**; it returns to it each time the app is restarted |
| Light or dark appearance | follows the phone's light or dark theme; nothing to set in the app | the phone's |

The text size, the typeface (Merriweather) and the colours cannot be changed in the app.

## Your data

Everything stays on the phone, in the app's private storage:

- the dictionary itself, unpacked at first start;
- your favourites;
- your history (the last hundred articles);
- the word of the day and its date.

Nothing goes over the network: the app does not have the Internet permission and cannot send or fetch anything. There is no account, no sync, no export, no import and no backup function in the app.

Uninstalling the app, or clearing its data in the phone's settings, removes the favourites and the history. The dictionary comes back with the app when you reinstall it; the favourites do not.

One thing is readable by other apps on the phone: the word of the day (the word, its nature and its definition). That is how Reader's Launcher displays it. Your favourites and history are not exposed.

## With the other apps

### Home-screen widget

The app offers one widget, **Mot du jour**. It shows the small heading **mot du jour**, the word, its grammatical nature and the first lines of its definition (three lines at most), in black on a white rounded card, whatever the theme of the phone. Tapping it opens the app on its main screen; to read the article, tap the word-of-the-day card there.

The widget and the app always show the same word. A word is drawn at random once a day, by whichever of the two is displayed first that day. The widget refreshes by itself about once an hour, so the new word appears within the hour after midnight.

### Reader's Launcher

Reader's Launcher has a **mot du jour** tile that reads the word from this app. The tile shows the word with **mot du jour** under it. Tapping it shows the whole definition and the etymology as plain text inside the launcher, with a row **open in Le Littré** (in French: **ouvrir dans Le Littré**) that opens this app. If Le Littré is not installed, the tile shows "Le Littré" instead of a word.

### What the app does not offer

The app does not appear in the share menu of other apps, does not appear in the text-selection menu, and does not open files.

## Limits

- French interface only.
- One dictionary only, in its 19th-century state: spellings, meanings and examples are those of 1873–1878.
- The search by word shows twenty entries at most, and does not find headwords written with œ or æ (164 entries). Use the search in the definitions for those.
- The search in the definitions shows fifty entries at most, finds whole words only, treats several words as an exact phrase, and does not look into the 1878 supplement.
- A large share of the cross-references do nothing when tapped: those that point to a word with an accented letter, to one particular entry among several with the same spelling, or to an entry whose headword carries an ending (for example ABONDANT, ANTE). The others open the first entry that has that spelling.
- The history keeps one hundred articles; older ones are dropped.
- Favourites cannot be sorted, grouped or exported.
- **Copier l'article** leaves out the pronunciation and the supplement.
- No audio pronunciation, no translation, no notes, no choice of text size.
- The widget is always black on white and opens the app, not the article.

## Messages and problems

| Message | Meaning | What to do |
|---|---|---|
| **Aucun résultat** | No entry matches what you typed in the current search mode. | Check the spelling; type fewer letters; check which mode the button shows. For a word with œ or æ, use **Recherche dans les définitions**. |
| **Aucun favori pour le moment** / **Appuyez sur l'étoile dans un article pour l'ajouter ici.** | You have not starred any article. | Open an article and tap the star. |
| **Aucun historique** / **Les articles consultés apparaîtront ici.** | No article opened yet, or the history was cleared. | Nothing; the list fills as you read. |
| **Article copié dans le presse-papiers** | The article's text is on the clipboard. | Paste it where you want it. |
| The widget shows **Le Littré** and **mot du jour** instead of a word | The widget could not read the dictionary. | Open the app once, then wait for the widget's next refresh. |

### The word I want is not in the list

The search by word shows the first twenty entries in alphabetical order. Type more letters. Adjectives are entered with their feminine ending (ABAISSÉ, ÉE) and are found by typing the masculine.

### Tapping an underlined word does nothing

The app did not find an entry with exactly that headword (see "Limits"). Go back to **Recherche** and type the word yourself.

### The word of the day is still yesterday's

The app picks the word when it displays the empty search screen. If the app stayed open overnight, close it and open it again, or type something in the search field and empty it.

### The About page says Version 1.0.0

That line is not kept up to date. The real version is the one shown by the phone in the app's information page.


# Clavier Plume

*Android 2.1.2*

## What it is

Clavier Plume is an Android keyboard for French and English, built first for writing French well: it restores elisions (l', d', qu', j'), accents and hyphenated forms, and applies French typography (curly apostrophe, non-breaking spaces, « » quotation marks). One key switches between the two languages, and the keyboard only learns a new word after you have used it three times. It is a modified version of the open-source FUTO Keyboard.

It works offline: the app has no Internet permission, no account and no advertising. It runs on Android 7.0 and later.

In this public edition, French correction comes from the dictionary and from the app's French rules; no neural language model is installed for French. A neural model for English, inherited from FUTO Keyboard, is built in and adds its proposals to the suggestion bar when you type in English. You can import other models from a file (see **Language models**).

It deliberately does not offer other languages than French (France, Switzerland, Canada) and English (US, UK), has no theme editor, does not sync anything, and does not update itself: updates come from the place you installed it from.

## First start

Open **Clavier Plume** from the app list. The first time, a walkthrough of eight steps appears. Each screen shows **Step 1 of 8** (and so on) with a progress bar. At the bottom: **Back**, **Skip** (steps 2 to 7) and **Next**; on the last step the button is **Done**. **Skip** changes nothing.

1. **Clavier Plume**: a presentation ("A French and English keyboard, built first for writing French well. One minute to set up."), a paragraph **What sets it apart**, and the line "Offline, no account, no ads: nothing leaves your phone."
2. **Your main language**: choose **Français** ("Curly apostrophe, non-breaking spaces, « » quotes") or **English** ("English spelling and punctuation"). The chosen card carries **Chosen ✓**. Below, **French of**: **Suisse**, **France** or **Canada**; and **English**: **US** or **UK**. The app pre-selects these from the phone's language and region. The main language "sets the typography and the toggle key. The other language stays available."
3. **Your layouts**: one layout **For French** (**QWERTZ (accents par appui long)**, **QWERTZ + é è à (Suisse)**, **AZERTY** or **QWERTY**) and one **For English** (**QWERTY** or **QWERTZ (accents par appui long)**), each with a preview. They are "suggested from the phone's region. Accents stay available with a long press on every layout." Tapping **Next** here installs these two languages with these layouts, replacing the previous list of languages.
4. **Two languages**: "Plume has a French and an English dictionary. How should they work together?" Choose **Robust — one language at a time** (pre-selected) or **Effortless — both languages at the same time**. "You can change this later in Settings → Languages."
5. **Enable the keyboard**: "Two Android settings, one tap each. You will come back here automatically."
   - **1. Allow Clavier Plume** ("In Android's keyboard list, turn on “Clavier Plume”") opens Android's keyboard list. Once done it reads **1. Clavier Plume allowed ✓**.
   - **2. Choose it as your keyboard** ("Pick “Clavier Plume” in the picker") opens Android's keyboard picker. Once done it reads **2. Keyboard in use ✓**.
   - "Next unlocks once both steps are done."
6. **Your theme**: tap a theme in the grid. "“Plume (auto)” follows the phone's light or dark mode. The Encre themes are high contrast." Below, the switch **Vibrate on key press** ("A light touch under the finger").
7. **The basics**: five reminders (**Switch language**, **Move the cursor**, **Read the suggestion bar**, **Learn and forget**, **Dictate**), then **Your contacts, optional**: "If allowed, the names in your address book are recognised and never corrected. They are read on the phone; nothing is sent." Tap **Allow contacts** to grant Android's contacts permission; the line then reads **Contacts recognised ✓**.
8. **Try it**: a text field ("Try typing here…") to test the keyboard. If your main language is French, the app suggests typing "jaime lordinateur voila" with a space after each word and watching the result. "Everything else lives in the settings, seven screens. This walkthrough stays available under About."

### Permissions

| Permission | When it is asked | What it is for |
|---|---|---|
| Microphone | The first time you tap the microphone | Dictation, done on the phone |
| Contacts | Only if you tap **Allow contacts** in the walkthrough | Recognising the names in your address book so they are not corrected |

The app also vibrates and reads and writes Android's personal dictionary. It has no Internet permission.

## The screens

### The keyboard

The keyboard has the letter rows, a shift key and a delete key, and a bottom row with the symbols key, the comma, the action key (emojis), the space bar, the period and the Enter key.

- **Long press on a letter** shows its accented variants and other characters.
- While a key is pressed, a balloon shows the character above your finger (setting **Key preview**).
- **Space bar, swipe sideways**: switches language (setting **Swipe on the space bar**).
- **Space bar, long press then slide**: the key labels fade and the keyboard becomes a trackpad that moves the cursor.
- **Delete key, press and slide left**: deletes characters as you slide.
- **Sliding over the letters** writes a word (setting **Swipe typing**).
- **Two spaces** insert a period followed by a space (setting **Double-space period**).

### The suggestion bar

Above the keys, from left to right:

- an arrow button that opens or closes the action row;
- the suggestions. "The middle word is applied on space. The word in quotes is exactly what you typed: tap it to keep it." A suggestion that comes from a neural model carries a small mark under the word;
- the **EN** / **FR** key, which shows the language you will switch to, and the microphone.

Other things can take the place of the suggestions:

- **↶ word**: just after an automatic correction, the bar starts with the word as you typed it, preceded by ↶. Tap it to undo the correction. It disappears as soon as you go on typing. The delete key right after a correction undoes it too.
- **Switch to English?** (or the other language): shown between two words after three consecutive words that belong to the other language and not to the active one. Tap it to switch; nothing is written. If you ignore it, it stays silent for the next twenty words. It only exists when exactly two languages are installed.
- the text you just copied, as a chip to paste it (setting **Copied text in the bar**);
- the fill-in proposals of your password manager, when the app you are typing in provides them.

**Long press on a suggestion** opens a question: "“word” — forget this learned word (the dictionary takes over again, it can be relearned later), or blacklist it for good?" with **Cancel**, **Forget (unlearn)** and **Blacklist**. On an emoji suggestion there is also **Disable emoji suggestions**.

**Long press on the EN / FR key** opens a list of your languages and layouts (tap one to use it), the other keyboards installed on the phone, and two buttons: **Switch Keyboard** (Android's keyboard picker) and **Settings**.

### The action row

The arrow at the left of the suggestion bar opens a second row with **All Actions** (three dots) followed by the favourite actions: **Undo**, **Redo**, **Text Editor**, **Clipboard Manager**, **Keyboard Modes**.

- **All Actions** shows every action as a grid: the favourites, **Emojis**, **Language switch key**, **Voice Input**, then **Settings**, **Paste from Clipboard**, **Theme Switcher**, **Cut to Clipboard**, **Copy to Clipboard**, **Select All**, **Arrow Up**, **Arrow Down**, **Arrow Left**, **Arrow Right**. Its title bar has an **Edit actions** button.
- **Edit actions** lets you drag each action into one of the groups **Action Key**, **Pinned Action(s)**, **Favorite Actions**, **More Actions**, **Hidden Actions**. Only one action can be the action key; pinning many actions shrinks the suggestion bar.

### Emojis

The action key next to the space bar opens the emoji panel: categories (**Recent**, **Smileys & Emotion**, **People & Body**, **Animals & Nature**, **Food & Drink**, **Travel & Places**, **Activities**, **Objects**, **Symbols**, **Flags**, **ASCII**), a **Search** field, a delete key and an **ABC** key to return to the letters. A long press on an emoji that has skin tones lets you choose one; the choice is remembered.

### Voice Input

The microphone opens the dictation panel. Speak; the panel shows **Try saying something**, then **Listening…**. Tap the panel to finish; the text is written at the cursor. Dictation also stops by itself when it detects silence. Recognition runs on the phone (Whisper).

### Clipboard Manager

Shows the clipboard history. While the history is off, the panel reads **Clipboard History Inactive** with the button **Enable Clipboard History**. Once on:

- tap an item to paste it; long press to pin or unpin it;
- the magnifier searches the history; the cross clears it (**Clear all unpinned items?** → **Clear**). When only pinned items remain it offers **Unpin**; when the list is empty it offers to **Disable** the history.

Up to 25 unpinned items are kept for 3 days; pinned items stay. Items marked as sensitive by the app they come from (password managers, private windows) are not kept.

### Keyboard Modes

A small panel with **Standard**, **One Hand**, **Split** (when the layout allows it) and **Float**, and the button **Resize Keyboard**. In resize mode, drag the handles, then **Apply**, or **Reset**. Size and mode are stored separately for portrait and landscape.

### Text Editor

A pad with four arrows, two toggles (Ctrl to move word by word, Shift to select) and keys to copy and paste.

### Theme Switcher

The grid of themes, above the keyboard, to change theme without leaving the app you are in.

### Settings home

Titled **Clavier Plume**, it lists seven screens: **Languages**, **Keyboard**, **Typing and correction**, **Action bar**, **Appearance**, **Backup**, **About**. The version number is shown under the list. At the very bottom, **Try typing here…** opens a field to test the keyboard.

### Languages

With exactly two languages installed, the card **Both languages** comes first, with two choices:

- **Effortless — both languages at the same time**: "Both dictionaries are consulted; the language you are typing in takes precedence. The EN/FR key still sets the layout and the typography."
- **Robust — one language at a time**: "Only the active language corrects; a word from the other language is treated as a typo until you switch."

Then one card per language with its **Layouts** (a cross removes a layout when there is more than one), **Add a layout**, and **Remove this language** (confirmation **Delete …?**, **Remove** / **Cancel**). When you have one language or more than two, each card has a checkbox **Suggestions in this language at the same time** instead of the **Both languages** card.

At the bottom, **Add language** opens **Select language to add**: French, French (Switzerland), French (Canada), English (US), English (UK). Choosing one opens **Select a layout to add** with a preview of each layout: besides the four of the walkthrough, Dvorak, Colemak, Colemak DH, BEPO, PC QWERTY and a few others.

### Keyboard

Sections **Size**, **Keys**, **Feedback** and **Space bar**; see Settings.

### Size and keyboard modes

Opened from **Keyboard** → **Size and keyboard modes** ("Resize, one-handed, floating, split"). The keyboard appears in resize mode. The screen reminds you that the same thing is available anywhere through the **Keyboard Modes** action, and has one entry: **Reset size settings** ("Tap to reset all sizes and modes for both portrait and landscape to default").

### Typing and correction

Sections **Correction**, **Typing**, **Neural model** and **Vocabulary**; see Settings. It leads to four screens:

- **Language models** ("Import a .gguf file and choose the model for each language"). The screen is titled **Transformer Models** and opens with the note "Neural language models. Clavier Plume's French/English model is imported from a .gguf file and assigned to a language." It lists the installed language models by language; tap one to see its details (in English only: **Details**, **Defaults**, **Actions**), make it the model of a language (**Set default model for …**), **Export to file** or **Delete**. Under **Dictation** it shows, for each of your languages, the dictation model in use: **Built-in model: Whisper multilingual 74** for French, **Built-in model: Whisper English 39** for English, or the name and size of a file you imported, with **Back to the built-in model**. **Precise dictation: download the 244 model (264 MB)** opens your browser on the download; "The 244 model is the most accurate but slower. Download it with the browser, then use Import a file below and choose the language." Last, under **Actions**: **Import a file (.gguf or .bin)**.
- **Learned words** ("Words the keyboard picked up, forget them in one tap"). Each line shows a word, its number of uses and its language, most used first. The star keeps the word for good (it goes to the personal dictionary and leaves this list); the cross forgets it. When empty: **No learned words yet.**
- **Personal dictionary** ("Your words and shortcuts"). First **For all languages** and one line per language with the number of words. Inside a list: **Add**, **Import a word list (txt, csv, Gboard, Hunspell, LibreOffice)**, then your words; tap a word to edit or delete it.
- **Ignored words** ("Words that are never suggested"). The switch **Block offensive words** ("Do not suggest potentially offensive words"), then **View and edit blacklisted words**: a field **Add to blacklist** with a + button, and the list, each word with a cross to remove it. When empty: **There are no blacklisted words.**

### Action bar

Sections **Action key**, **Voice input** and **Clipboard**; see Settings.

### Appearance

A grid of thirteen themes, each drawn as a small keyboard: **Plume (auto)**, **Plume clair**, **Plume sombre**, **Plume Ink (black on white)**, **Plume Ink inverted (white on black)**, **Plume Lavender**, **Plume Sage**, **Plume Nordic**, **Plume Terracotta**, **Plume Coquette**, **Plume Activist**, **Plume Sultry**, **Plume Nature**. Tap one to apply it. On Android 11 and later, a small round button at the bottom left shows or hides the keyboard so you can see the theme on it.

### Backup

Two entries: **Export configuration** ("Settings, personal dictionary and learned words to a file") and **Import configuration** ("Restore from an exported file").

### About

**Version 2.1.2** with **Tap to copy** (the message **Version copied** confirms), the lines "Clavier Plume is a modified version of FUTO Keyboard." and "Pierre Gallaz · developed with Claude Code", then:

- **Licences and credits** ("Contributors, third-party libraries"): **Translators**, **Layout contributors**, **Code contributors**, **3rd-party code & assets**.
- **Support FUTO Keyboard** ("Clavier Plume is built on FUTO Keyboard: pay for FUTO's license"): the **Payment** screen, titled **Pay for FUTO Keyboard**. The button **Pay (~$6.99 + tax)** opens FUTO's payment page in your browser; the money goes to FUTO, the makers of the keyboard Clavier Plume is built on. The screen also has **Already paid?** with **I already paid**, and **Other Options** → **Help** (FUTO's website). Nothing in Clavier Plume is locked if you do not pay.
- **Redo the walkthrough** ("Languages, layouts, theme, the basics").

### Import

When you choose a file to import, or open a keyboard file from a file manager (the app appears as **Import dictionary or model**), a screen titled **Import - …** names what the file is: **Voice Input Model**, **Transformer Model**, **Dictionary** or **Configuration Backup**. For a model or a dictionary: **Select the language to import for:** and the list of your languages. For a backup: the date of the file, a warning, and **Import** / **Cancel**.

## How to

### Switch between French and English

- Tap the **EN** / **FR** key at the right of the suggestion bar, or
- swipe sideways on the space bar, or
- tap **Switch to English?** (or the equivalent) when the bar offers it.

Switching changes the layout, the dictionary used for correction and the typography.

### Type French typography

In French (France and Switzerland), the keyboard does it for you:

- an apostrophe is written as a curly apostrophe (in English too);
- typing **;**, **!** or **?** after a word inserts a narrow non-breaking space before it, and **:** a non-breaking space. In Canadian French no space is added;
- the **"** key writes **«** followed by a non-breaking space, then, the next time, a non-breaking space followed by **»**;
- after "3e", "1er" and the like, the bar offers the ordinal forms (e, ème, er, re).

None of this applies in password, e-mail, web address and code fields, where no automatic space is added either. A web or e-mail address is not corrected.

### Accept or refuse a correction

1. Type the word. The middle suggestion is the one that will be applied when you type a space or a punctuation mark.
2. To keep exactly what you typed, tap the word in quotes at the left before typing the space.
3. If the correction has already been applied, tap the **↶** chip, or press the delete key once.

Undoing a correction counts as one use of the word you typed. Short everyday words (le, la, mes, mon, un, à, sur…; the, of, to, my…) are never replaced by another word.

### Move the cursor

Long press the space bar, then slide your finger. Or set **Swipe on the space bar** to **Move the cursor**. Or open **Text Editor** in the action row.

### Dictate

1. Tap the microphone in the suggestion bar.
2. The first time, allow the microphone.
3. Speak, then tap the panel to finish.

Dictation works in the language that is active on the keyboard.

### Get more accurate dictation

1. **Typing and correction** → **Language models** → **Precise dictation: download the 244 model (264 MB)**. Your browser downloads the file.
2. Back on the same screen, tap **Import a file (.gguf or .bin)** and pick the downloaded file.
3. On the **Import - Voice Input Model** screen, tap the language it should serve.

To go back: **Back to the built-in model** on the same screen.

### Make the keyboard learn a word, or forget one

- A word that is not in the dictionary is kept after its third use. A word you delete with the delete key right after typing it does not count.
- To keep a learned word for good: **Typing and correction** → **Learned words** → the star.
- To forget one: the cross on the same screen, or long press the word in the suggestion bar → **Forget (unlearn)**. It can be learned again.
- To ban a word: long press it in the suggestion bar → **Blacklist**, or add it in **Ignored words**.
- To stop learning altogether: turn off **Learn my words**.

### Add a word or a shortcut to the personal dictionary

1. **Typing and correction** → **Personal dictionary**.
2. Tap **For all languages** or a language.
3. Tap **Add**.
4. Fill **Word:** ("Type a word") and, if you want, **Shortcut:** ("Optional shortcut"): typing the shortcut will propose the word.
5. Tap **OK**.

To change or remove a word, tap it: **Edit word**, then **OK** or **Delete**.

### Import a list of your words

1. **Typing and correction** → **Personal dictionary** → a language (or **For all languages**).
2. Tap **Import a word list (txt, csv, Gboard, Hunspell, LibreOffice)** and pick the file.
3. A window **Import** reports "… added, … already known, … rejected".

Accepted: a plain text file with one word per line; a CSV or tab-separated file (the first column is the word); a Gboard dictionary export (zip); a Hunspell or LibreOffice .dic file. Words already in the built-in dictionaries or in your personal dictionary are counted as already known.

### Add a language or a layout

1. **Languages** → **Add language**, pick the language, then the layout.
2. For a second layout of a language you already have: **Add a layout** on its card.

With several layouts, the EN / FR key goes through them in turn; a long press on it shows the whole list.

### Change the size or use one hand

Open the action row → **Keyboard Modes**, then **Standard**, **One Hand**, **Split** or **Float**; **Resize Keyboard** to change the height. Or go through **Keyboard** → **Size and keyboard modes**.

### Use the clipboard history

1. Open the action row → **Clipboard Manager** → **Enable Clipboard History** (or turn on **Clipboard history** in **Action bar**).
2. From then on, what you copy appears there. Tap to paste, long press to pin.

### Put the language key or the emojis where you want

In **Action bar**: **Action key next to the space bar** shows or hides the key left of the space bar; **Language switch key** puts the language switch there instead of the emojis. For anything else, open the action row → **All Actions** → **Edit actions** and drag the actions between the groups.

### Save your configuration and restore it

To save:

1. **Backup** → **Export configuration**.
2. Choose where to save the file (its proposed name starts with "FUTOKeyboardSettings_" and ends with ".backup").
3. Wait for **Exporting configuration…** to finish ("Please do not close this menu until the process is completed (5 to 10 seconds)").

To restore, on the same phone or another one:

1. **Backup** → **Import configuration** and pick the file.
2. Read the warning, then tap **Import**.

Importing replaces everything: settings, personal dictionary, learned words, imported files.

### See the walkthrough again

**About** → **Redo the walkthrough**. The walkthrough starts again from its first step with its own proposals, not your current choices: use **Skip** on the steps you do not want to change. **Next** on **Your layouts** reinstalls the two languages and layouts shown there, and **Next** on **Two languages** applies the mode shown there.

## Settings

### Languages

| Setting | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **Both languages** (two languages installed) | **Effortless — both languages at the same time** / **Robust — one language at a time** | Robust |
| **Suggestions in this language at the same time** (one language, or more than two) | Checked / unchecked, per language | Unchecked |

### Keyboard

| Setting | What it changes | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **Number row** | "Show 1234567890 above the letters" | Off |
| **Key preview** | "Show the character above the key while pressing" | On |
| **Long-press hints** | "Small character in the corner of the keys" | Off |
| **Long-press duration** | How long to hold a key for a long press, 100 to 700 ms | 300 ms |
| **Vibration on key press** | Vibrates at each key | On |
| **Vibration duration** | Shown only when vibration is on: **System**, or up to 100 ms | **System** |
| **Sound on key press** | Plays a sound at each key | Off on a phone, on on a tablet |
| **Swipe on the space bar** | **Nothing**, **Move the cursor** or **Switch language** | **Switch language** |

### Typing and correction

| Setting | What it changes | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **Auto-correction** | "Correct the word when you type a space or punctuation" | On |
| **Show suggestions** | "Word suggestions above the keyboard" | On |
| **Emoji suggestions** | Offers emojis among the suggestions | On |
| **Auto-capitalisation** | "Capital letter at the start of each sentence" | On |
| **Double-space period** | "Two spaces insert a period followed by a space" | On |
| **Swipe typing** | "Slide your finger over the letters to type a word" | On |
| **Learn my words** | "Suggest words you type that are not in the dictionary" | On |
| **Block offensive words** (in **Ignored words**) | "Do not suggest potentially offensive words" | On |

With auto-capitalisation on, no capital is put after the abbreviations ex., max., op., cf., p., pp., e.g. and i.e.

### Action bar

| Setting | What it changes | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **Action key next to the space bar** | Shows the key left of the space bar; its line names the action it carries (**Emojis**). Turning it off moves that action to the action row. | On |
| **Language switch key** | "Replaces the emoji key with a language key"; the emojis go to the action row. Turning it off again brings the emoji key back and puts the language switch in the action row. Shown only while the action key is on. | Off |
| **Microphone key** | "Dictate text from the suggestion bar". Off: dictation is found in **All Actions**. | On |
| **Copied text in the bar** | "Offer the last copied text as a suggestion" | On |
| **Clipboard history** | "Keep copied items in the clipboard panel" | Off |

### Appearance

One choice among the thirteen themes. The default is **Plume Lavender**. **Plume (auto)** follows the phone's light or dark mode.

## Your data

Everything stays on the phone. The app cannot use the network: it has no Internet permission. The two links it contains (the 244 dictation model and FUTO's payment page) open in your browser, not in the app.

What the keyboard keeps:

- **Your settings**, languages, layouts and theme.
- **The personal dictionary**: your words and shortcuts are stored in Android's own personal dictionary, which is shared across the system.
- **Learned words and word counts**: the keyboard counts each word you validate, per language (words of 2 to 40 letters without digits, at most 50,000 entries), and remembers which words follow which, to rank its suggestions. Nothing is counted in password, e-mail, web address and code fields.
- **A correction journal**: each time a word is corrected automatically, picked in the suggestion bar or restored with ↶, the keyboard writes a line with the date, the word typed, the word written and the language. The file stays on the phone, in the app's own folder; when it reaches 50 MB it is set aside and a new one begins. There is no setting to turn it off.
- **The clipboard history**, only if you turned it on.
- **Imported files**: dictation models, language models, dictionaries.
- **Contact names**, read from your address book if you allowed it; they are not copied elsewhere.

**Export** puts all of the above (except the contacts) in one file that you save where you want; the app sends it nowhere. Keep this file private: it contains your words, your clipboard and the correction journal. **Import** replaces the current data with the file's. A file made by a newer version of the keyboard shows a warning and may not import properly.

There is no sync and no conflict handling: one phone, one configuration.

Uninstalling the app removes its settings, learned words, journal, clipboard history and imported files from the phone. The app also lets Android's own backup include its data, where the phone has such a backup.

## With the other apps

- Clavier Plume works in every app that has a text field. It offers no home-screen widget.
- It also registers with Android as a spell checker, under the name **Clavier Plume — correcteur**.
- It opens files: a dictionary, a dictation model, a language model or a configuration backup opened from a file manager can be handed to **Import dictionary or model**.
- It shows the fill-in proposals of your password manager in the suggestion bar.
- From the clipboard history it can paste an image into apps that accept images; otherwise it says **App does not support image insertion**.

## Limits

- Languages: French (France, Switzerland, Canada) and English (US, UK) only.
- No French neural language model is included; the keyboard does not say where to obtain one.
- Language models must be .gguf files made for this keyboard; dictation models are .bin files; dictionaries are compiled .dict files, not word lists.
- A word list of more than 20,000 words is refused. Entries with a space or longer than 48 characters are rejected.
- The clipboard history keeps 25 unpinned items for 3 days; these figures cannot be changed.
- The themes cannot be edited and no theme can be added from the settings.
- The model screens (details, export, delete) are in English only.
- The export file keeps the name "FUTOKeyboardSettings_…".
- No automatic updates from within the app.

## Messages and problems

| Message | Meaning and what to do |
|---|---|
| **Next unlocks once both steps are done.** | In the walkthrough, turn the keyboard on in Android's list, then choose it in the picker. |
| The walkthrough reappears when you open the app | The keyboard was turned off or replaced by another one. The app reopens on **Two languages**; tap **Skip** to reach **Enable the keyboard** and redo the two steps. (**Next** on **Two languages** would apply the choice shown on that screen.) |
| **Switch to English?** (or another language) | The last three words belong to the other language. Tap to switch, or keep typing to ignore. |
| **… added, … already known, … rejected** | Result of a word list import. Rejected lines contained a space, were too long or were headers. |
| **This file has … words — more than a personal list (limit 20000). Nothing imported.** | The file looks like a whole language dictionary. Import a shorter list. |
| **Error: Shortcut may not start with symbol** | Begin the shortcut with a letter or a digit. |
| **Error: This file could not be recognized. It may be invalid or it may have been corrupted. Try re-downloading it, or try a different file.** | The file is not a model, a dictionary or a backup of this keyboard. Check that the download finished. |
| **Error: This file appears to be a source wordlist (_wordlist.combined) instead of a compiled dictionary (.dict) file.** | For a list of your own words, use **Import a word list** in **Personal dictionary** instead. |
| **This file appears to be intended for a language (…) which is not enabled in settings** | Add that language first, or choose one of your languages knowingly. |
| **Are you sure you want to import this configuration backup? This will replace all of your settings, personal dictionary (system-wide), learned dictionary words, any imported resources, and more. If you’re unsure, make another backup before this.** | Importing overwrites. Export the current state first if in doubt. |
| **This configuration backup was created by a newer version of FUTO Keyboard, and it might not import properly. Try updating FUTO Keyboard first.** | The backup comes from a newer version of the keyboard. Update Clavier Plume, then import. |
| **Importing configuration… Please do not close this menu until the process is completed (5 to 10 seconds)** | Wait. |
| **Clipboard History Inactive** | Tap **Enable Clipboard History** to start keeping what you copy. |
| **Device Locked** — "Please unlock your device to access clipboard history" | The clipboard history and the theme settings are not available on the lock screen. |
| **Clipboard Error** | The history could not be read or saved. **Retry**, or **Delete** the history (all items are lost). |
| **App does not support image insertion** | The app you are typing in does not accept pasted images. |
| **Please go to Permissions, and enable Microphone permission to use voice input** | The microphone was refused. Tap the line to open the app's Android settings and allow the microphone. |
| **No audio detected, is your microphone blocked?** | Check that the microphone is not switched off in Android's quick settings. |
| **Error loading model. It may be corrupted or incomplete. Try reverting it to default or downloading again.** | The imported dictation model is damaged. **Back to the built-in model**, or download and import it again. |
| "Unable to load Transformer model for …, it may be corrupted or unsupported." | The language model of that language cannot be used; the keyboard carries on with the dictionary. Delete the model or choose another in **Language models**. |
| "This model is damaged, its metadata could not be loaded. It may be corrupt or it may not be a valid model file." | Delete the model from its screen. |
| **No learned words yet.** | Nothing has been learned yet: a new word appears here after its third use. |
| **There are no blacklisted words.** | The list of ignored words is empty. |

If a word you want keeps being corrected: tap the word in quotes to keep it; after three uses it is learned. To have it at once, add it to the **Personal dictionary**.

If a learned mistake keeps being proposed: long press it in the suggestion bar → **Forget (unlearn)**.

If French words are corrected while you write in English (or the reverse): switch language with the EN / FR key, or choose **Effortless — both languages at the same time** in **Languages**.


# Funky's 2P Games

*Android 1.35*

## What it is

Funky's 2P Games is a collection of 16 games for two players. You play them face to face on a single phone, each player using one half of the screen or taking turns; 8 of the games can also be played online, each player on their own phone, with a four-character room code or a quick match.

Every game carries its own rules behind a **?** button. There are no ads, no accounts and no purchases.

The app never plays against you: there is no computer opponent and no solo mode. It keeps no scores from one game to the next. Its texts are in English only.

## First start

The app asks for nothing: no permission, no account, no server to enter. It opens straight on the menu of games.

It uses the Internet connection only for online play. Without a connection, every game still works in local mode.

## The screens

### The menu

The menu is titled **2 Player Games**. Next to the title is a speaker button that switches the background music on or off.

Below, sixteen coloured cards in two columns, one per game: Tennis, 4 in a Row, Pool, Memory, Word Clash, Air Hockey, Tank Wars, Carrom, Mini Golf, Star Clash, Caro, Awalé, Duck-Day Chess, Hangman, Reversi, Horse Jump. Scroll to see them all. Tap a card to open the game.

A small green dot in the top right corner of a card means the game can also be played online. The eight games with the dot are 4 in a Row, Memory, Word Clash, Tank Wars, Caro, Awalé, Duck-Day Chess and Reversi.

At the bottom of the list, a line of credits: **Pierre Gallaz · developed with Claude Code**.

### The game screen

Every game uses the same frame. The bar at the top holds, from left to right:

- **◀**: leaves the game and returns to the menu, at once and without confirmation. The game in progress is lost.
- the name of the game;
- **?**: opens the rules of the game; tap **?** again, **Got it** or **×** to close them. The game is not paused while the rules are shown.
- the speaker button: music on or off, the same setting as on the menu;
- the status: whose turn it is, the score, or the result. In turn-based games it reads for example **P1's turn**, **Red's turn**, **Black's Turn**; online it reads **Your turn** or **Opponent's turn**.

The rest of the screen is the playing area. **P1** is player 1 and **P2** is player 2. While a game is open the screen stays lit.

In several turn-based games (Memory, Word Clash, Tank Wars, Carrom, Pool, Mini Golf, Hangman) a banner **Player 1, your turn!** or **Player 2, your turn!** flashes for a second when the turn passes.

### Play Mode

When you open a game with the green dot, a panel **Play Mode** offers two buttons:

- **Local**: both players on this phone.
- **Online**: each player on their own phone.

The other eight games start directly in local mode.

### Online Play

After **Online**, the panel **Online Play** offers:

- **Create Room**: opens a room and shows its code.
- **Join Room**: asks for the code of a room someone else created.
- **Quick Match**: joins any room waiting for this game, or opens one.
- **Back**: returns to the menu.

The waiting panel shows **Room:** followed by the code, **Waiting for opponent...**, the line **Share the room code with your friend** and a **Cancel** button, which returns to the menu.

The joining panel is titled **Enter Room Code**, with a field (its example reads **ABCD**), a **Join** button and a **Back** button that returns to **Online Play**.

### The end-of-game panel

When a game ends, a panel covers the playing area with the result (for example **P1 wins!**, **You win!**, **Draw!**) and often the final score. Under it, a button starts a new game: **Rematch** in most games, **Restart** in Caro, **New Game** in Hangman. The **×** in the top right corner closes the panel without starting again.

Pool and Mini Golf show the result without a button: to play again, go back to the menu with **◀** and reopen the game.

## How to

### Start a game on one phone

1. On the menu, tap the card of the game.
2. If **Play Mode** appears, tap **Local**.
3. Put the phone between you. In games played on two halves, P1 has the bottom half and P2 the top half (in Horse Jump, P1 has the top lane).

### Read the rules of a game

1. Open the game.
2. Tap **?** in the top bar.
3. Tap **Got it** to close.

### Play online: create a room

1. Both players open the app on their own phone, with an Internet connection.
2. Tap the game (one with the green dot), then **Online**.
3. Tap **Create Room**.
4. Read the four characters that follow **Room:** and give them to your friend. The characters `\n` that the screen shows right after the code are not part of it.
5. Wait. The game starts by itself as soon as your friend joins. You are the first player.

A code is made of capital letters and digits. It never contains the letters I, L and O nor the digits 0 and 1.

### Play online: join a room

1. Tap the same game as your friend, then **Online**.
2. Tap **Join Room**.
3. Type the four characters of the code under **Enter Room Code**. Capitals are set automatically.
4. Tap **Join**. The game starts at once. You are the second player.

### Play online: quick match

1. Tap the game, then **Online**, then **Quick Match**. The panel shows **Searching...**.
2. If a room is already waiting for this game, you join it immediately as the second player.
3. If none is waiting, the app opens a room for you and shows its code. You wait as after **Create Room**: the next player who taps **Quick Match** for the same game, or a friend who types your code, becomes your opponent.

### Play again

Tap the button of the end-of-game panel (**Rematch**, **Restart** or **New Game**).

Online, the button resets the game on your phone only: each player taps it on their own phone.

In Tank Wars, **Rematch** reloads the whole app and brings you back to the menu; reopen the game from there.

### Leave a game

Tap **◀**. Online, your opponent then sees **Opponent disconnected** and the game stops for them.

### Switch the music off

Tap the speaker button, on the menu or in the top bar of a game. Tap again to switch it back on.

### Tennis

One phone only. P1 is the red paddle at the bottom, P2 the blue paddle at the top.

- Aim: send the ball past your opponent's paddle.
- Play: slide your finger on your half of the screen (the strips marked **slide here**); your paddle follows it sideways. The ball bounces off the side walls and gets a little faster at every paddle hit. After a point the ball restarts from the centre towards the player who lost the point.
- Power-ups: a coloured disc appears on the court from time to time and vanishes after about ten seconds. When the ball touches it, the player who last hit the ball gets it. **BIG**: your paddle grows. **TINY**: the opponent's paddle shrinks. The lightning bolt: the ball speeds up at once. **\~\~**: slow motion. **?**: the ball becomes almost invisible. **U**: the ball curves towards your paddle. **\~**: the paddles shake. **2x**: a second ball comes into play, and each ball that gets through counts as a point. A short text such as **+Paddle → P1** names the effect and who received it.
- End: the first to 5 points wins. The status shows **P2: 0 | P1: 0**.

### 4 in a Row

Local or online. Red plays first; the second player is Yellow. Online, the creator of the room is Red.

- Aim: line up four of your discs, across, down or diagonally.
- Play: tap any cell of a column; your disc falls to the lowest free cell. The turn passes.
- End: **Red wins!** or **Yellow wins!** at the first line of four; **Draw!** if the board (7 columns, 6 rows) fills up.

### Pool

One phone only. P1 shoots first.

- Aim: pocket all the balls of your group, then the 8-ball.
- Play: put your finger on the table and pull away from the cue ball (the white one): the ball leaves in the direction opposite to your finger, and the further you pull, the stronger the shot. A dashed line shows the direction; the view zooms out when you pull far. Release to shoot.
- Groups: the first ball pocketed decides. Balls 1 to 7 are **solid**, 9 to 15 **stripe**; the player who pockets first gets that group, the other player gets the other one. The status then reads for example **P1's shot P1:solid P2:stripe**.
- Turn: you shoot again if you pocketed a ball of your group; otherwise the turn passes. If you pocket the cue ball, it returns to its starting spot and the turn passes.
- End: pocket the 8-ball after all your balls and you win. Pocket it before and you lose: **P1 pocketed 8-ball early! P2 wins!**

### Memory

Local or online. 32 cards, 16 pairs of fruit.

- Aim: collect more pairs than your opponent.
- Play: tap two cards to turn them over. If they match, they stay face up, you score 1 and play again. If not, they turn back and the turn passes.
- End: when all pairs are found, the player with the most pairs wins; equal scores give **Draw!**. The status shows **P1: 0 P2: 0** and whose turn it is (online: **You:** and **Opp:**).

A double tap on the empty space around the cards replaces one of the fruits with a surprise picture until the end of the game.

### Word Clash

Local or online. The game first asks **Choose Language**: **English** or **Français**. Online, the creator of the room chooses and the other player sees **Waiting for host to pick language...**.

- Aim: score more points than your opponent on a shared crossword.
- The board: a small crossword of hidden words, all made from the seven letters shown on the wheel below. The top line shows **P1:** and **P2:** scores and the number of tries left; under the grid, the count of words found (for example **2/6 words**).
- Play: put your finger on a letter of the wheel and slide to the next ones to spell a word of at least three letters; slide back to the previous letter to remove the last one; lift your finger to submit. A word of the grid fills in with your colour (P1 red, P2 blue) and scores its number of letters. A valid word that is not in the grid is a bonus word: 1 point, listed after **Bonus:**.
- Tries: you have 3 tries per turn. Every submitted word, right or wrong, uses one. **Hint (uses 1 try)** reveals one random letter of the grid and uses one too. After the third try the turn passes.
- The round arrow in the middle of the wheel shuffles the letters; it costs nothing.
- End: when every word of the grid is found and the current turn is over, the highest score wins; equal scores give **It's a tie!**

### Air Hockey

One phone only. P1 is the red mallet at the bottom, P2 the blue mallet at the top.

- Aim: push the puck into the opponent's goal, the dark slot in the middle of their end.
- Play: drag your mallet with your finger; it cannot leave your half. The speed of your mallet matters: a fast swipe smashes the puck (sparks, screen shake), a gentle one taps it. After a goal the puck returns to the centre.
- End: the first to 5 goals wins. The status shows **P2: 0 | P1: 0**.

### Tank Wars

Local or online. P1 is the red tank on the left, P2 the blue tank on the right. Each tank starts with 100 HP.

- Aim: destroy the opponent's tank.
- Play: on your turn, drag anywhere above the control area: left or right changes **Angle**, up or down changes **Power** (up for more). The barrel moves as you drag. Then tap the red **FIRE** button.
- The shell is pulled by gravity and pushed by the wind. The wind (**Wind:** with an arrow and a strength) is drawn at the start and stays the same for the whole game.
- The explosion digs a crater in the ground and damages any tank nearby, yours included: up to 40 HP for a direct hit, less the further away it lands. A shell that leaves the screen does nothing.
- End: a tank at 0 HP is destroyed and the other player wins.

### Carrom

One phone only. P1 plays White from the bottom baseline, P2 plays Black from the top one. The board holds 9 white pieces, 9 black pieces and the red Queen in the centre, with a pocket in each corner.

- Aim: pocket all nine pieces of your colour before your opponent.
- Play: touch the coloured strip along your baseline (marked **‹ ›**) to slide the striker left or right. Then touch outside the strip, or keep your finger down and drag off it, and pull back like a slingshot: the striker leaves in the direction opposite to your finger. A dashed line shows the direction and a bar under the board shows the power. Release to flick.
- Your colour pocketed: 1 point each, and you play again.
- The opponent's colour pocketed: they score the point, and your turn ends unless you also pocketed one of yours.
- The Queen: pocket her and you keep the turn. If you pocket one of your pieces in the same shot or in the next one, you score 3 more; the status shows **Cover Queen!** while you owe that shot. If you miss it, the Queen returns to the centre.
- Foul: if the striker falls in a pocket, your turn ends, a pending Queen returns to the centre and, if you had already pocketed pieces of your colour, one of them returns to the centre and you lose 1 point.
