- End: the first player with no piece left on the board wins. If you pocket your opponent's last piece, they win.

### Mini Golf

One phone only. Six holes.

- Aim: finish the six holes in fewer strokes than your opponent.
- Play: P1 plays the hole until the ball is in, then P2 plays the same hole from the same tee, then both move to the next hole. To putt, put your finger down and pull away from the ball: the ball leaves in the direction opposite to your finger, and the further you pull, the stronger the putt. Dots show the direction and a small bar the power; the view zooms out when you pull far. Release to putt. The ball bounces off the wooden walls.
- The screen shows **Hole 1/6**, the current player with their strokes, and both totals.
- End: after the sixth hole the lower total wins; equal totals give **Draw!**

### Star Clash

One phone only. P1 is the red ship at the bottom, P2 the blue ship at the top. A formation of aliens sits between them.

- Aim: be the last ship standing.
- Play: slide your finger on your half (the strips marked **P1 — slide here** and **P2 — slide here**); your ship follows it sideways and fires by itself as long as your finger is down.
- Lives: each ship has 3 hearts. Alien shots cost one heart, and so does a shot from the other ship that gets through. Small barriers in front of each ship stop shots and crumble; they are rebuilt at each wave.
- Score: 10, 20 or 30 points per alien depending on its row. Kills in a row build a multiplier (3 kills: x2, 5: x3, 8: x4); taking a hit resets it.
- Glowing aliens give an effect for a few seconds to the player who shoots them: a lightning bolt (faster fire), an explosion (bigger shots), a snail (slow ship and slow fire), scissors (short-range shots).
- Every 100 points you score, a red alien dives at your opponent, firing; shooting it is worth 50 points. Your shots cancel alien shots they meet.
- When all the aliens of the formation are destroyed, a new wave arrives (**Wave 2**, and so on). From wave 5, aliens move and fire much faster.
- End: the game stops as soon as one ship loses its last heart; the other player wins, whatever the scores.

### Caro

Local or online. A board of 13 by 13. Black plays first; online, the creator of the room is Black.

- Aim: line up exactly five stones, across, down or diagonally.
- Play: tap an empty point to place a stone. The turn passes.
- A line of six or more does not win. A line of five does not win either if the opponent blocks both ends, or blocks one end while the other touches the edge of the board.
- End: **Black Wins!** or **White Wins!**, with the winning stones highlighted; **Draw!** if the board is full. The button to play again is **Restart**.

### Awalé

Local or online. Twelve pits with 4 seeds each. P1 owns the bottom row, P2 the top row (online: **You** and **Opp**); each pit shows its number of seeds. When the phone is held upright, the board is drawn turned a quarter turn: P1's row is then on the left and P2's on the right.

- Aim: capture at least 25 seeds.
- Play: tap one of your pits that holds seeds. Its seeds are sown one by one in the following pits, counter-clockwise, skipping the pit they came from.
- Capture: if your last seed lands in one of the opponent's pits and brings it to 2 or 3 seeds, you capture them, and also those of the preceding opponent pits, one after the other, as long as they hold 2 or 3.
- End: the game stops when a player reaches 25 seeds, or when the player whose turn comes has no seed left on their side; in that case the player who just moved takes all the seeds that remain. The higher score wins.

### Duck-Day Chess

Local or online. A full game of chess, White first, with two ducks added. Online, the creator of the room is White and the other player sees the board from Black's side.

- Aim: checkmate the opponent's King.
- A turn has two steps. First, move: tap one of your pieces (dots mark where it can go, a ring marks a capture), then tap the destination. Castling and en passant captures are available; a pawn reaching the last row opens **Promote:** with Queen, Rook, Bishop and Knight. Second, the status asks you to place the Yellow Duck: tap any empty square.
- The Yellow Duck blocks its square: no piece can stop on it or pass through it. There is a single Yellow Duck: it stays in place while the other player chooses their move, is lifted when that move is played, and is placed again by the player who just moved.
- The Red Duck appears by itself and jumps to a random empty square after each Yellow Duck is placed. It blocks its square in the same way.
- The laser: every 5 moves (counting both players' moves), the Red Duck fires at one piece next to it, chosen at random, of either colour, and removes it. The gauge above the board, on the right, fills square by square until the shot.
- Kings are safe from the laser during the first 25 moves of each player. After that a skull appears next to the gauge, and a King hit by the laser loses the game at once.
- If the ducks leave a player with no possible move although they are not in check, the app moves or removes a duck by itself; the log reads **Canard de Secours!**
- The log under the board lists the last moves and events; the square of a King in check glows red.
- End: checkmate (**White wins by checkmate!**), a King removed by the laser (**White wins — King vaporized!**), or a draw by stalemate (**Draw by stalemate!**).

### Hangman

One phone only. The app picks the word; it is a French word if the phone is set to French, an English word otherwise.

- Aim: have the most points when the word is complete.
- Play: on your turn, tap a letter. If it is in the word, you score 1 point for each place it fills and you play again. If it is not, a part of the body is added to the gallows (**Wrong: 1/6**) and the turn passes. Letters already tried are greyed out and listed after **Used:**.
- End: when the word is complete, the player with more points wins; equal points give **It's a tie!**. At the sixth wrong letter the panel shows **Hanged!** with the word, and nobody wins. **New Game** draws a new word and resets both scores.

### Reversi

Local or online. A board of 8 by 8 starting with two black and two white discs. Black plays first; online, the creator of the room is Black.

- Aim: have the most discs of your colour at the end.
- Play: tap one of the squares marked with a small dot; these are your legal moves. Your disc must trap one or more straight lines of opposing discs between itself and another of your discs; all the trapped discs flip to your colour.
- If a player has no legal move, the status reads for example **White passes — no legal move** and the other player moves again.
- The status shows whose turn it is and the count, black first (**Black's turn 2–2**).
- End: when neither player can move, the larger count wins (**Black wins 40–24**); equal counts give a draw.

### Horse Jump

One phone only. The start screen is titled **EQUINE EDGE**. P1 rides the white horse in the top lane, P2 the brown horse in the bottom lane.

- Aim: stay in the race longer than your opponent.
- Play: tap anywhere to start (**Tap to start!**). Then tap your half of the screen to make your horse jump. Both lanes receive the same obstacles: hurdles, and holes in the ground once fifteen obstacles have been cleared. The pace quickens as the count of cleared obstacles rises.
- With a keyboard attached, P1 jumps with the keys on the left (Q, W, A, S, Space and their neighbours) and P2 with those on the right (P, O, L, K, Enter, the arrows and their neighbours).
- End: the first horse that hits a hurdle or falls in a hole loses (**P1 Wins!** or **P2 Wins!**); if both fall at the same instant, **Dead Heat!**. The panel shows **Hurdles cleared:** with the count.

## Settings

The app has a single setting.

| Setting | What it changes | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| Speaker button (menu and top bar of a game) | Background music during the games | on, off | on |

The choice is remembered. The music plays while a game is open and stops when you return to the menu; switching it on from the menu starts it at once. The sound effects of the games are not affected by this button; there is no switch for them.

The language of the words in Word Clash is chosen at the start of each game and is not remembered; the button that matches the phone's language is highlighted.

## Your data

On the phone, the app stores one thing: whether the music is on or off. It keeps no scores, no history of games, no names. Uninstalling the app removes that setting; there is nothing else to remove.

Local games send nothing over the network.

Online games go through a Firebase Realtime Database, a service run by Google. For each room the app sends: which game it is, the room code, a random number used to shuffle both phones the same way, the time the room was created, the presence of each of the two players, and the moves. A move is erased from the service once the other phone has received it. No name, no account and no address is sent.

A room is protected by its code only. While you wait in a room, another player who taps **Quick Match** for the same game can join it instead of your friend.

The part of the app that handles online play is fetched from Google's servers when the app starts. If it could not be fetched, **Play Mode** does not appear and the games open in local mode.

## Limits

- Two players, always: no computer opponent, no solo mode, no more than two.
- Only eight games can be played online; Tennis, Pool, Air Hockey, Carrom, Mini Golf, Star Clash, Hangman and Horse Jump are for one phone only.
- Online: no chat, no list of friends, no reconnection. If a player leaves or loses the connection, the game is over.
- No scores or statistics are kept after you leave a game.
- A game cannot be paused or saved; **◀** ends it.
- The interface is in English only. Word Clash has English and French words; Hangman follows the phone's language (French or English). French words are written without accents.
- Duck-Day Chess has no clock, no draw offer and no resign button.
- Pool and Mini Golf have no button to play again at the end.

## Messages and problems

| Message | Meaning | What to do |
|---|---|---|
| **Enter 4-char code** | The code typed under **Enter Room Code** has fewer than four characters. | Type the four characters. |
| **Joining...** | The app is looking for the room. | Wait. If it stays, check the Internet connection. |
| **Room not found** | No room has this code. | Check the code with your friend; O, I, L, 0 and 1 are never used. |
| **Wrong game** | The room exists but was opened for another game. | Go back, open the same game as your friend and join again. |
| **Room full** | The room already has its two players, or its game has started. | Ask your friend to create a new room. |
| **Searching...** | **Quick Match** is looking for a waiting room. | Wait. If it stays, check the Internet connection. |
| **Waiting for opponent...** | Your room is open and nobody has joined yet. | Give the code to your friend, or wait for a quick match. **Cancel** returns to the menu. |
| **Waiting for host to pick language...** | Word Clash online: the creator of the room has not chosen the language yet. | Wait. |
| **Opponent disconnected** | The other player left the game or lost the connection. The game is over. | Go back with **◀** and start a new online game. |
| **Opponent disconnected**, right after **Quick Match** | You were sent to a room whose creator had already left. | Go back with **◀** and tap **Quick Match** again. |
| **White passes — no legal move** (or **Black passes**) | Reversi: that player cannot move; the other plays again. | Keep playing. |
| **Cover Queen!** | Carrom: you pocketed the Queen and must pocket one of your pieces with this shot to keep her. | Aim at one of your pieces. |
| **P1 pocketed 8-ball early! P2 wins!** | Pool: the 8-ball went in before all the balls of the shooter's group. | The game is lost; reopen Pool to play again. |
| **Canard de Secours!** | Duck-Day Chess: the ducks left a player without a move, and the app moved or removed one. | Keep playing. |
| **Move 25 reached — Kings are no longer immune to the Red Duck laser!** | Duck-Day Chess: from this point the laser can remove a King. | Keep your King away from the Red Duck when the gauge is nearly full. |
| **Hanged!** | Hangman: six wrong letters; nobody wins. | Tap **New Game**. |

**Play Mode** does not appear on a game with the green dot: the online part of the app could not be loaded at start. Close the app, check the Internet connection and open it again.

After **Rematch** in an online game, nothing moves: your opponent has not tapped **Rematch** on their phone yet.

After **Rematch** in Tank Wars you are back on the menu: this is how the game restarts; open Tank Wars again.




<!-- Meditation -->


# Retreat Timer

*Android 1.13.1*

## What it is

Retreat Timer rings singing-bowl bells at the times of day you set, every day, so that a meditation retreat keeps to its schedule with no one at the bell. Each time rings three bells or one, and recorded talks can be scheduled to play at a set time exactly like the bells. What makes it different is that it is built to ring whatever the phone is doing: screen locked, hours of deep sleep, a restart during the night. A light on the first screen turns green when the phone is ready to be left alone.

The app has no countdown timer, no sitting of X minutes, no statistics, no account, no ads and no tracking. It uses the network for one thing only: downloading talks when you ask for them.

The app exists in English only; there is no language setting. It works on Android 8.0 and later, in portrait.

## First start

On Android 13 and later, the app asks once for permission to show notifications. The notification is where the playback controls appear while a bell or a talk is playing, and where a download or the speaker keep-alive shows that it is running.

There is no account and no server to enter. The app opens on the **Schedule** tab, under the title **Retreat Timer** and the line **Bells and talks, on time, all day — even with no one present.**

When nothing is set up yet you see:

- an amber card, **⚠ One step left for guaranteed playback**, with the text **Tap below so Android never pauses the app. Without this, deep sleep could delay playback.** and one or two buttons: **Allow background running** and, if you refused notifications, **Allow notifications**;
- the **Bell sound** card, with **Singing bell** chosen;
- the **Levels** section, with **Bells** and **Talks** both at **100%**;
- the **Keep Bluetooth speaker awake** card, switched off;
- the line **Nothing scheduled yet — add a bell below or schedule a talk.**;
- the button **Add first bell**.

The one thing to do on the first day is to tap **Allow background running** and accept the phone's request to stop optimising the battery for this app. Once this is done and notifications are allowed, the card turns green: **✓ Ready — bells and talks will play on time**. The card checks again about every half minute, so it can take a moment to change.

## The screens

The app has two tabs, **Schedule** and **Library**, under the title. A player bar appears at the bottom of both whenever a scheduled bell or a talk is playing.

### Schedule tab

From top to bottom:

**The readiness card.** Green, with **✓ Ready — bells and talks will play on time** and **The phone can be locked or asleep all day. Keep it charged and everything plays without anyone present.**, when the app is exempt from battery optimisation and notifications are allowed. Amber, with **⚠ One step left for guaranteed playback**, otherwise. **Allow background running** opens the phone's request to exempt the app from battery optimisation; **Allow notifications** opens the app's notification settings in the phone. Each button is shown only while its step is missing.

**Bell sound.** One line per sound, each with a round selector on the left and **Preview** on the right:

- **Singing bell**
- **Tibetan bowl (E♭)**
- **Gong bowl**
- **Satipanya**
- **enpleineconscience.ch**

Tap the selector to make that bowl the sound of every bell. Tap **Preview** to hear its three-strike recording; previewing does not change the choice. Below the five bowls is the line **Your own sound** with **Choose file**. Once you have chosen a file, the line carries the file's name, a play button to hear it once, and **Change** to pick another file; its selector becomes usable. Two notes close the card: **Your own sound is an mp3 or wav file from the phone; it stands in for the single strike, and a three-bell slot plays it three times over.** and **The chosen bowl rings out fully between strikes. Whether a slot rings three times or once is set on the bell itself — tap its time.**

**Levels.** The text reads **Overall loudness follows the phone's volume — and, over Bluetooth, your speaker's. These only trim bells and talks down from there, so the two stay matched through an unattended day.** Two cards follow:

- **Bells**: a percentage, a **Test bell** button and a slider from 5% to 100%. **Test bell** rings one strike of the chosen sound at the level shown. If you move the slider while it rings, you hear the change at once. The note reads **Trims the bells down from the phone/speaker volume. 100% = full. Test rings one bell — enough to judge the level without sitting through three.**
- **Talks**: the same, with **Test talk**. It plays the next talk on the schedule, or, if no talk is scheduled, the first talk of the Library; the note names it: **Trims talks independently of the bells, so speech and bowl strikes stay matched. Test plays “…”; adjust while it runs.** With no talk in the app the button is greyed and the note reads **Trims talks independently of the bells. Add a talk in the Library tab to test it.**

**Keep Bluetooth speaker awake.** A switch, off by default. Under the title, one line says whether a wireless speaker is there: **✓ A Bluetooth speaker is connected** or **No Bluetooth speaker connected right now**. The explanation reads: **Portable speakers often disconnect after a few minutes of silence, so the next bell comes faintly from the tablet instead. This holds the link open with a silent keep-alive signal between bells — the same trick as the “silence + bells” loops centres play. Turn it on only when using an external speaker, and keep the tablet charged.**

**The next line.** **Next at 06:30 — three bells — in 1h 5min**, with a small bell symbol before the words **three bells** or **one bell**, or a headphones symbol before the title of a talk. Under one hour it reads **in 12min**. With nothing switched on in the schedule it reads **Nothing scheduled yet — add a bell below or schedule a talk.**

**The schedule.** One card per entry, sorted by time of day. Bells and scheduled talks are in the same list. Each card shows:

- the time, on 24 hours, in large type. Tap it to change the time (and, for a bell, three bells or one);
- for a bell, a badge **×3** or **×1** beside the time;
- under the time, **Three bells** or **One bell** after a bell symbol, or the talk's title after a headphones symbol;
- a play button: for a bell, it rings that bell now, three strikes or one, as a preview; for a talk, it starts the talk in the player bar;
- a switch: off, the entry stays in the list, greyed, and does not play;
- a bin: removes the entry at once, without asking.

**Add bell** (written **Add first bell** while the schedule is empty) opens the time picker.

At the bottom: **Tip: leave the phone plugged in. Each bell entry plays three singing-bowl strikes, or one if you set it to (×3 / ×1 beside its time); scheduled talks play your chosen recording.**, then **Pierre Gallaz · developed with Claude Code**.

### Pick a time

This panel opens when you add a bell, tap a time in the schedule, or schedule a talk. It is titled **Pick a time**.

- It opens ready for typing: two fields, hours and minutes, on 24 hours. Any minute can be typed. For a new entry the fields start at the current time.
- **Dial** switches to a clock face; **Type** switches back to the fields.
- For a bell, the section **This bell rings** offers **Three bells** and **One bell**, each with a play button to hear it with the chosen sound at the current bell level. A new bell starts on **Three bells**. This section is absent for a talk.
- **Set** saves. **Cancel**, or a tap outside the panel, closes it without changing anything.

### Library tab

The Library holds the talks. At the top: **Add dharma talks from your phone, a kDrive share link or a podcast feed, play them, or schedule one to play at a set time — exactly like the bells.**

Three buttons:

- **Add from this phone (MP3)** opens the phone's file picker. Despite the label, any audio file the phone can play is accepted.
- **Download from kDrive link** opens the kDrive panel.
- **Download from podcast feed** opens the podcast panel.

Below, **No talks yet.** or one card per talk, with its title (the file's name without its ending, or the episode's title) and:

- **Play**: plays the talk now; the player bar appears;
- **Schedule**: opens **Pick a time**, then adds the talk to the schedule at that time, every day, and shows the Schedule tab;
- a bin: removes the talk from the Library at once, without asking, together with every schedule entry that plays it.

### Download from kDrive

A panel titled **Download from kDrive**.

1. The field **Public share link** takes the address of a public kDrive share; the example shown is **https://kdrive.infomaniak.com/app/share/…**. The last link you used is already filled in.
2. **Connect** (it reads **Connecting…** while working) lists the audio files of the shared folder, most recently changed first. The result line reads **3 audio file(s) found.** or **No audio files found in that share.**
3. Each file shows its name and size, and a download button. Tap it: a turning circle replaces the button while the file is waiting or downloading, then **✓** when the talk is in the Library. A file whose name matches a talk already in the Library shows **✓** from the start.
4. **Done** closes the panel. Downloads go on without it.

After you tap a download button the result line reads **Downloading in the background — it lands in your Library when done. You can close this.**

### Download from podcast feed

A panel titled **Download from podcast feed**, built the same way.

1. The field **Feed URL** takes the address of a podcast feed; the example shown is **https://…/feeds/teacher.xml**. The last address you used is already filled in.
2. **Fetch episodes** (it reads **Fetching…** while working) lists the episodes in the order of the feed. The result line reads **12 episode(s) found.** or **No episodes found in that feed.**
3. Each episode shows its title and, when the feed gives them, its length (**1h 05m** or **39m 55s**) and its size, and a download button that behaves as in the kDrive panel.
4. **Done** closes the panel.

### The player bar

A dark bar at the bottom of both tabs while a talk plays, and also while a scheduled bell rings. It shows:

- the title of the talk (or **Retreat Timer** for a bell);
- a progress bar you can drag to any point of the recording;
- **0:42 elapsed** on the left and **−38:10 left** on the right (hours appear past one hour); **Playing…** if the length is not known;
- five buttons: back to the beginning, back 10 seconds, pause or play, forward 10 seconds, stop.

Stop ends playback and the bar goes away. The bar also goes away when the recording ends.

The previews (**Preview**, **Test bell**, the play button of a bell in the schedule, the play buttons in **Pick a time**) do not use the player bar. A preview stops when it ends, when you start another preview, or when the app is closed.

### The notifications

The app has three kinds of notification, each in its own category in the phone's settings.

**Bell ringing** (described as **Plays the bells at scheduled times.**). Shown while a bell rings or a talk plays; it makes no sound and no vibration of its own. Its title is **Retreat Timer** for a bell or the talk's title. Its text is first **Ringing the three bells…**, **Ringing the bell…** or **Playing dharma talk…**, then the position, for example **0:42 / 38:52 · 38:10 left**, with a progress bar. Four buttons: **−10s**, **Pause** or **Play**, **+10s**, **Stop**. Tapping the notification opens the app.

**Keep speaker awake** (described as **A silent keep-alive signal so a Bluetooth speaker won't sleep between bells.**). Shown for as long as the keep-alive runs: **Keeping the speaker awake**, **Holding the Bluetooth link open with a silent signal.**, with a **Stop** button.

**Downloads** (described as **Downloads dharma talks in the background so a transfer survives the screen locking.**). Shown while talks download: the talk's title and **Downloading talk…**, followed by **(2/3)** when several are queued.

## How to

### Make the phone ready to be left alone

1. On the **Schedule** tab, tap **Allow background running** in the amber card and accept the phone's request.
2. If the card also shows **Allow notifications**, tap it and allow notifications for the app.
3. Wait for the card to turn green: **✓ Ready — bells and talks will play on time**.
4. Leave the phone plugged in.

### Add a bell

1. On the **Schedule** tab, tap **Add bell** (or **Add first bell**).
2. Type the hour and the minutes, on 24 hours.
3. Under **This bell rings**, choose **Three bells** or **One bell**.
4. Tap **Set**.

The bell is on straight away and rings every day at that time.

### Change a bell's time, or switch it between three bells and one

1. Tap the time on the bell's card.
2. Change the time, or the choice under **This bell rings**, or both.
3. Tap **Set**.

### Switch a bell off without removing it

Turn off the switch on its card. The time turns grey and the bell no longer rings. Turn the switch on again to bring it back.

### Remove a bell or a scheduled talk

Tap the bin on its card. It is removed at once. Removing a scheduled talk leaves the talk itself in the Library.

### Hear a bell before the day

Tap the play button on the bell's card: it rings as it will at its time, three strikes or one, with the chosen sound and at the **Bells** level.

### Choose the bell sound

1. In the **Bell sound** card, tap **Preview** on a bowl to hear it.
2. Tap the selector to the left of the one you want.

The choice applies to every bell of the schedule. The five bowls are recorded at matched loudness, so changing bowl does not change the volume in the room.

### Use your own sound as the bell

1. In the **Bell sound** card, tap **Choose file** on the line **Your own sound**.
2. Pick an mp3 or wav file in the phone's file picker.
3. The app keeps its own copy, plays it once, and selects it.

A bell set to one bell plays the file once; a bell set to three plays it three times in a row. To go back to a bowl, tap that bowl's selector; to return to your file, tap its selector. To replace it, tap **Change**; the new file takes the place of the old one.

### Set the volume

1. Set the phone's alarm volume (and the speaker's own volume, with a Bluetooth speaker) to the loudest you will need. Bells and talks are played as alarms, so it is the alarm volume that counts, not the media volume.
2. In **Bells**, tap **Test bell** and drag the slider until the level is right for the room.
3. In **Talks**, tap **Test talk** and drag the slider while the talk plays. If the talk opens with a silence, drag the progress bar of the player bar to reach the speech.
4. Tap stop in the player bar.

### Add a talk that is on the phone

1. Open the **Library** tab and tap **Add from this phone (MP3)**.
2. Pick the audio file.

The talk appears in the list under the file's name. The file stays where it is on the phone; do not move or delete it afterwards.

### Download talks from a kDrive link

1. In the **Library** tab, tap **Download from kDrive link**.
2. Paste the public share link and tap **Connect**.
3. Tap the download button of each talk you want.
4. Tap **Done**. Each talk appears in the Library when its download ends.

### Download talks from a podcast

1. In the **Library** tab, tap **Download from podcast feed**.
2. Paste the feed's address and tap **Fetch episodes**.
3. Tap the download button of each episode you want.
4. Tap **Done**.

Downloads run one after the other and continue with the panel closed, the app closed or the screen locked.

### Play a talk now

In the **Library** tab, tap **Play** on the talk. Use the player bar, or the notification, to pause, move by 10 seconds, go back to the beginning or stop.

### Schedule a talk

1. In the **Library** tab, tap **Schedule** on the talk.
2. Type the time and tap **Set**.

The app shows the **Schedule** tab with the talk in the list. It plays every day at that time until you switch it off or remove it. To change its time, tap the time on its card.

### Remove a talk from the Library

Tap the bin on the talk's card. The talk and all its entries in the schedule are removed at once.

### Ring through a Bluetooth speaker

1. Connect the speaker to the phone in the phone's Bluetooth settings.
2. In the app, check that the **Keep Bluetooth speaker awake** card reads **✓ A Bluetooth speaker is connected**.
3. Turn the switch on, so the speaker does not disconnect during the silences.
4. Set the levels with **Test bell** and **Test talk**.
5. Keep the phone and the speaker plugged in.

When a Bluetooth speaker is connected, the app sends bells and talks to it rather than to the phone's own speaker.

### Stop a bell or a talk that is playing

Tap stop in the player bar, or **Stop** in the notification.

## Settings

All settings are on the **Schedule** tab; there is no separate settings screen.

| Setting | What it changes | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Bell sound** | The sound of every bell | **Singing bell**, **Tibetan bowl (E♭)**, **Gong bowl**, **Satipanya**, **enpleineconscience.ch**, or your own file | **Singing bell** |
| **Your own sound** | A file from the phone used as the bell | one mp3 or wav file | none |
| **Bells** (under **Levels**) | How far the bells are turned down from the phone's volume | 5% to 100% | 100% |
| **Talks** (under **Levels**) | How far the talks are turned down from the phone's volume | 5% to 100% | 100% |
| **Keep Bluetooth speaker awake** | Sends a silent signal between bells so a wireless speaker stays connected | on, off | off |
| **This bell rings** (in **Pick a time**, per bell) | How many strikes this bell rings | **Three bells**, **One bell** | **Three bells** |
| Switch on each schedule card | Whether this entry plays | on, off | on |

The two readiness steps belong to the phone, not to the app: the exemption from battery optimisation and the permission to show notifications. Both are missing on a fresh install.

The app also remembers the last kDrive link and the last podcast address you used.

## Your data

Everything stays on the phone: the schedule, the settings, the list of talks, the copy of your own bell sound and the talks you downloaded. There is no account, no sync and no tracking.

The network is used only when you ask for talks:

- **Connect** and the kDrive downloads contact Infomaniak's kDrive service, with the public link you pasted;
- **Fetch episodes** contacts the address of the feed, and each episode is downloaded from the address the feed gives for it.

Nothing else is sent, to anyone.

Talks are kept in two ways. A talk added with **Add from this phone (MP3)** is not copied: the app remembers where the file is and reads it there, so the file must stay in place. A talk downloaded from kDrive or from a podcast is saved inside the app. Your own bell sound is copied into the app when you choose it; the original file can be moved or deleted afterwards.

Removing a downloaded talk from the Library takes it out of the list and out of the schedule; the app does not erase the downloaded file itself, which keeps its place in the app's storage until you clear the app's data or uninstall it.

The schedule is set again by itself after the phone restarts, after a change of time or time zone, and after an update of the app. If the keep-alive switch is on, the silent signal starts again at the same moments.

There is no export and no import. The app does not exclude itself from Android's device backup; if you have turned that on in the phone, Android may include the app's data in it.

Uninstalling the app erases the schedule, the settings, the downloaded talks and the copy of your bell sound. Files you added from the phone are not touched.

## With the other apps

Retreat Timer has no home-screen widget and no tile in Reader's Launcher, and it does not receive content shared from other apps.

It reads audio files through the phone's file picker (for talks and for your own bell sound), public share links from kDrive, and podcast feeds.

## Limits

- The schedule repeats every day. You cannot choose days of the week or dates.
- There is no countdown and no timed sitting: only times of day.
- One bell sound serves the whole schedule. Only the number of strikes, three or one, is set per bell. One sound of your own is kept at a time.
- A bell or talk that could not play at its time because the phone was off is skipped, not played late.
- Only one thing plays at a time. A bell or a talk that starts while another is playing cuts it off; a bell placed in the middle of a scheduled talk ends the talk.
- The levels cannot go under 5%, so that a slip of the finger never silences a bell.
- The kDrive panel lists the audio files at the top of the shared folder only (mp3, m4a, aac, wav, ogg, flac, opus, mp4), not those in sub-folders, and only for a public share.
- The podcast panel lists only episodes that carry an audio file. Episodes are downloaded, never streamed.
- A download shows no percentage. A failed download is discarded whole and starts over from the beginning.
- A talk cannot be renamed, and the Library cannot be reordered.
- The app is in English only, in light colours only, and in portrait only.

## Messages and problems

| What you see | What it means, what to do |
|---|---|
| **⚠ One step left for guaranteed playback** | The app is not exempt from battery optimisation, or notifications are not allowed. Tap the button or buttons in the card. |
| **✓ Ready — bells and talks will play on time** | Nothing to do. Keep the phone charged. |
| **Nothing scheduled yet — add a bell below or schedule a talk.** | The schedule is empty, or every entry is switched off. |
| **No Bluetooth speaker connected right now** | The phone sees no wireless speaker. Bells will come from the phone itself. Reconnect the speaker in the phone's Bluetooth settings. |
| **No talks yet.** | The Library is empty. Use one of the three buttons above. |
| **Trims talks independently of the bells. Add a talk in the Library tab to test it.** | **Test talk** is greyed because the app has no talk to play. |
| **Could not connect: Not a kDrive share link** | The address is not a kDrive share link. It must contain **/app/share/** followed by a number and the link's code. |
| **Could not connect: This share is not public (…)** | The share is protected. Ask its owner for a public link. |
| **Could not connect: …** with another text | The kDrive service could not be reached or refused the request. Check the connection and the link, then tap **Connect** again. |
| **No audio files found in that share.** | The shared folder holds no audio file at its top level. |
| **Could not read the feed: …** | The address could not be reached, or what it returns is not a podcast feed. Check the address and tap **Fetch episodes** again. |
| **No episodes found in that feed.** | The feed has no episode with an audio file. |
| **Downloading in the background — it lands in your Library when done. You can close this.** | The download is under way; you can tap **Done**. |
| **Failed: … — tap to retry** under a file or an episode | The download failed and nothing was kept. Tap the download button on that line to try again. |
| **Playing…** in the player bar | The length of the recording is not known; the progress bar is not shown. |

**The green card does not appear after you allowed everything.** The card checks about every half minute. Wait a moment.

**Nothing happens after choosing your own sound.** The phone could not read the file as a sound, so the app kept what it had. Choose another file, mp3 or wav.

**A talk added from the phone no longer plays.** The file was moved, renamed or deleted. Remove the talk from the Library and add it again from where it is.

**A scheduled talk stops before its end.** Another entry of the schedule started while it was playing. Leave enough time after a talk before the next bell.

**The bell comes from the phone instead of the speaker.** The speaker disconnected. Reconnect it, then turn on **Keep Bluetooth speaker awake**.

**You tapped Stop in the notification Keeping the speaker awake and the switch in the app is still on.** The silent signal is stopped all the same. To start it again, turn the switch off and on.

**The bell is too quiet even at 100%.** The levels only turn the sound down. Raise the phone's alarm volume, and the speaker's own volume.

**The bell rings while the phone is on silent.** That is intended: bells and talks are played as alarms. To have silence, turn off the switches in the schedule.

**An alarm icon shows in the phone's status bar.** Each entry of the schedule is set as an alarm-clock alarm, the kind the phone fires at the exact time, so the phone shows its usual alarm sign.


# Retreat Player

*Android 1.5.1*

## What it is

Retreat Player plays the recordings of a meditation retreat: talks, bells, guided meditations and chanting. You load recordings from the phone's files, from a shared kDrive folder or from a podcast feed; each one is copied into the app, so playing needs no network and airplane mode is fine. Its one principle is that once a recording has started, nothing interrupts it: it keeps playing with the screen off, it does not pause or lower itself for another app's sound, and when it ends nothing else starts. It is not a music player: there are no playlists, no automatic next recording, no shuffle, no playback speed, no sleep timer, no account and no advertising.

The app is in English only, whatever the phone's language. It works in portrait only and needs Android 8.0 or later.

## First start

On Android 13 and later, the app asks once for permission to show notifications. It uses two notifications: one while a recording plays (with pause, stop and 10-second buttons) and one while recordings download. The app does not insist: it carries on whether you accept or refuse.

The app asks for no other permission. To load files from the phone it opens the phone's own file chooser, and it only receives the files you pick there.

There is no account and nothing to fill in. The first screen shows:

- the title **Retreat Player** and the line **Talks and chanting, stored on this phone, uninterrupted.**;
- a round **+** button at the top right;
- three sections, **Bells & chanting**, **Guided meditations** and **Dharma talks**, each with the line **Nothing here yet — load recordings with the + button.**;
- a dark band at the bottom with **Nothing playing — tap ▶ on a recording**.

Tap **+** to load your first recordings.

## The screens

The app has one screen (the home page), with the player as a band at its bottom, and one panel for loading recordings.

### Home

At the top are the app's name, its subtitle and the round **+** button, which opens the **Load recordings** panel.

Below, the recordings are listed in sections, always in this order:

1. **Bells & chanting**
2. **Guided meditations**
3. **Dharma talks**
4. **Just loaded — move into a category**

The first three sections are always there; an empty one shows **Nothing here yet — load recordings with the + button.** The fourth, written in brown, appears only while it holds something: every recording you load arrives there, at the bottom of the page, and waits to be filed.

Each recording is a white tile. From left to right it carries:

| Control | What it does |
|---|---|
| **▶** | Plays this recording from its beginning. If another recording was playing, it is replaced. On the tile of the recording that is in the player, the button shows the pause sign while it plays and pauses or resumes it. |
| Title and length | The title (two lines at most) and, in brown, the length: **< 1 min**, **58 min**, **1 h 02**. The length is missing when the app could not read it. Tapping the title does nothing; only **▶** starts playback. |
| **▲** | Moves the tile one place up within its section. Greyed out on the first tile of a section. |
| **▼** | Moves the tile one place down within its section. Greyed out on the last tile of a section. |
| **⋮** | Opens the tile's menu. |

The tile of the recording that is in the player is outlined in brown.

The **⋮** menu holds:

| Entry | What it does |
|---|---|
| **Move to Bells & chanting** | Files the recording at the bottom of that section. |
| **Move to Guided meditations** | Files the recording at the bottom of that section. |
| **Move to Dharma talks** | Files the recording at the bottom of that section. |
| **Delete** (in red) | Removes the recording from the app at once, without asking for confirmation. If it was playing, playback stops. |

The menu lists only the sections the recording is not in. No entry sends a recording back to **Just loaded — move into a category**. Tap outside the menu to close it without choosing.

At the very bottom of the list is the line **Pierre Gallaz · developed with Claude Code**.

### The player

The dark band at the bottom of the home page is always visible.

When nothing is in the player it reads **Nothing playing — tap ▶ on a recording**.

When a recording is in the player, playing or paused, the band shows:

- the title of the recording (two lines at most);
- a progress bar with a round handle: drag the handle either way to move through the recording. The two times follow your finger while you drag, and the recording jumps to that place when you lift it;
- on the left, the time played so far, in large figures, with **elapsed** beneath;
- in the middle, the total length, as **of 58:12**;
- on the right, the time left, preceded by a minus sign, with **remaining** beneath.

Times are written minutes:seconds, or hours:minutes:seconds from one hour up. The progress bar and the total length are missing when the app cannot tell how long the recording is.

Under the times are five buttons, from left to right:

| Button | What it does |
|---|---|
| Back to beginning (a bar and a triangle pointing left) | Returns to 0:00. If the recording was playing it goes on playing from the start; if it was paused it stays paused. |
| Back 10 seconds (an arrow circling back, marked 10) | Goes back 10 seconds, or to the beginning if fewer than 10 seconds have played. |
| Play / Pause (large brown disc) | Pauses, or resumes where you paused. |
| Forward 10 seconds (an arrow circling forward, marked 10) | Skips 10 seconds ahead, or to the end if fewer than 10 seconds remain. |
| Stop (a square) | Ends playback and empties the player. The place you had reached is not kept. |

When the recording reaches its end, the player empties itself and the band returns to **Nothing playing — tap ▶ on a recording**. Nothing else starts.

As long as a recording is in the player, playing or paused, and the app is on screen, the screen does not switch off by itself. It behaves normally again as soon as you press Stop or the recording ends.

### Load recordings

Tapping **+** opens a panel titled **Load recordings** with three buttons:

| Button | What it does |
|---|---|
| **Files on this phone** | Opens the phone's file chooser at once. |
| **Shared kDrive folder (public link)** | Shows a field for the link of a shared folder. |
| **Podcast feed** | Shows a field for the address of a feed. |

**Done**, at the bottom right, closes the panel. You can also close it by tapping outside it or with the phone's back button, except while it is connecting or copying. The panel has no way back to the three buttons: to change source, tap **Done** and then **+** again.

**From this phone.** The phone's file chooser lets you pick one or several files. Back in the panel, now titled **From this phone**, the picked files are listed, all ticked, under **3 file(s) picked — press Load.** (or **Nothing picked.** if you left the chooser without picking). The title of each recording is the name of the file without its ending.

**From shared kDrive folder.** The panel shows the field **Public share link — no password needed**, with the example **https://kdrive.infomaniak.com/app/share/…**, and the button **Connect**, which reads **Connecting…** while the app contacts kDrive. The app then lists the audio files found in the folder, most recently modified first, under **12 audio file(s) found — tick the ones to load.** None is ticked. Only the files directly in the shared folder are listed, not those in its sub-folders, and only files whose name ends in mp3, m4a, aac, wav, ogg, flac, opus or mp4.

**From podcast feed.** The panel shows the field **Feed URL**, with the example **https://…/feeds/teacher.xml**, and the button **Fetch episodes**, which reads **Fetching…** while the app reads the feed. The episodes are then listed under **40 episode(s) found — tick the ones to load.** None is ticked. Only episodes that have a title and an audio file appear.

**The list.** Each line has a tick box, the title and, in small grey print, the length (when the feed gives it) and the size in KB or MB (when known). At the left of a line you see one of three things:

- a tick box, for a recording you can choose;
- a small turning circle that fills up, for a recording being downloaded;
- a green **✓**, for a recording whose title is already in the app.

Under the list is the button **Load**, which becomes **Load 3 recording(s)** once you have ticked some.

What happens when you press it depends on the source:

- Files from the phone are copied straight away. The panel shows **Copying 1/3 — title** with a bar, and cannot be closed until the copy ends. It then reads **Loaded 3 recording(s) — stored on this phone, available offline.**
- kDrive files and podcast episodes are downloaded in the background, one after the other. The panel reads **Downloading 3 in the background — they appear on your homepage as they finish. You can close this.** You can close the panel, leave the app or let the screen go dark: the downloads go on.

A download that fails shows **Failed: … — tick to retry** on its line, in brown, and gets its tick box back.

### The notifications

**While a recording is in the player**, a notification shows its title, a progress bar and a line such as **12:40 elapsed  ·  45:32 left** (or **Playing…** when the length is not known). It carries four buttons: **Back 10 seconds**, **Pause** (or **Play** when paused), **Forward 10 seconds** and **Stop**. They do exactly what the same buttons do in the app. Tapping the notification itself opens the app. The notification goes away when the recording ends or when you press Stop.

**While recordings download**, another notification shows the title of the recording being fetched, a bar, and **Downloading (42%)**, followed by **·  2/5** when several are queued. Tapping it opens the app. It goes away when the last download ends.

In the phone's notification settings for the app, the two kinds are named **Playback** (**Shows what is playing and its transport controls.**) and **Downloads** (**Downloads recordings in the background.**). Both are silent.

## How to

### Load recordings from the phone's files

1. Tap **+**.
2. Tap **Files on this phone**.
3. In the phone's file chooser, pick one or several files and confirm.
4. Back in the panel, untick any file you do not want.
5. Tap **Load 3 recording(s)**.
6. Wait for **Loaded 3 recording(s) — stored on this phone, available offline.**, then tap **Done**.

The recordings are now under **Just loaded — move into a category** at the bottom of the home page. The app has made its own copy: you may delete or move the original files.

The chooser also offers files that are not audio, because some audio files (FLAC and Opus especially) are not labelled as audio by the phone. Pick only recordings.

### Load recordings from a shared kDrive folder

The folder must be shared from Infomaniak kDrive with a public link, without a password.

1. Tap **+**.
2. Tap **Shared kDrive folder (public link)**.
3. Paste the link in **Public share link — no password needed**. It looks like **https://kdrive.infomaniak.com/app/share/…**
4. Tap **Connect**.
5. Tick the recordings you want.
6. Tap **Load 3 recording(s)**.
7. Tap **Done** if you wish; the downloads continue and each recording appears on the home page as it arrives.

The link is remembered once a connection has succeeded: next time the field is already filled and you only tap **Connect**.

### Load episodes from a podcast feed

1. Tap **+**.
2. Tap **Podcast feed**.
3. Type or paste the address of the feed in **Feed URL**.
4. Tap **Fetch episodes**.
5. Tick the episodes you want.
6. Tap **Load 3 recording(s)**.
7. Tap **Done** if you wish; the downloads continue in the background.

The address is remembered once the feed has been read: next time the field is already filled. The app remembers one kDrive link and one feed address, the last ones that worked.

### Follow a download

Pull down the phone's notifications: the **Downloads** notification gives the recording being fetched, its percentage and its place in the queue.

In the app, the recording appears under **Just loaded — move into a category** as soon as it is complete. If you kept the panel open, the line shows a circle that fills up, then a green **✓**.

### Retry a download that failed

1. In the panel, find the line marked **Failed: … — tick to retry**.
2. Tick it.
3. Tap **Load 1 recording(s)**.

If you had closed the panel, open it again with **+**, choose the same source and tap **Connect** or **Fetch episodes** to get the list back. A download that fails leaves nothing behind; it starts again from zero.

### File a recording in a section

1. On the recording's tile, tap **⋮**.
2. Tap **Move to Bells & chanting**, **Move to Guided meditations** or **Move to Dharma talks**.

The recording goes to the bottom of that section. The same menu moves it later from one section to another.

### Change the order of the recordings

Tap **▲** or **▼** on a tile to move it one place up or down. A tile moves only within its own section. The order is kept.

### Play a recording

Tap **▶** at the left of its tile. It starts from the beginning, its tile gets a brown outline, and the player at the bottom shows its progress.

When playback starts, the app asks the phone for the sound, so that other players that respect this pause. From then on the app ignores every such request made by another app: it neither pauses nor lowers its volume.

The loudness is the phone's media volume.

### Pause and resume

Tap the large brown disc in the player, or the button at the left of the tile, or **Pause** in the notification. Tap again to resume at the same place.

### Go to a passage

- Drag the handle of the progress bar and lift your finger where you want to be; the **elapsed** and **remaining** times tell you where you are while you drag.
- For a small step, tap the back-10-seconds or forward-10-seconds button.
- To hear the recording again from the start, tap the back-to-beginning button.

### Stop

Tap the square in the player, or **Stop** in the notification. The player empties; playing the recording again starts it from the beginning.

### Listen with the screen off or from another app

Press the phone's power button, or leave the app: playback continues. Use the notification to pause, move by 10 seconds or stop without opening the app.

### Use the app without network

Load everything you need while you have a connection and wait until every recording has arrived on the home page. After that you can switch the phone to airplane mode: playing, filing, ordering and deleting need no network.

### Delete a recording

1. On its tile, tap **⋮**.
2. Tap **Delete**.

The recording disappears at once; there is no confirmation and no way to undo. Only the app's copy is removed: the original file on the phone, in the kDrive folder or in the podcast is untouched, and you can load it again.

## Settings

The app has no settings screen and no option to set. Its only choices are the order and the section of each recording, and the link and feed address it remembers for you.

The notifications can be turned off, separately for **Playback** and **Downloads**, in the phone's own settings for the app.

## Your data

Everything lives on the phone, inside the app's private storage, which other apps cannot read:

- a copy of every recording you loaded;
- the list of recordings, with each one's title, length, section and place;
- the last kDrive share link and the last podcast feed address that worked.

The app has no account, no sync and no server of its own. It contains no advertising and no tracking.

The network is used only when you load from kDrive or from a podcast:

- **Connect** and the downloads that follow contact kdrive.infomaniak.com, with the share link you gave;
- **Fetch episodes** contacts the server of the feed address, and the downloads contact the servers where the episodes are hosted, following redirections.

Feeds and episodes served over plain http (not https) are accepted. Loading files from the phone and playing recordings use no network at all.

There is no export, no import and no backup function in the app. The app does not opt out of Android's own backup of app data; whether your phone uses it depends on the phone's backup settings.

Uninstalling the app, or clearing its data in the phone's settings, removes every loaded recording, the list and the remembered addresses. The original files, the kDrive folder and the podcast are not affected.

## With the other apps

Retreat Player stands alone. It offers no home-screen widget, does not appear in the "share" or "open with" menus of other apps, and exchanges nothing with the other apps of the family. The only way to bring a recording in is the **+** button.

Its sibling is Retreat Timer, a meditation timer; the two apps are independent of each other.

## Limits

- One recording at a time, played once. No queue, no playlist, no automatic next recording, no repeat.
- The place you reached in a recording is not remembered: after Stop, after the end, or if the app is closed by the phone, the recording starts again from the beginning. Pause keeps the place only as long as the recording stays in the player.
- No playback speed, no sleep timer, no bookmarks, no equaliser.
- A recording cannot be renamed. Its title is the file's name, or the episode's title in the feed.
- No search. Three fixed sections; you cannot add, rename or remove a section.
- A tile moves one place at a time, with the arrows; there is no dragging.
- Deleting is immediate, one recording at a time.
- kDrive: public links without a password only; only the files directly in the shared folder; only names ending in mp3, m4a, aac, wav, ogg, flac, opus or mp4.
- Podcast: the app does not subscribe to a feed and does not check for new episodes; you fetch the list each time you want to load something. Feeds that require a login are not supported.
- Whether a file plays depends on the audio formats the phone itself can play.
- Downloads run one at a time. A download that is cut is not resumed; it starts again from zero when you retry. If the app is closed by the phone while downloads are waiting, those that had not finished are dropped and must be chosen again.
- In the list, the green **✓** is given by title: a file or episode with the same title as a recording already in the app is shown as already loaded and cannot be ticked. Files picked on the phone are the exception: they arrive ticked, and pressing **Load** copies them again even when they show a **✓**, which gives two tiles with the same title.
- Portrait only. English only. No dark theme.

## Messages and problems

| Message | What it means, what to do |
|---|---|
| **Nothing here yet — load recordings with the + button.** | This section is empty. Load recordings with **+**, then file them here with **⋮**. |
| **Nothing playing — tap ▶ on a recording** | The player is empty. |
| **Nothing picked.** | You left the phone's file chooser without picking a file. Tap **Done**, then **+** and **Files on this phone** again. |
| **3 file(s) picked — press Load.** | The files are chosen but not yet copied. Tap **Load 3 recording(s)**. |
| **Copying 1/3 — title** | A file from the phone is being copied into the app. Wait. |
| **Loaded 3 recording(s) — stored on this phone, available offline.** | The copy is done. The recordings are at the bottom of the home page. |
| **“title” failed: …** | A file from the phone could not be copied; the end of the message is the reason given by the phone. Pick the file again; check that it is still there and that the phone has free space. |
| **Could not connect: Not a kDrive share link** | The link does not have the form of a kDrive share link. Copy the whole link again from kDrive; it contains **/app/share/** followed by a number and a code. |
| **Could not connect: This share is not public (right=…)** | The folder is shared with a password or with restricted access. Ask for a public link without a password. |
| **Could not connect: …** (anything else) | kDrive could not be reached or answered with an error (shown as **HTTP** followed by a number). Check the connection and the link, then tap **Connect** again. |
| **No audio files in that share.** | The shared folder holds no audio file at its top level. Recordings in sub-folders are not seen: ask for a link to the folder that holds the files themselves. |
| **12 audio file(s) found — tick the ones to load.** | The folder was read. Tick, then tap **Load**. |
| **Could not read the feed: …** | The address could not be reached or is not a podcast feed. Check the address and the connection, then tap **Fetch episodes** again. |
| **No episodes in that feed.** | The feed was read but holds no episode with an audio file. |
| **40 episode(s) found — tick the ones to load.** | The feed was read. Tick, then tap **Load**. |
| **Downloading 3 in the background — they appear on your homepage as they finish. You can close this.** | The downloads are queued. You can tap **Done**. |
| **Failed: … — tick to retry** | This download did not complete: the reason is given (for example **HTTP** followed by a number, **Too many redirects**, **Download failed**, or a network message from the phone). Tick the line and tap **Load** again. |
| **Playing…** (in the notification) | A recording is playing and its length is not known. |

**The Load button is grey.** Nothing is ticked, or the panel is busy connecting or copying.

**A line shows a green ✓ and no tick box.** A recording with that title is already in the app.

**You tap ▶ and nothing happens, the player stays empty.** The file is not audio the phone can play. Delete the tile and load the recording in another format, MP3 for example.

**A tile shows no length.** The app could not read the length of the file. If it plays, it plays normally.

**A downloaded recording has not appeared.** Check the **Downloads** notification: downloads arrive one at a time. If the notification is gone and the recording is missing, the download failed; load it again.

**The screen stays on.** This is intended while a recording is in the player, even paused, so that the progress stays readable. Press Stop to let the screen switch off by itself, or switch it off with the power button: playback continues.

**Music from another app stopped when a recording started.** This is intended: the app takes the sound when it starts playing.

**You cannot find how to go back to the three source buttons.** Tap **Done**, then **+**.


# Retreat Walk

*Android 1.1.0*

## What it is

Retreat Walk rings a singing bowl at a fixed interval, every 10 minutes for example, again and again until you stop it. It is meant for switching partners in a paired exercise, for walking meditation, or as a regular reminder to stay awake while sitting. The whole app is one number and one button: the minutes between bells, and **Start**. Each bell is counted from the moment you pressed **Start**, not from the bell before, so the bells stay on the clock even after hours, with the screen locked and the phone asleep.

It deliberately does nothing else: no end time, no choice of sound, no statistics, no account. It cannot use the network at all.

The app is in English only, whatever the language of the phone. It runs on Android 8.0 and later.

## First start

There is no account, no server and nothing to fill in. The app opens on its single screen, with **10** already in the field **Minutes between bells**, and it is ready to use.

The app asks one permission, and only on Android 13 and later: the permission to show notifications. It asks the first time you press **Start**, not when the app opens. The notification it is for is the silent one that stays visible during a session and carries the **Stop** button, which lets you end a session from the lock screen.

- The session starts as soon as you press **Start**, while the question is still on screen.
- If you refuse, the session runs and rings all the same; you then stop it from the app only. The app asks again the next time you press **Start**.

## The screens

The app has a single screen, always upright (it does not turn with the phone), dark text on a paper-coloured background. There is no menu and no settings screen.

### The main screen, before a session

From top to bottom:

- The title **Retreat Walk** and the line **A bell every so many minutes, until you stop it.**
- The field **Minutes between bells**, followed by **min**. Tap it to type a number. It accepts digits only, three at most.
- Under the field, the line **The bell rings at that many minutes, then twice that, then three times — on and on.** If the number is outside the allowed range, this line turns red and reads **Choose between 1 and 180 minutes.**
- The large **Start** button. It is greyed out while the field is empty or the number is not between 1 and 180.
- If the phone's alarm volume is below 85 %, a red line **Alarm volume is at 40% — the bell can be no louder than that.** (with the real percentage) and, under it, **Open sound settings**, which opens the phone's sound settings. Both disappear by themselves, within a couple of seconds, once the alarm volume is at 85 % or more.
- The line **For switching partners in a paired exercise, or a regular reminder to stay awake while sitting.**
- At the very bottom, **Pierre Gallaz · developed with Claude Code**.

### The main screen, during a session

The **Start** button becomes a red **Stop** button, and the minutes field is greyed out: the interval cannot be changed while a session runs.

Under the button (and under the volume warning, if it is shown) you see:

- **Next bell in** and a large countdown, in minutes and seconds (**07:42**), or in hours, minutes and seconds when more than an hour remains (**1:02:30**).
- The number of bells so far: **No bell yet — every 10 min from the start** before the first one, then **1 bell so far**, **2 bells so far**, and so on.
- The reminder **You can lock the screen. Stop is in the notification too.**

### The notification

During a session, a silent notification stays in place. It makes no sound and no vibration: the bowl is the only sound the app makes.

- Its title is **Bell every 10 min** (with your interval).
- Before the first bell, its text is **First bell at 14:30 — tap Stop to end**. After that it reads **3 rung · next at 15:00 — tap Stop to end**. Times are always shown on the 24-hour clock.
- Its **Stop** button ends the session, also from the lock screen.
- Tapping the notification itself opens the app.

The notification disappears when the session ends.

## How to

### Start a bell every so many minutes

1. Open Retreat Walk.
2. Tap the field **Minutes between bells** and type a whole number from 1 to 180.
3. Press **Start**.

Nothing rings at the start. The first bell rings after that many minutes, the second at twice that, the third at three times, and so on, with no end.

### Stop the bells

Press **Stop** in the app, or press **Stop** on the notification (this works from the lock screen). The session ends at once; if the bowl is sounding at that moment, it is cut short.

### Change the interval

1. Press **Stop**.
2. Type the new number of minutes.
3. Press **Start**. The count starts again from that moment.

### Lock the screen or use another app during a session

Just do it. The session continues with the screen off or with another app in front. To come back to the countdown, open the app again or tap the notification.

### See when the next bell will ring

In the app, read the countdown under **Next bell in**. Without opening the app, read the notification: it gives the clock time of the next bell.

### Make the bell louder

The bell plays on the phone's alarm volume, not on the media or ringtone volume, and at the fullest level that volume allows.

1. If the app shows **Alarm volume is at … % — the bell can be no louder than that.**, tap **Open sound settings**.
2. Raise the alarm volume.
3. Come back to the app. The warning disappears once the volume is at 85 % or more.

You can also reach the alarm volume with the phone's volume keys.

### Find the last interval again

You do not need to do anything: the field opens with the interval of the last session you started.

## Settings

The app has no settings screen. The only thing you set is the interval.

| Setting | Choices | Default |
|---|---|---|
| **Minutes between bells** | a whole number from 1 to 180 | 10, then the interval of your last session |

Two things are set in the phone, not in the app:

- the alarm volume, which decides how loud the bell is;
- the notification category of the app, named **Bell session** (**Shows the running session and its Stop button.**), which you find in the phone's notification settings for Retreat Walk.

## Your data

Everything stays on the phone. The app keeps only the interval you last used and, during a session, when it started, its interval and how many bells have rung. It has no account, no ads and no tracking, and it has no permission to use the network: it sends nothing to anyone.

There is nothing to export, import or synchronise. If the phone's own backup is turned on, Android may include the app's saved interval in it. Uninstalling the app removes everything it stored.

## With the other apps

Retreat Walk offers no home-screen widget, receives nothing shared from other apps and opens no files.

The bell is one strike of the Satipanya singing bowl, from the same recording as the bell of Retreat Timer.

While the bowl sounds, the app asks the phone for priority on sound: music, a podcast or any other audio is lowered or paused by its own app for those few seconds, then resumes. The bell rings whether or not the other app steps aside.

## Limits

- The interval is a whole number of minutes, from 1 to 180. No seconds, no fractions.
- There is no bell at the start, and no end time: the bells continue until you press **Stop**.
- One session at a time, with a single interval.
- The sound cannot be changed: it is always one strike of the same bowl, about fifteen seconds long.
- There is no pause. Stopping and starting again begins a new count from zero.
- A session does not survive a restart of the phone. After a restart the app shows **Start**, and no bell rings until you start a new session.
- A bell whose moment has passed while the phone could not ring it is skipped, not rung late; the following bells stay at their planned times, and the count of bells includes the skipped one.
- The app is in English only, in light colours only, upright only.

## Messages and problems

**Choose between 1 and 180 minutes.** The number typed is 0 or above 180. Type a number from 1 to 180; **Start** becomes available again.

**Start is greyed out.** The field is empty or holds a number outside 1 to 180.

**Alarm volume is at … % — the bell can be no louder than that.** The phone's alarm volume is below 85 %. The bell cannot be louder than that volume, so it may be faint or, at 0 %, silent. Tap **Open sound settings** and raise the alarm volume.

**I did not hear the bell.** Check the alarm volume first: the media and ringtone volumes have no effect on the bell. Outdoors, keep the phone's loudspeaker uncovered.

**There is no notification, and no Stop on the lock screen.** The notification permission was refused. The session still runs and rings; stop it from the app. To get the notification back, press **Start** again and accept the question, or allow notifications for Retreat Walk in the phone's settings.

**The app shows Start although a session was running.** The phone was restarted, which ends the session. Press **Start** to begin a new one.

**My music or podcast pauses or drops in volume every few minutes.** This is the bell taking priority for the few seconds it sounds. It resumes afterwards.

**The phone shows an alarm at the time of the next bell.** Each bell is set in the phone as an alarm, which is what makes it ring on time with the screen off. The phone may therefore show its alarm symbol during a session. It disappears when you press **Stop**.




<!-- Browser extensions and Linux tools -->


# PageTurn

*Chrome extension 1.0.0 · bookmarklet for other browsers and phones*

## What it is

PageTurn takes the article on the web page you are looking at and lays it out as the pages of a book: no scrolling, and pages that change at once, with no animation, which is what an e-ink screen needs. It keeps the text and the pictures of the article and leaves out the menus, side columns, advertisements, subscription boxes and comments.

It exists in two forms: an extension for Chrome, and a bookmarklet (a bookmark that runs PageTurn when you open it) for any browser, phones included.

It has no settings: the page is always white, the text black, in a serif typeface of fixed size. It does not remember where you stopped reading, and it keeps nothing about you.

The interface is in English only.

## First start

### The Chrome extension

1. Download the ZIP from gallaz.ch/eink and extract it.
2. In Chrome, open `chrome://extensions/`.
3. Turn on **Developer mode**.
4. Choose **Load unpacked** and select the extracted folder.

The extension asks for two permissions: to act on the tab you are in at the moment you call it, and to insert its reading layout into that tab. It does nothing on a page until you call it there.

### The bookmarklet

The extracted folder contains a page named `bookmarklet.html`. Open it in the browser where you want PageTurn.

- **On a computer**: drag the **PageTurn** button of that page to the bookmarks bar.
- **On an iPhone or iPad (Safari)**: tap **Share**, then **Add Bookmark** on that page; then edit the bookmark and replace its address with the text shown under **Show bookmarklet URL (for manual install)**.
- **On Android (Chrome or Brave)**: bookmark that page, edit the bookmark and replace its address with the text shown under **Show bookmarklet URL (for manual install)**. To use it, type `PageTurn` in the address bar and tap the bookmark that is suggested.

## The screens

### The extension's window

Click the PageTurn icon in Chrome's toolbar. A small window shows the name **PageTurn**, one button and the line **Shortcut: Alt+P**.

- **Start Reading Mode** turns the current tab into pages. The window closes.
- When the tab is already in pages, the button is red and reads **Stop Reading Mode**: it brings the original page back.

### The reading view

The article fills the whole window, on a white background, with margins on all sides. Text is set in a serif typeface, headings in a sans-serif one. A picture is never taller than four fifths of the window and is never cut between two pages.

- A counter shows the page you are on and the number of pages, for example **3 / 12**. It is at the bottom right with the extension, at the bottom centre with the bookmarklet.
- A round **×** button at the top right leaves the reading view. With the extension, its tip reads **Exit reading mode (Esc)**.

Keys, in both forms:

| Key | What it does |
|---|---|
| Right arrow, or Space | Next page |
| Left arrow, or Shift+Space | Previous page |
| Home | First page |
| End | Last page |
| Esc | Leave the reading view |

Touch, with the bookmarklet only:

| Gesture | What it does |
|---|---|
| Swipe to the left | Next page |
| Swipe to the right | Previous page |
| Tap in the right 30% of the screen | Next page |
| Tap in the left 30% of the screen | Previous page |
| Tap in the middle | Nothing: the middle is left free so that you can select text |

A swipe counts when the finger travels at least 50 pixels sideways in less than half a second.

## How to

### Read an article as pages

1. Open the article.
2. With the extension: click the PageTurn icon, then **Start Reading Mode**, or press **Alt+P**. With the bookmarklet: open the **PageTurn** bookmark.
3. Turn the pages with the arrow keys or Space (or by swiping or tapping, with the bookmarklet).

### Go back to the normal page

Press **Esc**, or click **×**, or call PageTurn a second time (**Alt+P**, **Stop Reading Mode**, or the bookmark again). The page comes back as it was, scrolled to where you had left it.

### Jump to the beginning or the end

Press **Home** for the first page, **End** for the last.

### Change the size of the window, or turn a tablet

Just do it: PageTurn cuts the pages again for the new size and puts you at the page that corresponds to where you were.

### Change the shortcut

**Alt+P** is the shortcut the extension proposes to Chrome. Chrome lists the shortcuts of extensions at `chrome://extensions/shortcuts`, where you can give it another one.

### Clear the ghost images of an e-ink screen while reading

On a Lenovo ThinkBook Plus running Tinta4PlusU with the e-ink screen enabled, there is nothing to do: the extension asks Tinta4PlusU for a full refresh of the screen every five page turns. The bookmarklet does not do this.

## Your data

PageTurn stores nothing: no account, no history, no reading position. It works on the page already loaded in your browser and sends nothing to the internet.

The extension makes one kind of request, to your own computer only: when the reading view starts it checks whether Tinta4PlusU is listening (address `127.0.0.1`, port 19849), and then asks it for a screen refresh every five page turns. The request carries nothing about the page. If nothing answers, the extension stops asking until the next time you start the reading view. The bookmarklet makes no request at all.

## With the other apps

- **Tinta4PlusU**: clears e-ink ghosting every five page turns, as described above (extension only).

## Limits

- No choice of typeface, size, margins or colours.
- The place where you stopped is not kept: calling PageTurn again starts at page 1.
- The extension has no touch gestures and no tap zones: swiping and tapping exist in the bookmarklet only.
- PageTurn looks for the article with what it knows of Substack, Medium, WordPress, Ghost, Blogger, Dev.to, Hashnode, Hugo and Jekyll sites, then of pages built in the usual way; failing that it takes the largest block of text on the page. On an unusual page it can pick the wrong block.
- Embedded frames are removed, so a video or another embedded item inside the article disappears. Comments, related-article lists and sharing buttons are removed too.
- Anything the site marks with a name containing "ad-" or "promo" is removed as advertising; a legitimate part of an article can be removed by mistake.
- The pages are made from the page as it is when you call PageTurn: what a site loads later is not included.
- It cannot run on the browser's own pages (such as `chrome://` addresses).

## Messages and problems

PageTurn shows no message of its own.

- **Nothing happens when I click Start Reading Mode.** The tab holds one of the browser's own pages, where no extension may act. Try on an ordinary web page.
- **Alt+P does nothing.** Another program or extension may already use that shortcut; check `chrome://extensions/shortcuts`.
- **The reading view shows the wrong part of the page, or a part is missing.** PageTurn chose the wrong block or removed too much. Leave with **Esc** and read the page normally.
- **Ghost images stay on the e-ink screen.** Tinta4PlusU must be running, connected to its helper, with the e-ink screen enabled. The refresh comes every five page turns, not at each one.


# Social Media Blocker

*Chrome and Firefox extension 1.1*

## What it is

Social Media Blocker counts the time you really spend on twenty social media and video sites and closes them all once your limit for the day is reached. You can also close them at once, with one button. To open them again you must solve five maths problems of rising difficulty: enough to break the reflex, not enough to lock you out.

The list of sites is fixed, and the limit is one total for all of them together. There is no password, no timetable and no per-site limit. The extension sends nothing anywhere.

The interface is in English only.

## First start

### Installing in Chrome

1. Download the ZIP from gallaz.ch/eink and extract it.
2. Open `chrome://extensions/`.
3. Turn on **Developer mode**.
4. Choose **Load unpacked** and select the extracted folder.

### Installing in Firefox

Firefox 128 or later is needed. The folder holds two description files, one for Chrome (`manifest.json`) and one for Firefox (`manifest.firefox.json`); Firefox needs a package where its own file bears the name `manifest.json`. A script in the folder builds that package:

1. In a terminal opened in the extracted folder, run `bash package.sh firefox`. It creates `social-blocker-firefox.zip` (the `zip` program must be present on the computer).
2. In Firefox, open `about:debugging#/runtime/this-firefox`.
3. Choose **Load Temporary Add-on** and pick `social-blocker-firefox.zip`.

An add-on loaded this way is temporary: Firefox removes it when it closes.

### What the extension is allowed to do

- See which site is in the tab in front of you, to count the time. It keeps no list of what you visit.
- Keep its own settings in the browser.
- Wake up at regular intervals to add up the time.
- Know when you are away from the keyboard and mouse, to stop counting.
- Redirect the listed sites to its own page when they are blocked.

The permission it requests covers all websites; it acts only on the listed sites.

No account is asked. After installation nothing is blocked and nothing is counted: the window shows **Status: Not Blocking** and a limit of 60 minutes.

## The screens

### The extension's window

Click the extension's icon in the browser's toolbar. The window, titled **Social Media Blocker**, has three states.

**Not blocking** (green line **Status: Not Blocking**):

- **Daily usage limit (minutes)**: the number of minutes allowed per day, 60 at first. The arrows of the field go from 1 to 1440; an empty field counts as 60.
