- **Start Tracking Usage**: starts counting, from zero, with that limit.
- **Block Now**: blocks all the sites at once.
- At the bottom, a reminder: "Tracks time spent on social media & video sites. Blocks them when you hit the limit or press Block Now. Solve 5 math problems to unblock."

**Counting** (yellow line, for example **Tracking: 12m 40s / 60 min**):

- A bar fills as the time is used, with the same figures under it, for example **12m 40s / 60 min used**. The window refreshes every five seconds.
- **Stop Tracking**: stops counting.
- **Block Now**: blocks all the sites at once.
- The limit field is hidden while counting.

**Blocked** (red line **Status: BLOCKED**):

- **Unblock (Solve Math)**: opens a new tab with the maths challenge already started. It is the only button.

### The blocked page

When the sites are blocked, opening any of them shows this page instead (tab title **Site Blocked**): **This site is blocked**, "Social media and video sites are currently blocked.", "To unblock, you must solve 5 increasingly difficult math problems.", and the button **Start Math Challenge**.

### The maths challenge

- A bar and the line **Question 1 of 5** show how far you are.
- The level is named: **Difficulty: Easy**, **Difficulty: Medium**, **Difficulty: Hard**, **Difficulty: Very Hard**, **Difficulty: Expert**.
- Type the answer, a number, in the field **Your answer...** and press **Submit** or the Enter key.
- **Correct!** leads to the next question. **Wrong answer. Try again.** leaves the same question in place; there is no penalty and no limit on attempts. After two wrong answers to the same question a line beginning with **Hint:** appears.
- After the fifth correct answer: **Sites Unblocked!**, "You solved all 5 problems. Social media is now accessible.", "Close this tab and browse freely."

The five levels:

| Question | Level | Kind of problem |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Easy | An addition and a multiplication in one line (multiply first) |
| 2 | Medium | A percentage of a number, or a product minus a square |
| 3 | Hard | A power minus a number, or a first-degree equation to solve for x |
| 4 | Very Hard | The larger root of a second-degree equation, a square root plus a number, or two equations with x and y |
| 5 | Expert | The sum of an arithmetic series, a remainder ("mod"), a number of arrangements, or the sum of the roots of a second-degree equation |

The numbers are drawn at random each time. Reloading the page starts again at question 1 with new problems.

## How to

### Give yourself a daily limit

1. Open the extension's window.
2. Type the number of minutes in **Daily usage limit (minutes)**.
3. Press **Start Tracking Usage**.

When the time is used up, all twenty sites are blocked, the tabs where one of them is open switch to the blocked page, and counting stops.

### See how much time is left

Open the extension's window: the status line and the bar show the time used and the limit.

### Block the sites right now

Open the window and press **Block Now**. It works whether or not counting is on.

### Open the sites again

1. Open the window and press **Unblock (Solve Math)**, or press **Start Math Challenge** on the blocked page.
2. Answer the five questions.
3. Close the tab.

The sites are open again and the counter is back to zero. Counting does not start again by itself: press **Start Tracking Usage** if you want the limit to apply again.

### Change the limit

1. Press **Stop Tracking**.
2. Type the new number of minutes.
3. Press **Start Tracking Usage**. The counter starts again from zero.

### Stop counting

Press **Stop Tracking**. No maths is asked for this.

### Know what is counted

- Time is added in steps of five seconds, while one of the listed sites is in the tab in front of you, in the browser window you are using.
- It is not counted for tabs in the background, when the browser is not the window in use, when the browser reports that keyboard and mouse have been idle for 60 seconds, or when the computer is locked. It resumes when you come back.
- A new day starts the counter at zero. The extension looks for the change of day when the browser starts and every half hour; the day changes at midnight in universal time (UTC), not at midnight on your clock.
- If the sites are blocked when the day changes, they are opened again and counting restarts with the same limit. This is also true of a block started with **Block Now**: it lasts until the change of day at the latest.

### Know which sites are concerned

Facebook, Twitter and X (twitter.com, x.com), Instagram, LinkedIn, Snapchat, Reddit, Tumblr, Pinterest, Threads (threads.net), Mastodon (mastodon.social only), Bluesky (bsky.app), Discord, YouTube (youtube.com, youtu.be), TikTok, Rumble, Twitch, Dailymotion, Vimeo, Telegram (telegram.org) and WhatsApp (whatsapp.com), with all their sub-addresses (for example the mobile or web versions).

## Settings

| Setting | What it changes | Choices | At first |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Daily usage limit (minutes)** | The total time allowed per day on the twenty sites together | A number of minutes (the field's arrows go from 1 to 1440) | 60 |

## Your data

Everything stays in the browser, on your computer: whether the sites are blocked, your limit, the time used today, whether counting is on, and the date of the last return to zero. No list of visited pages is kept. Nothing is sent over the network; there is no account, no statistics, no third party.

Removing the extension removes these few values with it.

## Limits

- The list of sites cannot be changed, and no site can be exempted.
- One total for all sites; no limit per site, no timetable, no different limit by day of the week.
- **Stop Tracking** needs no maths, and so does turning the extension off in the browser: the extension is a brake on a reflex, not a lock.
- After the maths, counting is not switched on again by itself.
- Only pages opened as such are blocked: a video from one of these sites embedded inside another site's page still plays.
- It acts in the browser where it is installed, not in other browsers or in apps.

## Messages and problems

- **Please enter a number.** The answer field holds something that is not a number. Answers are numbers only; a decimal point is accepted.
- **Wrong answer. Try again.** The same question stays; after two errors a hint is shown.
- **Hint: …** A pointer to the method to follow.
- **The sites opened again by themselves in the morning.** That is the change of day: the block is lifted and counting restarts with your limit.
- **I solved the problems and the limit no longer applies.** Press **Start Tracking Usage** again.
- **In Firefox the extension has disappeared.** A temporary add-on is removed when Firefox closes; load it again.


# eInk Reader

*Linux 0.6.eInk*

## What it is

eInk Reader is an e-book reader for the computer, made for the e-ink screen of the Lenovo ThinkBook Plus. It keeps a library of your books with their covers and opens EPUB, MOBI, FB2, PDF and comic-book files, and a few more formats when extra components are present.

What sets it apart: text is shown in whole pages only, one or two at a time, never as a scrolling column; and while you read, it asks Tinta4PlusU to clear the ghost images of the e-ink screen, once every ten page turns.

It is a variant of the reader Lector, and still carries that name in several places: the window is titled **Lector**, and its files are kept in folders named `Lector`.

It does not synchronise anything between computers and has no account. Its interface exists in several languages, but only partly: with a computer set to another language than English, many labels remain in English.

## First start

### Installing

The installer is written for Ubuntu and systems derived from it.

1. Download the ZIP from gallaz.ch/eink and extract it.
2. Open a terminal in the extracted folder and run `sudo bash installer.sh`.

The installer adds the components the reader needs, copies the program to `/opt/eink-reader`, creates the command `eink-reader` and adds **eInk Reader** to the applications menu. It writes what it does to `/tmp/eink-reader-install.log`.

To remove the program: `sudo bash installer.sh --uninstall`.

### Which formats you get

| Format | Files | Available |
|---|---|---|
| EPUB | `.epub` | After installation |
| Kindle | `.mobi`, `.azw`, `.azw3`, `.azw4`, `.prc` | After installation |
| FictionBook | `.fb2`, `.fb2.zip` | After installation |
| Comic books | `.cbz`, `.cbr` | After installation; compressed `.cbr` files also need the `unrar` program |
| PDF | `.pdf` | If the installer managed to add PyMuPDF (it says so, or warns "PDF support will not be available.") |
| DjVu | `.djvu` | Only if the Python component for DjVu (python-djvulibre) is on the computer; the installer does not add it |
| Markdown | `.md` | Only if the Python component `markdown` is on the computer; the installer does not add it |
| Plain text | `.txt` | Only if the Python component `textile` is on the computer; the installer does not add it |

A format whose component is missing is simply not offered: its files do not appear in the file chooser and are not found when folders are scanned.

### What you see the first time

Start **eInk Reader** from the applications menu, or type `eink-reader` in a terminal. The reader asks for nothing: no permission, no account. The window shows one tab, **Library**, empty, with a toolbar above it.

For the ghost-clearing to work, Tinta4PlusU must be running, connected to its helper, with the e-ink screen enabled. Without it the reader works the same, minus the clearing.

## The screens

### The library

The **Library** tab is always the first tab. Its toolbar holds, from left to right (the names are the tips shown when the pointer rests on a button):

- **Add book**: choose one or more files to add to the library.
- **Delete book**: remove the selected books from the library.
- **View as covers** and **View as table**: the two ways of showing the library.
- **Scan Library**: look through the library folders chosen in the settings for new books.
- **Filter library**: a menu with one line per library folder, a line **Manually Added** for books added by hand, and **All**. Untick a line to hide its books.
- **Library background color**: the colour behind the covers.
- **Settings**: opens the settings window on its **Library** page.
- **About**: opens the settings window on its **About** page.
- On the right, the search field **Search for Title, Author, Tags...**: type to keep only the books whose title, author, tags, folder or file name contain the text. The Esc key empties it.
- The box **Sort by**, in the cover view only: **Title**, **Author**, **Year**, **Newest** (last added first), **Last Read** (last opened first; books never opened are hidden), **Progress** (most advanced first; books not started are hidden).

In the cover view each book is its cover. A small mark at the bottom right of a cover shows the progress: a pie that fills as you read, a tick when the book counts as read, a warning sign (and a paler cover) when the file is no longer where it was. Resting the pointer on a cover shows the title, the author and the year.

The table view has the columns **Title**, **Author**, **Year**, **Last Read** (time since the book was last opened, such as `3d`, `5h`, `12m` or `<1m`), **%** (the same progress mark) and **Tags**. Click a column's heading to sort by it.

In both views:

- Double-click a book to open it in a new tab.
- Right-click a book for the menu: **Start reading**, **Edit** (when a single book is selected), **Delete**, **Mark read**, **Mark unread**.
- Drop files from the file manager onto the library to add them.
- Several books can be selected at once.

A line at the bottom of the window appears while you search (it shows the number of books), while books are being added (**Adding books...** and a progress bar) and during a scan (**Checking library folders**, then **Parsing files**). When a file could not be read, a warning button appears there; click it to open the log.

### The window "Edit metadata"

**Edit** in a book's menu opens a small window with the cover and four fields: **Title**, **Author**, **Year**, **Tags** (comma separated).

- Click the cover (**Cover (click to change)**) to choose another picture, a `.jpg` or `.png` file.
- The tick button (**OK**), or the Enter key in a field, saves. The cross button (**Cancel**) closes without saving.
- The colour button changes the background colour of this kind of small window.
- If the year is not a number, the former year is kept.

### A book in pages (EPUB, MOBI, FB2, Markdown, text)

Each open book has its own tab, with its cover and title. The toolbar changes to:

- **View settings**: replaces the buttons below with the appearance controls (see further down); click again to come back.
- **Add bookmark**: marks the current place.
- **Bookmarks (Ctrl + B)**, **Annotations (Ctrl + N)**, **Search (Ctrl + F)**: open the side panel on that tab.
- **Toggle distraction free mode (Ctrl + D)**: hides the toolbar and the tabs.
- **Fullscreen (F)**: the book alone on the whole screen.
- On the right, the **Table of Contents** box: choose a chapter to go to it.

Moving through the book:

| Action | Effect |
|---|---|
| Down arrow, or mouse wheel down | Next page; after the last page of a chapter, the next chapter |
| Up arrow, or mouse wheel up | Previous page; before the first page of a chapter, the previous chapter |
| Right arrow | Next chapter |
| Left arrow | Previous chapter |
| Space | Forward by one screen; at the end of a chapter, a second press goes to the next chapter |
| F | Full screen on and off |
| Esc | Closes the side panel or the note if one is open, otherwise leaves full screen |

The Up and Down arrows turn pages in **Single page** only; in **Double page**, turn the pages with the mouse wheel.

Right-click in the text for the menu:

- **View** › **Single page** or **Double page**. Every book opens in single page.
- **Exit fullscreen**, or **Exit Distraction Free mode**, when one of them is on.
- With text selected: **Define "word"** (the first selected word), **Search for "…"** › **In this book**, **Google**, **Wikipedia**, **Youtube**, and **Annotate** › the list of your annotation styles (or **&lt;No annotations set&gt;**).
- With nothing selected: **Search**.
- On a passage already annotated: **Annotation** › **Edit note**, **Delete annotation**.
- **Add Bookmark**: a bookmark at the place you clicked.
- In full screen or distraction-free mode, also **Bookmarks** and the table of contents.

The mouse pointer disappears after two seconds without movement.

### A book in images (PDF, DjVu, comics)

PDF, DjVu and comic-book files are shown as pictures of their pages. The toolbar is the same without **Annotations** and **Search**.

| Action | Effect |
|---|---|
| Down and Up arrows | Move within the page by a small step; at the bottom or top, go to the next or previous page |
| Space | Move down by a large step; at the bottom, next page |
| Right and Left arrows | Next and previous page |
| Mouse wheel | Moves within the page, then changes page at the bottom or top |
| Hold the mouse button and move | Drags the page |
| D | Double page on and off |
| M | Manga mode on and off |
| + (or =) and - | Zoom in and out |
| W, B, O | Fit to width, best fit, original size |
| F, Esc | Full screen on, off |

Right-click on the page for the menu: **Save page as...** (saves the page as a `.png`, `.jpg` or `.bmp` picture), **View** › **Double page mode (D)**, **Manga mode (M)**, **Invert page colors**, **Zoom in (+)**, **Zoom out (-)**, **Fit width (W)**, **Best fit (B)**, **Original size (O)**; and, when relevant, **Exit fullscreen**, **Exit Distraction Free mode**, **Bookmarks** and the table of contents.

### The appearance controls

Press **View settings**. For a book in pages, the toolbar shows:

- A box with **Profile 1**, **Profile 2**, **Profile 3**: three sets of appearance you can switch between. Every change below is stored in the profile shown.
- The typeface box and the **Font size** box (8 to 46 in steps of 2, then 56, 64 and 72).
- **Text color** and **Background color**.
- **Increase line spacing** and **Decrease line spacing**.
- **Increase padding** and **Decrease padding**: the margins on the left and right.
- **Left align text**, **Right align text**, **Center align text**, **Justify text**.
- **Reset profile**: gives the profile shown its original values.

The three profiles as delivered:

| Profile | Typeface | Text | Background |
|---|---|---|---|
| Profile 1 | Noto Sans Fallback | Black | Light grey |
| Profile 2 | Roboto | Light grey | Near black |
| Profile 3 | Clear Sans | Grey-blue | Dark blue |

All three start with size 30, justified text, margins of 150 and line spacing of 110.

For a book in images, the toolbar shows instead: **Double page mode (D)**, **Manga mode (M)** (in double page, the right-hand page comes first), **Invert page colors**, **Rotate image clockwise**, **Rotate image anti-clockwise** (turning the page switches double page off), **Zoom in (+)**, **Zoom Out (-)**, **Fit Width (W)**, **Best Fit (B)**, **Original size (O)** and **Background color**. As delivered: best fit on a black background.

### The side panel

The panel opens on the right, over the book, with three tabs (one only, **Bookmarks**, for a book in images).

- **Bookmarks**: your bookmarks, grouped by chapter. Click a bookmark to go there, a chapter's name to go to its beginning. Right-click a bookmark for **Edit** (rename it) and **Delete**.
- **Annotations**: the list of your annotation styles. Double-click a style, then select text in the book: the style is applied to it.
- **Search**: type in the field **Search**. Three buttons refine it: **Search entire book** (otherwise only the current chapter is searched), **Match case**, **Match word**. Results are listed by chapter; click one to go there, with the found text selected. The field turns red when nothing is found.

### The navigation bar

When **Show navigation bar** is ticked in the settings, moving the mouse over a book shows a small bar at the bottom right: previous chapter, table of contents, next chapter. It hides again after two seconds.

### The definition window

**Define** in the text menu asks an online dictionary for the selected word and shows the word, its root and its definitions by category. Three buttons: close, **Play pronunciation of root word**, and the background colour. When nothing is found the window says **No definitions found in** followed by the language code (for example EN).

### The settings window

The **Settings** window has four pages listed on the left: **Library**, **Switches**, **Annotations**, **About**. They are described under Settings below.

## How to

### Add books

- Press **Add book** (or Ctrl+O) and choose the files; or
- drop the files onto the library; or
- declare a library folder (see below) and let the reader find them.

A book already in the library is not added twice. Books are not copied: the library remembers where each file is.

### Declare library folders

1. Open **Settings**; the **Library** page shows the folders of the computer.
2. Tick the folders that hold your books. Their sub-folders are included.
3. Optionally, double-click in the **Name** column to give the folder a name (it appears in the **Filter library** menu), and in the **Tags** column to give tags to all its books (the search finds them).
4. Press **Scan Library**.

The scan adds the new books and removes from the library the books that are no longer in a ticked folder; books added by hand stay. **Close** leaves the window without scanning and forgets the changes made to the folders.

### Open a book and come back to it

Double-click it. The book opens where you left it. Position, bookmarks and annotations are saved when you close the tab or quit the reader. If **Remember open files** is ticked, the books open when you quit are opened again at the next start.

### Read on the e-ink screen

1. Start Tinta4PlusU and enable the e-ink screen.
2. Open the book and press **F** for full screen.
3. Turn pages with the Down arrow or the mouse wheel.

The reader counts each page turned with the arrows or the wheel and each change of chapter (or of page, in a book in images); every tenth time, it asks Tinta4PlusU for a full refresh of the screen. In a book in pages, moving with the Space bar inside a chapter is not counted.

### Change the look of the text

Press **View settings**, choose a profile, then the typeface, size, colours, spacing, margins and alignment. Press **View settings** again to get the normal toolbar back.

### Read two pages side by side

Text: right-click, **View** › **Double page**. Images: press **D**.

### Put a bookmark and find it again

Press **Add bookmark**, or right-click the exact place and choose **Add Bookmark**. The side panel opens with the new bookmark, named **New bookmark**, ready to be renamed. Later, press Ctrl+B and click it.

### Highlight a passage

1. Create a style once: **Settings** › **Annotations** › **New**, give it a name and tick what it does (see Settings).
2. In the book, select the passage, right-click, **Annotate**, and choose the style. Or press Ctrl+N, double-click the style, then select the passage.

To add a note to it: right-click the passage, **Annotation** › **Edit note**, type in the **Note** window; the note is kept when the window closes (Esc). To remove the highlight: **Annotation** › **Delete annotation**.

### Search in a book

Press Ctrl+F and type. Or select a word, right-click, **Search for** › **In this book**.

### Look a word up

Select that single word, right-click, then **Define**, or **Search for** › **Google**, **Wikipedia** or **Youtube**, which open your web browser.

### Mark a book read or unread

Right-click it in the library: **Mark read** or **Mark unread**. **Mark unread** also sends the book back to its beginning.

### Correct a title, an author, a cover

Right-click the book, **Edit**.

### Remove a book

Select it and press **Delete book** (or the Delete key), then answer **Yes** to **Delete book(s)?**. The book leaves the library; the file itself is not deleted.

### Save a page of a comic or PDF as a picture

Right-click the page, **Save page as...**.

## Settings

### Page "Switches", group "Library"

| Setting | What it changes | At first |
|---|---|---|
| **Consider book read at percent** | The progress from which a book gets the "read" tick; 90 or more | 95 |
| **Icon theme:** **Dark** / **Light** | The colour of the toolbar icons. "Restart application to see changes" | Dark |
| **Startup: Refresh library** | Scans the library folders each time the reader starts | Off |
| **Remember open files** | Reopens at start the books that were open when you quit | On |
| **Cover shadows** | Draws a shadow behind each cover | On |
| **Load covers only when needed** | Loads only the covers in view. "Enabling reduces startup time and memory usage" | On |
| **Generate tags from files** | Takes the tags written inside the book files when adding them | On |
| **Shrink long book titles** | Cuts tab titles longer than 30 characters | Off |
| **Show navigation bar** | Shows the navigation bar over books opened afterwards | Off |
| **Download missing covers** | "Attempt to download missing book covers from Google books - SLOW". Applies when books are added | Off |

### Page "Switches", group "Reading"

| Setting | What it changes | At first |
|---|---|---|
| **Hide scrollbars when reading** | Hides the scroll bars. "Horizontal scrolling with Alt + Scroll", "Reopen book to see changes" | Off |
| **Cache comic / pdf pages** | Prepares neighbouring pages in advance. "Greatly reduces page transition time at the cost of more memory" | On |
| **Small increment** | Books in images: number of presses on Up or Down to cross a page; 4 to 10 | 4 |
| **Large increment** | Books in images: number of presses on Space to cross a page; 1 to 10 | 2 |
| **Dictionary language** | Language used by **Define**: **English**, **Spanish**, **Hindi** | English |
| **Scroll speed** | Speed of scrolling inside a page; slider from 3 to 15 | 7 |

### Page "Library"

The folder tree described under "Declare library folders", with the buttons **Scan Library** (its tip: "Save changes and start library scan") and **Close**.

### Page "Annotations"

The list of your annotation styles, with the buttons **New**, **Delete**, **Edit**, **Move Up** and **Move Down**, and a preview. **New** and **Edit** open the **Annotation Editor**:

- **Annotation Name**: the name of the style (it cannot be empty).
- **Type**: **Text markup**, the only kind.
- **Foreground**, with its colour button: the colour of the text.
- **Highlight**, with its colour button: the colour behind the text.
- **Bold**, **Italic**.
- **Underline**, with the kind of line (**Solid**, **Dashes**, **Dots**, **Wavy**) and its colour.
- **OK** saves the style, **Cancel** abandons.

### Page "About"

Two tabs: **About** (the name Lector, the version and the original author) and **Log** (the reader's journal). Two buttons:

- **Clear Log** empties the journal.
- **Reset Application** asks **Delete database and exit?**; **Yes** erases the whole library (books listed, positions, bookmarks, annotations in books) and quits. The book files are not touched.

### Kept without a setting

The reader also remembers the size of the window, the view (covers or table), the library's background colour, the three profiles, the zoom and background for books in images, and the double-page, manga and inverted-colours switches.

## Your data

- Your books stay where they are on the disk. The library keeps, in the file `~/.local/share/Lector/Lector.db`, each book's title, author, year, tags, cover, reading position, bookmarks and annotations.
- The settings are in `~/.config/Lector/Lector.conf`, the journal in `~/.local/share/Lector/Lector.log`.
- Uninstalling the program leaves these three files in place.
- There is no synchronisation, export or import.

The reader uses the network in three cases only, each started by you:

- **Download missing covers**, when ticked: the title of each book without a cover is sent to Google Books.
- **Define**: the selected word is sent to an online dictionary service (Oxford Dictionaries); the pronunciation, if you play it, is fetched from the address that service gives.
- **Search for** › **Google**, **Wikipedia**, **Youtube**: the selected text is handed to your web browser.

On the computer itself, the reader sends Tinta4PlusU the request to refresh the e-ink screen; nothing about the book is in it.

## With the other apps

- **Tinta4PlusU**: receives the refresh request every ten page turns. If it is not running, the request is dropped silently.
- **Files**: the applications menu entry lets the file manager offer eInk Reader for PDF, EPUB, Kindle and comic-book files.

## On the computer

Keyboard shortcuts valid everywhere in the reader:

| Shortcut | Effect |
|---|---|
| Ctrl+O | Add books |
| Ctrl+W | Close the current book's tab |
| Ctrl+D | Distraction-free mode on and off |
| Ctrl+Q | Quit |
| Delete | In the library, remove the selected books |

In a book: Ctrl+B (bookmarks), Ctrl+N (annotations), Ctrl+F (search), F (full screen), Esc, and the keys listed with each kind of book above.

From a terminal:

- `eink-reader book.epub other.pdf` adds the named files to the library and opens them.
- `eink-reader debug` starts with a detailed journal, after emptying the previous one.

## Limits

- No scrolling mode for text.
- The ghost-clearing comes every ten page turns, not at each page, and only through Tinta4PlusU on a ThinkBook Plus.
- **Double page** for text is not remembered: each book opens in single page.
- PDF and DjVu pages are pictures: no text selection, no search, no annotations, no definitions in them. The same goes for comics.
- Links inside a book do nothing when clicked.
- A Markdown or text file is one single chapter; its title is the file name.
- The dictionary knows English, Spanish and Hindi only.
- The interface is only partly translated, and the program calls itself Lector.

## Messages and problems

- **Delete book(s)?** (window **Confirm deletion**): confirms removing the selected books from the library.
- **Delete database and exit?** (window **Confirm**): see **Reset Application**.
- **Adding books...**, **Checking library folders**, **Parsing files**: the reader is at work; wait for the line to disappear.
- **Library scan in progress...**: shown on the scan button while a scan runs; a second scan cannot be started before the first ends.
- **No definitions found in EN** (or ES, HI): the dictionary service gave no answer for that word, or could not be reached.
- **&lt;No annotations set&gt;**: you have not created any annotation style yet.
- **A warning button at the bottom of the window**: a file could not be read. Click it: the **Log** tab names the file, for example "Unsupported extension: …" or "Error initializing: …".
- **My PDF, DjVu, Markdown or text files are not offered.** The component for that format is missing; see "Which formats you get". At start, the journal says for instance "pymupdf is not installed. Will be unable to load PDF files."
- **A cover is pale with a warning sign.** The file was moved, renamed or deleted. Put it back, or remove the book and add it again from its new place.
- **Scan Library opens the settings instead of scanning.** No library folder is ticked yet.
- **The e-ink screen keeps ghost images.** Check that Tinta4PlusU is running with the e-ink screen enabled; the refresh comes after ten page turns.
- **The installer stops with "This installer must be run as root (sudo bash installer.sh)".** Start it with `sudo`.
- **The installer ends with "Missing dependencies:".** It lists what could not be installed; the formats that depend on it will be missing.


# Tinta4PlusU

*Linux 0.1.0 alpha · Lenovo ThinkBook Plus Gen 4 IRU*

## What it is

Tinta4PlusU drives the colour e-ink screen on the lid of the Lenovo ThinkBook Plus Gen 4 IRU under Linux. From one small window you switch the computer from its OLED screen to the e-ink screen and back, clear the ghost images, choose between a fast and a clean display mode, and set the frontlight.

It is made of two parts: the window you see, and a helper that works with administrator rights, because the e-ink screen's controller and the frontlight can only be reached that way. The window starts the helper and asks for your password when it does.

It is an experimental program, a variant of Tinta4Plus by Jon Cox. It writes directly to the laptop's hardware, was developed without any documentation from Lenovo or E Ink, and comes with no warranty: its own licence text warns that it can make the hardware misbehave. It works on this one laptop model only.

The interface is in English only.

## First start

### What is needed

- A Lenovo ThinkBook Plus Gen 4 IRU.
- Ubuntu 24.04 or later, or a system derived from it (Xubuntu, Kubuntu, Linux Mint).
- One of these desktops: GNOME, Cinnamon, XFCE or KDE Plasma. Only GNOME and XFCE in an X11 session have been properly tested; GNOME and KDE Plasma under Wayland, Cinnamon and KDE Plasma under X11 are supported but may have issues.
- For the frontlight: Secure Boot turned off in the BIOS. The e-ink screen itself works with Secure Boot on.

### Turning Secure Boot off

1. Restart the laptop and press **Enter** repeatedly right after power-on to get the boot menu.
2. Press the function key that opens the BIOS settings.
3. Go to **Security**, then **Secure Boot**, and set it to **Disabled**.
4. Save and restart.

Turning Secure Boot off lowers the protection of the computer at start-up; the licence text says so.

### Installing

1. Download the ZIP from gallaz.ch/eink and extract it.
2. Open a terminal in the extracted folder and run `sudo bash installer.sh`.
3. At **Choose installation mode [1/2]:** answer **2** (Python scripts). Choice 1 (compiled binary) is only available after building the program yourself with `bash build.sh`.
4. At **Install PolicyKit policy? [y/N]** answer **y** to avoid, in the installer's words, "re-entering your password every time the helper daemon starts"; the first start still asks for it. The default answer is no.

The installer recognises your desktop, adds the components needed, copies the program to `/opt/tinta4plusu`, creates the command `tinta4plusu`, adds **Tinta4PlusU** to the applications menu and makes it start at each login. It writes what it does to `/tmp/tinta4plusu-install.log`.

At the end it checks what is missing. If it warns that neither `feh` nor `imv` was found, install one of them (`sudo apt install feh` for an X11 session, `sudo apt install imv` for Wayland): the program needs one to show the privacy image described below.

To remove the program: `sudo bash installer.sh --uninstall`.

### The first launch

Start **Tinta4PlusU** from the applications menu, or type `tinta4plusu` in a terminal.

1. A window **End User License Agreement** opens ("Please read carefully"). Read the text to the end: the **Agree** button only becomes available once you have scrolled to the bottom. **Disagree** closes the program. The window cannot be closed any other way. Your agreement is remembered; the window does not come back.
2. The main window opens and starts the helper: the system asks for your password.
3. When the status line reads **Status: Connected to helper daemon**, the program is ready. Nothing has changed on the screens yet: the big button reads **eInk Disabled**.

When the program is started automatically at login, it does not start the helper, so that no password is asked at that moment. The status line reads **Status: Click 'Connect to Helper' to start**; press **Connect to Helper** when you want to use the e-ink screen.

## The screens

### The main window

The window is titled **ThinkBook E-Ink Control**. From top to bottom:

- The **status line**, beginning with **Status:**, says what the program is doing.
- The **Secure Boot** indicator: **Secure Boot: OFF** on green, **Secure Boot: ON** on red, or **Secure Boot: Unknown** on grey.
- **Connect to Helper**: starts the helper, or reconnects to it. It is greyed out once connected.

The frame **Display Control**:

- The big button, orange with **eInk Disabled** or green with **eInk Enabled**: switches from one screen to the other. During the countdown it reads **Flip now... 5**, then 4, 3, 2, 1.
- **Refresh eInk (Clear Ghosts)**: redraws the whole e-ink screen once to remove ghost images.
- **Set Dynamic**: the fast mode, for scrolling and moving things, with more ghosting.
- **Set Reading**: the clean mode, for text, slower and with less ghosting.
- **Refresh period (s):** a slider from 0 to 60, in steps of 5. Above 0, the program clears the ghosts by itself at that interval, in seconds.
- **Display Scale:** a slider from 1.00 to 2.00, in steps of 0.05: how large things are drawn on the e-ink screen.
- **Autoswitch theme (HighContrast ↔ Adwaita-dark)**: when ticked, the desktop takes a high-contrast theme on the e-ink screen and a dark theme back on the OLED.

The three buttons **Refresh eInk (Clear Ghosts)**, **Set Dynamic** and **Set Reading** are greyed out while the e-ink screen is off.

The frame **Frontlight Control**:

- **Brightness (0-8):** a slider for the light of the e-ink screen, 0 being off. When the helper connects, the slider takes the value the laptop actually has.
- With Secure Boot on, a red notice appears here and the slider is greyed out: "⚠ Secure Boot is enabled. Frontlight controls disabled. Please disable Secure Boot in BIOS (Press ENTER during boot)."

The frame **Activity Log** lists, with the time, everything the program does: successes in green (lines with ✓), errors in red (lines with ✗ or ⚠).

At the bottom: **Version 0.1.0 alpha**, and **Buy Me A Coffee**, which opens in your web browser the donation page of the original author.

### The floating refresh button

While the e-ink screen is on, a square light-blue button with a circular arrow (⟳) floats on the left edge of the screen, halfway down, half transparent and above all windows. Click it to clear the ghosts. Drag it to put it elsewhere. It goes away when you return to the OLED. Under Wayland it may be missing; the log then says "Floating refresh button unavailable (Wayland limitation)".

### The privacy image

An e-ink screen keeps its last picture when it is switched off. So that your last window does not stay readable on the lid, the program fills the e-ink screen with one of three pictures, chosen at random, just before switching it off.

## How to

### Switch to the e-ink screen

1. Press **eInk Disabled**.
2. The log says "Flip your screen to eInk now!" and the button counts down five seconds: turn the screen over during that time.
3. Wait for the button to turn green and read **eInk Enabled**.

In order, the program turns the e-ink screen on at the chosen scale, lights the frontlight at the slider's level, sets the fast mode (Dynamic), turns the OLED off and attaches the touch layer to the e-ink screen. If **Autoswitch theme** is ticked, the desktop takes the high-contrast theme.

### Return to the OLED screen

1. Press **eInk Enabled**.
2. The log says "Flip your screen to OLED now!" and the button counts down five seconds.
3. The e-ink screen shows the privacy image for about three seconds; then the frontlight and the e-ink screen go off, and the OLED comes back with the scale it had before.

Closing the program's window while the e-ink screen is on does the same thing first, without the countdown.

### Clear ghost images

Any of these, while the e-ink screen is on:

- press **Refresh eInk (Clear Ghosts)**;
- click the floating ⟳ button;
- press the **Help** key of the keyboard (Fn+F9);
- set **Refresh period (s):** above 0 to have it done at regular intervals.

### Read comfortably, or scroll comfortably

Press **Set Reading** before reading text; press **Set Dynamic** before scrolling or working with windows. The e-ink screen is in Dynamic mode each time it is switched on.

### Set the frontlight

Move the **Brightness (0-8):** slider. Or, while the e-ink screen is on, use the brightness keys of the keyboard (Fn+F6 for more, Fn+F5 for less): they then act on the frontlight, one level per press.

### Make everything larger or smaller on the e-ink screen

Move the **Display Scale:** slider. The log confirms "will apply on next display switch": the new scale is used the next time you switch to the e-ink screen. Under GNOME with Wayland, the nearest scale the system accepts is used.

### Check the touch layer

The downloaded folder contains a test tool that is not installed with the rest. With the e-ink screen on, run `python3 touch_diagnostic.py` from that folder: it shows nine targets to tap and reports the gap between each target and where the touch was registered.

## Settings

| Setting | What it changes | Choices | At first |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Refresh period (s):** | Interval of the automatic ghost-clearing | 0 (never) to 60 seconds, by 5 | 0 |
| **Display Scale:** | Size of everything on the e-ink screen | 1.00 to 2.00, by 0.05 | 1.00 |
| **Autoswitch theme (HighContrast ↔ Adwaita-dark)** | Changes the desktop theme with the screen | Ticked or not | Not ticked |
| **Brightness (0-8):** | Frontlight level | 0 to 8 | The level the laptop has |

The first three are remembered from one session to the next. The brightness is not stored by the program: it is read from the laptop.

Under KDE Plasma, the two themes are the colour schemes Breeze High Contrast and Breeze Dark.

One value has no control in the window: the length of the countdown before a switch, 5 seconds. It is the line `flip_countdown` of the settings file; 0 removes the countdown.

## Your data

The program uses no network and has no account. The only thing it opens on the internet is the donation page, when you press **Buy Me A Coffee**.

- Settings: the file `~/.config/Tinta4PlusU/settings`. Your agreement to the licence: the empty file `~/.config/Tinta4PlusU/agree`.
- Journals, rewritten at each start: `/tmp/tinta4plusu.log` (the window) and `/tmp/TintaHelper.log` (the helper).
- Uninstalling removes the program, its menu and start-up entries and the PolicyKit policy; it leaves the folder `~/.config/Tinta4PlusU`.

While the helper runs, other programs on the same computer can ask it things: any program run by any user of the computer can send it the same commands as the window, and any web page open in a browser on the computer can ask for a ghost-clearing (nothing else). This is how eInk Reader and PageTurn work with it.

## With the other apps

- **eInk Reader** asks for a ghost-clearing every ten page turns.
- **PageTurn** (the Chrome extension) asks for one every five page turns.

Both need the helper to be running and the e-ink screen to be on; otherwise their requests are ignored.

## On the computer

Keys that work in any program while the e-ink screen is on (they go through the helper):

| Key | Effect |
|---|---|
| Help (Fn+F9) | Clear ghost images |
| Brightness up (Fn+F6) | Frontlight one level up |
| Brightness down (Fn+F5) | Frontlight one level down |

The command `tinta4plusu --autostart` starts the window without starting the helper; it is what the login entry uses.

The helper stops by itself when it has heard nothing from the window for 60 seconds. If the window loses the helper, it says **Status: Helper disconnected - attempting restart...** and starts it again; the password may be asked.

After the laptop wakes from sleep, and three seconds after the program starts, it checks that the right screen is on, at the right size, with the keyboard layout and the touch layer as they should be, and corrects what is wrong. What it corrected appears in the log on a line beginning with "↻ Post-resume:" or "Startup check:".

## Limits

- One laptop model only: the Lenovo ThinkBook Plus Gen 4 IRU.
- Alpha quality; only GNOME and XFCE under X11 have been properly tested.
- The frontlight cannot be controlled with Secure Boot on.
- According to the licence text, it probably does not work correctly with an external monitor connected.
- If a switch back to the OLED fails, or the computer is turned off while the e-ink screen is on, the e-ink screen keeps showing its last picture until a full restart. Check the lid before leaving the laptop in view.
- The floating button may be missing under Wayland.
- The licence text shown at first launch still describes an older state of the program (Xubuntu with XFCE only); the list of supported desktops above is the current one.

## Messages and problems

- **Secure Boot Enabled** (window): "Secure Boot is currently enabled in your BIOS. Frontlight controls require direct hardware access which is blocked by Secure Boot." The window lists the steps to turn it off and adds: "Note: E-Ink display controls will continue to work normally."
- **Error** (window): "Not connected to helper daemon. Please click 'Connect to Helper' first." Press **Connect to Helper** and give your password.
- **Status: Launching helper daemon (password required)...**: the system is waiting for your password.
- **Status: Failed to launch helper: Helper process exited (code …) — password cancelled or pkexec failed**: the password was cancelled or wrong, or the desktop has nothing to ask it with. Under GNOME or Cinnamon, install it with `sudo apt install policykit-1-gnome`, then log out and in again, and press **Connect to Helper**.
- **Failed to launch helper: Helper started but socket not available after 12s**, or **Helper not found: …**: the helper did not start; reinstall the program, and look at `/tmp/TintaHelper.log`.
- **EULA Not Found**: "EULA file not found: … The application will now exit." The licence text is missing from the installation; install again.
- **Switch already in progress...**: you pressed the big button during the countdown; wait.
- **✗ Command failed: …**: the helper could not carry out an order; the line gives the reason.
- **Frontlight enable failed (readback mismatch)**, or the same for the brightness: the laptop did not confirm the value. The light is usually on all the same; look at the screen.
- **Warning: Could not display privacy image**, or **Warning: Privacy image not found: …**: install `feh` or `imv`, and check the e-ink screen before leaving the laptop.
- **⚠ Failed to enable E-Ink display on eDP-2**, and similar lines naming eDP-1 (the OLED) or eDP-2 (the e-ink screen): the desktop refused the change of screen; try again, and see whether your desktop is among the tested ones.
- **The OLED stays black after switching back.** Close the lid and open it again.
- **The laptop no longer responds, or the e-ink screen or the frontlight behave erratically.** Usually a restart is enough. If not, reset the laptop's embedded controller: turn the laptop off and unplug the charger; with a pin, hold the small reset button in the hole under the laptop, next to the fan grille, for at least 60 seconds; then hold the power button for 60 seconds; then press the power button normally (the screen can stay blank for up to a minute). Afterwards check in the BIOS that Secure Boot is still off.
- **The installer says "This installer must be run as root (sudo bash installer.sh)".** Start it with `sudo`.
- **The installer says "Installation failed at line …".** The reason is in `/tmp/tinta4plusu-install.log`. After correcting it, run the installer again; `sudo bash installer.sh --uninstall` cleans a half-finished installation.
